<meta>This is the beginning of the Sixth Epistle. The text focuses on the mystical nature of humanity as a reflection of both the visible and invisible worlds.</meta>

<page-num>83</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

# THE
# SIXTH EPISTLE:
---
## BEING
---
### A very deep and Philosophic Letter,
## WRITTEN
---
### To Doctor **GODFREY FREU-**
### **DEN-HAMMER**; and to Master
### **JOHN HEUSER**.
---
### Concerning the Knowledge of God,
### and of all things.
## ALSO
---
### A Short Declaration of the
### False and True **LIGHT**.
---

> *The Salvation of our Lord Jesus Christ by his entrance and manifestation in humanity, work with his love in us all.*

---

1. Much respected Sirs, and dearly beloved brethren in Christ; when God opens the right understanding to us by His grace, so that we are able to know and understand aright the Image of God (namely, Man)—what he is in body, soul, and spirit—then we acknowledge that he is the visible, and also the invisible spiritual world; namely, an extract <note>original: "Extract"; in this context, it refers to a quintessential summary or a being drawn out from the whole of creation.</note> of all the three principles of the Divine Being.

2. In whom the hidden God, through the expression and impression <note>The text cuts off here at the catchword "fion" for "impression."</note>

<sig>M 2</sig>

<summary>In this introductory page of the Sixth Epistle, the author addresses his colleagues and explains that humanity is a spiritual microcosm, containing the essence of both the visible and invisible worlds and the three principles of God.</summary>
<keywords>Godfrey Freuden-Hammer, John Heuser, Image of God, Three Principles, Divine Being, spiritual world, mysticism, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

--- Page 2 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "expressed Word" and how God’s internal wisdom is manifested in the physical world and within humanity.</meta>

<page-num>84</page-num>

# The Sixth Epistle.

[...The manifestation] of his various *distinct* Power and eternal *Wisdom* has set forth himself in a visible *Image*, through which he does conceive <note>original: "Idea"; here used as a verb meaning to form an internal image or concept</note>, form, and shape, in the same *Being*, the wonders of the expressed *Word*; in this way, the *Word* of his powers makes itself *substantial* <note>original: "essentiall"</note>.

3. And so in or by man, he has represented and set forth an *Image* of his speaking and outspoken *Substantial* word, in which the *Divine Knowledge* <note>original: "Science"</note> with the various divisions of the *Eternal* speaking is contained.

4. And from this, he has the understanding and knowledge of all things, so that he is able to understand the union <note>original: "conjunction"</note> of Nature, and also its separation; for no spirit has any higher rule or deeper reach than in its own Mother <note>the source or origin of its being</note> from which it takes its *Origin*, and in that foundation <note>original: "ground"</note> it stands or remains in its *Center*.

5. We see this in the *Creatures* of the *Stars* and *Elements*, that their understanding and knowledge is no higher than that of their Mother in whom they live; each leads its life according to the nature and condition of its Mother, within which it is bounded in the distinct division of the expressed *Word*. And no *Creature* in the four Elements whose *Origin* is not out of the *Eternal Knowledge* is able to attain the knowledge of the hidden spiritual World of Power, except for *Man* alone, who with his soul and understanding spirit is contained in the eternal *Breathing* <note>original: "Spiration [or generation]"</note> of the Divine power and various distinct divisions of the eternal *Word* of God.

6. Therefore, *human knowledge* receives both good and evil into the *Center* of its understanding, and does *conceive* and shape itself in both good and evil, making itself *substantial* within them; and so, with and by that knowledge, it brings itself into will, desire, and essence.

7. In this way, the *unfathomable <term>Abysfall</term> will* <note>The "Abyssal will" refers to the "Ungrund" or the bottomless, preceding will of God in Boehme's philosophy</note>—arising out of the eternal word of various distinct divisions—brings itself into an *Existence* <note>original: "Ens"</note> and *Being* within the *Creaturely Word* (namely, in the creaturely *knowledge* of the Soul). It does this in the same manner and form as the *Breathing* of God has brought itself through the various divisions of the Eternal will into the visible world, into diverse properties: namely, into good and evil, into love and enmity. This is done so that in such a contrast <note>original: "Contrary"</note>, the *Being* might be distinct, separate, formal, sensible, and perceivable; so that each thing might, in such a *Contrast*, find and perceive itself.

<margin>☞</margin>
8. For in God, all Beings are but one *Being, namely, an eternal One*, or unity, the Eternal only *Good*; which Eternal One, without distinctness <note>original: "severalty"</note>, would not be manifest to itself.

9. Therefore, the same has breathed itself forth out of itself, so that a plurality and distinct variety might arise, which variety or distinctness has brought itself into a specific *WILL* and properties.

<summary>Boehme explains that humanity is a unique image of God's "spoken word," capable of understanding both the physical and spiritual realms because of its divine origin. He argues that the diversity and conflict (good and evil) in the world are necessary for the Eternal One to become self-aware and perceivable.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Eternal Word, Divine Science, Image of God, Abyssal Will, Duality, Self-Perception, Spiration, Ens</keywords>

--- Page 3 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the Divine Word differentiates itself into the physical and spiritual properties of the world.</meta>

# The Sixth Epistle.

properties; the properties into desires, and the desires into beings.

10. Inasmuch as all visible things (both *animals, vegetables,* and *minerals*) arise from the diversity and comprehensive impression, or formation of the expressed *Word*, out of the knowledge <note>original: "Science," used here in the sense of an active principle or inner wisdom</note> of the great *Mystery*; every thing comes from the *experience of the differentiated Word*.

11. Every thing has its own separation within itself; the center of every thing is [ a ] *Spirit* from the origin of the *Word*; the separation in a thing is a unique self-will of its own impression, or forming, where each spirit brings itself into being, according to its *essential desire*.

12. The form and features of bodies arise from the experience of the will, where the center of every thing (being a particle or spark from the expressed word) again expresses or speaks itself forth, and brings itself into a various, distinct particularity, in the manner and form of the Divine *Speaking* [ or the operation of the *Eternal Word* in its *generation* and *manifestation* ].

<margin>☛</margin> 13. Now if there were no *free will* in such *Speaking*, then the *Speaking* would have a law, and would stand confined and compelled, and no *desire* or *free imagination* might arise. Then the speaking would be *finite* and *just beginning* <note>original: "inchoative"</note>, which it is not.

14. But it is a *spiration* <note>original: "Spiration"; a breathing out or spiritual emanation</note> of the *Abyss*, and a distinct separation of the *Eternal Stillness* [ or immobility ], an outpouring or distribution of itself where the particularity again stands in its own distinct separation in a peculiar *self-will*, and is again an expressing of itself. From this, *Nature* and *creaturely life* take their origin, and hence in every thing a self-will has arisen.

15. Thus, every thing brings itself from its own experience into form, feature, and shape, and likewise into life and operation as it stands in its center, in the *universal experience*; namely, in the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum," the chaotic potential from which the universe is manifested</note>, in the *Mother* of all beings.

16. This we see in the Earth, which in the beginning of its materials rose out of the separation of the *Divine Breathing* in a spiritual manner; where the separation of the word has formed itself by its own proper will into an *Ens* <term>Ens: a philosophical term for a being, entity, or essential substance</term> and being, and so by the formation or impression it has brought itself into the perception of the *essence*.

17. In which perception or sensibility the *magnetic desire* has arisen, so that the properties of the various, distinguishing, or separating will have brought themselves through the desire into bodies, according to and from the nature of the three principles of the *Divine Manifestation*.

18. From which origin the Earth has so many and diverse bodies—

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains how the physical world and individual beings arise from the "Divine Speaking" and the differentiation of the Eternal Word into specific forms. He emphasizes that free will and "self-will" are essential components of this creative process, allowing for the diversity of nature.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Divine Word, Great Mystery, Spiration, Free Will, Nature, Ens, Magnetic Desire, Creation, Mysterium Magnum</keywords>

<lang>English</lang>
<page-num>26</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle</header>
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the three divine principles manifest in physical matter and spiritual centers.</meta>

bodies, good and evil, such as earths, salts, stones, metals, etc.; and such bodies lie mixed in the earth, for the three principles are mutually in one another as one being.

19. And they stand only in three differences or distinct degrees of centers (representing the divine manifestation), where each center does make and produce out of itself its own breathing forth <term>original: spiration</term> [expression], nature, and being; and yet all arise originally from the eternal ONE.

<margin>☞</margin>
20. The first center is the breathing forth or "spiration" of the abyss—that is, God’s speaking, the comprehensive impression, and the divine perception of itself—where God bears and begets himself in Trinity, and speaks himself forth into powers.

21. The second center, or breathing, is the *Expressed Essence of the Divine Power*, and it is called *God’s Wisdom*. Through this same wisdom, the eternal Word breathes itself forth into knowledge—namely, into an infinity of plurality—and brings that plurality of knowledge into imagination, and the imagination into desire, and the desire into nature and strife, until it reaches fire.

22. In that fire, the strife of the painful agony dies away as the fire consumes its own natural right and property. Yet this is not to be understood as a literal death; rather, the power brings itself into sensibility. By killing the self-desire of the properties—through the dying or mortification <term>the "killing" or subduing of the selfish nature</term> of selfhood—it brings itself through the fire into the light.

23. There, in the light, another principle (being the *Grand Mystery* of the true divine manifestation) is understood; and in the fire, the first principle (being eternal nature) is understood. They are two in one, like fire and light.

24. The fire gives the soul, and the power of the light gives the spirit. In this power of the light of the divine breathing—through wisdom and the manifestation of the fire (meaning the spirit’s fire)—the mother of the eternal spirits (namely, of angels and the souls of men) is understood. So too is the spiritual angelic world—that is, the hidden internal world of power, which is the mother of the heavens, stars, and elements; that is, of the external world.

25. The third center is the *Verbum Fiat* <note>Latin: "The Word made" or "Let it be done," referring to the creative Word of God</note> (that is, the *Natural Word of God*) from the power of the first and second principles. It is a <term>Separator</term>, or <term>Creator</term>, and maker of all creatures in the internal and external worlds, each according to its own nature.

26. This <term>Separator</term> or <term>Spirator</term> <note>One who breathes forth</note> of the division of the divine powers has spoken itself forth from itself, out of the first and second principles—namely, out of the fiery and light world—and also

<summary>Boehme describes the three "centers" of divine manifestation, explaining how God's internal "breathing" (spiration) moves from the abyss through fire and light to create the spiritual and material worlds. He defines the soul as originating in fire and the spirit in light, all overseen by the creative "Verbum Fiat."</summary>
<keywords>three principles, centers, spiration, divine wisdom, fire and light, mortification, soul and spirit, Verbum Fiat, Jacob Boehme, cosmology</keywords>

--- Page 5 ---
<lang>English</lang>
<page-num>87</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle</header>
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Three Principles and how the spiritual world manifests into the material world. The catchword "sensibility" refers to the transition of spirit into sensory existence.</meta>

...out of the *impression* and enclosure of the adumbration <note>original: "Adumbration"; meaning a faint representation or shadowy precursor</note>—that is, out of the darkness. By the separation of the spiration <note>original: "Spiration"; the divine act of breathing out or spiritual manifestation</note>, it has modeled and formed itself, making itself material, moving, and sensible <note>meaning: able to be perceived by the senses</note>.

27. From this, the Third Principle—which is the visible world with its being and life—has arisen. Likewise, the whole creation of the visible world, whose life and being emerged from the *Three* into a state of existence; namely, out of eternal nature and the Great Mystery. This means it came out of the darkness, fire, and light—specifically, out of love and anger.

28. The fire is called anger, being a source of pain and enmity; and the light signifies, or is called, love, being a sweet yielding and giving of itself. The darkness serves as a separation of knowledge and skill, so that it may be understood what light and life are, as well as what is evil and painful.

<margin>☞</margin>
29. There is a twofold fire and also a twofold light to be understood. Namely, according to the dark impression, there is a *cold fire* and a false light arising through the imagination of the harsh impression. This light has its origin only in the imagination and has no true foundation <note>original: "Ground"</note>.

30. The second fire is a *hot fire*, and it has a fundamental, true light arising from the origin of the *Divine Will*, which also brings itself forth in nature through the fire and into the light.

31. Within this twofold fire and twofold light, two principles and a twofold will are understood.

32. For the false light of the imagination arises out of the self-will of nature—specifically from the impression of properties where those properties test <note>original: "prove"</note> one another. From this, self-lust and an imagination arise, wherein nature models and fancies the *abyss* to itself in its own desire. It desires to bring itself, by its own power and without the will of God, into a dominion and government of its own self-will and rule.

33. In this state, the self-will refuses to be obedient and submissive to the unsearchable and abyssal will of God (which has its origin in itself, apart from nature or any creature, in the *Eternal One*). It will not resign and wholly give itself up to Him to be *one* will with Him, but instead makes itself its own separator and maker.

<margin>* Or, wicked</margin>
34. It draws and makes for itself a *science* <note>original: "Science"; here used in the sense of a self-contained knowledge or state of consciousness</note> within itself and severs itself from God’s will. We can see this in the Devil and also in the *false fallen man* <note>meaning: humanity in its state of sin</note>. Consequently, they were cast out of the divine separation <note>meaning: the holy division or order ordained by God</note>, so that the Devil must remain with his own will in the "separator" of the dark impression, where the Word brings itself into nature and into the pain of sensory existence <note>original: "sensibility"</note>.

<summary>Böhme describes the creation of the visible world from the interplay of divine love and anger, distinguishing between the "true light" of God's will and the "false light" of self-will that led to the fall of the Devil and humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Three Principles, Divine Will, self-will, the Fall, Cold Fire, Hot Fire, Love and Anger, sensory existence, Great Mystery</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Separator" and the "fire-source" within the context of divine and natural light, specifically how the human soul relates to the eternal Word versus the temporal world.</meta>

# The Sixth Epistle.

sensibility; namely, in the origin of the fire-source, which nevertheless cannot reach or obtain the true fire, in which the will of God brings itself into the sensible life and nature; namely, into a shining light.

35. For the Separator <term>the internal agent that divides, forms, and gives qualities to nature</term> of the natural selfhood has no true *Ens* <term>essence or spiritual substance</term> in which its light may remain steadfast; for it does not draw with its desire out of the eternal One (namely, out of the meekness of God) but draws itself into Essence; its light arises only in its own self-possession <note>original: "ownedhood of Selfe"</note>.

31. <note>Note: The original text skips from 35 to 31, likely a printer's error for 36.</note> Therefore there is a difference between God's light and the false light, for God's light arises (or takes its origin) in the Eternal One; namely, in the Essence of the Divine Generation <note>the eternal process of God's self-manifestation</note>, and brings itself through the Will of God into Nature and Being. It is formed and brought by the Divine Separator into an *Ens*, and shines in that same Nature in the darkness, *John 1*.

37. For the formed, or modeled knowledge <note>original: "immodellized Science"</note> is (in respect of the impression) a darkness; but the Divine light illuminates it, so that it becomes a fiery light, in which the breathing out <note>original: "Spiration"</note> or Speaking of God is made manifest in Nature and Creature, and stands in a sensible or perceivable life; of which Saint John speaks, *Chapter 1: The life of man was in him* (namely, in the Word) and *CHRIST* says, *John 8: That he is the light of the World, that gives life to the World.*

38. For without this Divine light proceeding from the generation of the Divine Trinity <note>original: "Try-unity"</note>, there is no constant true light, but only a light of the imagination of the natural impression of self-will.

<margin>☞</margin> 39. Therefore man (being the Image of God) must open and lift up the eyes of the understanding (in which God's light inclines and offers itself to him, and desires to shine in him) and not be like a beast, which does not stand with its Separator in the internal in Eternity, but only in an external figure of the expressed word.

40. Which has only a temporal life in a finite and beginning <note>original: "inchoative"</note> Separator, in which the Eternal Separator represents itself as in a glass (or mirror of Ideas) and brings the Divine Science <term>spiritual knowledge or wisdom</term> into forms and figures, like a pattern or imitation of the Grand Mystery of the Spiritual World, where the Eternal Principles cooperate and play in a type or resemblance, both according to fire and light.

41. And yet man, in respect of his external comprehensible or finite body, stands only in such a fleeting figurative shadow or resemblance; but with his spiritual body he is the true essential Word of the Divine property, in which God speaks and begets His Word,

<summary>This page distinguishes between the false light of "selfhood" and the true Divine light that originates in the Trinity. It emphasizes that while animals exist only as temporal reflections, humans possess a spiritual body capable of housing the eternal Word of God.</summary>

<keywords>Separator, Ens, Divine Generation, Spiration, Try-unity, immodellized Science, image of God, light of the world, John 1, John 8</keywords>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Divine Science" (divine wisdom) and the role of the "Separator" in the creation and fall of humanity.</meta>

<page-num>89</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

and there the Divine Wisdom <note>original: "Divine Science"</note> does distribute, import, impress, form, and beget itself into an image of God.

42. In this image, God is manifest (in a sensory and created being) and dwells and wills there himself. Therefore, man must break his own will and entirely submit himself to God’s will.

<margin>Note.</margin>
43. But if man’s self-will will not do it, then he is more void of understanding and more harmful to himself than the wild earth, which nonetheless stands still for its own Separator <note>original: "Separator"; in Boehme’s philosophy, the internal power that distinguishes and organizes qualities or properties within a being</note> and lets it form and make out of it whatever it pleases.

44. For God has made all things in his divine play or operation out of his Spiration <note>original: "Spiration"; the divine "out-breathing" or creative breath</note>, through and in his Separator, and all things hold or stand still for him; only the false light causes the Separator of the creature to bring itself into a self-will, so that the creature opposes the will of God.

<margin>☞</margin>
45. This false light in man has its foundation in the *will of the Devil*, who, through the insinuation of his false desire, has made man *monstrous*. Thus, man also assumed a false imagination, whereby (*through his own desire*) he has made the false, introduced desire of the Devil *essential* <note>meaning: part of his very essence</note> within him.

46. Consequently, in the human body (which was formed out of the *limus* <note>original: "Limus"; Latin for mud or clay, referring to the "dust of the ground" in Genesis 2:7</note> of the earth in the *Divine Fiat* <note>original: "Divine Fiat"; the Latin command "let it be done" by which God created the world</note>), a bestial *Separator* has arisen. This has revealed or made manifest the properties of all beasts, which is why there are such manifold and diverse lusts, imaginations, desires, and wills in man.

47. This false Separator has advanced itself to the height and gained dominion, attracting all the principles to itself and creating a monster outside of God’s order.

48. In its will and desire, this monstrous image has wholly turned itself away from the will of God and from the *Divine light*. Because of this, the *Divine Ens* <note>original: "Divine Ens"; the holy essence or being</note> from the essence of the holy world vanished or disappeared in him. Man remained only as a *Monster of Heaven* and was made by his Separator a *Beast of all Beasts*, which even now rules in, over, and with all beasts. In him, the Spirit of the World—along with the stars and elements—has obtained rule and dominion.

<margin>☞</margin>
49. Therefore, man now runs and seeks again his first right home or *native country*; for in this condition or property, he stands in mere unquietness. Sometimes he seeks in one thing and then immediately in another, supposing he can bring himself to rest in this *monster*; and yet he runs on in the false, awakened bestial will, which cannot reach the will of God.

50. He now runs only in the false light of his selfhood, which is born in his imagination with a *monstrous Separator* which

<sig>N</sig>
<meta>catchword: maketh</meta>

<summary>This page explains how humanity fell from a divine image into a "monstrous" state by following a self-will inspired by the Devil, leading to the dominance of animalistic desires. It describes the "Separator" as the organizing principle that, when corrupted, subjects man to the influence of the stars and elements rather than God.</summary>

<meta>The text continues from the previous page's discussion of man's fallen state and his subjection to earthly influences.</meta>

<page-num>50</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

...makes him an earthly mind, in which the constellations have their influence and operations; and he has the whole visible world for his enemy.

51. And he stands like a rose in a bush of thorns, which is continually rent, scratched, and torn by the thorns; and yet he could not be called a rose if divine grace had not come to assist and restore him, and once again inspired and given itself into his internal ground <note>internal ground: the innermost core of the soul or being</note>, wherein the *love of God for the new regeneration* is offered to him.

52. Therefore, I say that it is exceedingly necessary for a person to learn to *know themselves* and what they are before they run and seek; otherwise, their seeking will prove to be only a torment, by which they torment and perplex themselves in a false separator <note>separator: in Boehme's theology, the "Separator" is the creative power that differentiates the unity of God into the variety of the natural world; a "false separator" refers to a mind divided from God</note>, and yet cannot obtain rest and true satisfaction.

53. For all these earthly wills [and ways] by which he thinks to bring himself into rest and quietness are only an adverse will, striving against God—namely, the Eternal One.

<margin>☛</margin>
54. For it does not depend on any man’s own willing, going, or running, as *Saint Paul says*, but on *God’s mercy*—that is, on the *grace* that is inspired into him.

55. For without grace, man is dead and blind in respect to God; he is not able to attain any true life unless grace is stirred up, awakened, and revealed in him.

56. Moreover, there can be no awakening in these earthly wills, for they cannot reach or obtain grace, much less awaken it. Therefore, the whole man—in soul and mind—must only immerse himself into *grace* and be willing to become a "nothing" to himself; he must desire nothing but grace, so that grace might be living and working in him, and his own will might be stilled <note>original: "Stupifyed"</note>, overcome, and mortified.

57. As the sun breaks forth in the night [or darkness] and changes the night into day, the same is to be understood concerning man; of which Christ says: "Unless you turn and become like children, you shall not see the kingdom of God"—namely, the divine separator, from which all things have arisen and originally spring.

58. For no knowledge is right or fundamental unless it comes from divine science <note>science: here used in the sense of "scientia" or deep spiritual wisdom</note>, out of the differentiation of the divine spiration <note>original: "Spiration"; the "breathing out" or emanation of God’s spirit into creation</note> from which all things have their origin.

59. Now, if such knowledge is to arise again in man, then the divine separator must stand in an essence like itself—namely, in a divine *ens* <note>ens: a spiritual essence or being</note>, in which the divine word speaks and the divine light shines within that same speaking or breathing out.

60. And even then, human science (which fundamentally <note>original: "inchoatively"; at its starting point or in its elementary stage</note> is...

<summary>Boehme explains that true spiritual peace cannot be found through human effort or earthly will, but only through the immersion of the self into divine grace. He emphasizes that spiritual knowledge must spring from the "Divine Spiration" rather than human intellect.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Divine Grace, Regeneration, Self-Knowledge, Separator, Spiration, Divine Science, St. Paul, Spiritual Awakening</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the human soul, when enlightened by the Divine Word, can understand the internal nature of all things. It emphasizes that true knowledge comes from within (the "internal ground") rather than from external study of "the shell."</meta>

<page-num>91</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>
<sig>N 2</sig>

# The Sixth Epistle.

arisen from the breathing out <note>original: "Spiration"</note> of the Word) may in that same light see, not only itself, but likewise all other natural things according to the division <note>original: "severation"</note> of the Words; and in a magical manner work in and with all things in a Divine way, nature, and property.

<margin>☛</margin>
61. For man is blind to all of God’s works and has no true knowledge unless the Divine <gloss>breathing</gloss> out of speaking is revealed in his internal ground, according to the nature and property of the breathing from which all beings originally proceed.

62. All the searching and seeking of man, by which he tries to find the foundation <note>original: "ground"</note> of a thing, is blind and is performed only on the shell or outside with which the essence of the tree is covered.

63. If there is to be a true finding, then human knowledge <note>original: "Science"</note> must enter into the property of the thing and be able to behold the very Divider <note>original: "Separator"; the internal power that distinguishes and shapes individual things</note>.

64. Therefore, it is the greatest and most laborious misery of mankind that they run and seek entirely in blindness, beginning to seek the shell in the literal word <note>original: "bare letter"</note> and its expression <gloss>or many forms of writings</gloss>, whereas all things are outwardly marked <note>original: "signed"; referring to the "signature" or outward appearance that reveals inner nature</note> as they are in their internal being <note>original: "Ens"</note> and essence. The Divider of all things has shown and revealed himself visibly and formally, so that the Creator *is known in and by the Creation.*

65. For all beings are but one single Being, which has breathed itself out of itself and has divided and shaped <note>original: "severized, and formized"</note> itself; and yet it proceeds out of that same impression or formation into a uniquely distinct center—that is, with each impression and shaping of the desire.

66. Where the divided, parted, and separated will impresses and shapes itself into a unique individuality, a center arises; and in that center, a Divider <note>original: "Separator"</note> or Creator of its own self <gloss>or Being</gloss> appears—namely, a shaper of the re-expressing or re-breathing <note>original: "re-spirating"</note> Will. We see this in the earth, where every herb has its own Divider within itself, which creates and divides it into its specific form.

<margin>☛</margin>
67. Now if man (being God’s image, in whom the Divine speaking is manifest according to Divine knowledge) wishes to search the creatures and gain real understanding and knowledge of them—whether in animals, vegetables, or metals—he must then again obtain grace from God so that the Divine light may shine in his knowledge. Through this, he may be able to pass through the natural light, and then all things will be opened and revealed in his understanding.

68. Otherwise, he continues his seeking like a blind man who speaks of colors but neither sees nor knows any color; this all

<summary>Boehme explains that true knowledge of nature requires Divine enlightenment to see beyond the "shell" of things to their internal "Separator" or formative essence. He argues that because all creation is a "breathing out" of God, man can only understand the natural world if the Divine light shines within his own internal ground.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Spiration, Separator, Signature of All Things, Divine Science, Natural Light, internal ground, Ens, creation as expression</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's discourse on the relationship between human reason, divine light, and the nature of God, specifically how God makes himself visible through nature.</meta>

# The Sixth Epistle.

All ranks and societal orders in the world must consider that they all run blindfolded without the Divine Light, operating only in an astral, sidereal, and imaginary fancy, according to how the constellations of the stars form and frame themselves within and upon Reason.

69. For *Reason* is nothing else but a *human constellation*; it is a dark sketch, or a resemblance of *all the Principles*. It exists only in an imaginary figure and not in Divine Science <note>original: "Science"; here meaning certain, revealed knowledge or a systematic understanding of divine truth</note>.

70. But if the Divine Light is made manifest and shines within it, then the Divine Word begins to speak (or work) within it out of the eternal knowledge [Science or Wisdom]. Then Reason becomes a true dwelling place or vessel for divine knowledge and revelation, and only then may it be rightly and truly used; but when it is void of this, it is nothing more than an *Astrum* <note>original: "Astrum"; a star or celestial influence that governs the physical and mental world</note> of the visible world.

<margin>☞</margin> 71. It is therefore declared to all lovers of the arts (whose <term>Separator</term> <note>In the philosophy of Jacob Böhme and alchemy, the "Separator" is the creative power that divides the unified divine essence into the diverse forms of nature</note> is a highly subtle artist within them) that they should first seek God's love and grace and resign themselves to it, becoming wholly one with it. Otherwise, all their seeking is but a delusion or the chasing of a shadow and is to no purpose; nothing of any fundamental worth is found unless one person entrusts another with something.

72. For it is forbidden to the children of God, in whom grace is revealed, *to cast pearls before swine*, upon pain of eternal punishment.

73. It is only freely granted to them to declare the light and to *show the way of attaining the Pearl*.

74. But to give the divine <term>Separator</term> into a bestial <note>original: "bestiall"; referring to a person driven by animal instincts rather than spiritual light</note> hand is prohibited, unless a man knows the way and the will of that person [who desires it].

75. Thus, my beloved brothers, upon such consideration and instruction, I will (through the permission of divine grace and the cooperation of this present time) briefly explain and represent to you the Divine Mystery: how God, through his Word, has made himself visible, sensible, perceivable, and moreover, created and formal. Please consider this further, yet let it be done as mentioned above; otherwise, I shall be as if I were mute to you, and the blame for that should not be attributed to me.

76. God (as he is in himself) is neither nature nor creature, neither this nor that, neither high nor deep; he is the <term>Abyss</term> and the <term>Byss</term> <note>Böhme uses "Abyss" (Ungrund) to mean the bottomless nothingness before creation, and "Byss" (Grund) to mean the foundation or ground of being</note> of all beings, an eternal One where there is no ground or place. To the creature, in its own strength or capacity, he is a *Nothing*, and yet he is *through all things*.

77. Nature is *his Something* with which he makes himself visible, sensible, and perceivable, both according to eternity and time.

<summary>The author explains that human reason is merely a reflection of the stars unless illuminated by Divine Light, and warns against sharing sacred knowledge with the unworthy. He defines God as both an "Abyss" of nothingness and the "Something" of nature through which He becomes perceivable.</summary>

<keywords>Reason, Divine Light, Separator, Abyss, Byss, Jacob Böhme, spiritual discernment, nature of God, pearls before swine</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the Sixth Epistle of Jakob Böhme, concluding his introductory remarks before presenting a symbolic table or diagram of divine manifestation.</meta>

<page-num>93</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

# The Sixth Epistle.

78. All things have arisen through the Divine Imagination and still exist within such a birth, state, or governance <note>original: "Government"</note>.

79. The four elements likewise have such a foundation [birth or origin] from the imagination of the Eternal One. Regarding this, I will set down a table here showing how one unfolds and breathes itself forth <note>original: "spirate"</note> out of another.

80. In the attached table, the basis <note>original: "ground"</note> of all the mysteries of the Divine Manifestation is portrayed for further consideration. Understanding and comprehending this is not within nature's own ability without the light of God; but it is easily understood by those who are in the Light, and it is childlike [simple and plain].

81. As my writings sufficiently and extensively show, this is only represented here briefly in a figure or *diagram* <note>original: "Scheme"</note>.

82. And so, sirs, I commend and commit you to the greeting of the *love of Jesus Christ*, who by his presence <note>original: "aspect"</note> and greeting is the very key to understanding this table.

->Dated: 11 November,<-
->1623.<-
->J.B.<-

->*Our salvation is in the life of Jesus Christ in us.*<-

<margin>1.</margin>
**N**oble and Right Honorable Sir: [I offer] the Divine light and the internal Divine contemplation of the soul within itself, along with all physical well-being, sincere wishes, and shared desires for fellowship and member-like communion in our *Immanuel* <note>original: *IMMANUEL*; Hebrew for "God with us," referring to Jesus Christ</note>.

<margin>2.</margin>
2. Since I have observed that you are a lover of Divine Wisdom <note>original: "Divine Wisedome"; likely referring to Sophia, a central figure in the author’s mystical theology</note> and also a growing branch on the Tree of Life in God through Christ—in whom all the children of God are members—and since I have further perceived how the Father's drawing <note>The internal spiritual attraction or calling of God</note> has led you to a hungry desire for the true sap and Divine power, and has in some measure enriched you with knowledge of that same Tree of Life; I have therefore taken this opportunity (in the spirit of Christian fellowship and a shared desire for the same Tree of Life in Christ) to greet you once more. Let us mutually encourage one another as laborers placed in the vineyard of Christ and called to this work.

<margin>☞ 3.</margin>
3. Especially let us look well to ourselves in this valley of darkness, and lift up our eyes and heads, as we see the darkness—

ness and the very workings thereof before our eyes; and put ourselves in mind that Christ has taught us that **Our Redemption draws near**; and indeed, *Go out from Babel* <note>Babel here refers to the confused, false religious systems or worldly captivity.</note>, which has for a long time held us captive and imprisoned.

<margin>* Externally</margin>

6. Now, whoever is born of Christ, and lives and walks in him, and puts him on (according to his inner man) in his *suffering, death, and resurrection*; he is a member of his body. From him alone flow the streams of living water through the powerful Word of Christ, which as to the internal ground has become man in him, and speaks itself out of him through the creature in the <gloss>cosmic spirit of the external man</gloss>.

8. 9. For in whatever property the Mystery in man is excited and awakened, such a word utters itself from his powers—as we plainly see that nothing else but vanity is uttered by the wicked.

10. Now, how should there be a good expression and will where the Mystery behind the speaking <gloss>and willing</gloss> is a false ground, poisoned by the Devil in the wrath of nature? This false Mystery can

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the necessity of divine grace and the internal transformation required to be a true Christian, building on the previous argument that human will alone cannot achieve righteousness.</meta>

# The Seventh Epistle.

...can neither will nor do any good that may be acceptable to God unless it is first ignited by God, so that it obtains a godly will and desire; from which a divine expression and the performing of good follows.

11. For Christ said, "*An evil tree cannot bring forth good fruit*" <note>original: "An Evill tree cannot bring forth good fruit" — Matthew 7:18.</note>. How then will he bring forth good fruit where a false tree stands under a deceptive appearance [or glittering hypocrisy]? The purple mantle of Christ <note>A metaphor for the outward appearance of Christianity or the covering of Christ's righteousness used as a disguise by the insincere.</note> has its fruits within it; but what does that matter to a false beast who is full of poison and tries to cover himself with that mantle and take it for his own, yet produces nothing but hellish fruit? Or what right does the nominal <note>original: "Titular"</note> Christian have to boast and glory that he is a Christian when he lives, walks, and exists without Christ?

<margin>☞</margin>
12. No one is a Christian unless he is <term>tinctured anew</term> <note>original: "Tinctured anew"; an alchemical term used here to mean deeply infused, dyed, or transformed in nature by a new spiritual quality.</note> with the spirit of Christ and has sprung forth out of God’s love, so that the grace of God in Christ is manifest in the mystery of his life—as far as the soul is concerned—and cooperates and wills within the human life.

<margin>In the spirit of the world <note>original: "In Spiritu Mundi."</note></margin>
13. Now, if he wishes to become such a person, he must turn away from his *imagining in the cosmic spirit* <note>The "cosmic spirit" (spirit of the world) refers to worldly desires and earthly influences that distract the soul from God.</note> with which the soul is covered and disguised, entering into earthly workings. He [must] become like a child who only turns with its whole affection toward its mother and draws into itself the mother's *milk* of grace. From this, a new <term>ens</term> <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for "being," "essence," or "substance."</note> grows, in which the life of grace arises. That is, the *imputed grace* must be born and become man in him as to the internal ground. Without this, no one is a *Christian*, no matter how many devout shows he makes. Though he may dissemble, flatter, and do what he will, his sins must be forgiven him only through the <term>divine colloquy</term> <note>original: "Divine Alloquy"; a direct conversation, communication, or "speaking-to" between God and the soul.</note>, or through <term>spiration</term> <note>original: "Spiration"; the act of breathing in or being inspired by the Holy Spirit.</note> within himself.

<margin>* Or, breeding or hatching.</margin>
14. For when Christ is conceived in the inspired [in-spoken] word of grace—which the soul takes and impresses into itself from His promise—then the foundation is laid in the corrupt or decayed mystery for becoming a child of God. Then the divine <term>impregnation</term> <note>In this context, the spiritual fertilization or the beginning of the growth of the "new man" within the believer.</note> begins and proceeds, in which the humanity of Christ is conceived and born; this alone is the *temple of the Holy Spirit*. From this new birth, the fiery soul eats God’s bread, which comes from heaven; and without this, man has no life in him (*John 6*). No hypocrite under the purple mantle of Christ can enjoy this, but only that person who is not born of flesh and blood, nor of the will of man, but is reborn <note>original: "regenerate"</note> of God, in whom the Word of God (from which the first man was created) speaks, rules, lives, and wills.

15. For the life of man was in the beginning in the Word (*John 1*), when it was inspired or breathed into the created image. But when the life turned away from the speaking of the Word into a peculiar self-willing and speaking of good and evil—that is, into its...

The author emphasizes that without this internal transformation and "breathing in" of grace, outward religious rituals are merely hypocrisy.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of how the human soul, having fallen into its own selfish desires and imagination, must return to the "Speaking Word" of God to avoid eternal separation.</meta>

<page-num>97</page-num>

# The Seventh Epistle.

its own lust and calculated imagination, then the first good will in the creature to express God again perished. And now he must enter again into the ***first Speaking Word*** <note>Boehme refers to the "Speaking Word" as the creative, divine power or Logos that originally spoke all things into existence.</note> and speak with God, or he is eternally **without God**.

16. This present world cannot nor will not understand this, for it has wholly and fully turned itself into a selfish way of speaking to the pleasures of the flesh. It speaks forth from self-will nothing but mere earthliness and transitory things, such as honor, might, power, and authority; moreover, it speaks pride, covetousness, envy, and malice. It utters nothing else but the cunning, crafty serpent with its young; and when these young ones cannot get or uphold what the self-will desires, then it speaks from a place of cunning, mischievous malice and iniquity. It speaks **with money**, through selfish power and violence, and **many thousand soldiers** who must maintain its will by force, so that the self-will that has departed from God may be truly upheld—as we now see before our eyes. By this very expression, this self-will is also beaten down, and it kills and destroys itself.

17. Therefore, beloved sir and fellow member of the Tree of Life of Christ, I would entreat you in a Christian way and stir you up (as one member is bound to do for another) in the present expression <gloss>[ways and courses]</gloss> of the world—where the ***<term>Turba Magna</term>*** <note>Latin: "Great Disturbance" or "Great Turmoil." In Boehme’s theology, this refers to the destructive awakening of God's wrath or the chaotic breakdown of a system.</note> also plays and expresses itself, and where a great contesting, pulling down, or degrading shall occur—constantly and steadfastly to keep yourself in the internal speaking of God's mercy. Continually enter into your internal ground, and in no way be persuaded or misled by the serpent toward the false speaking of **brother-slaughter**; but you (as a famous lord) must continually behold yourself in the looking glass <note>mirror</note> of Christ's process and doctrine.

<margin>\* Punishment, or severe execution. Note.</margin>

18. For this present speaking is spoken in the wrath of God through His awakened and kindled anger; it is very evil and dangerous to have a hand and voice in it, especially when the \* ***<term>Turba</term>*** must be spoken. It is altogether unfaithful; it devours the father and mother that bring it forth, and it is a broom <note>original: besom</note> of God’s anger.

19. Also, great care must be taken regarding accepting and joining any of the **so-called religions** for which men contend and fight. Do not assent with a conscience of faith to whichever party gets the victory; for there is no other true faith which saves except only **CHRIST IN US**. He alone destroys sin in us and bruises the head of the serpent’s imagination within us; He arises in God’s righteousness (which He has fulfilled in us with His blood) from the **sleep of death**.

20. Christ must arise from death in our poor soul—namely, in a new humanity which walks and dwells with and in Christ in—

<summary>Boehme warns against the worldly "self-will" that leads to violence and political strife, urging the reader to avoid the "Turba Magna" (divine wrath/turmoil) and focus on the internal presence of Christ rather than outward religious factions.</summary>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the Seventh Epistle of Jacob Boehme, concluding a spiritual exhortation and transitioning into practical requests regarding the distribution of his manuscripts.</meta>

### The Seventh Epistle

Heaven is within the new man; from there, the works of Love proceed, as is fitting and necessary for the children of God.

<margin>👉</margin> 21. And although the external man lives in earthly weakness and infirmities, yet that does not take away the Temple of Jesus Christ. For Christ, in the internal foundation <note>original: "ground," referring to the *Ungrund* or the deepest part of the soul in Boehme's theology</note>, continually bruises the head of the serpent in the flesh; and Christ must be continually stung by the serpent in the heel, until we are freed from this beastly nature.

22. Furthermore, dear Sir, I entreat you in a Christian, brotherly way to seriously take notice of these present times in the true fear of God. If you are pleased to accept my good intentions, you will never regret it. For I speak what has been made known to me from the *Most High*, out of His *grace*. Please consider it diligently and let the Spirit of God be your *meditation*.

<margin>👉</margin> 23. For a time shall shortly come when good friends shall be sifted and tested so that we might stand steadfast in Christ. I would lovingly remind you of this; for the time of *refreshment* <note>A biblical reference to a period of spiritual renewal or the millennium</note> comes soon after, when faithful people shall love one another entirely. I continually hunger and thirst for this love, and it is my sincere and constant wish that *Babel* <note>Symbolizing the corrupt, divided church or worldly confusion</note> may soon come to her end, and Christ may come into the *Valley of Jehoshaphat* <note>A reference to Joel 3:2, traditionally associated with the final judgment</note> so that all nations might see and praise Him.

24. I entreat you to send my three treatises (namely: 1. *Of Repentance*; 2. *Of the New Birth*; 3. *Of Resignation*—some of which I gave you myself, and the rest I sent by Mr. *Rudolph*) to Mr. *Rudolphus* of *Gersdorf*. I have written to him asking that he send them to me at *Zagan* <note>Now Żagań, Poland</note> to Mr. *Christianus Bernhard*, from whom I shall receive them by one means or another. Or, if you yourself have any reason to go toward *Zagan*, please send them to Mr. *Christianus Bernhard*, who lives at the marketplace. A year ago, he was a <term>customs officer</term> <note>original: "Customer," a collector of customs duties</note>; he is a young companion of the <term>Theosophical School</term> <note>Boehme’s circle of followers who studied "divine wisdom" or theosophy</note>. I have a convenient opportunity to reach him every week.

25. These treatises are greatly desired by the "Lovers" <note>Likely referring to the "Lovers of God" or "Lovers of Wisdom," the informal network of Boehme's readers</note> and may do much good. I pray you send them at the first opportunity, for it is of great importance. When you find convenient leisure to study, I will send you something deeper, for I have written without ceasing this autumn and winter. I commend you to the love of Jesus Christ and His gracious protection.

->Dated, 19 Feb. 1623.<-

### THE
## EIGHTH EPISTLE:
### An Answer to four
# QUESTIONS.

1. *Of the Serpent, which after the Curse must eat Earth and creep upon its Belly.*
2. *Of Paradise and the Garden of Eden.*
3. *Whether the beasts (since they were in Paradise and, moreover, wholly earthly) did also feed upon Paradisiacal <note>original: "Paradiſicall"; fruit belonging to the spiritual state of Paradise</note> Fruit?*
4. *Whether the beasts before the Curse were as wild, hairy, and rugged as they are now?*

---

->Our Salvation is in the Life of Jesus Christ within us.<-

---

**W**ORTHY, much respected, very Learned, and beloved Friend and Brother, in Christ our only life; I heartily wish you a happy New Year, that you may begin the same in a Divine will, in the drawing of the Father to Christ <note>A reference to John 6:44: "No man can come to me, except the Father which hath sent me draw him."</note>, and likewise finish it in this time through an effectual working power of the spirit of Christ in his Vineyard; and that many grapes may grow in the Garden of Christ within you, and that God would be pleased to preserve you in the bundle of the living <note>original: "bundle of the living"; a biblical idiom from 1 Samuel 25:29 meaning to be protected and among the saved</note>, in this year when the constellation of his Wrath draws the Sword <note>Likely referring to the political upheavals of the Thirty Years' War or astrological omens interpreted as divine judgment</note>; as I do not doubt,

<summary>This page introduces the Eighth Epistle, which provides answers to four questions regarding the state of the Serpent, Paradise, and animals before and after the Curse. The author begins with a warm, spiritual New Year's blessing to the recipient.</summary>

<keywords>Serpent, Paradise, Garden of Eden, Curse, Paradisiacal Fruit, Salvation, Vineyard of Christ, Constellation of Wrath</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>The previous page concludes with a call for the recipient to show the fruits of their faith, continuing the metaphor of the vine and branches.</meta>

<lang>en</lang>
<page-num>100</page-num>
-> # The Eighth Epistle. <-

...but that you (as a cooperative branch on the vine of Christ) will show yourself in good, new, and heavenly fruits.

2. For the gate of grace and knowledge likewise stands in a specific motion [and opens itself in a unique desire and affection] so that the children of Christ might also reap their harvest [and fruits of Christian love and piety, just as the children of Babel <note>original: "Babel"; refers to the "City of Confusion," Boehme's term for the divided and worldly institutional churches</note> reap their harvest of envy, contention, and desolation] if they would only work seriously and not lie so faint and sluggish in the sleep of the Antichrist—as the grace of the Most High has given me to know and understand.

3. I intended to have spoken with you again when I last returned, but by the guidance of God, I was led another way. I shall still do so whenever I come that way, if the opportunity permits.

4. I also think of the conversation we had at our *meeting*, where many things were questioned. Because of the many objections made then (which caused much misunderstanding and confusion), they could not be as well resolved as they should have been, especially since I was in a hurry.

5. Yet (at the request of some high-ranking people with whom I spoke during the Christmas holidays when I left you—where some very learned men from *Jauer* <note>Modern Jawor, Poland</note> and *Strieg* <note>Modern Strzegom, Poland</note>, along with other fine gentlemen, were present), I have written a fairly large book concerning *Election* <note>referring to Boehme's work *De Electione Gratiae* or *On the Election of Grace*, concerning predestination</note>. In it, all those questions and more are set down in detail and resolved at their deepest foundation.

<margin>☛</margin>
6. And I hope that this book will put an end to many contentions and controversies, especially regarding certain points between the *Lutherans* and *Calvinists* and other controversial *sects* as well. For there, the true foundation is set down clearly before their eyes, every opinion is addressed, and the two opposing sides are, as it were, united into one body—if anyone is able to see, know, and understand this despite the devil's poison and earthly imagination <note>original: "Imagination"; in Boehme's philosophy, this refers to the soul's faculty of forming internal images or "idols" that distract from divine truth</note>. I do not doubt that the time is at hand when strife and controversy shall be transformed into truth.

7. Yet among true Christians and the children of God in all nations, there has never been any controversy or division; for in Christ, we are all but one tree spreading out into many branches and twigs.

8. Controversy has arisen because the world has fallen into its own selfish lust and imagination, by which people have torn themselves away from Christ, their stem, in whom Christians should stand in unity. Instead, they have turned themselves toward "images" [mental idols or opinions] and questions.

9. From these questions, such controversies and contentions...

<summary>Jacob Boehme discusses the necessity of spiritual fruitfulness and mentions his newly written book on Divine Election, which he hopes will reconcile the theological divisions between Lutherans and Calvinists by revealing a deeper spiritual unity.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Divine Election, Predestination, Lutheranism, Calvinism, Christian unity, Babel, Jauer, Strzegom, spiritual imagination</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's argument from page 100 regarding the necessity of a true internal spiritual transformation rather than a superficial profession of faith.</meta>

# The Eighth Epistle.

...are arisen; where the pride of the Devil has been involved in the questions, and has so imprinted and modeled itself on the image of man, that they have sought for their own images and opinions, and have therein extolled and advanced themselves. Meanwhile, Christ’s humility—wherein we should die in Christ to our evil nature—is quite forgotten; so that for the present we are more a monstrous image than a living Christendom in spirit and in power.

<margin>☞</margin>
10. For a Christian must and ought to stand in the "Tree of Christ" with the increase and growth of Christ’s life, and also live in the spirit of Christ and bear fruit. In such a person, Christ himself (as to the internal ground <note>original: "internall ground"; referring to the deepest essence or soul of a person where God dwells</note>) lives and is all in all. He continually breaks the head of the "Serpent’s will" in the flesh and brings the works of the Devil to nothing. A Christian must know, will, and act from Christ; it must come to effectual doing (that is, into the Divine working), for without this, no one is a Christian.

11. Christ must wholly receive and take possession of the internal ground of the soul, so that the severe justice of God (which holds us captive in His anger <note>In Boehme’s theology, the "Anger" or "Wrath" refers to the harsh, fiery foundation of nature apart from God's light</note>) might be satisfied with Christ’s fullness [and effectual merit]. Thus, Christ may fulfill the anger of God in us with love and mortify <note>put to death</note> the will of the Devil, as well as the nature in the wrath of God, and wholly annihilate its will. In this way, the person may die in the love of Christ and bring forth a new will in the love-spirit of Christ through the nature of the soul, which lives and walks in God, as *St. Paul says: "Our conversation <note>original: "converſation"; in Early Modern English, this meant one's conduct, citizenship, or way of life</note> is in the Lord."*

12. **Lip service** <note>original: "Lip-labour"</note> and the **pratings** of the mouth avail nothing; these do not make a Christian. A Christian must always be born (or regenerated) of Christ; otherwise, he is no Christian. No outward "imputed righteousness" or grace helps at all [without this inner rebirth].

13. All comforting, flattering, soothing, and dissembling is but in vain where the "purple mantle" of Christ is simply draped over a man of iniquity and malice [or a Cain-like hypocrite], who wants to be an adopted child of grace only from the outside.

<margin>* One who is pregnant.</margin>
14. For no prostitute, or *one who is pregnant*, can be a virgin, even if she puts on a maiden's flower garland; indeed, no Prince or Potentate can grant her virginity by decree.

<margin>☞</margin>
15. Thus, flattering hypocrisy and comforting oneself [with Christ’s merits and promises] is to be understood in this way: unless we are converted and become like children who hang on the breasts of their mother, and receive and conceive within us the *Ens* <note>original: "Ens"; a Latin philosophical term meaning "essence" or "being"</note> [or essential power] of Christ which destroys the harlot [the corrupt nature], then a new spirit cannot be born in us out of Christ. This new spirit must contain Christ’s sufferings and death, so that we may be born out of His resurrection and resign ourselves into the *whole process of Christ,* which is the true *imputed grace in Christ.*

<summary>The author argues that true Christianity requires a literal internal rebirth and the "essential power" of Christ rather than mere verbal profession or outward "imputed" grace. He uses the metaphor of a pregnant woman unable to pass for a virgin to illustrate that spiritual status cannot be granted by decree without an actual change in nature.</summary>
<keywords>regeneration, internal ground, Ens, hypocrisy, imputed grace, Jacob Boehme, spiritual rebirth, St. Paul</keywords>

<page-num>103</page-num>
<header>The Eighth Epistle.</header>

<margin>pointing hand icon</margin>
16. A Christian must be born and reborn of the same grace so that he may be a Christian in and of Christ; namely, a true branch in the tree which is Christ. Thus the engrafted word of life may spring forth and become essential, living, and working in the soul. Then the merit of Christ and the imputed grace are effective when he is growing on the tree, as if from the internal ground.

17. Beloved friend Mr. Frederick, your questions require a long explanation. In the treatise or *Commentary upon Genesis*, they are all determined at length. If you obtain the eyes and sight of Christ, then there would be no need for such questions at all regarding such small things, which indeed are too high for <term>Reason</term> <note>original: "Reason"; here referring to human logic unaided by divine revelation</note>; but in Christ, they are nothing but a child's game. However, I will answer you briefly thus:

### The First Point.

->*Concerning the Serpent, which after the Curse must eat dust and crawl on its belly.*<-

18. First, concerning the article of the serpent (which after the curse must feed upon the earth and crawl upon its belly), I answer that its *shape and form were such*, but its body and spirit were not as evil in the fiery <term>Science</term> <note>original: "Science"; meaning the inner knowledge or the working principles of nature</note> of the ground of nature as they were after the curse.

<margin>pointing hand icon</margin>
19. For both <term>Tinctures</term> <note>original: "Tinctures"; alchemical or spiritual essences that transmit qualities</note> of good and evil, from the origin of the first and second principle, were manifest in it; and therefore it was so exceedingly cunning that its nature was able to see in its center—within the tinctures—the ground of the whole creation.

20. Before its creaturely origin (when it was put into <term>Severation</term> <note>original: "Severation"; the process of being separated or distinguished into a specific individual form</note> as a creature from the <term>Grand Mystery</term> <note>original: "Grand Mystery" or *Mysterium Magnum*; the source of all things in Böhme’s theology</note>), it was in its ground a fair and excellent <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens"; a Latin term for "being" or "essence"</note> of great power and virtue.

21. But the Devil’s imaginations poisoned this *essence* (when he sat like an enthroned prince in the ground of nature in very high power and dominion). In the separation, this essence formed itself into a serpent; and therefore the Devil made use of it as his instrument through the same cunning and by the same poison, in which lay the mightiest power to make Eve monstrous.

22. You <term>Physicians</term> <note>original: "Physitians"; likely referring here to alchemists or medical practitioners who sought spiritual cures</note>! It is necessary for you to know and understand the mystery of the serpent and what lies hidden under its poison. If you extract the same and proceed correctly in its processing, then you may obtain a <term>Tincture</term> against poison, the like of which is not to be had elsewhere.

23. It was in the *essence* of the <term>Grand Mystery</term> before it was a

<meta>catchword: Creature,</meta>

<summary>Jacob Böhme explains the spiritual regeneration of a Christian and begins an occult interpretation of the Serpent from Genesis, describing its original "excellent essence" before it was corrupted by the Devil's imagination.</summary>
<keywords>regeneration, Christ, serpent, the Fall, Genesis, Tincture, Ens, Grand Mystery, alchemy, spiritual medicine</keywords>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Eighth Epistle, specifically addressing the nature of the serpent, the Fall, and the distinction between Paradise and the Garden of Eden.</meta>

A creature, a Virgin; but after the Curse, she became a whore—to be understood magically [or parabolically].

24. She saw in herself the basis of the inward and outward world; therefore, one must emerge from the inward world and kill her monster, which she has placed upon Eve. An entire book could be written about what the Devil's desire has produced by and through her.

25. But when she helped to deceive the Image of God <note>referring to humanity, created in God's image</note>, then God cursed her so that she became blind to the internal ground. She was also fully manifest in the four elements, and so she fell into the earth from which the body was taken. Moreover, she fell only into the wrathful [or bad] part of the earth. She can no longer obtain or reach the good part or quality of the earth as other beasts do; therefore, she must eat earth—namely, the property of the curse in the earth.

26. She was a flying worm <note>original: "Worme"; in Early Modern English, this often referred to a serpent or dragon</note>; otherwise, nature would have provided her with legs, as other worms on earth have. But her nimbleness, wily subtlety, and cunning made Eve long [after the forbidden fruit].

# The Second Point

## *Concerning Paradise and the Garden of Eden*

<margin>☞</margin>
27. Paradise was the "temperature" <note>original: "temperature"; here meaning a state of perfect balance or harmony between opposing qualities</note> in Man, when he did not know what good and evil were; where the Divine light shone through Nature and <term>tinctured</term> <note>original: "Tinctured"; meaning to infuse with a spiritual essence or quality</note> and tempered all things. For Paradise is revealed in us again in Christ, regarding the internal ground.

28. But when God saw and knew that man would fall, then Paradise no longer produced fruit through the earth in the whole world (even though it was manifest everywhere), but only in the Garden of Eden where Adam was tempted. For Eden is the place, but Paradise is the quality—that is, the life of God in the similitude [or harmony of the universal Being].

# The Third Point

## *Whether the beasts—since they were in Paradise and also entirely earthly—likewise fed upon Paradisiacal fruit?*

29. Beloved friend, Mr. Frederick, every spirit eats of its

<summary>This page discusses the spiritual and physical transformation of the serpent after the Fall and defines Paradise not as a geographic location, but as a state of divine harmony or "temperature" within humanity.</summary>

Mother; they likewise ate from that which they were made of; namely, from the <margin>The fifth essence in the world-spirit <note>original: Spiritu Mundi</note>.</margin> *quintessence* <term>the purest or most essential part of a substance</term> of the Earth in the *world-spirit*. For the deepest nature of the beasts is many degrees below that of man. Thus they fed upon their Mother; that is, their spirit feeds upon the *world-spirit*, and their body upon the four elements.

30. God knew very well that man would not stand but fall; of what use or profit then would paradisiacal food be to the beasts? In the quintessence, there indeed lies a paradisiacal property, and they feed upon that even to this day. For in every beast, there is an incorruptible power which the *world-spirit* draws into itself until the separation of the Last Judgment.

## The Fourth Point.

->*Whether the beasts were as wild and rough before the Curse as they are now?*<-

31. Beloved Mr. *Frederick*: the garment which *Adam* had before the Curse—when he was still naked—was exceedingly beautiful and suited him very well. Likewise, the hides of the beasts were rough and hairy even then; but during the Curse, all things (both in the beasts and in the plants of the Earth) were deformed and changed into a strange, monstrous form. They did have such coverings, but they were far more glorious in colors, features, and ornament because of the pure *tincture* <term>in alchemy and Boehme's philosophy, the spiritual essence or "dye" that gives life and quality to matter</term>.

<margin>Or, the book called the *Great Mystery* <note>original: Mysterium Magnum</note>, an exposition of the Book of Genesis.</margin>
32. Regarding these questions, I urge you to look into the *Great Mystery* with the eyes of Christ, in whom all the treasures of wisdom lie; then you shall understand them better than I can briefly set down in writing in this haste. I commit you to the love of Jesus Christ.

<summary>This page explains that animals derive their life from the "world-spirit" and the four elements, and that their physical appearance became degraded and "monstrous" following the Fall of Man and the subsequent Curse on the Earth.</summary>
