# THE NINTH EPISTLE.

->*The open fountain of God in the heart of Jesus Christ be our refreshment and constant light.*<-

***

1. Worthy, much respected, and very learned sir: I heartily wish for you exactly what my own soul desires from God. Specifically, I wish for the real, true *divine knowledge* <note>original: Divine Knowledge</note> in the love of *Christ*. I pray that God would deign to open the *center* of your soul, so that the paradisiacal lily-twig <note>A central metaphor in Boehme’s mysticism representing the spiritual growth of the soul as it is reborn in Christ.</note> in *Christ's* rose garden might spring forth, grow, blossom, and bear fruit. I hope that the streams from *Christ's* fountain might flow from you, and that you might be taught by God, so that His Holy Spirit might guide and rule you. *As it is written: Those who are led and moved by the Spirit of God are the children of God.*

2. I have received your letter, and I understand from it that you have read my writings and that you delight in them. I wish from my very heart that their sense and true meaning may be grasped and understood; then there would be no need for any further asking or searching.

<margin>☛</margin>
3. For the book in which all mysteries lie is man himself. He himself is the "book of the being of all beings," seeing as he is the likeness (or similitude) of God. The great *arcanum* <term>arcanum: a deep secret or mystery; in alchemy and mysticism, it refers to the hidden essence of reality.</term> lies within him, and its revelation belongs only to God’s Spirit.

4. But if the lily in the humanity of Christ springs forth in the new birth out of the soul, then from that same lily the Spirit of God proceeds, as if from its own origin and ground. That same Spirit seeks and finds *all mysteries in the divine wisdom.*

5. For the lily-branch which springs forth in the new birth out of Christ's humanity—meaning the *newborn spirit* out of the soul's essence and out of Christ's power—is the true, real branch springing from and remaining in God’s tree.

<vocab>Jesus Christ, divine knowledge, paradisiacal lily-twig, Spirit of God, arcanum, divine wisdom, new birth</vocab>

<summary>In this epistle, the author explains that the "Book" of all divine mysteries is found within the human being, who is made in God's image. He emphasizes that true spiritual understanding comes through a "new birth" in Christ, symbolized by a blooming lily.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, mysticism, divine knowledge, the Arcanum, new birth, Lily of Christ, spiritual growth, internal revelation</keywords>

--- Page 24 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's mystical exploration of the "new man" and the process of spiritual rebirth, transitioning from the previous page's discussion on the nature of God's grace and the soul's transformation.</meta>

<page-num>106</page-num>
### The Ninth Epistle

6. Just as a mother bears a child, so is the "new man" born in and out of God; there is no other way at all for him to be God’s child and heir, a child of Heaven and of Paradise.

<margin>☛</margin>
7. No imputed righteousness <note>the theological concept that righteousness is credited to a person by God rather than being an internal quality</note> is sufficient (a stranger cannot inherit God’s Kingdom). Instead, there must be an innate righteousness originating from God’s very essence <note>original: "Essentiality"</note>, from the water and spirit of God. As Christ told us, *we must become like children* and be conceived in God’s essence. Like new children in God, we must spring forth and be born anew, just as a beautiful flower springs out of the wild earth, or as precious gold grows in a rough stone or impure ore <note>original: "drossy Oare"</note>. Otherwise, we cannot see nor inherit the Kingdom of God.

8. For whatever intends to possess the internal spiritual world must be born out of that same world; the earthly flesh, made from the four elements, cannot inherit the Kingdom of God.

9. But the *Quintessence* <note>original: "Quint-essence," the "fifth element" or purest essence in ancient philosophy; here identified as Paradise</note>—which is the "one element," namely, *Paradise*—is the source from which the four elements have their origin, birth, and progression. That same essence must be predominant and rule over the four elements, just as light contains the darkness as if it were swallowed up, even though the darkness is still really within it. This is exactly how it must be with humanity.

10. However, it cannot be this way with the "outward man" <note>the physical, worldly self</note> during this earthly life, because the outward world rules over the outward man. This was made manifest in man during the Fall.

11. Therefore, the external man must perish, just as the external world perishes and passes away. Because of this, man cannot attain perfection in this lifetime; the true man must continue in combat and struggle <note>original: "strife"</note> against the earthly, corrupt life, which is its own enemy, where eternity and time struggle against one another.

12. For through this struggle, or the mutual combat within nature, the great *Arcanum* <note>original: "Arcanum," a profound secret or mystery</note> is opened, and the eternal wonders of God’s wisdom are made manifest out of the soul's essence.

<margin>☛</margin>
13. Just as the eternal God has manifested himself through time and brings his eternal wonders through time into combat and contest—so that through the struggle, that which is hidden (lying in the *Mysterious Nothing* <note>original: "Mysterious Nothing," a reference to the *Ungrund*, the divine void or "groundless" source from which all things emerge in Boehme's philosophy</note>) might open itself and be brought to light—likewise, through struggle and combat, the "Great Mystery" must be revealed in man, where God’s anger and love struggle against each other like fire and light.

14. For the soul arises out of the eternal fire, out of the Father’s nature <note>original: "property"</note>—that is, out of the eternal nature that has no beginning <note>original: "un-inchoative Nature"</note> and out of the darkness. Therefore, the light (which went out and disappeared in Adam) must be renewed and born again through the coming of Christ. Then, the Kingdom of Christ and of God is freely given to him out of grace.

<meta>catchword: 15. For</meta>

<summary>Boehme explains that spiritual rebirth requires an internal transformation into God's essence rather than a mere legalistic "imputed righteousness." He describes the internal struggle between the "outward man" of the elements and the "new man" of the spiritual quintessence as a necessary process for revealing divine mysteries.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual rebirth, Quintessence, Arcanum, the Fall, internal struggle, Mysterious Nothing, Jacob Boehme, theosophy, four elements, new man</keywords>

--- Page 25 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's argument regarding the necessity of the "new birth" over external religious doctrines.</meta>

<page-num>107</page-num>

# The Ninth Epistle.

<sig>P 2</sig>

15. For no one can take this for themselves, unless the love of God again presses out of grace into the center of the soul, and brings the divine will out of the fire of the soul as a new sprout or new image into heavenly essentiality, just as light shines out of fire.

16. Therefore, everything that Babel <note>original: "Babel" — a term frequently used by Jakob Böhme to describe organized, sectarian, and confused religious institutions that focus on external forms rather than internal transformation</note> teaches concerning external imputed righteousness, external assumed adoption, [and particular election and rejection from eternity] is without foundation or footing. Christ said, *“You must be born again, or else you cannot see the Kingdom of God.”*

17. The seemingly holy, flattering comfort regarding Christ's death is of no value, except as a way to enter into Christ's death, to spring up anew in him, and to arise in him and with him, and become Christ [or an anointed child of God] in the new man.

<margin>☞</margin>
18. Just as Christ has mortified <note>original: "mortifyed" — put to death or subdued the impulses of the flesh</note>, extinguished, and overcome the world, and also the anger of his Father (being the center of the eternal nature) in the soul’s property with his love—that is, with the new love-fire introduced into the soul’s essence, into which the Devil had previously brought and placed his desire—even so must we, in and with Christ's spirit, quell and quash the earthly Adam in God's anger and mortify it through God's love, so that the new man may spring forth. Otherwise, there is no forgiveness of sin, nor any adoption [or filiation <note>original: "filiation" — the state of being a son or child</note>], nor any righteousness.

19. The Kingdom of God must be inwardly innate and born within us; otherwise, we cannot see with the eyes of eternity into the angelic world.

20. All imaginations, inventions, and ways—all reading, studying, and teaching—is to no purpose [without this way of the new birth]. No art or reason can attain it; we must enter only through the gate which God has opened to us in Christ, and spring forth in God's Kingdom and die to the earthly will, so that it neither hinders us, nor sticks to us, nor clogs us. The seed of the woman must continually bruise the serpent’s head in us.

21. Self-reason cannot make a child of God, for it does not lie in our willing, running, and keeping busy <note>original: "keeping a doe" — making a great effort or fuss</note>, as Saint Paul says, *“but on God’s mercy and compassion.”*

<margin>☜</margin>
22. My selfhood cannot attain it; my selfhood must die in Christ's death and fall or resign into the nothing <note>The "nothing" refers to the *Ungrund*, a concept in Böhme’s philosophy representing the absolute, undetermined abyss or pure potentiality of God</note>. And then my selfhood falls into God's mercy and is in the limit of the first man, and stands again in the word *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let it be done" — God’s creative command in Genesis</note>, where God's mercy, through Christ's entrance into our humanity, makes [or recreates] the new man out of grace.

23. And therefore, the corrupt earthly will must die in a real, true, upright repentance and enter into resignation <note>original: "Resignation" — the total yielding of the individual self-will to the divine will</note>; that is,

<meta>catchword: into</meta>

<summary>This page argues that true salvation is found only through an internal "new birth" and the mortification of the self-will, rather than through external religious doctrines or academic study. The author emphasizes that the believer must "resign" their selfhood into God's mercy to be recreated through Christ.</summary>

<keywords>Jakob Böhme, New Birth, Resignation, Babel, Mortification, Imputed Righteousness, Self-reason, Fiat, Mysticism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of spiritual rebirth, focusing on the surrender of the self-will to God's mercy.</meta>

<page-num>108</page-num>
<header>The Ninth Epistle</header>

into nothingness, and wholly surrender the will of reason unto death, and neither will or know himself any more, but enter into the mercy and compassion of God.

24. And then this saying has its place and meaning, as God speaks through the Prophet: *My heart breaks within me, so that I must take pity on him. Can a mother forget her child, so that she should not have compassion on the son of her womb? And even if she should forget, yet I will not forget you; behold, I have marked you in my hands.* <note>The author is paraphrasing Jeremiah 31:20 and Isaiah 49:15–16.</note>

<margin>☛</margin>
25. In this (namely, in God's mercy) the new man <term>the new man</term> <note>A common theological term for the spiritually reborn person, as opposed to the "old man" of sinful human nature.</note> arises and springs up in the Kingdom of Heaven and Paradise, though the earthly body remains in this world.

26. For Saint Paul says, *Our citizenship <note>original: "Conversation"; in the 17th century, this meant one's conduct, community, or citizenship (Philippians 3:20).</note> is in Heaven.* Thus the new man walks in Heaven, and the old man in this world, for the Heaven in which God dwells is within the new man.

27. Thus (beloved Sir and Brother), and in no other way or manner, have I found the <term>Mystery</term>; I have not studied or learned it, but if you or any other person thirsts after it, I am committed as a brother in my affection and love to show him the way I encountered it, as I have set down at length in my writings—chiefly in the book *The Threefold Life of Man* and in the book *The Three Principles of the Divine Being*.

28. Indeed, I wrote it for myself as a spiritual exercise in the knowledge of God, in the mystery of the great wonders of God; nevertheless, by God’s providence and guidance, it has come so far as to be published and read. I would gladly let everyone who earnestly desires to understand it have access to it. I wish from my heart that it may be truly revealed and made known to the reader of this epistle, and to everyone within themselves, and then there would be no need for any further searching and seeking.

29. But seeing as God has promised through the prophets (especially in *Joel*) that he will pour forth his spirit in the last days upon all flesh, the time must therefore be considered and taken notice of.

<margin>Note.</margin>
30. I say, as I have known it, that whoever at present will die to himself, the spirit of the Lord shall seize him according to Joel’s prophecy and reveal His wonders through him; therefore, if anyone is in earnest, he shall find it by experience.

<margin>☛</margin>
31. Yet let everyone be faithfully warned: if God’s light arises in him, he must continue steadfast in great humility and <term>resignation</term> <note>The total surrender of the individual's will to the will of God, a core concept in the author's mysticism.</note>, namely in the death of Christ.

32. (For the Heaven shall now at last pour forth its long-contrived *outpouring* <note>original: "Egestum"; a Latin term for a discharge or something cast out.</note> of the <term>constellations</term> <note>Refers to the astrological and spiritual influences of the stars upon human nature.</note> which it has worked into the human property), lest he also be seized by the starry heaven and go beyond the limit of resignation.

<meta>catchword: 33. As</meta>

<summary>In this passage, Jacob Boehme describes the process of spiritual rebirth as a total surrender to God's mercy, leading to a state where the "new man" lives in Heaven while the body remains on Earth. He claims his insights come from direct experience rather than formal study and warns readers to remain humble as they encounter divine light.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, spiritual rebirth, resignation, self-denial, New Man, Joel's prophecy, mysticism, Threefold Life of Man, Three Principles of the Divine Being</keywords>

--- Page 27 ---
<meta>This page continues the Ninth Epistle, concluding the author's warnings against certain radical spiritualist sects and offering a final exhortation regarding the apocalyptic nature of the current times.</meta>

<page-num>109</page-num>

# The Ninth Epistle.

<margin>The proselytes or disciples of Isaias Stiefel and Ezekiel Meth, who held that perfection could be achieved in this outward flesh.</margin>

33. This may be seen in the Metists <note>original: "Metists," followers of the radical spiritualist Ezekiel Meth</note>, who reached even to the gates of the Deep <note>the spiritual abyss or divine mystery</note> but were again captivated by the "starry heaven" <note>the physical or astral world</note>. They turned inward toward themselves and exalted themselves, ceasing their struggle against the Serpent. They entered into a "selfhood"—a singular <term>Luciferian</term> <note>Luciferian: characterized by pride or arrogance, mirroring Lucifer’s fall from heaven</note> conceit of their own holiness—supposing that they had been transformed into a Deity. In doing so, they have confused the external world with the internal spiritual world.

34. This belief is unsound and lacks any foundation. We must take great care and ensure we remain steadfast in the deepest humility, so that the seed which is sown may grow into a tree and come to blossom, allowing the Spirit of God to take form within us.

35. For from that blossom arises the Morning Star, so that a person may learn to know what they truly are, and what God and Time are.

36. <note>Manicule in left margin pointing to this paragraph</note> Out of my sincere affection for you, Sir, I wish to make you understand that these present times must be taken very seriously. The Seventh Angel in the <term>Revelation</term> <note>the final book of the New Testament, describing the apocalypse</note> has prepared his trumpet, and the powers of Heaven are in a state of peculiar motion. Furthermore, both gates stand open, and light and darkness are in great desire; as each thing is received, so shall it enter in.

37. While some shall rejoice exceedingly at this, others shall mock it; whereupon follows the painful and severe judgment upon <term>Babel</term> <note>Babel: symbolically referring to the "confused" or corrupt institutional church systems</note>.

38. And so I entrust you and your family to the pleasant and lovely affection of Jesus Christ.

---

->Dated at Görlitz, October 27, 1621.<-

<page-num>110</page-num>
<header>The Tenth Epistle.</header>

# THE TENTH EPISTLE
### OF
## The Killing of **Antichrist** in Ourselves.
### AND ALSO
## How we may attain unto **Divine Contemplation.**

->*Our Salvation is in Christ Jesus.*<-

---

<margin>1.</margin> **Worthy** and much-respected Sir, I wish you the grace, knowledge, and blessing of God in **Christ Jesus**. I was informed by *D. K.* <note>original: "D. K."; likely Dr. Kober, a physician and friend of the author.</note> that you, as a Christian brother and fellow member in the Lord, maintain a sincere *desire* for the Father’s drawing toward **Christ Jesus**. I am told you also labor in your mind to discover how you may achieve **divine contemplation** and vision within yourself. Therefore, at the doctor's request, I would not miss the opportunity to visit and greet you with a short epistle, and to briefly declare to you—out of my **gifts** and Christian love—the way to **divine** *vision* and *feeling*. By this, I wish to present to you in brotherly love the sap of my little *coral* <note>original: "Corall"; a metaphor common in the author’s work for the small but precious growth of spiritual wisdom</note> in the spirit and life of **Jesus Christ**, just as one branch or twig on a tree is bound to do for another. I hope that I might be well understood, if perhaps I might further encourage your zeal.

<margin>2.</margin> Since you clearly perceive within yourself that **Antichrist** in **Babel** <term>Babel: In this context, a symbolic name for the "confused" and corrupted state of the institutional church and worldly systems.</term> holds power and government over **Christendom**, and act—

<summary>This epistle introduces the author's guidance on achieving direct spiritual vision and overcoming the internal influence of "Antichrist" and "Babel." It emphasizes the interconnectedness of believers as branches of the same spiritual tree.</summary>
<keywords>Antichrist, Babel, Divine Contemplation, Christ Jesus, spiritual vision, D.K., Christian brotherhood, inner life</keywords>

--- Page 29 ---
<meta>This page continues a discussion on the nature of falsehood and the lust of the flesh, contrasting these worldly desires with the spiritual requirements of the Kingdom of Heaven.</meta>

<page-num>111</page-num>

### The Tenth Epistle.

<margin>* Or, His seamless coat.</margin>
...[promotes] falsehood and the lust of the flesh; and our dear Immanuel <note>A Hebrew name for Christ meaning "God with us."</note> has faithfully warned us of this and said, *That flesh and blood shall not inherit the Kingdom of Heaven, John 6.* <note>The phrase "flesh and blood shall not inherit the kingdom" is traditionally found in 1 Corinthians 15:50, though the author cites John 6.</note> Yet the Antichrist seeks and desires nothing else but temporal honor, might, and power, to climb up and advance himself in the lust of the flesh. Moreover, this Antichrist has for a long time so civilly and demurely decked and adorned himself with Christ's * Purple Mantle, that men have not recognized him; instead, they have honored and adored him as a saint. This has been clearly revealed to me by the grace of the Most High, and therefore I wish to briefly declare to you what a Christian is, and also what the Antichrist within a person is, for your further consideration.

<margin>* Or, Employment.</margin>
3. Christ says: *Whoever does not forsake houses, land, money, goods, wife, children, brothers, and sisters, and deny himself and follow me, he is not my disciple or servant;* also, you must turn and become like children, or be born anew of water and the spirit, otherwise you shall not see the Kingdom of God. This does not mean that one should abandon his vocation and *calling—leaving his wife and children for a solitary desert and wilderness and forsaking everything—but only that he must forsake the Antichrist; that is, the SELF in all [the *meum and tuum*, or the "mine and thine"].

4. Whoever wishes to attain divine contemplation and feeling within himself must mortify <term>to put to death or subdue through spiritual discipline</term> the Antichrist in his soul and depart from all self-interest <note>original: "ownehood"</note> of the will—indeed, from all creatures—and become the poorest creature in the selfhood <note>original: "selfenesse"</note> of his mind, so that he possesses or owns nothing as his own property <term>propriety: in this context, the legal or spiritual claim of private ownership</term>, regardless of his rank or condition.

5. And even if he is a **King**, his mind must forsake all self-interest and regard himself in his position, dignity, and temporal goods as nothing other than a servant of God. He must realize that he ought to serve God and his brothers in that role, and that he possesses all he has not by natural right—as if it were his own to do with as he pleased—but that it belongs to his fellow brothers and members. God has placed him as a steward and officer over it, and he must remember that he serves his Lord, who will require an account from him.

6. He must wholly and fully resign his own will (which drives him toward such possession of self-interest [or selfish affection and union with the world]) to the suffering and dying in the death of **Jesus Christ**. He must humbly beseech God in truly earnest repentance and conversion to mortify this evil will toward selfhood and temporal lust through the death of **Jesus Christ**, and bring the will of his soul into the true adoption or filiation <term>filiation: the state of being a child of God</term> of God, so that he no longer wills or desires for himself, but that...

<summary>The author describes the "Antichrist" as the selfish ego and explains that true Christianity requires renouncing "self-interest" and private ownership in favor of serving God as a humble steward, regardless of one's social status.</summary>
<keywords>Antichrist, selfhood, stewardship, spiritual rebirth, Immanuel, ownership, meum and tuum, filiation</keywords>

--- Page 30 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the spiritual transformation of the soul, moving from self-will to a state of child-like submission to God.</meta>

<page-num>112</page-num>

### The Tenth Epistle.

God's will might be in him his will and desire; that he might be dead (as to the will of the soul) in and to his "self-hood" <note>original: "owne-hood"; the state of being focused on one's own ego or selfish desires.</note> and that God in Christ might be his life.

7. He must wholly immerse his will in deepest humility into God's mercy, and lay hold on such a will and resolution in the Divine promise of grace, that he—this very hour—will depart from all self-interest in the pleasures of this world; and never enter any more into them; even if he should thereby become the fool of all the world. He must wholly immerse himself into the deepest submissive lowliness and unworthiness before God with repentance; and yet in the soul apprehend and hold fast the promise of grace, and stand firm in it, like a soldier before his enemy when it concerns his body and life.

<margin>☞</margin> 8. When this comes to pass, then his own will (*being the Antichrist* <note>In this context, the "Antichrist" refers to the human ego or self-will that opposes the spirit of God.</note>) will be captured and put to death *in the death of Christ*. His soul will soon become like a young, simple child which has lost its natural understanding of self-interest; it begins to lament before God like a young child before its mother, trusting in the mother to give what she pleases to it.

<margin>☞</margin> 9. And this is what Christ meant when he said, "you must turn and become as children, and forsake all, and follow me." For *Adam* departed from God's will into self-will. Through his own self-desire, he brought the influence of the Serpent and the will and desire of the Devil into himself. Thus, he brought himself and his life's comfort—which before stood in mutual harmony and agreement in the one single will of God—into division and disunion. The properties of nature departed from their balanced agreement and concordance; each property entered into its own "self-hood" (being a private or peculiar self-desire). At that point, the lust and imagination for both good and evil arose in him; heat and cold immediately fell upon him, and he died to the holy life of balanced harmony. In that holy life, he had lived in one single pure *Element*, in which the *four Elements* <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water.</note> were in him in equal weight or *temperature* <term>temperature: a balanced state of being (temperament) where no single element or quality dominates.</term>.

10. And of this God warned him, saying, "*Eat not of the Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil, or else you shall die*"; meaning thereby the death to the Kingdom of Heaven—namely, the fading away of the beautiful Angelic Image. That image died immediately in the false, introduced desire of the Serpent. Therefore, this false will of the Serpent must first die in Christ's death by true *conversion*; and out of this death, Christ arises in his spirit again in us, in the heavenly image which died in *Adam*. Thus, the inward man is regenerated and born again in Christ's spirit.

11. This new Spirit comes to Divine vision or contemplation in himself;

<meta>catchword: himself;</meta>

<summary>This page explains the necessity of "mortifying" the self-will to achieve spiritual rebirth, comparing the state of a redeemed soul to a simple child. It details how Adam's fall led to the internal division of the four elements and how Christ's spirit restores the "Angelic Image" within humanity.</summary>
<keywords>self-hood, self-will, Antichrist, humility, repentance, Adam, the Fall, Serpent, four elements, temperature, Angelic Image, regeneration, rebirth</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of the "new birth" in Christ, contrasting the inward spiritual transformation with the outward, superficial religious practices of the day.</meta>

<page-num>213</page-num>

# The Tenth Epistle.

himself; it hears God’s Word and has Divine understanding and inclination, and may behold the *Grand Mystery* <note>original: *Grand Mystery* (Mysterium Magnum); a central concept in Böhme’s theology referring to the hidden source or "chaos" from which the manifestation of the universe arises</note> in Divine and natural mysteries. And although the earthly flesh still clings to him in its inclination, yet it does not hurt or spoil [the newborn spirit] in him at all.

12. In this new birth, he is like solid, fine gold in a rough, drossy stone; the drossy crust of the stone is unable to destroy or spoil the gold. His true will is dead to earthly desire and continually desires to kill and mortify <term>put to death</term> the lust of the flesh, and likewise kills it without ceasing. For here the *Seed of the Woman*—namely, the new man born in Christ—bruises the *head of the serpent's will* in the flesh, which [will] is *Antichrist*.

13. And beloved Sir, I give you to understand—as a Christian and brother in all faithfulness, sincerity, and uprightness—that in our *Supposed Religion* (where men do nothing but contend, confute, and revile one another over *the Letter* <note>the literal text of scripture as opposed to its living spirit</note> [and the different opinions derived from it]), we are yet in the midst of *Babel* <note>referencing the Tower of Babel; a state of spiritual confusion and sectarian division</note>. It was never *worse* than it is now, even though men boast that they have left *Babel* and possess the true religion—which I leave to its own merit [to be well considered].

14. But as far as is known to me in the Lord my God, through the exceedingly precious *talent* given to me by God, I say that men have indeed dipped Christ’s mantle—with its purple color—in the blood of Christ and put it on as a covering. But with it, they have only covered the Antichristian child of self-will, and so have painted over the *Antichristian Bastard* with an external color.

15. For men flatter it exceedingly and cover it with Christ’s suffering, merit, and death; they comfort it with the idea that Christ has paid for everything. They say it only needs to [apply or] comfort itself with the merit of Christ and receive it in faith as a *satisfaction* <note>a legalistic view of atonement</note>. In this way, they show us only an outward, imputed righteousness.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. But the true understanding has a completely different *A.B.C.* <note>the fundamental principles or alphabet of faith</note>. No amount of comforting oneself, self-will, frantic effort, or ritual <note>original: "keeping a round"</note> avails anything. The suffering and death of Christ will not be given to the Antichristian beast of *Self*, but to those who depart from and relinquish all ownership [and attachment] to created things. It is for those who wholly resign themselves to the suffering and death of Christ Jesus—who die to their own will in and with Christ, are buried with him, and also rise in him to a new will and obedience, hating sin. It is for those who clothe themselves in Christ during his suffering, reproach, and persecution—who take up his Cross and follow him under his *Red Banner*. To them, I say, it will be given; these put on Christ in his process and become, in the inward spiritual man...

<summary>The author critiques superficial Christianity, arguing that true faith requires an inward death to the self-will and a complete spiritual rebirth rather than just an outward reliance on Christ's "imputed" righteousness.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, new birth, Antichrist, Babel, self-will, imputed righteousness, spiritual transformation, Seed of the Woman</keywords>

<page-num>114</page-num>

-># The Tenth Epistle<-

Christ's members, and the Temple of God, who dwells in **us**.

<margin>☞</margin>
17. No one has the right to comfort himself with Christ’s merits unless he desires wholly to "put on" Christ within himself. He is also no Christian before he has put Him on through true repentance and conversion to Him with absolute resignation and sincere self-denial; so that **CHRIST** espouses and betroths <note>original: "espouseth and betrotheth"; meaning to enter into a spiritual marriage or binding covenant</note> Himself to him.

18. The beginning of this takes place in the Covenant of Baptism, where the child promises and swears under Christ’s Red Banner. That which follows must then happen in actual deed or real practice; or, if someone has turned away [from the practice of what he then promised], he must, through such a conversion of his will, turn back into it again. I say on sure ground that for many people, the mantle <note>original: "Mantle"; here referring to a protective or covering garment</note> of Christ will turn to hellish fire, because he covers the "Antichrist" <note>The author uses "Antichrist" here to refer to the internal spirit of pride or hypocrisy that opposes Christ's nature.</note> within himself with it, yet remains nothing but a beast.

19. For a Christian must be born of Christ and die to the Adamic <note>original: "Adamicall"; referring to the fallen nature of humanity inherited from Adam</note> will. He must have Christ in him and be a branch or member of His flesh and spirit—not according to the animal beast <note>Boehme frequently uses "the Beast" to describe the unregenerate, purely physical, and rational parts of human nature that lack spiritual life.</note>, but according to the spiritual man.

20. For the Spirit of God does not possess the beast [the outward, sensual, natural, or rational man], but rather the Temple of Christ—namely, *Christ's spiritual flesh and blood within us*. For Christ said, *Whoever shall not eat the flesh of the Son of Man has no life in himself.* <note>A reference to John 6:53.</note>

21. Now, there must be a mouth which is indeed proper and fit to eat it, for it will not be given to the beast, much less to the <term>Ens</term> <note>original Latin: "Ens"; meaning the fundamental essence, entity, or "being" of a thing.</note> of the serpent; for every spirit eats of its own mother from which it has arisen. I leave this for every understanding person to consider, and here I only mention what a Christian ought to be if he wishes to consider himself a Christian.

<margin>☞</margin>
22. For a "beast" is no Christian. Rather, a Christian is he who is baptized with the *Holy Spirit* in the *Death of Christ*, who has put on Christ and lives in Christ’s heavenly flesh and blood, who has tasted *Christ’s Supper* and sits with Christ at the table. He is a Christian who walks in Christ’s footsteps and continually mortifies <note>original: "mortyfieth"; to put to death or subdue through discipline.</note> the Antichristian, evil beast in flesh and blood (which still clings to a Christian), binds it, and deprives it of its strength, and patiently resigns himself in temptations, which are offered to him in many hundreds of ways for his trial and purification.

23. A Christian must learn the alphabet <note>original: "A. B. C."</note> backwards and consider the wisdom of his own reason as foolishness, so that Christ may take a form in him and he may be made capable of heavenly wisdom.

24. For the wisdom of the outward world is blind in respect of...

<summary>This page defines a true Christian not by outward labels, but by a radical internal transformation where the individual "puts on" Christ and subdues their lower, animalistic nature. The author emphasizes that spiritual nourishment (the flesh and blood of Christ) is only accessible to the regenerated spiritual man, not to human reason or the "beast" nature.</summary>

of God, and does not see him; although <note>original: "Albeit"</note> all things live and move in God, and he himself is through all things, yet he possesses nothing, except that which dies to its own will. That he must possess, and he possesses it willingly, for it wills nothing without him; and it is in the *End* of the *Creation*, and also in the *Beginning*.

25. I could mention more of this to you, if the occasion here permitted it. I have described and declared these things at length in my *Writings* from the center and origin <note>original: "Originall"</note> of all *essences* <note>original: "Essences"; in Boehme's thought, these are the fundamental spiritual qualities or substances that make up reality</note>. Here I have only briefly hinted at what a *Christian's* state, being, and condition is; I hope that you will consider it further and give yourself up to this process, as I also hope that you are already within it.

26. But for a more brotherly recreation <note>meaning spiritual refreshment or mutual encouragement</note>, I thought it good to visit you with a short *Epistle* <note>letter</note>, and to comfort myself a little with you in the hope and faith which works and is in *us*. This continues until we are once freed from this *cottage* <note>original: "Cottage"; a common 17th-century metaphor for the physical body or earthly life</note>, and are afterward refreshed and quickened <note>made alive</note> perfectly one with another in *Divine* and brotherly union and *vision*.

27. And I have done this at the request of the *Doctor* mentioned above, in all sincerity and uprightness; and so I commend you to the tender love of *Jesus Christ*.

<page-num>116</page-num>
<header>The Eleventh Epistle.</header>

# THE
# ELEVENTH EPISTLE:

->*Our salvation is in the effective, working love of Jesus Christ in us.*<-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
Dear Brother in the life of Jesus Christ, I wish from my heart that the divine, working love would be within you, so that your noble lily-twig <note>lily-twig: a signature metaphor in the writings of Jacob Boehme, representing the birth of the soul or a new spiritual growth within the believer</note> sprung forth in Christ may grow great in the power of Christ and bring forth much fruit in the paradise of God, to the heavenly joy of us all and our eternal brotherly fellowship.

2. I rejoice in my soul when I perceive that a good, virtuous, fair *branch* has sprung up in our tree of life, *Christ*; and I also hope to be a partaker of its good fruit.

3. Just as one branch on the tree enjoys the sap and power of the others, and they all grow and bear fruit together in one single *power*, so likewise in the tree of Christ we are all only *one*, and that tree is *Christ in us all*.

4. Since you freely and sincerely, with all acknowledgment of heart and mouth, commit yourself to this tree of life and, on the contrary, renounce and reject the poison and delusion of Satan, I wish for nothing more at present than to be able—in the power of this tree, which is *Christ*—to share the influence of the power I have received from him with you, so that we might grow together as members in one power.

<margin>☛</margin>
5. And I have no doubt that the Most High has begotten his lily-twig in you; for without divine power, we have no *longing* or *hunger after God*, and we also cannot know him without his spirit in us. All that we understand and know of him fundamentally <note>original: "fundamentally [or in reality]"</note> comes from his revelation and operation.

6. For although the world chatters <note>original: "prateth"</note> and talks much of God, it does so only out of custom and receives its knowledge from the

<meta>catchword: History</meta>

<summary>The author uses the metaphor of a "tree of life" and a "lily-twig" to describe the spiritual growth of a fellow believer, emphasizing that true knowledge of God comes through internal divine revelation rather than mere external tradition.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Lily-twig, Tree of Life, Union with Christ, Divine Revelation, Spiritual Growth, Mysticism</keywords>

<page-num>117</page-num>
<header>The Eleventh Epistle.</header>

# The Eleventh Epistle.

History of the literal word [by the habitual faculties of its natural reason], yet it is void of true knowledge.
> For no one knows the Father except the Son and he to whom the Son will reveal him. <note>original: "For none knoweth the Father but the Sonne and he to whom the son will reveal him." This is a reference to Matthew 11:27.</note>

7. And therefore we have no true knowledge of God, unless it be given to us by the Son, who lives in us, if we are [living] branches on the vine.

8. For Christ said, whoever is of God hears God’s word; and to the "reason-learned" <note>original: "Reason-learned," referring to those who rely solely on intellectual study and logic rather than spiritual experience.</note> who were only skillful and taught in the letter <note>original: "taught in the letter," meaning the literal text of the law or scripture without its spiritual essence.</note>, he said, "Therefore you do not hear, because you are not of God; also you are not my sheep; you are ravening wolves and hirelings."

9. Therefore I say, that if we will speak rightly of God and understand his will, then his words must abide in us in a quick <note>original: "quicke," meaning alive or vital.</note> and living working; for Christ said, *“without me you can do nothing”*; also no one can call God "Lord" without the *Holy Spirit in him*; for his calling him Lord must be born of God and flow from the *Holy Ghost*.

<margin>☞</margin>
10. Nothing is pleasing to God, and also nothing is accepted by God, except that which he himself does and works in and through the spirit of man; therefore Christ says, *“all the plants which my Heavenly Father has not planted shall be rooted out and burnt with fire.”*

11. Therefore, my beloved brother, you do well to hold and keep yourself to the Source of life and desire power and strength from it; you shall be well enlivened, refreshed, and strengthened. You are an acceptable guest to God and the members of Christ in [this] your purpose.

12. And if you persevere steadfastly and resist the devil, the world, and the earthly flesh and blood; and prepare yourself to fight like a true noble champion for conquest against all these; and overcome in yourself the powerful and open enemy, self-love; and come rightly into our Universal LOVE; then you shall certainly know and find by experience that the noble and exceedingly worthy trophy, or crown, of Christ's conquest—which he obtained in the overthrow of death and Hell—shall be set upon you, with the Heavenly Kingdom of joy.

13. And then all the children of Christ, together with the holy angels, shall exceedingly rejoice more with you than for ninety-nine righteous ones who have already obtained it.

14. And the fair and noble *Sophia* <note>original: *Sophia*, the Greek word for Wisdom, personified in Boehme’s theology as the divine bride of the soul.</note> shall be given as a spouse unto your soul. She now stands at the door of your soul and earnestly entreats and calls you with her voice; and she knocks, bidding you to come forth and hold out rightly in battle against sin, death, the devil, and hell. With your earnestness or fixed resolution, you must set the great <term>Petards</term> <note>A petard was a small bomb or explosive device used in 16th and 17th-century warfare to breach gates and walls.</note> against the strong fort of nature, and she will help you to blow up and demolish this strong fort.

<summary>In this epistle, Boehme distinguishes between "reason-learning" (intellectual study) and true spiritual knowledge, which only comes through Christ's presence within the soul. He encourages the reader to overcome self-love and the "fort of nature" to achieve union with Divine Wisdom (Sophia).</summary>
<keywords>reason-learning, Holy Spirit, self-love, Universal Love, Sophia, spiritual warfare, Jacob Boehme, Petards, Fort of nature</keywords>

### The Eleventh Epistle.

<margin>Shepherd's Crook.</margin>
15. And then you shall see great wonders, and at the hour of victory <note>original: "Conquest"</note> the joyful nuptials or marriage of the Lamb shall be celebrated in heaven within you. Then the shepherd’s staff shall be given by Christ into the hand of your soul.

16. But be sure to remember to keep truly and faithfully until the end what you have promised to this chaste *Sophia* <note>Sophia: In mystical tradition, the personification of Divine Wisdom and the spiritual bride of the soul</note>. You must be in right earnest and not look back again toward Sodom, like *Lot’s wife*, who was turned into a pillar of salt.

<margin>* Or, Mark.</margin>
17. Instead, you must go out of Sodom with Lot according to God's command and follow in the footsteps of Christ. Do not regard the scoffing, reproaches, and insulting <note>original: "opprobrious"</note> speeches of the world at all. Love the *brand <note>Mark/brand: A reference to the "stigmata" or the spiritual marks of suffering for one's faith</note> of Christ more than the friendship, honor, and goods of the whole world; then you may walk along with us upon the pilgrim's path of Christ.

18. But if you do not find this appealing <note>original: "relish this"</note>, but instead mind and desire the pleasure and honor of the world, then you are not yet ready and prepared for the marriage, nor to come to your spouse, our loving and dear *Sophia*.

19. Therefore, examine yourself well. Look and search your whole heart. If you find a longing desire and an attraction toward this (as I partially perceive and notice), then do not delay or put it off for even an hour. Go forward and enter into repentance with true earnestness <gloss>[or a fixed mind]</gloss>. Yield up your will wholly and fully to enter into it instantly, and never leave it again—even if you should have to *forsake your body, LIFE, honor, and goods* for it.

20. And if you do this, then you are rightly prepared, and the true *pioneer* <note>pioneer: A spiritual trailblazer or guide who prepares the way within the soul</note> will come into your soul and do within you what you cannot do without him.

21. And although <gloss>[vain]</gloss> inclinations and great strife and opposition in the flesh might stick and cling to you afterward, and your reason might call you a fool—yes, even though God's anger should cover and cloud you in soul and body—none of this will hurt you. You shall spring forth from among such thorns with a new mind and walk with the Spirit in *heaven*.

22. And although the earthly body must interact <note>original: "be conversant"</note> with other creatures, it shall be like a rough stone in which fine gold grows.

23. Do not be at all offended by my tribulation and persecution; neither be afraid, for it is the mark of Christ. Look back into the Scriptures and see how it went for the children of God: how they were continually persecuted and killed by those who should have taught the ways of God.

24. For I am entrusted with a precious pearl, which God does
<meta>Catchword: fo (likely "for" or "follows")</meta>

<summary>This page exhorts the reader to remain faithful to their spiritual commitment to "Sophia" (Divine Wisdom), warning against the distractions of the world and the inevitability of persecution while promising a mystical union with Christ.</summary>
<keywords>Sophia, spiritual marriage, repentance, persecution, Lot's wife, mystical rebirth, shepherd's staff, trials</keywords>

--- Page 37 ---
<meta>This page continues the conclusion of Jacob Boehme’s Eleventh Epistle, where he discusses the spiritual blindness of the prideful and his own commitment to his divine calling despite persecution.</meta>

# The Eleventh Epistle.

24. [God does] so cover it, that the unworthy do not see it, but are blind to it and are offended by the simplicity of the person. In this way, they remain blind to themselves in the wisdom of their own Reason; they see this and yet do not understand it, because they scorn and despise the simplicity of Christ.

25. But the time is very near at hand when they shall give a serious <note>original: "earnest"</note> account for it.

26. But because God has given you the ability to understand what it is and from where it comes, be thankful to him for it; it has come to you out of grace. For you have humbled yourself before him, and even greater grace may yet happen to you if you persevere steadfastly in *humility* and earnest *Prayer*.

27. I will willingly share my love with you as a fellow member of Christ, through prayer and cooperation; for it is pure joy in my heart to do so. Although I must suffer bodily trouble and calamity because of it, I still rejoice to see what God the Lord has done through this poor man <note>Boehme frequently refers to himself in the third person as a "poor man" or "simple layman" to emphasize that his wisdom comes from God, not academic study.</note> so far.

28. Satan cannot hinder the ways of God; even though it seems as if he hinders them by his murderous cries, they are actually spread and made known even more because of it, so that the Children of God inquire after the true foundation <note>original: "ground"</note>.

29. The wicked crowd is hardened and hindered by this; but others are called because of it. You shall see this happen before a year passes; and even if they *kill* me, it must move forward, for it is from the Lord.

30. And I commit you to the tender, effective, working love of Jesus Christ; and I commend myself to your brotherly love, favor, and affection.

->*Dated at Görlitz <note>original: Garlits</note>, April*<-
->*25, 1624.*<-

<margin>☛</margin>
->**J. B.**<-

***

<summary>In the conclusion of this epistle, Jacob Boehme encourages his correspondent to remain humble and prayerful, asserting that his spiritual writings are divinely inspired and will prevail despite the opposition of "Reason" or the threat of death.</summary>

# THE TWELFTH EPISTLE:

## TO Mr. JOHN BUDOFSKY.

### Our Salvation in the life of Jesus Christ.

---

->*Worthy, much respected Sir,*<-

**All sincere wishes of Divine Salvation in holy power, and of all temporal welfare, are offered here.**

1. I have received your letter, sent to me for Christian friendship and refreshment in divine knowledge, divine desire, and affectionate, hearty Christian love; it is exceedingly welcome and acceptable to me.

2. I also rejoice that God still has His children and His "little flock" <note>A reference to Luke 12:32: "Fear not, little flock; for it is your Father's good pleasure to give you the kingdom."</note> here and there, whereas otherwise the world is currently deeply drowned in wickedness and is seized by the fire of [God's] anger. This shall shortly cause a great tear <note>original: "rent"</note> in Antichristian Christendom, as is well known and manifest.

<margin>☞ Or, following,</margin>
3. That man does very well and *rightly* who learns to *know himself* truly for what he is. This cannot be achieved through human reason and sharp searching—that is, through deep study and speculation in the natural acumen <note>original: "acruments," likely meaning the sharp intellectual attainments or logical arguments of human reason</note> of reason—but only in the true *process of Christ*. It occurs in a truly resigned soul <note><term>resigned soul</term>: a soul that has surrendered its own individual will and ego to the will of God</note> which disclaims and forsakes human reason, its own wit, and human selfishness. Such a soul enters into a true conversion, turning from the earthly way into the highest simplicity of Christ and into the true and deepest humility under the Cross of Christ, as Christ has faithfully taught us, saying: *Unless you turn and become as children,*

<meta>catchword: Children,</meta>

<summary>In this opening to his twelfth epistle, Jacob Boehme encourages John Budofsky to seek self-knowledge through spiritual surrender and the "process of Christ" rather than through intellectual reason. He warns that the world is currently facing divine judgment, which will soon cause a great schism in Christendom.</summary>
<keywords>John Budofsky, self-knowledge, resignation, humility, Antichristian Christendom, little flock, process of Christ, divine anger</keywords>

--- Page 39 ---
<meta>This page continues the Twelfth Epistle, focusing on the necessity of spiritual rebirth and the limitations of human reason in understanding God.</meta>

<page-num>121</page-num>
<header>The Twelfth Epistle.</header>

> *Children, [unless you] be born anew of the water and Holy Spirit, you cannot see the Kingdom of God.* <note>John 3:5</note>

4. To this belongs a true, real **resignation** <note>original: "Resignation"; in Boehme’s theology, this refers to 'Gelassenheit' or a total surrender of the self-will to God</note> and renouncement of the human self. A person must completely commit themselves to their **inward ground** <note>original: "inward Ground"; the deepest part of the soul where God dwells</note> and completely annihilate their own selfhood. They must turn themselves through earnest repentance, with an entire inward desire, away from this world’s **being** and toward God. They must die to their own strength and self-will in the death of Christ, and fall down or sink into the mercy and compassion of God. In this way, they may be apprehended by the Holy Spirit in their own inward ground, so that the Spirit may see, will, and act through them what is pleasing to God. The Spirit alone is the true searcher of divine knowledge and the light of the soul. In this light, the soul beholds and knows God; in no other way can it attain divine and natural knowledge, vision, and contemplation.

5. For the natural, rational man understands nothing of the mystery of God’s kingdom because he is outside of God, and not in Him. This is plain and manifest in those who are learned in human reason; they contend and wrangle about the being and will of God and yet do not know Him, for they do not hear God’s Word within themselves.

6. And everything is dead and void of understanding regarding God if it does not have the living voice and the *divine hearing of the new birth in the Ens of Christ within it* <term>Ens: a Latin philosophical term meaning "essence" or "being"; Boehme uses it to describe the spiritual substance or reality of Christ within the believer</term>. The Spirit of God must give testimony to his outward hearing and teaching. In this internal seeing, God alone is known and His being understood. To this internal seeing, the outward literal or written Word is only a form and a prepared **instrument**.

<margin>☛</margin>
7. But true understanding must flow from the inward ground, out of the living Word of God (which must first be opened and revealed in a person) and enter into the written Word. There must be one concordance and harmony; otherwise, all teaching of the divine being and will is nothing but building upon the great **Babylon** <note>Babylon: symbolic of confusion and the prideful, earthly structures of human reason</note> of earthly reason and wonders.

8. All my knowledge concerning the divine and natural ground has its rise, beginning, and understanding in this internal ground. For I was not born of the schools of this world (nor educated in the scholarly arts) and I am a plain, simple man. But by God’s spirit and will, I have been brought—without my own purpose or desire—into divine knowledge and high natural investigations.

9. I shall heartily and willingly share this knowledge and free gift of grace with my dear brothers and fellow members in the tree of life of Jesus Christ. I pray daily to God that their hearts might be opened to divine hearing and understanding, so that such knowledge might also be made manifest and known in

<sig>R</sig>
<meta>catchword: them</meta>

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that divine knowledge requires the total surrender of self-will (resignation) to allow the Holy Spirit to act within the soul's "inward ground." He argues that scholarly debate is useless without this internal spiritual rebirth, as true understanding must harmonize the inner living Word with the outer written Word.</summary>

# The Twelfth Epistle.

...them, so that we might be delivered out of the contentious, disputing <note>original: "Babylon"; referring to the confusion of conflicting religious sects and dogmas</note> Babylon, and be brought into one brotherly love, bearing within us what God’s will and being is.

10. And Sir, I want you to know that your acknowledgment and offered friendship are very welcome to me. I also wish to discuss <term>Divine matters</term> with you and find refreshment in them; however, because we are so far from one another, this cannot be done easily. Yet, it may still happen, for I fully intend (when the days are a little longer and we are somewhat more certain of steady weather—if God grants such favor and a time of peace) to speak with you and other good brothers and friends in those parts in person. Then I will answer the points you have proposed and speak with you thoroughly <note>original: "fundamentally"</note> about them, which cannot be done now in haste. I commend you and your family to the gentle love of Jesus Christ.

->Dated December 13, 1622.<-

# THE
## THIRTEENTH
### EPISTLE.

---

### A LETTER Written to one In TEMPTATION, and trouble of Mind, showing <note>original: "shewing"</note> whence it arises.

---

->*Our Salvation is in the life of Jesus Christ in us.*<-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
Dear Sir, my *fellow member* and brother in Christ our *Savior*; my heartfelt <note>original: "cordiall"</note> wish and cooperating desire for **Divine Love** and **Grace** having been stated first <note>original: "premised"</note>: I wish to let you know in Christian love that I have considered your condition with Christian sympathy and fellow-feeling. I have brought it before the gracious compassion of the Most High <note>meaning: God</note> to see what he would be pleased to reveal to me about it.

2. Therefore, Sir, I must tell you that through that same gracious compassion, I obtained such an insight and *vision* of your condition and temptation that its basis <note>original: "ground"</note> and cause have been made known to me; and I will set it down briefly as a *memorandum* <note>meaning: a note to help the memory</note> so that you may consider and ponder it seriously by yourself.

3. The first cause of such a strong, active <note>original: "working"</note> temptation is the supernatural, superabundant, and unspeakable love of God—that is, the

<summary>In this opening of the thirteenth epistle, the author writes to a person suffering from mental distress and spiritual temptation, claiming to have received a divine insight into the cause of their suffering.</summary>

Divine good will, and then the creaturely <note>original: "creaturall"</note> will of man struggling one against another) that the human will refuses to fully resign and give itself up with total confidence unto such great grace of God, which is offered <note>original: "tendred"</note> unto it out of pure love. Instead, it seeks itself and its own love of transitory things; it loves itself and the beings <margin>* Or, things.</margin> of this world more than God.

4. Therefore human nature—which in its own center without the love of God stands in mere anguish, strife, enmity, and unquiet contrariety—tempts him. Into this nature the Devil also shoots his false desire to lead man astray from such high grace and love of God.

5. This temptation is the greatest; it is even the combat which Christ maintains with His love (poured forth into the nature of man) against such selfishness <note>original: "selfeness"</note>, and also against God’s anger, sin, death, the Devil, and hell. In this combat, the human Dragon <note>Boehme uses "the Dragon" to symbolize the fierce, fallen, and ego-driven nature of the human soul that resists God.</note> must be devoured by the love of Christ and changed into an angelic image.

<margin>☞</margin> 6. And if the love of God in Christ *did not have its influence in you*, you would have none of this combat; instead, the Dragon (that is, the false, devilish will) would maintain his natural right and possession without any such conflict or disturbance.

7. Now, therefore, this perplexing and distressing temptation is felt very sensibly in nature by the Dragon, who is in the labor pains <note>original: "travelling anguish"; "travail" refers to the pains of childbirth or intense struggle.</note> of his own nature when such great love of God comes into him and would change his natural right into a divine, self-denying will.

8. For here Christ, the Serpent-destroyer <note>original: "Serpent-stroyer"</note>, stands in man—even in hell—and storms or assaults the strong fortress of the Devil. From this arises such strife where Christ and Lucifer fight for the soul, as God has given you to see and know through experience <note>original: "experimentally"</note> in the first temptation.

<margin>☞</margin> 9. *Thus Christ bruises the head of the Serpent, and the Serpent stings Christ in the heel* <note>A reference to the messianic prophecy in Genesis 3:15.</note>, and the poor soul stands in the middle in great trembling and sadness. It can do nothing but only stand still in hope; it is also unable to lift up its face before God and pour forth its effective prayers, for the Dragon turns its face toward the vanity of this world and shows it the beauty and glory of this world. It mocks the soul because it wants to become another creature, and represents to it the kingdom in which it lives and its natural foundations.

10. And here the soul stands with Christ in the wilderness, in the *forty days' temptation*, where the might, glory, riches, and plea—

<header>The Thirteenth Epistle.</header>

[The plea]sure of this World is offered <note>original: "tendred"</note> unto it; alluring it to elevate itself and enter into its own self-will.

<margin>☞</margin> <note>The manicule (pointing hand) symbol indicates a point of particular importance.</note>
11. The *Second Temptation of Lucifer*, and the selfish Dragon of nature, is this: when the soul has tasted the Divine love and has been once illuminated, the soul then wants to have that same light as its own property <note>original: "propriety," meaning legal ownership or possession</note>, and work in it by its own power and ability as its own peculiar possession. Understand that the nature of the soul—which without God's light is a Dragon like *Lucifer*—wants to have it for its own possession. But this Dragon will not resign his natural rights; he wants to be a maker and disposer of the Divine power and live in it with great joy in his fiery [<insert>selfish</insert>] nature; and this cannot be.

12. This Dragon (*namely*, the fiery nature) must be changed by its own will into a Love-fire and forego its natural rights; but he is unwilling to do it. Instead, in such a change or transformation <note>original: "transmutation"</note>, he looks for a power of his own, and yet finds none. Therefore, he begins to doubt grace because he sees that in such work he must forsake his natural desire and will. Because of this, he is continually afraid and will not *die to his own natural rights in the Divine light*, but always thinks that the light of *Grace* (which works without such sharpness and fiery might) is a false light.

<margin>☞</margin>
13. This is why the outward Reason (which is blind anyway [<insert>and sees nothing rightly by itself</insert>]) continually thinks: **Oh!** Who knows how it is with you, whether it is true or not that God has illuminated you, or that He is in you? It might likely come from such a *delusion* <note>original: "Fansie"</note>; you do not see the same in other people, and yet they think they will be saved as well as you. You only make yourself the fool of the world by this, and stand in fear and trembling at God’s anger more than those who comfort themselves only with the *promise* of grace based on a future revelation.

14. Thus it happens that the internal ground <note>The "ground" (German: *Grund*) refers to the deepest, most fundamental essence of the soul.</note> sighs and pants after the enkindling <note>original: "inflammation"</note> and motion of the light, and would gladly have it; but nature is able to do nothing. It is as if it were wholly rejected by God—which is also true regarding the Self-will; for God has planted a new will *into it*. It must die to its own will and be changed into God's will.

15. And because the will of nature must die here and resign its own rights to the will of God, there are such grievous temptations within it; for the Devil will not have his *Prey-Fort* <note>A metaphorical fortress or stronghold where the soul is held captive as "prey."</note> fall or be demolished. For if Christ shall live in Man, then the spirit of

<meta>catchword: self-lust</meta>

<summary>This page describes the soul's second temptation, where the "fiery nature" of the soul attempts to claim divine light as its own property rather than submitting to God's will. This conflict between the "Dragon" of the self-will and the light of grace causes internal doubt, fear, and spiritual struggle.</summary>

--- Page 44 ---
<meta>This page continues the Thirteenth Epistle, focusing on the spiritual struggle between the human self-will and the influence of Christ within the soul.</meta>

# The Thirteenth Epistle.

Self-lust and imagination must die, and yet they do not die completely in this life because of the flesh; instead, they die daily and yet live. Therefore, there is a conflict which no wicked person feels, but only those who have "put on Christ," in whom Christ fights with *Lucifer*.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. The Third Temptation is in the strongholds of the Devil—namely, in the will and mind, as well as in flesh and blood. This is where the false centers lie in man, such as a personal self-will toward the proud temporal <note>original: "temporall"; referring to worldly life as opposed to eternal life</note> life, the lust of the flesh, and earthly things. It also includes many curses of men that have been wished upon his body and soul through his temptation; all the sins which have taken root and concentrated, and yet remain in the *Astral spirit* <term>the "star-spirit" or the part of the human psyche influenced by the physical cosmos and earthly desires</term> as a strong fort. Christ now fights in this fort and will destroy it; yet the human will still values and holds this stronghold of power, pleasure, and the beauty of this world as its own property <note>original: "propriety"; meaning private possession or legal right</note> and best treasure, and will not resign it or be obedient to Christ.

17. Therefore, beloved Sir and Christian brother, I tell you and give you to understand what our loving Lord Jesus Christ has shown me in my contemplation: examine yourself to see what your temptation is. Our dear Lord said, *“We must forsake all and follow him,”* and so we should be <margin>Or, truly spiritually poor.</margin> truly poor in the likeness of Christ.

18. Now, if you still remain stuck in your mind in self-lust, imagination, and the love of earthly things, then you have such temptation within those centers which still work in you.

<margin>☞</margin>
19. But if you will follow my child-like counsel, I tell you this: when such temptation arises in you, you must imagine nothing else to yourself except the bitter suffering and death of our Lord. Consider his reproach and scorn, his contempt and poverty in this world, which he underwent and did for us poor men. Resign your *desire* and whole will into that, so that you would very gladly be conformable to *his image* and follow him with all sincere willingness in his <margin>Or, way of life.</margin> *process* <term>the spiritual journey or pattern of life, death, and rebirth modeled after the life of Jesus</term>. Patiently endure whatever is laid upon you to suffer, and do so willingly for his sake. Desire only to be conformable or like him; for the sake of his love and will, be content to be lowly, despised, in contempt, and in affliction, so that you might maintain and keep this *his love in you*. Will nothing for yourself anymore, but only what Christ wills through you.

20. Dear Sir, I fear that there is still something in you that is displeasing to Christ, which is why there is such strife in you. Christ wills that you should die to your own will in his death, and arise in his will and live with him; *Christ is at present in your soul and strives for your soul.*

<summary>Jacob Boehme describes the "Third Temptation" as a battle for the human will and mind, urging the reader to surrender worldly attachments and follow the "process" of Christ's suffering and humility. He warns that internal spiritual conflict is a sign that Christ is currently fighting for the reader's soul against the "Astral" influences of the self.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Third Temptation, Astral spirit, self-will, resignation, spiritual poverty, process of Christ, soul-struggle</keywords>

### The Thirteenth Epistle.

<margin>☞</margin>
21. Let all earthly will go, and resign yourself completely and fully; let joy and sadness, comfort and conflict, be all the same to you; and so you shall, with Christ, be a conqueror over the world, the devil, death, and hell. At last, you will find by experience what Christ has been in you, and why this has happened to you—which has been the process <note>the spiritual path or journey</note> for all the children of Christ. I speak out of Christian affection <note>original: "Christian affection"; Boehme's expression of brotherly love and spiritual concern for the recipient</note>.

<header>The Fourteenth Epistle.</header>

# THE
# FOURTEENTH
# EPISTLE.
## TO
## Frederick Crausen,
### AT
### GOLTBERG.

---

->*The open fountain in the heart of Jesus Christ be our refreshment and constant Light.*<-

<margin>1.</margin> **W**orthy, learned, and kind Sir, my friend and beloved brother in Christ; all hearty wishes of the love, illumination, and blessing of God premised; your diligent study in the *Divine Wisdom* <note>original: "Wisedome"</note> is very acceptable and joyous to me.

2. And so much the more, because I perceive in your letter that *God* has opened your heart and spirit to a right understanding; and I wish from my heart (as indeed I do not at all doubt) that the precious *coral* <note>original: "Corall"; in Böhme’s mystical vocabulary, this likely refers to the "red tincture" or the life-force of Christ’s blood that revitalizes the soul</note> in the humanity of Christ might again spring forth (in the spirit of Christ, and his tender humanity *in us*) from the inward man in your Paradisiacal plant <note>original: "Paradificall Plant"; the spiritual seed within a person that was withered by the Fall of Adam</note> withered in *Adam*, and bring forth true fruits for the table of God.

3. And that the noble and precious branch may be firmly engrafted in the vine of Christ, and spring forth afresh from the same; and may blossom with us amidst this present wicked, thorny world; and help to foretell the *Summer of Christ* <note>a Böhmean term for a coming era of spiritual renewal</note> in his time of the *Lily* <note>original: "Lilly"; a symbol of the blooming of the Holy Spirit and the age of peace</note>;

<meta>Catchword: indeed</meta>

<summary>Jacob Böhme writes to Frederick Crausen, celebrating Crausen's spiritual progress and expressing hope for his internal spiritual rebirth through Christ's humanity. He uses mystical metaphors like the 'Paradisiacal plant' and the 'time of the Lily' to describe this transformation.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Frederick Crausen, Divine Wisdom, spiritual rebirth, Adam, humanity of Christ, Summer of Christ, the Lily, mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 47 ---
<meta>This page continues the correspondence of Jacob Boehme, specifically a letter addressed to a man named Frederick regarding the reception of Boehme’s mystical writings.</meta>

## The Fourteenth Epistle.

Indeed, some branches out of *Christ's Rose-garden* <note>original: "Christs Rose-garden"; a metaphor for the spiritual community of true believers or the state of grace</note> do here and there appear; and they spring forth as a wonder of *God* in the midst of the fire of tribulation in *Babel* <note>original: "Babel"; in Boehme’s writing, this refers to the confused, corrupt state of the established churches and worldly society</note>.

<margin>☞</margin>
4. But as for your saying that my writings have given you some direction: well then, be thankful to *God* for it. He thus manifests His wonders and deep, hidden wisdom through mean <note>original: "mean"; humble or of low status</note> and unexercised people, and sets them as a light to the children who, in the cradle of the world, work in their *Babel* and fables. They are thus convinced by humble simplicity that their works, will, and life are only a carved image and a forged, selfish invention that has no foundation or root in Him.

5. For at present, the Most High has given us to understand in many ways how, in a short time, His wonders in His hidden wisdom are [and shall] be declared to the world in writings for its enlightenment; in which our posterity, and all those who obtain understanding from *God* through them, shall not only wonder, but exceedingly rejoice.

6. I understood by *Mr. Walter* that you have received some of my writings, which delight you very much; yet I could wish that you also had the latest pieces, which are more plain and clear and have a sweeter foundation, in which the manifested *God* may be known very clearly in all His wonders and works.

7. They will in many places provide more light to you in your practice; for the ground of nature is very clearly discovered <note>original: "discovered"; revealed or uncovered</note> therein, as is our very fair, pleasant Garden of *Christ* of the new birth.

8. Beloved *Frederick*, it will give you much furtherance in your temporal and eternal exercise; and I hope that you (being an engrafted scion <note>original: "cyon"; a shoot or twig used for grafting, implying Frederick has been spiritually joined to Christ</note>) shall not break off from the tree of Divine wisdom; for shortly there will come a time when it shall be of necessary use, and you shall rejoice among the firstlings who go out from *Babel*.

9. I thank you highly for your *present* which I have received; and I shall, as a recompense, carry it in my will into the Mystery of the Most High; and it shall be received as a treasure for you; and I acknowledge hereby your true, open heart.

10. However, the *Pearl* <note>The "Pearl of Great Price," representing divine wisdom or the kingdom of heaven</note> will not be given for that reason, but for nothing; just as *God* has done for us in *Christ*. And so, one member is bound to the other; and I commend you to the sweet and pleasant love of *Jesus Christ*, and exhort you further to seek after the *Pearl*.

->Dated, 17 July 1622.<-
->*J. B.* <note>Jacob Boehme</note><-

<summary>Jacob Boehme encourages his follower, Frederick, to persevere in his spiritual studies, explaining that God reveals deep wisdom through humble people to challenge the corruption of "Babel." He mentions his latest writings, which clarify the "ground of nature" and the process of spiritual rebirth.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Frederick, Babel, spiritual rebirth, the Pearl, Divine Wisdom, Mr. Walter, mysticism</keywords>
