<meta>This page begins the Fifteenth Epistle. The author addresses a friend using the metaphor of a garden, comparing the spiritual life to a plant growing in the body of Christ.</meta>

<page-num>110</page-num>
<header>The Fifteenth Epistle.</header>

---

# THE FIFTEENTH EPISTLE.

---

> The Salvation and Light of God in the life of Jesus Christ enlighten you, and give you a further understanding of His will.

---

1. **C**ourteous Sir, and good Friend; I cannot but rejoice with you, in that you have given up your life to [be] a plant of God; and so it springs and buds forth in the body of Jesus Christ the Son of God, who has reborn us as a living creature in Himself, and has set us before His Father as a lovely plant in His pleasant Paradisiacal Garden, for His joy and wonderful deeds.

<margin>* Or, Things.</margin>
2. And I find (if I consider it rightly) that you are not only a Plant of God for yourself, but like a pleasant herb and flower that does not hold its strength and virtue <term>virtue: In this context, "virtue" refers to the inherent power, healing property, or spiritual essence of a thing.</term> within its *self*, but casts forth and diffuses its virtue for a sweet relish <note>original: "rellish," meaning a pleasant taste or scent</note> unto all living beings; and freely presents itself unto all creatures, whatever becomes of it. And so it does not spare itself, but continually produces its power and scent.

3. And thus I find it to be with the soul of man, which continually grows and freely yields its power or virtue for him to taste who desires it, and is capable of receiving its power; be it either to love, or anger; to the life of God in Christ; or to the life of pride, leading into the utmost course of misery, which in the end befalls those who are not grown in God.

<margin><note>A manicule (pointing hand) is printed here.</note></margin>
4. But praise, glory, and honor be unto those who are reborn in Christ, who—although they do here lose their life and appear before the sting of the thorny plant as a poor useless herb that is trampled underfoot—are like an herb that is cut down and is no more seen or discerned. Reason says it is quite gone, but for all that, it has its root in the Earth and springs up [again].

<meta>Catchword: thus</meta>

<summary>In this letter, the author uses the metaphor of a "plant of God" to describe the spiritual growth of a believer, explaining how a soul reborn in Christ naturally diffuses its spiritual "virtue" to others. He encourages the reader by noting that even when a believer appears defeated or "trampled" by the world, their spiritual root remains alive in God.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual growth, rebirth, Jesus Christ, botanical metaphor, virtue, suffering, providence</keywords>

--- Page 49 ---
<meta>The previous page concludes the author's initial thoughts on spiritual rebirth. This page continues the metaphor of the soul as a plant growing within the life of Christ, reaching toward God while navigating the dangers of the physical and spiritual worlds.</meta>

<page-num>131</page-num>

...thus likewise the soul of the Saints is engrafted into the *holy life* of *Jesus Christ*. It stands in God the Father and springs forth again through *Death*.

5. At this (since we have known it effectively), we do rejoice; and therefore we value the life of this world—which consists of the influence <note>original: "source," referring here to the astrological and elemental forces governing physical life</note> of the stars and elements—as the *least* important thing, and we rejoice that we are the children of God.

6. Since we know that God is *really* in *us*, and yet he is hidden from our earthly life, we know that our soul is in God and springs up in God. Meanwhile, the body is under the dominion of the stars and elements, according to the workings of this world.

7. Thus we are God’s image and likeness, who himself is everything. Should we not, therefore, rejoice? *Who will separate us from God, if the soul is in God, where no death or destruction exists?*

☞ 8. Therefore, my dear faithful friend and brother in Christ, I consider it a great joy that I have found in you a precious plant of God. My soul sensed <note>original: "smelt," likely referring to discerning a spiritual fragrance or "savour"</note> its fragrance and was strengthened by it when the Oppressor would have torn it out of the land of the living; when it lay under the power of oppressors, and the *thorny plants of Antichrist* intended to devour us.

9. But as God comes to help the branches that remain in him with his power so that they might not perish—even though the Devil and death storm against them—so they must spring forth again through death and through the wrath or fierceness of the anger and the *sting of death*. Even though God bestows the most noble and precious herb of his garden upon it, his will must still prevail; whatever is sown in him must grow in him.

10. We know this because he has given his heart (<term>Viz.</term>—meaning "namely"—the most precious plant within himself) to become man for us, as a strong, sweet fragrance <note>original: "savour"</note> of regeneration in him. Thus, when we were in death, we might be able to spring forth—with and through him—out of death and into God the Father, to bring forth the fruits of Paradise.

11. Since we then know that we are the plants [fruits or *nursery* <note>original: "Nurserie," referring to a plot where young plants are cultivated before being transplanted</note>] of God, let us fear nothing, but continually grow in *the life of God* and bring forth fruit for God’s honor. Regarding these deeds of wonder, we shall enjoy them eternally.

<margin>* Sin, iniquity</margin>
☞ 12. And since we know that our precious life stands in great danger (caught between the Kingdom of Heaven and the Kingdom of Hell, and held captive by both during this lifetime), we must walk warily and carefully so that our pearl <note>A common mystical reference to the "pearl of great price," representing the divine spark or the soul's salvation.</note> is not broken. We must not let the *scent* of * wrath into us to corrupt and spoil us, which would hinder the growth of the precious fruit. Otherwise, God might complain of us that he is like a *vine-dresser who gleans* <note>meaning to gather the small, leftover scraps of a harvest</note> and would gladly enjoy the *precious grapes*.

<summary>Jacob Boehme uses botanical metaphors to describe the soul as a plant engrafted into Christ, emphasizing that while the body is ruled by nature, the soul resides in God. He encourages his reader to persevere through spiritual oppression and remain wary of sin's corrupting influence on their "precious fruit."</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, mysticism, regeneration, soul, Christ, Antichrist, spiritual growth, vine-dresser, pearl of great price</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the fifteenth letter, likely written by Jacob Boehme to a friend or supporter. ## The Fifteenth Epistle.

13. Therefore, let us be watchful to fight against the Prince of Wrath <note>original: "Prince of wrath" - a reference to Satan or the antagonistic spiritual force of anger and darkness</note>; that the precious grapes and fruits of God may grow in us, in which God may find a good smell and taste; so that we may be a pleasant, sweet fragrance to Him in Christ.

14. We shall fully enjoy it when we are freed from the vanity of this life; then we shall live and blossom in God and eat of the pure life of God without defect. He will be our food, and we will be His, so that there may be a pleasant, mutual paradisiacal growth in one another—we in God, and God in us—within the eternal source of the holy life in God’s nursery, where there is pure perfection in absolute love.

15. For this cause we labor so earnestly and suffer the scorn and contempt of the world. While our earthly life grows in the midst of death, our heavenly life may blossom through death, so that the earthly life may appear as a thing of scorn compared to the heavenly. Indeed, the earthly is *not worthy* to be called a life when comparing it with the heavenly.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. And therefore we suffer patiently in this earthly life and rejoice in the heavenly, in the hope that we shall be freed from vanity. Then we shall be well refreshed with consolation. What we have been obliged <note>original: "faine"</note> to sow here in tribulation and trouble, we shall reap in great joy.

<margin>
* That is: By his writing to him to employ his talent. For he intended to write no more after his first book was forcibly taken from him.
</margin>
17. Therefore, my very dear brother in the life of God in which you stand, you are more precious to me because you have awakened me out of the *sleep*, so that I might go on to bring forth fruit in the life of God, and afterward rejoice in it with the children of God.

18. And I give you to understand that after I was awakened again, a very strong fragrance was given to me in the life of God. I hope to bring forth fruit in it and to awaken the *sleepy*—just as God has awakened me out of the sleep in which I lay.

19. And I entreat you, for the sake of the holy life of God in Christ, that in the future you would not be faint or weary. Instead, animate and revive your life in Christ, so that our spirits may be grasped and understood—which cannot happen without the *Divine Power*.

<margin>
Note. ☞
† Out of, or proceeding from, God.
</margin>
20. For everyone speaks from their own spiritual essences <note>original: "Essences" - the fundamental spiritual qualities or "being" of a person</note> within the wonders of God, according to how their life is ignited in God. No man can bring us to an understanding except the only Spirit † of God. On the day of Pentecost, this Spirit changed the tongues of all nations into one in the mouths of the Apostles, so that the people of all nations could understand the Apostles' speech. Though the Apostles spoke with only one tongue, the hearts and spirits of the hearers were opened in God, so that they all understood them—each in their own language—as if the Apostles had spoken in their native tongues.

<meta>21. Thus</meta>

<summary>In this passage, Boehme reflects on his spiritual awakening—credited in part to the recipient's encouragement—and describes the mystical union between God and the believer as a "paradisiacal growth." He uses the miracle of Pentecost to illustrate how the Divine Spirit facilitates true understanding between people across different languages and backgrounds.</summary>

<header>The Fifteenth Epistle.</header>

## The Fifteenth Epistle.

21. Thus, it is only possible in God for one spirit to grasp and understand another. I fear that in many places in my writings, I shall be difficult to understand; but in God, I am very easy and plain to the reader if their soul is grounded in God—the source from which I write.

22. For I have gained very little from the historical art <note>original: "historicall Art"; here referring to academic, scholastic, or formal book-learning</note> of this world, and I do not write for the sake of the pride and honor of their art. I am not born of their art, but out of the life of God, so that I might bear fruit in the paradisiacal Rose Garden of God.

23. And I do this not for myself alone, but for my brothers and sisters, so that we might be one holy body in Christ for God our Father, who has loved and chosen us before the foundation of the world was laid.

<margin>☞</margin>
24. Therefore, just as Christ did not spare his life—as was also true of his disciples—but freely preached the Kingdom of God, even though they suffered scorn and death in this world for it (and that only for the sake of the heavenly Kingdom), so must we not fear temporal scorn and death for the sake of the heavenly life. We should pray that God would deliver us from all evil and give us unity of mind.

25. But I am sorry that I am so difficult for you to understand in some points of my writings, and I wish that I could share my soul with you so that you might grasp my meaning.

26. For I understand that it concerns the deepest points on which the main matter depends, where I have used some Latin words; but my meaning rests in truth, not merely in the Latin tongue, but much more in the *Language of Nature*. <term>Language of Nature</term> <note>Böhme’s concept of "Natursprache," a mystical idea that the sounds and letters of words reflect the essential nature and spiritual signature of the things they describe.</note>

27. For it has been revealed to me in some measure how to discern the *spirits of the letters* from their very origin. I would very gladly give you the meaning and interpretation of those words which I have used and which you have misunderstood; but since it will take up some space and I am currently in a hurry, it cannot be done now. I am very willing to provide you with a very clear interpretation of them very shortly.

28. For I have been so busy with traveling and other affairs that I could not satisfy your request in this. I pray you have a little patience while you wait for it.

29. For I still have so much to do because of my brother's daughter (who recently died) that I must travel into the country every week; I was also required to make two prior journeys, which has consumed my time.

<margin>* Or, withers.</margin>
30. If it pleases God that my traveling is finally over, I hope that my work shall do many a poor, hungry soul good service. Yet, let whatever God wills be done; just as many a blade of grass <margin>* Or, withers.</margin> perishes...

<summary>Jacob Böhme explains that his writings come from spiritual revelation rather than academic study and promises to soon clarify his use of difficult Latin terms and the "Language of Nature." He also apologizes for his delay in writing, citing family obligations and recent travels.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Language of Nature, spirits of the letters, spiritual revelation vs. academic art, divine knowledge, Rose Garden of God, Natursprache</keywords>

# The Fifteenth Epistle.

...when the heavens do not give rain, worldly affairs likewise hinder God's kingdom.

31. Yet I know of no other remedy or means at present to maintain the earthly body, along with my wife and children; therefore, I will use all diligence and set heavenly things before all earthly things, as much as lies in my power. If you have a desire to read any of my writings, they shall be faithfully communicated to you; although I would gladly learn from God's children and also refresh myself through their writings.

<margin>☛</margin>
32. For I consider myself the most simple among them; I have written only a little for my own memory and divine exercise; but since you are so pleased to read it, I have no reason to hide it from you.

33. For I acknowledge the great pains you take in this, and I thank God that he has sent me a man in this world with whom I may boldly discuss God's kingdom, whereas otherwise everything is so full of such blindness and madness that I barely dare to open my mouth.

34. I hear the scoffers who come along, but I care little for their mocks; I know what kind of spirits they are <note>original: "what spirits Children they are," meaning what kind of spiritual parentage or influence they follow</note>. I wish they had my knowledge, for then they would stop their jeering.

35. Regarding the transcribing of my writings which I am to send, I do not know if they can be safely done by *N.*, for he cannot keep a secret <note>original: "hold his peace"</note>, and I often hear vain, scoffing men speak of my writings, which I assume comes from him and cannot believe otherwise. He is only a worldly man, born entirely from the school of this world; we would find little loyalty or security with him.

36. We should not at first cast pearls (seeing they are costly) in the path, but wait for another time until they are more common, lest the *Oppressor* <note>original: "Oppressour"; Boehme often uses this term to refer to the spirit of persecution or the "Antichristian" power of the era</note> devour them.

37. It might well be handed to him to transcribe, but not the *first* time; only after it has been copied once, so that the *Oppressor* may not be able to destroy it.

* *Bethlen Gabor.*

the *Turk* and very easily come to the *River Rhine*.

40. At that time, the great slaughter of the children of *Babel* <note>original: "Babel"; refers here to the corrupt ecclesiastical and worldly systems</note> may come to pass, where two great rods <note>instruments of punishment</note> of God shall appear: one by *war* and the other by *mortality* <note>original: "Mortality"; referring to plague or pestilence</note>, through which *Babel* shall be ruined. <gloss>* The astral spirit, or the understanding of reason.</gloss>
42. For to God, a thousand years are like a single day. <margin>☞</margin>

44. For God keeps the time and hour to himself, and yet shows through his spirit the wonders that are to come.

45. I inform you that *H. N.* has sought to copy out my requested book and seeks to obtain the correct original of the † first one, which I understand shall be accomplished. It may most conveniently be delivered by *N. N.*

<gloss>
* The worldly.
† The spiritual.
* Confused Christendom.
</gloss>
46. Regardless of how it happens, the * *new Antichrist* triumphs nightly in the growth of the † *old* one, and burns like a fire in *juniper wood* <note>known for burning with intense heat and lasting a long time</note>. He thinks it is joy and a golden age, but it is actually misery, and * *Babel* is a flaming fire.

47. Being in haste at present, I have no time to write to you more extensively, for nothing more has yet begun. However, I hope to begin it soon, as the impulse in my mind continually shows me. I shall faithfully send it to you at the appointed place.

48. And I faithfully commend you into the gentleness <note>original: "Meeknesse"</note> of *Jesus Christ*.

->*Dated Thursday after St. Martin’s Day,*<-
->**1 6 1 9.**<-

# THE
# SIXTEENTH
# EPISTLE.

->*The open Fountain in the heart of Jesus*<-
->*Christ be our refreshment.*<-

<margin>* Text: The Fountain of Wisdom <note>original Latin: "Fons Sapientiæ"</note>.</margin>
1. **N**oble, Honored, and much-respected Sir: The salutation and kind wish of Divine love, and fullness of joy in our *Immanuel* <note>Hebrew for "God with us," a title for Jesus Christ</note>, in his wonderfully sweet power, together with all temporal <note>earthly or worldly</note> prosperity of body having been first mentioned; I desire to let you know that I am informed how you are a supporter of the *Fountain of Wisdom*, and do make use of some of my writings, and also that you bear a great desire for the well-spring of Christ and the Noble *Wisdom*, which has moved me to write to you, seeing you have perused some of my writings.

2. But some are found who, out of envy, misapprehension, and misunderstanding of them, talk idly and rage against them, as may be seen by the attached pamphlet; how the poor, proud, foolish man blusters <note>original: "vapoureth"</note> and storms, and yet has not the least understanding of the source from which my writings flow.

3. Indeed, he puts a false and most strange sense and meaning upon them so that he might thereby only confirm his own miserable opinion; for he has spread abroad some writings concerning *God’s Election* <note>the theological doctrine of predestination, or how God chooses those who will be saved</note> of us, and thereby thinks to entangle and ensnare us in despair, and so to open a gate to all wickedness and unrestrained behavior.

4. And therefore he does not like the taste of the open fountain of Christ in my writings; whereupon I have made a short declaration regarding his attached pamphlet, and have given it only briefly for the consideration of him who reads my book, seeing the foundation is elsewhere sufficiently and satisfactorily to be found in my writings; so that men may see how this fault-finding <note>original: "Carping"</note> pamphleteer thinks to deceive and rob us of the chiefest treasure, on which our Eternal Salvation

<meta>Catchword: and</meta>

<summary>In this letter, the author greets a supporter of his spiritual writings and defends his work against a critical pamphleteer who has misinterpreted his views on predestination.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Divine Wisdom, Immanuel, Predestination, God's Election, Religious Polemics, Salvation</keywords>

--- Page 55 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's critique of an opponent's interpretation of scripture, specifically regarding the Virgin Mary and the doctrine of Election (predestination).</meta>

<page-num>137</page-num>
<header>The Sixteenth Epistle.</header>
<sig>T</sig>

and happiness depends, and that with cunning words by alleging and quoting of the Scripture; as a toad that sucks poison out of honey; even so he <margin>* Text: Pulls it about by the hair (meaning: forces the meaning).</margin> *perverts* the Scripture; as is to be seen in his description of the Virgin Mary and the promised seed of the woman, how he therein falsifies and embitters the Scripture whereupon he builds the *Election* <note>The theological doctrine of predestination, or God's choosing of those who will be saved.</note>.

5. At which I am much grieved in my heart, that the man is so burdened and possessed with such an opinion. This burden is very heavy, and he cannot get rid of it unless he learns to understand the *Center of all Beings*—whence good and evil arise, what God’s love and anger is—and learns to understand the *Three Principles* <note>A core concept in Boehme's philosophy describing the three realms of existence: the dark world, the light world, and the visible world.</note>; otherwise, he will not be freed from such opinions.

6. Although I do not wonder that my writings seem strange to him, for there is something new in them that surpasses the reach of reason. They have another sense and another understanding than his, and another root from which they spring; for I have not gathered them together from the *Letter* <note>original: "the Letter"; refers to literal, scholarly study of scripture without spiritual inspiration</note>, neither did I learn them from other men's writings. I was an ignorant child in that respect, as laypeople usually are; I knew nothing of such things and I did not seek it in such a way. I sought only the heart and open well-spring of *Jesus Christ*, to hide myself from the *tempest* of God’s anger and from the opposition of the Devil, so that I might find a guide and leader who might rule and direct me in my life.

7. But when this pressed so hard upon me, and my mind forced so strongly into the combat against sin and death and toward the mercy of God—so that I was resolved rather to part with my life than to give in or desist—such a *garland* <note>A symbol of spiritual victory or divine revelation.</note> was then set upon me, which I hope to enjoy and rejoice in forever. I have no pen sufficient to describe it, much less can I express it with my mouth; and from that experience my knowledge came, as well as the desire to set it down, only for my own memorial. I intended to keep it by me until the last of my days, and how it came to pass <insert>[that it was published]</insert> you know very well, Sir, through Mr. N. But God's providence and permission in this was such that you and your brother were called as *first-fruits* <note>original: "Firstlings"; the first people to receive or support a new spiritual movement.</note> to it, by whose means it was spread abroad.

<note>A manicule (pointing hand) appears in the margin here to emphasize the following paragraph.</note>
8. Therefore I exhort and entreat you, for the sake of eternal salvation, to heed and mind well the *pearl* <note>A reference to the "pearl of great price," representing spiritual truth.</note> that God favors us with; for there will come a time when it shall be sought after and greatly accepted. Let no stormy gusts drive you to and fro, but look upon it rightly, and pray to God the Most High that He would be pleased to open the door of knowledge, without which no man will understand my writings. For they surpass and transcend *astral reason* <note>original: "Astrall Reason"; human logic based on the physical world and natural influences rather than divine revelation.</note>; they apprehend and comprehend the *divine birth*; therefore

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that his spiritual insights come from direct divine revelation during a period of intense personal struggle, rather than from academic study. He warns that his writings cannot be understood through ordinary human reason alone, but requires divine assistance.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Election, Three Principles, spiritual revelation, astral reason, divine birth, Virgin Mary, religious persecution</keywords>

--- Page 56 ---
<meta>This page continues Böhme’s discussion on the necessity of divine inspiration over human reason for understanding spiritual truths. It concludes the sixteenth epistle with a reflection on his first book, Aurora.</meta>

<page-num>138</page-num>

# The Sixteenth Epistle

...therefore, there must also be that very same spirit to understand them correctly. No speculation [or sharp perception or intellectual concept] reaches them unless the mind is illuminated by God. To find this illumination, the way is faithfully shown to the seeking reader.

9. I speak in good truth and sincerity before God and man, and I also appeal to God's judgment and declare that there is no good at all in any *disputing* <note>original: "disputing"; here referring to academic or theological arguments</note> without God's light and spirit. Also, nothing that is permanent, constant, firmly grounded, or well-pleasing to God can arise from it.

<margin>☛</margin>
10. Therefore, whoever wishes to learn and fundamentally understand the right and true way to God, let them depart from and forsake their own human reason and enter into a penitent, humble, and God-resigned childlike (or filial) life. In this way, they shall obtain heavenly power and skill, and shall put on Christ’s filial spirit <note>original: "filial spirit"; the spirit of a child or son toward a parent, characterized by trust and obedience.</note>, which shall lead them into all truth. Otherwise, there is no true way to God but this one alone.

<margin>The Pearl of Sophia. Divine illumination.</margin>
11. If it reaches the point where the *Virgin’s Garland* <note>The "Virgin Sophia" represents Divine Wisdom in Böhme's mystical theology; the garland symbolizes spiritual awakening or enlightenment.</note> is placed upon them, they shall no longer need to say, "*Teach me*," etc. For it is written, "*They shall all be taught by God*." Otherwise, I have no knowledge, skill, or understanding. In my writings, I have been like a young scholar going to school, or like a passing rain shower that hits whatever it falls upon. This is how my understanding has been, even to this day.

12. The book **Aurora** <note>Böhme’s first major work, *Aurora: Die Morgenröte im Aufgang* (1612), which circulated in manuscript before being published without his consent.</note> was my first childish beginning. I also wrote it contrary to the notions of reason, guided only by the appearance of light. I understood it very well in a magical [cabalistic or parabolic] manner, but it was not sufficiently explained; it needed a more extensive description and exposition. I intended to keep it to myself, but it was taken from me against my will and published, as you, Sir, know. I commend myself to your favor, and all of us into the gentle love of Jesus Christ.

->Dated the third of July, in the year<-
->1621.<-

<summary>Böhme argues that spiritual truth is found through humility and divine illumination rather than academic debate, reflecting on his first work, Aurora, as a preliminary step in his mystical journey.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Aurora, Sophia, Divine illumination, Reason vs. Spirit, Filial spirit, 1621, Mysticism</keywords>

# THE
# SEVENTEENTH
## EPISTLE.

***Worthy and much respected, Noble Sir,***

1. The hearty salutation and desire for Divine love and fullness of joy in our *IMMANUEL* <note>original: "IMMANUEL"; Hebrew for "God with us."</note>, in his wondrously sweet power, along with all bodily and temporal welfare, are here offered. I kindly wish you to understand that I have fully considered the conversation we recently held. Since I perceive you to be a *zealous lover of the truth and Divine Mysteries*, I would not miss the opportunity to visit you with this *Epistle*. I have been given the chance to respond somewhat to the "Article" <note>An "Article" here refers to a specific point of religious doctrine or a theological argument.</note> of a person who opposed me regarding the doctrine of God’s *Free Grace of Election* <note>original: "Gods Free-grace [of] Election"; the doctrine of predestination, concerning how and why God chooses certain individuals for salvation.</note>. I have sent this, my answer, for you to read.

2. But insofar as the mind cannot rest satisfied with this small amount, I am ready and willing—if it is desired—to write such a book and to enlarge and unfold it so thoroughly from the Center <note>original: "out of the Centre"; a foundational concept in the author's mysticism, referring to the source of divine reality from which all things originate.</note> that the heart might find *satisfaction* in it. However, I would assume that a Christian might find enough in this little bit to be satisfied regarding this and other doctrines.

<margin>P</margin>
3. Nevertheless, seeing that this doctrine has perplexed many men, and that opinions have been established and concluded from it which open a gate for all wickedness <note>original: "iniquity."</note> into the world, I am deeply grieved by it. It has been given to me to know from the Most High that this doctrine has *not as yet* been understood from its very foundation <note>original: "from the very ground."</note>. I wish from my heart that it might be *understood*, so that we might not look so strangely at one another as "men-devils" <note>original: "Men-Devills"; humans who act with demonic cruelty or hatred toward one another over religious disagreements.</note>, but as loving brothers and the innate, dearly purchased children of Christ. We should walk in a right and true love toward one another, which can *never be done* while holding the notion and assumption that God *chooses one and not another*.

4. But if I look upon my brother as my [own] flesh and

<meta>catchword: spirit</meta>

<summary>In this letter, the author addresses the controversial doctrine of predestination (the "Election" of grace), arguing that common misunderstandings of the topic cause social and religious strife. He expresses a desire to explain the subject from its mystical "foundation" to promote brotherly love over sectarian hatred.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Predestination, Election, Free Grace, Divine Mysteries, brotherly love, theology, 17th century epistles, Immanuel</keywords>

--- Page 58 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's theological argument regarding the nature of the human spirit and the importance of brotherly love, specifically addressing his reasons for writing to the recipient.</meta>

<page-num>140</page-num>

# The Seventeenth Epistle.

spirit, then it may be truly effected; which the Scripture and also the origin of mankind powerfully testify and convince us of. And yet my conscience in the spirit of the Lord convinces me even more that I ought to love my brother as my own life, or as my God; would God command me to love [one who is] a damned devil? No, but [one who is] a member of my [own] body.

<insert>☞</insert>
5. Therefore, for this reason, I have taken the opportunity to write to you and Christianly entreat and advise you to better consider this article. In that consideration, do not allow anything at all to possess your mind or take place within you except the pleasant Name of *Jesus*, who has come into this world and manifested himself in our humanity to seek and save us poor lost men—dead to the Kingdom of God—and to restore again what was lost in *Adam*.

6. I do not write this to be a master over you, but in a brotherly manner, for a mutual search and refreshment <note>original: "recreation"</note> with you, so that our faith and confidence towards God may be strengthened in the Lord. For we are all only men, and we should rightfully behave toward one another in doctrine and life as members [of one body]; for he who finds his brother in the spirit of Christ finds *himself*.

<margin>
* Or, Understanding.
</margin>
7. Much disputing is not at all profitable; it only creates confusion. Go with me in my writings to the *Center of all Beings*, and you shall see the origin in good and evil and be freed from all this error. For you shall find so much in my writings that will give real satisfaction to the mind; as far as the center of all beings is understood <note>original: "apprehended"</note>, there arises such joy in the mind which surpasses all the joy of this world. For the noble and precious *stone of the wise men* <term>The Philosopher's Stone: In alchemy, a substance capable of turning base metals into gold; Boehme uses it as a metaphor for the spiritual transformation found in Christ.</term> lies within it, and he who finds it accounts it of higher excellence than the outward world with all its glory. Should it not be a joy to find and *know God*, so that a man is able to see and find *all things* within himself—things that are barely described in a thousand books—and to know it truly in everything? With whom shall I contend and wrangle about religion if that same religion is manifest in my heart, so that I am able to truly behold all things in their root and origin?

<margin>
* Or, The Philosopher's Stone.
</margin>

8. I do not speak this to boast of myself, for I am as *nothing* and God is *all in me*; but I speak so that if anyone has a mind to seek, they might also seek and obtain. Although I did not seek it in that manner and also did not understand—indeed, I knew nothing of it—I only sought the pleasant, loving heart of *Jesus Christ* to hide myself within from the wrathful anger of God and the enemy, the devil. But then more was revealed to me than I understood or sought for; and from that I have written, not thinking I would be known among such high-ranking people.

<meta>9. For</meta>

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that his writings are intended for mutual spiritual inquiry rather than academic mastery, emphasizing that true religious understanding comes from finding the 'Center of all Beings' and the 'Philosopher's Stone' of Christ within oneself.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Seventeenth Epistle, brotherly love, Center of all Beings, Philosopher's Stone, spiritual revelation, mysticism, Adam, humanity</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the personal correspondence of the author, Jacob Boehme, regarding the circulation and nature of his spiritual writings.</meta>

## The Seventeenth Epistle.

<margin>☞</margin>9. For I thought that I wrote only for myself and intended to have kept it by me even until my end; yet now it is manifest and has come into many men's hands without my knowledge or effort. Therefore, I am urged to entreat you and others, and to remind you, not to look upon the simplicity or meanness <note>original: "meanneſſe," referring to a lack of formal education or low social status</note> of the author, or to be offended by my person. For it pleases the Most High to manifest His counsel through foolish people who are accounted as nothing before the world, so that it may be known and acknowledged that it comes from His hand.

10. Therefore, if my writings come into your hands, I would have you look upon them as the work of a *child*, in whom the Highest has performed His work. For there is content couched within them which no human reason may understand or apprehend; yet it is childlike, plain, and very easy to the <term>illuminate</term> <note>those who have been spiritually enlightened by God</note>. It will not be apprehended by Reason unless Reason is enkindled with God’s light; without that, there is no finding. This I desire to remind you of, and all those who read them.

<margin>☞</margin>11. Christ said, *“Seek and you shall find, knock and it shall be opened unto you; my Father will give the Holy Spirit to those who ask him for it.”* Herein lies the hidden <term>Pearl</term> <note>a reference to the "Pearl of Great Price," signifying divine wisdom or the Christ-spirit within</note>. He who would have it must obtain it this way, otherwise there is no finding—only a half-blind knowledge, like a delusive shadow of fancy or a "painted sound" <note>an empty, intellectual understanding without spiritual life</note>. In the Pearl, there is a living knowledge where a person never needs to ask, "is it true?" For it is written, *“They shall be taught of God.”* Also, *“We will come to you and make our abode in you.”* Also, *“He who has not the spirit of Christ is none of His.”* And therefore Christ says, *“Seek first the Kingdom of God and His righteousness, and then all other things shall be added unto you.”* He bids us to endeavor and seek after it—not to sit still and wait upon <term>Election</term> <note>the theological doctrine that God has already chosen who will be saved</note>—but to come to Him (Matthew 11 <note>original: "Matth. 10," likely referring to the call to discipleship, though "come unto me" is Matthew 11:28</note>) and to labor in His vineyard; not to wait and expect to be driven, but to come willingly.

12. Seeing then that I have found a *wise heart* in you, I am the more emboldened to write to you, hoping that you will judge wisely. Now, if anything in my writings should seem dark, obscure, or difficult to be understood, I pray you set it down and send it to me as the occasion serves; I will make it more childlike, plain, and clear. I commend you and yours, and myself with them, in brotherly union, into the meek, tender love of *Jesus Christ*.

---

->**Dated, July 3, 1621.**<-

<header>The Eighteenth Epistle.</header>

# THE
---
## EIGHTEENTH
### EPISTLE.
---

<margin>1.</margin> **NOBLE** Sir, After wishing you divine love and fullness of joy in our *Immanuel* <note>original: "Immanuel," Hebrew for "God with us"</note>, in his wondrous sweet power, and all bodily and temporal prosperity; I friendly certify to you, as opportunity now allows, that I have considered the *conference* <note>original: "Conference"; a meeting or formal discussion</note> we had lately. I observed that yourself and others there were present in high divine *zeal* as lovers of God and His truth, desiring to search with earnestness the mystery and ground of all beings and to come into the light. Therefore, I would not omit writing to you to remind you all of it, and also to give further *advantage* <note>original: "advantage"; here meaning assistance or opportunity</note> to such *zealous* seeking, and to declare how the Pearl <note>A reference to the "Pearl of Great Price" from the Gospel of Matthew, representing spiritual truth or the kingdom of heaven</note> is to be sought and found.

2. For I am also among the *seekers*, and it is my chief concern not to hide or bury that which God has entrusted to me, but to set it forth so that God’s will might be known in us, and His kingdom might come and be manifest in our *seeking* and *desire*. Thus, we might be found as children of the Most High toward one another, and acknowledge one another as *members* and brothers, and not as strangers and *outcasts*, or as devil-men toward one another—as the article of *Election* <note>original: "Election"; the theological doctrine concerning who is chosen by God for salvation (predestination)</note> (as it has been *handled* by some until now) suggests and implies.

3. And although we are apprehended and held captive in the heavy fall of *Adam* in the *anger* <note>The author refers to the state of divine wrath or "darkness" resulting from original sin</note>, such that his anger has indeed *chosen* us to be children of *damnation*; yet God has bestowed His beloved heart—*namely, the center of the Deity*—upon us. He has manifested it in humanity so that He might again regenerate us in Him and manifest life again *in us*.

<margin>☞ 4.</margin> And as the heavy fall came from one upon all, and passed [or pressed] upon all; so likewise the grace came from one and passed upon all; and the Apostle says that *Jesus Christ came into the world*

<meta>The text ends mid-sentence with the catchword "to," likely leading into the phrase "to save sinners."</meta>

<summary>The author encourages a noble correspondent to continue seeking spiritual truth, arguing that God's grace is available to all humanity despite the fall of Adam. He critiques harsh interpretations of the doctrine of Election (predestination) that create division among believers.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Divine Zeal, The Pearl, Article of Election, Predestination, Fall of Adam, Center of the Deity, Regeneration, Immanuel</keywords>

--- Page 61 ---
<lang>English</lang>
<page-num>243</page-num>
<header>The Eighteenth Epistle.</header>

...to seek and save that which is lost—that is, the poor, lost, damned sinner, captured in the wrath of God and destined for damnation. He did not come for the righteous, such as Abel, Seth, Enoch, Noah, Shem, Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob, who are already included in His love. Rather, He came for the poor sinful man held captive by God’s anger, like Cain, Ishmael, Esau, and the like. [He came] to seek and call these to return and be converted. As God said to Cain: "Rule over sin; do not let it have its power." <note>Genesis 4:7</note> If Cain could not have done this, God would not have commanded it of him. Likewise, if it had not been possible for Adam to have remained steadfast, God would never have forbidden him the tree.

5. However, since man cannot absolutely conclude, demonstrate, and satisfy the mind in this way—for the mind searches further into God’s omnipotence—another **study** is required. A person must learn to truly know the center of all essences: both love and anger. One must learn what the eternal love of God is, and what the eternal anger of God is (which hardens and devours man and makes him a child of eternal death), and how man is able, during this lifetime, to be freed from this prison and captivity [of God's anger].

6. But because I have explained and set this forth at such length in my books, I thought the mind should be satisfied. I have done this especially in the *Book of the Threefold Life*, and in the *three Books of the Incarnation of Jesus Christ*, and even further and deeper in the *Book of the Six Points concerning the Grand Mystery*. There I discuss the eternal birth of the deity and the three principles of the three worlds: how they exist within each other as one, how there is an eternal peace and agreement between them, how one gives birth to the other and desires the other, and that one without the other would be nothing. I supposed that the mind would find satisfaction there, seeing as this can be demonstrated in every being and thing.

7. But since Master *N.* <note>Böhme frequently uses "N." or "N.N." as a placeholder for a specific name or "nomen" (name).</note> has some of these writings in his hands, though not all, please inquire after them. If you have a mind to read them, you shall find the foundation not only of this article concerning *election*, but of all articles—indeed, of everything the human mind turns toward—if the foundation opened there is followed and achieved.

<margin>10</margin> 8. My noble heart, I pray you do not take as a joke what God reveals to us out of His love. Do not look upon the simplicity of the men through whom He does this; it is His good pleasure to manifest His power in those the world considers weak and foolish <note>original: "silly," which in the 17th century meant simple or lowly</note>. This is done for the instruction of the world. Since everyone lives in contention and strife and will not allow His spirit to *draw them*—so that they might know and acknowledge God’s kingdom to be within **US**—therefore the

<summary>Böhme argues that Christ came to save those "lost" in God's anger (like Cain or Esau) rather than the already righteous, and he directs the reader to his previous books for a deeper understanding of the "Grand Mystery" and the nature of God's love and wrath.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's exhortation to turn away from religious conflict toward a brotherly love rooted in the divine Center. The author, Jacob Boehme, emphasizes that spiritual truth is found through humility rather than intellectual debate.</meta>

### The Eighteenth Epistle

The Center of His Being, and of all Beings, is manifested to them. This is all done out of His love for us, so that we might yet depart from the miserable strife, contentions, and wranglings, and step into a brotherly and childlike love.

9. Sir, since I have found a longing mind in you, I would not conceal from you that it will be a time of serious earnestness. And I say, blessed and happy are those who are included under the *Sound* of the *Trumpet* that has already sounded; for there comes afterward such an earnest severity that *Babel* <note>original: "Babel" — Boehme uses this to refer to the "City of Confusion," specifically the fragmented and quarreling institutional churches of his day.</note> and contention—along with all pride, ambition, falsehood, and unrighteousness—shall drink an earnest draught, even of that which she has filled. I entreat you, for the sake of *Eternal Salvation*, to further consider this; it is known.

<margin>☛</margin>
10. I am ready—insofar as the mind might not find enough ground in my writings for its rest and satisfaction (if these points were noted down and sent to me)—to explain and enlarge upon them from the *Center* of all Beings, so that I hope the mind would be satisfied. However, it does not lie in searching, for no searching obtains or reaches the *Pearl* <note>original: "Pearle" — a reference to the "Pearl of Great Price" from the Gospel of Matthew, symbolizing divine wisdom or the inner Christ.</note> without God's light. A penitent, humble mind is required for this, one that wholly resigns itself to the grace of God and does not search for or desire anything except God's love and mercy. In that mind arises at last the *bright morning star*, so that the mind finds such a *Pearl* in which soul and body rejoice. And when this is found, then there is no need for further searching or teaching; for it is written, *They shall be taught of God.* Such a *Pearl* as this the sound of the *seventh trumpet* <note>The "seventh trumpet" in Revelation signals the completion of God's mystery and the beginning of His kingdom.</note> opens in the hearts of many who shall seek it with earnestness in a humble will resigned to God.

11. Therefore, my noble heart, I would not conceal this from you: much disputing and grubbing <note>original: "grubling" — searching or digging in an earthly or intellectual way.</note> in *Self-Reason* <term>Self-Reason: Human intellectual logic that operates independently of divine inspiration or spiritual submission.</term> does not find the Pearl. Instead, an earnest, fixed, penitent will finds it—which is more precious and costly than the world. And he who finds it would not give it for the riches of the whole world, for it provides him *temporal* and *eternal* joy, so that he is able to rejoice and be merry in the midst of the dungeon of darkness; and he accounts the goods of this world as *dirt* and *dung* in comparison to it. *Christ* said, *Seek, and you shall find; knock, and it shall be opened unto you.* Also, *My Father will give the Holy Spirit to those who ask him for it.* HEREIN LIES THE BASIS.

12. Let no man say, "My heart is shut up; I cannot pray." And if my heart says flatly "no," yet I will cast myself into the suffering and 

<summary>Boehme advises his correspondent that divine wisdom (the Pearl) is obtained through humble resignation and God's light rather than intellectual "Self-Reason" or religious disputes. He warns of a coming divine judgment on "Babel" while offering further personal guidance to help the seeker find spiritual peace.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, the Pearl, Self-Reason, Babel, Seventh Trumpet, resignation, spiritual enlightenment, humility, religious strife</keywords>

death of *Christ*; let him cast me into Heaven or Hell, yet I will be in his death; he is made an *eternal life* to me; and then, it is said, "*None can pluck my sheep out of my hands.*" <note>John 10:28</note>

13. The way to seek and know the precious pearl <note>A common metaphor in Böhme’s writing for spiritual wisdom or the "Pearl of Great Price."</note> is very sufficiently explained <note>original: "opened"</note> in the book *The Threefold Life*, otherwise I would have mentioned something of it. And I commend myself to your favor, and all of us into the meek love of *Jesus Christ*.

## THE
# NINETEENTH
## EPISTLE.

---

<margin>1.</margin>
**Noble**, worthy, and honored Sir: All sincere wishes (through the love of Christ wherewith he loves us in himself by his *incarnation in us*) for a happy and, in God, joyful new year, and all bodily welfare are offered here.

2. I am glad for your bodily health, and yet I am much more glad to observe how the *drawing of the Father* <note>original: "drawing of the Father"; a reference to John 6:44, "No man can come to me, except the Father which hath sent me draw him."</note> in the spirit of Christ continually stirs up and works in you a constant hunger for the precious pearl of divine knowledge. Since this happens within the same tree and growth where I also spring up and grow, it brings to me—as from a fellow branch in our angelic, *paradisiacal carol* <note>original: "Corall"; likely meaning "carol" (a song of joy) or "chorale," representing the harmonious unity of believers in heaven.</note>—pure desire and an acceptable will of love. It rejoices me in my meditation that the spirit of Christ still has his *church* and *temple* in the midst of the thorns, as it now appears. I wish from my heart with panting desires that it might flourish and grow yet stronger, so that *Babel* <note>In this context, "Babel" refers to the confusion, strife, and competing dogmas of the established institutional churches.</note> and the kingdom of contention and strife might be abolished and taken away, so that we might converse and walk together in love and union as the *children of Christ*.

3. I would be glad with all my heart—seeing as you read some of my writings—that they might be understood according to my own comprehension and mind. This is not for any temporal praise or glory to me, which is in Christ only and not *mine*, but for the sake of our eternal fellowship and fraternity, which we shall generally have with *one another* after this life.

4. I would very heartily share my *pearl* <note>original: "Pearl"; referring to the "Pearl of Great Price" (Matthew 13:46), a symbol for divine wisdom or spiritual illumination.</note> which God has given me with my loving brothers, so that they also, along with me, might bring their fruits to the table of God through divine knowledge and love. This work and labor is more acceptable to me than all the temporal praise, honor, and goods of the world.

5. And though I am, in comparison to you, like a child void of understanding, yet my Savior has been pleased out of his love and grace to bestow *his sense, mind, and understanding* upon me and

<lang>English</lang>

### The Eighteenth Epistle.

to open it through himself, so that I effectively know him and his will.

6. Which—although it seems like foolishness to human reason—is as clear as sunshine to me. It brings me joy and a desire that, in all temptations and afflictions from the Devil and his allies, I am able boldly and confidently to hide myself within it. My hope is stirred up and ignited there by God’s love-fire; and I have, as it were, a beautiful *Garden of roses* within it, which I do not only wish for my brothers to partake of, but I also desire and wish from my heart that the golden roses might also blossom in them.

7. I have understood that you are still anxious and troubled regarding the *Article* <note>A formal point of doctrine or belief.</note> about the will of God and his *Election* of mankind. You are still caught in a deep-seated conception regarding the *Decree* concerning man—as if God chose some according to his purpose and did not choose others out of his decree and purpose, and therefore he does not draw them in the spirit of Christ to the Father; or that the Father does not draw them in Christ. For my part, this has very often perplexed me, and I sincerely wish it might be understood how this matter stands in its own true nature.

8. For the words of the *Scripture* are right and true regarding *Election*, but they are not understood correctly. From this comes great evil and mischief, with fighting and heated contests. When I go into the center <note>Boehme refers to the spiritual core or point of origin of all things.</note>, then I find the whole foundation. There is nothing so subtle or profound, nor is there anything that can be asked about the will of God, that is not made as clear as the sun within that center.

<margin>☞</margin>
9. For I find the complete understanding of both good and evil, of God's love and anger, and both desires—namely, of the darkness and of the light. These I place into the humanity of Christ, seeing how God became man. I consider how the forms of human properties in the humanity of Christ were wholly and universally (without partiality) infused with the love of God in Christ—with the eternal word or voice of the deity (that is, with the *Divine mercury* <note>In Boehme’s theosophy, "mercury" refers to the creative, moving word or spirit that gives life and form.</note>) and with Divine essentiality (namely, in the blood of Christ). The wrath which was revealed through *Adam* in the human property was wholly drowned and shut up in eternal death; of which the Scripture now declares: *O Hell, where is your victory? O Death, where is your sting?*

10. Just as the Alchemist or Philosopher changes *Saturn* and *Mars* in the *Mercury* into a *Panacea* <note>original: "Panacéa"; a universal remedy or cure-all.</note>—that is, into a Paradisiacal source and property—whereas in their own fierce, wrathful <margin>* Or, Strength</margin> might, *Saturn* and *Mars* are an evil, poisonous source or quality. In this transformation, neither *Saturn*, *Mars*, nor *Mercury* are perceived in their wrathful pro-

<summary>Boehme addresses the confusing doctrine of divine election, arguing that it is misunderstood by many. He explains that through Christ's humanity, the "wrathful" properties of human nature (symbolized by Saturn and Mars) are transformed into a divine "Panacea" or universal remedy.</summary>

<meta>The previous page discussed the nature of the soul and the divine properties. This page continues the argument regarding the transformation of the soul from a state of wrath to one of love through Christ.</meta>

<page-num>148</page-num>

# The Nineteenth Epistle

<margin>☞</margin> properties; but out of their fierce, wrathful, malignant property there is an ascension of love and great joy. It is also this way now with the evil man, when he departs from his wrathful, malicious will, and in resignation wholly gives himself through the death of *Christ* into the *Panacea* <note>original: Panacéa; here meaning a universal remedy or restorative medicine, used by Böhme to describe Christ’s power to heal the soul</note> (*Christ*).

11. And as the sun in the firmament <note>the heavens or sky</note> shines upon the good and the evil, so likewise the desire of the *Panacea Christ* (being the Divine sun that shines therein) presents itself to all men. If they would but open their will, and depart from their selfhood, and set their desire into that, *Christ* would be born within them.

12. For the soul (as it is purely in itself) was spoken or breathed into the human body out of the eternal *speaking* word of the Father, out of the fire and light world, as out of God’s own Being. It has both wills free: out of the fire (that is, out of the Father’s anger, which is the eternal nature in which the soul is a creature, in the spiritual *sulfur, mercury, and salt* <note>original: Sulpher, Mercurie, and Salt; Böhme uses these Paracelsian chemical terms to represent fundamental spiritual principles: sulfur for fire/desire, mercury for motion/sound, and salt for substance/contraction</note>), and out of the light of the Divine power in the Divine sound <note>original: found; a printer's error for "sound" (Schall in German), referring to the creative word</note>, in which the soul is an angel and an image of God.

<margin>☞</margin> 13. And though it has lost the light through *Adam*, yet *Christ* has regained the same. He has again moved or awakened the center of love, so that the life of the light (if it stirs up its desire) may again enkindle itself in the humanity of *Christ* (which passes from one to all, just as the anger passes from one to all).

14. And though it might be said that he enkindles whom he pleases, yet I declare it as a precious truth worthy of acceptance: the Divine light is not ingressive <margin>☞</margin> [or a light coming into a man from without], but it is hidden even in the wicked man at the center. Just as God is hidden in time, it is *arisive* <note>original: Arisive; meaning a light arising or opening itself from within</note> [that is, a light springing up or opening itself from within], as the light of the candle arises out of the candle.

15. Man is not so altogether corrupt [forlorn or decayed] that there should be no possibility at all left in him. And though he be corrupt and spoiled, yet God (when he received and took pity on man) again stirred up and awakened the center of his love (being the true Deity which hid itself [or disappeared] in the sin [or fall of *Adam*]) in the human property.

<margin>☞</margin> 16. And as the sin and wrath of *Adam* (being yet but one) pressed upon and into all, so likewise the motion [or affection] of God’s love passed and pressed into *Christ’s* humanity, and out of *Christ’s* humanity through the whole humanity of all men.

17. *Christ* has again become the heart in the *Human Tree*. The Divine sound [voice or word] which has revealed itself in *Christ’s* humanity effectively now sounds through *Christ’s* humanity...

<summary>Böhme argues that Christ acts as a universal spiritual remedy (Panacea) available to all people from within, rather than as an external force. He describes the soul's composition through Paracelsian principles and emphasizes that Christ's restorative love is as universal as Adam's original sin.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Christology, Panacea, Adam, sulfur-mercury-salt, inner light, spiritual rebirth, humanity, redemption</keywords>

<page-num>149</page-num>
# The Nineteenth Epistle

<meta>This page continues the author's metaphorical discussion of humanity as a "Universal Tree" and the spiritual alchemy of the soul's transformation from wrath to love. The previous page likely established the "Tree" metaphor and the role of divine sap.</meta>

...in the Universal human Tree; and there is nothing missing, or in the way, except that the twig which is on the Tree will not draw the Sap of the Tree into itself.

18. It happens often that a person's character <term>original: "property"</term> is too much in love with *Mars* <note>In the author’s mystical system, Mars represents the fiery, aggressive, or wrathful quality of the soul.</note> and draws that influence into itself, stirring up a heat that causes the twig to wither. Thus, the *Mars* of the soul draws wrath and falsehood into itself, by which its *Mercury* <note>Mercury represents the active, moving life-force or spirit that animates the being.</note> becomes poisonous. Then *Saturn* <note>Saturn represents the contracting, hardening, and dark principle that gives form or "impression" to life.</note> (which is the impression of the life’s character) becomes altogether obscure and dark. As long as the *Mercury* of one's life lives in such a state, he cannot be drawn by the love of God, but only by the anger of God. He is chosen for damnation as long as he lives or continues in that *Free evil* wicked will.

19. The love of God offers itself to him, but he refuses to accept it. God desires him, but the wrath holds him fast, as Christ said:
> *O Jerusalem, Jerusalem! How often would I have gathered your children, as a hen gathers her chickens under her wings, but you would not!*

20. This unwillingness stands in the way. In his life, that man allows himself to be kept or held by God's anger, which is the wrath in the "out-spoken" (or expressed) *Mercury*, according to the fiery property of the Father. <note>The "fiery property of the Father" refers to the first principle of the divine nature—pure power and justice—which becomes "wrath" if not tempered by the Light/Son.</note>

21. Dear Brother, here lies the wicked child. Learn only to know what God Almighty is in Love and Anger, and how Man is that very same being and an image from the Eternal Spirit.

22. Do not say that God wills evil; He cannot will or desire anything that is evil according to the nature in which He is called "*God*." But if I should call this property [of Anger] "God," then I would be calling *Hell* "Heaven," darkness "light," and the Devil an "Angel."

23. It is true that all things belong to God, yet God is only understood in the source (or the working property) of the love of the light. The Anger in His light is actually a cause of the desire for love and of the Kingdom of joy. <note>The author argues that spiritual friction or "anger" is the necessary foundation that allows the "light" of joy to be felt and desired.</note>

24. When the soul brings its "fire-desire" out of its own self-will and into the "love-desire" of God—when it leaves its own selfishness, sinks into the mercy and compassion of God, and casts itself into the death of Christ—and when it no longer wills the source of fire but desires in its fire-life to be dead in the death of Christ, then the poison of the *mercurial* life dies in the will of iniquity. Then, a new twig and a budding of love-desire arises.

25. Loving Sir and Brother, know that I do not write as one who is blind or silent, without knowledge; I have found this myself by *Expe-* <margin>rience</margin>

<summary>The author explains that spiritual damnation results from the soul's own refusal to draw "sap" from the divine tree, choosing instead to dwell in wrathful, "mercurial" passions. Salvation is described as an alchemical-spiritual process of dying to the self-will to be reborn in God's love.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, spiritual alchemy, Mars, Mercury, Saturn, free will, wrath of God, love of God, Universal Tree, Christ's death</keywords>

# The Nineteenth Epistle

<center>->there<-</center>

experience: I have been as deep in your opinion as you yourself; yet my Savior has opened my eyes so that I see. I do not see by my own ability or power, but in *his*; as he knows me in *himself*, so he will see. I wish with all my heart that you might have an insight into my seeing, and that you might see with me through my vision. I would willingly give my heart and love to you as your own <note>original: "for a propriety," meaning as a personal possession</note>, and look through this glass out of you.

26. But I perceive that I am still mute to you, and that I am not yet known to you in the knowledge that has been given to me; and I wish from my heart that it might yet [once] happen.

27. I entreat and exhort you as a Christian, in all humility, that you would gather your *objections* together and send them to me in *writing*. I will deal with them according to my gifts, as a Christian ought, and I will declare and explain them in such a manner that I hope you shall acknowledge me as a brother in this matter. <margin>☜</margin>

28. <margin>☞</margin> Not that I presume to do it by myself, but my desire, which burns in me like fire, requires it of you. I (as I am myself) hope in God that we shall both so prosper in it that God will unite us in his love-desire and knowledge. It shall not result in any prejudice, scorn, or disgrace for you, for I have a heart that can keep a secret.

29. I exhort you in love toward a childlike <note>original: "filial"</note> humility in the true *resignation to Christ* <term>the spiritual practice of surrendering one's own will to the will of God</term>; only in that state may you be able to obtain and comprehend it. Otherwise, my well-wishing, good intent, and beginning are to no purpose; for I can give you nothing but my good affection and charitable will. If you will accept it, well! If not, I protest and testify before you and the face of God that I have begun my Christian duty <note>original: "devoir"</note> toward you and in you correctly. I have done my part, as I am bound in *conscience* to do.

30. I may come to see you myself, if my affairs permit, provided that it may lead to God's honor and humanity's salvation. For I know many thirsty souls in that area with whom I might refresh myself, and they in me.

31. I have at present found a very precious *jewel*, which might be profitable not only for the soul but for the body, and good for your *patients*.

32. If men would labor in Christ’s vineyard, God might even now give us such sunshine as would warm the apothecary’s shop <note>likely a metaphor for the restoration of true medicine or spiritual healing</note>, which many honest people have desired for a long time. This sunshine would boldly dispel the smoke in *Babel* <term>a common mystical term for the confused and divided state of the worldly church</term> and be a refreshment to the children of Christ in their oppression, misery, and tribulation.

33. But in truth, *because men will be so wicked and godless*,

<summary>In this letter, the author humbly offers to share his spiritual insights with the recipient, inviting them to submit their objections in writing for a brotherly discussion on faith and the "resignation to Christ." He suggests that true spiritual enlightenment could bring healing to both soul and body, potentially even reforming the medical and religious practices of the day.</summary>

<meta>The text continues the author's prophetic warnings and spiritual counsel regarding coming judgments and the eventual restoration of Christendom.</meta>

<page-num>151</page-num>
<header>The Nineteenth Epistle.</header>

...there shall be a horrible storm of hail and rain, at which the Earth shall quake, and many thousand souls will be drowned in the water.

34. I would gladly mention something to you here, but at this time it is not possible; please take notice of the storm toward the *East*; the one toward the *North* is not far from it. In the *South*, there is a great smoke that causes the eyes of those in the *West* to smart.

35. Let no one say, when the storm passes by, that this or that man is righteous before God, or that it shall go well with him because of his *Religion*. The anger of God is ignited against *everyone*, and they are *all* alike to Him despite their *Religion*, as long as they live no differently than each other.

<margin>☛</margin>
36. The Most High sweeps out one broom <note>original: "besom," a broom made of twigs</note> with another; but there springs up a *Lily* <note>In Böhme’s writing, the Lily represents the "Lily-time," a future age of spiritual purity and peace</note> for all nations; happy are those who understand it.

37. The thirsty soul must not say, "The Lord has forsaken me, He has forgotten me." God can no more forget than a mother can forget her child—and even if she should forget, yet the Lord has not forgotten His poor, exceedingly distressed, and afflicted *Christendom*. He has marked her in His wounds pierced with nails; *His light shall shine from the East to the West as a testimony to all people*.

<margin>Note</margin>
38. From the *South* there arises a *Lily* toward the *North*; whoever receives it as their own <note>original: "for a propriety"</note> shall sing the song of God's mercy. At that time, the Word of the Lord will flourish like grass upon the Earth, and the nations will sing the Song of *Babel* in one tune <note>This suggests a reversal of the confusion of languages at the Tower of Babel, leading to a unified spiritual understanding</note>; for the beginning has found the end.

39. Think upon my cryptic <note>original: "darke"</note> sayings, for at this time I could not be more plain; seeing that men have only sought after pride and greed, and have despised the mirror of God's anger. They have not repented but have worked sin upon sin, until sin devours itself and the wrath of God is fully satisfied.

40. *Human Reason* will offer little help here with its consultations, but will instead blow up the fire and provide further cause for trouble.

41. God was good before this distress; but since people forsake God, scorn and misery follow.

<margin>☛</margin>
42. Let everyone take care of themselves; but he who does not seek to save himself <note>Likely an allusion to Matthew 16:25: "For whoever wants to save their life will lose it, but whoever loses their life for me will find it."</note> shall be sought and preserved. And so, I commit you to the love of *Jesus Christ*.

<summary>The author warns of a coming divine judgment that will affect all regardless of their outward religion, while promising a future era of spiritual renewal symbolized by the "Lily." He emphasizes that human reason cannot prevent this cleansing process, which will eventually lead to a unified, peaceful Christendom.</summary>

# THE
## TWENTIETH
### EPISTLE.

---

->Our Salvation in the life of **Jesus Christ** in **us.**<-

---

<margin>1.</margin> Noble Sir, etc. I begin with all cordial wishes for divine light in effective divine working power, from the fountain of Love, *Jesus Christ*, and for all temporal prosperity. I thought it good to visit you with an epistle to remind you—out of a faithful Christian intent and affection—of the *conference* between *H. N.* and *D. S.* regarding the *divine* purpose and will concerning *man*.

<margin>2.</margin> And *D. S.* was not sufficiently answered at that time regarding his question. This was because I was not practiced in their *scholastic* <term>scholastic: a formal, academic method of theology and philosophy taught in European universities during the Middle Ages and Early Modern period</term> way of referring to this *article* <note>original: "Article"; here meaning a specific point of religious doctrine</note> through the contemplation of the internal ground <note>The "internal ground" refers to the spiritual essence or divine reality within, as opposed to the "outward ground" of physical appearances.</note> via the outward ground. It was also because such *meetings*, due to the *drink* (to which I am unaccustomed), darken and cloud subtle and pure understanding.

<margin>3. ☞</margin> Also, because of their *Latin* language [or scholastic expressions], I was hindered from comprehending the matter in its ground. Consequently, he began to triumph with his received *opinion*, yet without a sufficient understanding of the cited passages of *Scripture*, and also without a sufficient basis for *logical* conclusions based on *reason*—in which, truth be told, he is excellently well practiced in their *scholastic* way.

<margin>4.</margin> After this discourse, I turned myself in *divine* grace to the internal ground of *divine vision* to examine the matter. I prayed to the Lord that he would open the understanding of its whole basis to me, so that I might know it in its proper and peculiar *nature* <note>original: "Species"; from the Latin, meaning the specific kind or essential form of a thing</note>.

<margin>5.</margin> Thereupon it appeared to me that I (through a *divine* introduction into the wonderful works of God) have sufficient cause and

<summary>In this letter, the author reflects on a theological debate between H. N. and D. S., admitting that he was initially hindered by scholastic jargon, Latin, and the presence of alcohol. He explains that through prayer and divine vision, he sought a deeper spiritual understanding of the "divine purpose" for humanity.</summary>
<keywords>H. N., D. S., Scholasticism, Divine Vision, internal ground, Latin, theological debate, Christian doctrine</keywords>

<page-num>157</page-num>

# The Twentieth Epistle

...and grounded therein; because of this, a great desire came upon me to set down this **basis** <note>original: "Ground"</note> of the divine will and eternal purposes regarding **Predestination**, and to write it in a book; which I later took the opportunity to do, as it was requested by Mr. **B. T.** and others.

6. I did not intend to despise anyone's opinion, or to undertake to insult them with any unchristian disgrace or biting <note>original: "girding"</note> taunts; rather, it was out of a faithful, real Christian affection, and a brotherly sharing of my **talent**, lent to me by God.

<margin>
Note:
What is contained in the Book of Election.
</margin>

7. This work is so deeply and profoundly grounded that one may understand not only the basis of this question concerning God’s will, but likewise the hidden God may be known in His manifestation in all visible things. It includes a very clear explanation of how the basis of the Grand Mystery <note>original: "Grand Mystery"; the *Mysterium Magnum*, Böhme's term for the divine chaos or potentiality from which the universe is manifested.</note> is to be understood (which is the eternal, expressed Word of God, in which wisdom has worked from eternity, and in which all things have been seen in a <term>magical</term> <note>original: "Magicall"; here referring to the supernatural power of the divine will or imagination rather than sorcery.</note> manner or Idea, without any created being).

8. And also how the same Grand Mystery has brought itself—through the expression or speaking out of the divine <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: "Science"</note>, through the Word of God (in the place of this world)—into a differentiation <note>original: "severation"</note> and understandability for the creation. It explains how the origin of good and evil in the differentiation of the divine knowledge in the Grand Mystery, in the eternal principles, is to be understood in relation to divine manifestation and work. <note>A manicule (pointing hand) in the original margin highlights this paragraph.</note>

9. In this, the hidden God may be understood not only in His being and will, but likewise the whole basis of His manifestation through His expressed Word, out of the eternal powers of the Grand Mystery, which is the essence of eternity. It explains how the same has come into a visible, understandable, created, and external being, and what the basis of all mysteries is, and how they are sufficiently made known and revealed.

<margin>
* *Spiritus Mundi* <note>original: "Spirit of the World"</note>
</margin>

10. Also therein is a large, explanatory basis of the <term>universal</term> <note>original: "Cosmick"</note> spirit, in which the creation of this world lives; and withal, a very clear basis of the internal, spiritual, angelic, and soulful life. It also covers the rise, fall, and restoration of man, and the symbols <note>original: "Typifications"</note> of the Scripture in the Old and New Testaments concerning the Kingdom of Nature and the Kingdom of Grace; what God's righteousness and His election or purpose is, and how they are to be understood.

11. Also included is a clear demonstration of the lineage in the Kingdom of Nature, from Adam through his children, and of the kingdom of the manifestation of grace in the inspired voice of grace from the embodied divine knowledge, in the **Word of Love**, in the womb of grace.
