## The Twentieth Epistle.

<margin>* Or, Sensible or perceptible.</margin>
12. And then follows a clear explanation of certain passages of Scripture, especially chapters 9, 10, and 11 of the Epistle of Paul to the Romans. On these chapters, human reason tries to support itself, but a full <term>perceptible ground</term><note>original: "sensall ground"; refers to a basis of understanding that can be grasped by the senses or the intellect</note> is demonstrated through the quotation and examination of the Scriptures.

13. However, I do not treat this in a logical way, as is done in the academic schools <note>original: "Schooles"; referring to the universities and scholastic theologians of the era</note>. There, they do nothing but raise objections and contradictions against one another, devising knotty arguments and dilemmas. They refuse to test and examine each other's reasoning in a sensible way within the understanding. Instead, they attack, pressure, judge, and condemn one another as heretics and revilers. This is truly nothing but **Babel** <note>a biblical reference to the Tower of Babel, used here to signify a place of spiritual confusion and pride</note>, a mother of proud, arrogant, and great spiritual adultery <note>original: "Whoredome"; often used in a biblical sense to describe the corruption of divine truth or religious unfaithfulness</note>, errors, and confusion. In such a place, the name of God is blasphemed, and the Holy Spirit is insulted and judged by human reason through the literal word of Scripture.

14. I have entirely refused to follow this path with my own talent; for not one jot or tittle of the **Law** in the **Scripture** shall perish until all is fulfilled. The sayings of the Scripture, along with their symbolic types, must remain true and are not contradictory, despite what human reason might suppose.

15. Therefore, I have taken those passages of Scripture that seem *contrary* to one another—such as where it is written, "*God wills that all people shall be saved,*" and yet elsewhere, "*God hardens their hearts so they do not understand, even though they see it*"—and I have clarified and reconciled them. I hope in God and for His children that they will see and understand this divine manifestation of grace. I hope they will depart from such strife and controversy regarding the *will of God* and the person of Christ, and instead learn to understand the justification of a poor sinner before God.

16. I have faithfully and diligently done this for everyone, according to my gifts and out of a Christian, brotherly heart. I further offer that if anyone should still cling to such a private notion <note>original: "conceit"</note> or opinion and is not satisfied in his thoughts, he should set down his views and conclusions in writing in a Christian and friendly way and send them to me. He shall receive such an answer to his questions and objections that *he will see that my intentions were truly Christian and proceeded from a divine gift.*

17. Seeing, Sir, that you—along with your brother, Mr. **H. S.**, and the deeply learned doctors **J. S.** and **J. D. K.**—are my highly respected friends and my eternal fellow members and brothers in the **Life-Tree of Christ**, I also rejoice with them from a religious heart. I see that God has adorned and endowed them with understanding and

<meta>catchword: wisdom,</meta>

wisdom, and other Christian virtues; whom likewise I have acknowledged always as my favorable, charitable, and gracious *Masters*; thereupon I have taken order that they should get a copy of this treatise among them, desiring and entreating them to deal one with another in a Christian, brotherly way, and communicate it to each other for the transcribing of it, by reason of greater employments of my talent <note>original: "Talent"; a reference to the Biblical Parable of the Talents, meaning his spiritual gifts and duties</note>, and that would be a hindrance to me; otherwise I would have sent to each a copy of it.

**18.** Yet if this treatise should be intercepted and not come into their hands, then I will send them my own *manuscript*; and I entreat them that they would be pleased to read it over and consider it without prejudice or irksomeness; they shall find such rich sense and meaning therein, that it will be profitable to them in many things, but especially in the Christian exercise of the <term>New birth</term> <note>The spiritual transformation or regeneration of a person through divine grace</note>.

**19.** And wherein I can be further serviceable to them in my small gifts, I shall be mindful always faithfully to perform the same, as an obliged Christian, in thankful gratefulness for their good affections, uprightness, candor, and favors towards me.

**20.** And although I am a man of no repute, in respect of their greatness or highness, and also in respect of D.D. <note>likely referring to Doctors of Divinity, the established academic theologians of the time</note> yet let them but for a while shut out *Reason*, and think that it pleases the Most High to manifest his wonders through *simple people* and [such as are] accounted foolish in the world's eye; as it has come to pass from the beginning of the world until now, when *alterations* <note>significant historical or spiritual shifts</note> have been about to occur.

**21.** Sirs, know for certain, that I will not be lacking in giving an answer to any man’s high or profound questions, if they be virtuous, apt, and Christian; for the same is committed to me in trust from the Most High, and given as a free gift of grace.

**22.** Which I mention with a Christian intent and affection, so that if anyone still had any doubt in that opinion [to see] whether I in love could help him to rid himself of it, and bring him into the *temperature of the mind* <note>original: "Temperature of the minde"; a state of spiritual and mental balance or equilibrium</note>; I should not think any pains too much to impart my gifts and understanding to him. And so I commend you and yours into the love of *Jesus Christ*, and myself into your favor.

->**Dated, February 20, 1623.**<-

# A POSTSCRIPT.

23. The tribulation and destruction of Babel <note>original: "Babel"; a common metaphor for a corrupt or confused religious and social order</note> approaches with exceeding haste; the storm arises upon all coasts. It shall be a severe <note>original: "soar"</note> tempest; vain hope deceives, for the breaking of the tree is at hand, which is known by the wonders.

<margin>Domestic and internal <note>original: "Domestick intestine"</note></margin>
24. The homebred fire hurts its own native country; righteousness and truth are trodden underfoot; great heaviness, trouble, and calamity grow on apace.

<margin>Pride, covetousness, envy, wrath, and all falsehood, under a holy show of religion.</margin>
25. Men shall mourn for an old, empty, ruinous cottage on which salvation does not rely; they will be enraged for the nest wherein Satan has hatched his young.

26. The Tower of Babel is without foundation; men suppose they can prop it up, but a wind from the Lord overthrows it.

<margin>Or, Proof.</margin>
27. The hearts and thoughts of men shall be revealed; for there comes a <term>Proba</term> <note>Latin: a trial, test, or proof</note> from the Lord whereby the verbal, titular Christian <note>one who is Christian in name only</note> shall be exposed in his false heart and soul. He will be like a reed that is tossed to and fro by the wind; seeing his heart is wavering, now this way, now that way, his false foundation becomes manifest.

<margin>The Turk <note>The Ottoman Empire</note> shall turn into a true Christian.</margin>
28. Many shall betray themselves and ruin both body and goods through hypocrisy; the hypocrites and titular Christians shall faint <note>original: "quail"</note> for fear when their false foundation shall be revealed.

<margin>☛</margin>
29. The Oriental Beast <note>likely referring to the Ottoman Empire</note> gets a human heart; and before this comes to pass, he helps to tear down the Tower of Babel with his claws.

<margin>The Emperor of Germany <note>The Holy Roman Emperor</note>.</margin>
30. In the darkness of the North, there arises a Sun which takes its light from the sensory properties of the nature of all beings—from the formed, expressed, and re-expressing Word; and this is a wonder at which all nations rejoice.

31. An Eagle <note>symbol of the Holy Roman Empire</note> has hatched young lions <note>symbolizing subordinate princes or rising national powers</note> in his nest and brought them prey for so long until they have grown great. He hoped that they would likewise bring their prey to him again; but they have forgotten that. They take the Eagle and pluck off his feathers and bite off his claws for unfaithfulness, so that he can fetch no more prey, even if he should starve for hunger.

32. But they quarrel <note>original: "fall out"</note> about the Eagle’s nest and tear one another in anger, until their anger becomes a fire which burns up the nest; and this is from the Lord of all beings.

<summary>This postscript uses vivid allegorical imagery—including the Eagle, Lions, and the Oriental Beast—to predict the violent collapse of the Holy Roman Empire and the exposure of hypocritical "titular" Christians during a coming divine trial.</summary>
<keywords>Babel, Proba, titular Christian, Oriental Beast, Holy Roman Emperor, prophecy, Ottoman Empire, Germany, allegory</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the "Twentieth Epistle." The previous page likely discussed the spiritual state of man and the dangers of worldly riches or pride. The text continues with a series of numbered aphorisms and mystical prophecies, a style typical of the writer Jacob Boehme.</meta>

<page-num>161</page-num>

# The Twentieth Epistle.

33. If the rich man knew upon what foundation he stood, he would look within himself and consider his final end.

34. The Sun gives life to many things, and likewise brings death to many things.

35. But he who remains still in self-will <note>original: "Selfe-will"; in this mystical context, it refers to the personal ego or individual desire that acts independently of God's will</note> and allows his internal ground—from which humanity originally originates—to lead and guide him, he is the noblest and richest person on the Earth.

36. The Postillion <term>postillion: traditionally a guide for a horse-drawn carriage; here used symbolically as a spiritual herald or messenger of a new era</term> arising out of the ground of nature comes and carries the sword over the Earth. He has six winds as his assistants, which have ruled upon the Earth for a long time. These winds break the Postillion's sword through the revelation of the seventh wind, which they always kept hidden within themselves; but because of the Postillion's power, they must call upon and manifest him.

<margin>☞</margin>
37. A new fire reveals this seventh wind, and at this time, the fountain of grace shall flow with sweet water, and the afflicted and oppressed shall be refreshed.

<summary>This page contrasts the vanity of the rich with the spiritual nobility of those who surrender their self-will to their "internal ground." It concludes with a mystical prophecy regarding a "Postillion" and a "seventh wind" that brings a new era of grace and refreshment for the oppressed.</summary>

# THE TWENTY-FIRST EPISTLE.
## TO FREDERICK CRAUSSEN,
### DOCTOR OF MEDICINE. <note>original: "Physick"</note>

---

->*Our Salvation in the life of Jesus Christ in us.*<-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
Dearest sir, Christian, and faithful friend: I wish for you, your family, and all those who desire and love *Jesus* (in all faithful, real, cooperating love-desire), the light and effective working power of God in our Tree of Life, <note>original: "Life-Tree"</note> *Jesus Christ*, together with all bodily welfare.

<margin>2.</margin>
Following the request of yourself and Mr. *N.*, I have considered those passages of Scripture which Mr. *N.* set down in his letter, which you delivered to me. In that letter, I was urged to explain <note>original: "expound"</note> those passages in Christian love, according to my gifts and understanding; especially the ninth and eleventh chapters of the Epistle of Paul to the *Romans*, at which human reason <note>original: "Reason"</note> stumbles. This I have not only done willingly and readily in

<meta>catchword: Christian</meta>

<summary>In this opening to his twenty-first letter, the author greets Dr. Frederick Craussen and agrees to provide a spiritual interpretation of difficult passages in the Book of Romans concerning divine election.</summary>
<keywords>Frederick Craussen, Jesus Christ, Epistle to the Romans, human reason, biblical interpretation, Tree of Life</keywords>

<meta>The previous page concludes a discussion on Christian obligation and affection, transitioning into a defense of the author's scriptural interpretations and the underlying spiritual reality ("GROUND") of divine manifestation.</meta>

<page-num>159</page-num>

# The Twenty-First Epistle

...Christian obligation and good affection by explaining those cited texts; but I have also set down and described the true *GROUND* <note>original: "GROUND" - here meaning the fundamental basis or underlying reality or essence</note> of the Divine Manifestation in such a manner that I hope men will see the truth.

3. But if there is a mind divinely inclined and dedicated, which can give honor to God, I hope it will be understood and taken according to my meaning and conception, and not interpreted otherwise, as was done formerly. I will overlook that and rather prefer Christian love, as we are *bound in Christ to kindly instruct one another in our various gifts*. In this, we give God the honor and despise no one for his divine gifts; for he who does so *blasphemes the Holy Spirit*, and the Scripture pronounces a harsh sentence against such a person.

4. Now, although this treatise is somewhat long, let the reader not find it tedious or tiresome. I thought it of little importance for me to attempt to prove and clarify such a writing without a sufficient foundation; and therefore I have placed the cited passages of Scripture upon the deepest ground and shown how they *originally* arise out of *their center*, and what their sense and meaning is.

<margin>[manicule]</margin>
5. For it is not enough that I should gather together a great heap of scriptural passages to oppose and contradict those cited. No, no; this is of no value before God and the truth. For "not the smallest mark or letter of this *Law* shall pass away until all be fulfilled," says Christ. The sayings of Scripture must remain true and not clash with one another; and though they seem to be contrary and oppose each other, it is only to those to whom the understanding of them is not given, and who are not gifted or made capable of explaining and interpreting them.

<margin>* Spirit of the World <note>original: Spiritus Mundi</note></margin>
6. But he who undertakes to clearly interpret them correctly must have an understanding of the *Harmony* <note>original: "Accordance"</note>, so that he may know how to reconcile those places which seem contradictory to human reason. He must not base them on *conjecture* or opinion—whether it be so or not—if he wishes to teach thoroughly and with certainty. For from opinion and conjecture arise only strife and controversy, upon which great *Babylon* <note>referring to the biblical city as a symbol of spiritual confusion and false authority</note> is built—namely, spiritual pride and infidelity. In such a state, one wishes to be an *Apostle* and yet is not sent or acknowledged by God, but runs according to his own opinion and the prompting [or instigation] of the **Spirit of the World**.

7. And although many run by the *Father's attraction* <note>original: "Drawing of the Father," referencing the internal spiritual pull of God</note>, yet if the true light of eternal life in the Word of the Divine essence does not appear...
<note>The Word of the Divine essence is described here as an expression or breathing out (original: "spiration") of both the holy and natural Word in its differentiation (original: "severation"), from which creation, good, and evil originate.</note>

<summary>In this epistle, the author defends his deep, foundational interpretation of Scripture, arguing that apparent biblical contradictions can only be reconciled through divine gift rather than human opinion or "the Spirit of the World." He emphasizes that spiritual pride leads to the confusion of "Babylon," while true understanding requires recognizing the "Harmony" of the Divine Word.</summary>

<keywords>Divine Manifestation, Ground, Holy Spirit, Scripture interpretation, Accordance, Babylon, Spiritus Mundi, Spiration, Severation, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

## The Twenty-First Epistle

enlighten him; he will be far from being able to unite the supposed contradictions of the Scripture's sayings, and to speak from **ONE centre** in such a way that not the least detail is diminished in their harmony [or reconciliation].

8. I set this down, not to displease *N.* <note>The letter "N" was often used in this period as a placeholder for a specific name (Nomen) or to protect the identity of an individual, likely a theological opponent in this context.</note> or anyone else, but only because of the long-continued, conflicting disagreements of human reason, in which the world goes astray and truth lies veiled. Because of this, men judging this article about the *will of God* run on in reason and its arguments *without foundation*. But where Christ is born in man, there strife ceases, and God the Father speaks His Word in Christ through the soul of man. For such conclusions [and reasonings] there must be an inward divine light which provides certainty; otherwise, there is no grounding upon human reason alone.

<margin>☛</margin>
9. You may obtain this treatise from Mr. *Michael of Ender*, who has now received it. In my own handwriting, it contains about 42 sheets; <note>A "sheet" refers to a large piece of paper folded to create pages; 42 sheets would indicate a very substantial manuscript of over 150 pages.</note> and if you are pleased to share it with *N.* as your good friend and kinsman, I am content. However, provided that you tell him he should not understand it as if I had written anything in it passionately against him or any other; for such passions or impulses do not dwell in my soul without urgent and great cause. Although I am not without failings and lapses, my Savior Christ in me has shown me such grace that all insults and objections against me fall away. One word that proceeds out of Divine love toward me (where I perceive a divine earnestness) causes those objections to be rejected like a weed, which I would not willingly plant in my garden; for from a weed, nothing grows but a worthless weed again.

10. Furthermore, it is again requested of *N.* out of Christian love that—since at his request I have expounded his cited passages of Scripture according to my small gifts, which are known to God—he would be pleased (if my exposition displeased him and was not, in his opinion, sufficiently grounded and fundamental) to do me the favor of expounding those cited texts himself. I mean *especially the 9th and 10th chapters of the Epistle of St. Paul to the Romans*, and even those very Scriptures which I have explained, together with the whole basis of the Divine will regarding good and evil: how its origin is in man, or outside of man. Let him unfold and declare them in a sensible <term>sensall: clear and perceivable to the understanding</term> manner.

11. And then I desire that he would declare and expound to me the *inspired word of grace, in the "seed of the woman" in Paradise* <note>A reference to the promise in Genesis 3:15.</note>; and then the "two lines": *namely*, the kingdom of the corrupt human nature, and the kingdom of grace in the inward voice of grace.

12. Or if my exposition regarding *Abraham, Ishmael, Isaac,* and also *Jacob and Esau* did not please him, let him, out of Christian love, [explain them himself].

# The one and twentieth Epistle.

<margin>☞</margin>
12. I wish he would show his gifts and explain the basis of them so that I might find his gifts and understanding in those areas. Then, if I can see that God has gifted *him* with a larger measure of understanding in these <term>high mysteries</term> <note>original: "high Mysteries"; profound spiritual truths concerning the nature of God and the universe.</note> than me, I will accept it with great gladness. I will love him for his gifts and give thanks to our God for them. I will rejoice with *him*, as a fellow member, in our gifts in the spirit of Christ. All of this would lead more to the profit and benefit of our brothers and Christian fellow-members; it would be more godly, commendable, and praiseworthy than a raw contradiction born out of emotion for the sake of a person's willful ego.

13. But I pray to my God in Christ that He would be pleased to open his heart so that his soul may see into the ground of my gifts. For truly, I am a *simple* man <note>original: "simple"; meaning uneducated or lacking formal scholarly training.</note>; I never studied or learned this high mystery, nor did I seek after it in such a way, or know anything of it. I sought only the heart of love in Christ Jesus; but when I had obtained that, with exceeding great joy of my soul, then this *treasure* of divine and natural knowledge was opened and given to me. I have not boasted of this until now, but I heartily desired and begged of God to know if the time had come for this knowledge to be *revealed* in the hearts of many. Regarding this, I obtained an *effectual* answer, so that I know very well what I have hinted at and mentioned <margin>☞</margin> [ in my writings. ]

14. And although I am therefore hated by many in the world, men will *shortly see* why God has opened and revealed the <term>Grand Mystery</term> <note>original: "grand Mystery"; likely the *Mysterium Magnum*, or the foundational secret of how the divine and physical worlds are structured.</note> to a humble, lowly [despised] layperson. That is, the ground of all secrets and mysteries; and yet *I may not reveal* all that is made known to me. Nevertheless, it might be shared with worthy people if I found that it was God's will and profitable to men—as a while ago a very precious *pearl* <note>In this context, the "pearl" often refers to a specific, invaluable spiritual realization or the "Pearl of Great Price" mentioned in the Gospels.</note> was revealed to me, which has its time for effectual practical use and *benefit*. It is always a very profitable benefit in my soul. Do not wonder or marvel so much at the simplicity of what God does, for the time of the proud has come to its end.

15. Furthermore, I desire and entreat *Mr. N* to deal Christianly and candidly, in love, with his gifts, and not to taunt and slander my name as he did before, by which the gifts of the *Holy Ghost* are spoken of evilly. If he does this, he shall be answered with equal modesty, mildness, and respect. But if it should turn out—contrary to my good intent and hope—that I am *further* slandered by people or in writings, let him know (if I either see or hear of it with certain proof) that he shall not lack a purposeful answer through a *divine gift*; and he shall gain no advantage or praise from it.

16. And I mean this sincerely; and I encourage him out of Christian love and obligation to answer; if he will not declare and explain

When the "seed of the woman" <note>A biblical reference to the Messiah from Genesis 3:15, who defeats the serpent/evil.</note> breaks the head of the serpent, we return again into the *Temperature* <term>Boehme’s term for the state of perfect balance and harmony where all opposing qualities are unified</term>; and we are in *Christ* only one, just as a tree has many boughs and branches.

<header>The Twenty-Second Epistle.</header>

### THE
### TWENTY-SEC-
### OND EPISTLE.

---

<margin>1.</margin> Much respected Sir and dear Doctor, please give Mr. *N.* my letter to read, but *not* this note. Encourage him toward Christian humility to see if perhaps the eyes of his soul might be opened—which I heartily and freely wish for him. It will be no disgrace for him to love and embrace the truth; for I perceive clearly what stands in the way and holds him back: nothing other than *self-love*. This is because he has so far laid out and published his own foundation <note>original: "ground," referring to the underlying philosophical or theological basis of his belief</note> and gained great reputation and respect among many, and this foundation of mine does not entirely agree with him. Therefore, self-love drives him to the opposite view, even though he has not yet grasped my foundation and is still like an infant regarding it.

<margin>2.</margin> But if the honor of God and brotherly love <note>original: "memberlike love," referring to the love between members of the Body of Christ</note> take root in his heart, he truly has nothing against me or my writings. Indeed, they might even better him and help him grow as a Christian; but without a willing heart, he will remain blind to them. For no human reason can understand this foundation *without* the eternal love of God, in which all the treasures of wisdom are hidden.

<margin>3.</margin> But please send me word back in a letter regarding what his opinion is; his hidden foundation is revealed by this. I hope that he will also gain sight, since he otherwise has a sharp mind and has well studied *Logic*. Perhaps he will search further, but if he will not, his opinion does not cancel out the gifts of God. He cannot overthrow my foundation—especially the explanation of those biblical texts—with any Scripture. My intentions toward him are sincere.

<margin>4.</margin> Furthermore, I would ask you to do me this favor: send the *Treatise on Election* <note>referring to the author's work on the doctrine of predestination, or the "Election of Grace"</note> to Mr. *N.* to read. Since he is a courteous gentleman, and also because the debate regarding this doctrine <note>original: "Article"</note> turned out such that it should not be viewed as if a person were left completely lost in ignorance.

<sig>Y 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: 5. But</meta>

<summary>The author writes to a doctor, asking him to share a letter and a treatise with a scholar named Mr. N. while warning that Mr. N.'s pride and "self-love" may prevent him from understanding the author's spiritual "ground" or foundation.</summary>

## The twenty-second Epistle.

5. But if need requires, I shall so explain myself that they shall see from what ground I write. Let them give me whatever *Questions* they please—whether they be in *Nature*, or out of *Nature*, in *Time*, or in *Eternity*—I will not be at all lacking therein, through *Divine Grace*, but will give a sufficient answer, provided that it be done in a *Christian* way, and not out of affection, passion, caviling <note>original: "cavilling"; making petty or unnecessary objections</note>, or reviling; I shall deal with them in like manner.

6. <margin>☞</margin> In our recent meeting I was poorly disposed for such a debate <note>original: "Disputation"</note>, for wine and sumptuous food hide the *Pearl's ground* <note>The "Pearl's ground" refers to the inner spiritual foundation or the "Pearl of Great Price" mentioned in the Gospels; Böhme believed rich living dulled spiritual insight.</note>, especially because I am not accustomed to it; at home I live very humbly and soberly. Also, *Mr. N.* was not sufficiently answered; but I offer to answer *him and all others* who mean *Christianly*. Let them only give me their *Questions* in writing and explain their opinion therein, so that I may see what they conclude. I will give them a fundamental, large, and explanatory answer, and will not defend myself with any *Sect* or *Sectarian Name*, nor patronize myself with such things. In the ground of truth, I am not a *Flaccinian* <note>A follower of Matthias Flacius Illyricus (1520–1575), a Lutheran reformer who held the controversial view that original sin had become the very substance of human nature.</note> as *N.* supposes, but I shall stand in the ground of truth.

7. For I teach no self-ability without *Christ* to attain *Adoption* <note>The spiritual process of being made a child of God.</note> as *N.* thinks; only I am not satisfied with his opinion, much less with *Mr. N. N.’s*, which wholly clashes against the *Scriptures*. For I am dead to all opinions within me and have nothing but what is given to me by *God* to know. I leave all of you to judge how I know what I know—that I, as a *lay, uneducated* <note>original: "Illiterate"</note>, and untrained man, have anything to do with you who are bred up in the high schools and must set myself against *learned art* <note>Scholastic or academic philosophy and theology.</note>. And yet, in my own reason, I do not know how to attain this without *God’s* knowing; but I look upon what *God* does. In the ground of my gifts, I know well enough what I do <margin>☞</margin> in this purpose and intention; and yet it is no intention of my own, but rather the time brings it forth, and *HE* who rules all things drives and orders it.

8. <margin>☞</margin> Concerning our secret discourse (*as you know*), you must yet be patient to continue in that known process for a good while; in this beginning, no other way will be admitted. It may well be accomplished in this process in the seventh year, for it must be opened through all the six properties <note>original: "fix Properties"; Böhme’s system of the seven qualities or "forms" of nature, where the first six represent the active struggle and the seventh represents completion.</note> of the *spiritual ground*. Although it is already opened through the *Sun*, the *Key* has scarcely come into the first or second degree of the *center* of nature. For each property among the six forms of the *spiritual* life has a separate or peculiar *Sun* in it, from the strength, influence, and origin of the light of nature—that is, of the *Essential Sun*—and they are to be opened in order, just as their birth and origin occurred.

9. First, *Saturn's Sun* is opened through the *Key* of the outward *Sun*, so that the *separation* <note>original: "Severation"</note> of nature is discerned. Secondly,

# The Twenty-Second Epistle.

Jupiter’s Sun is opened, and then the powers are discerned like a blossoming tree; and you have come this far.

10. Thirdly, *Mars* (namely, the fire-soul) is opened, and then *Virgin Venus* appears in her white robes and plays with the soul to see if she might move it to the desire of love. She goes in and out, up and down with the soul, and lovingly embraces <note>original: "accolates"</note> it to see whether it would introduce the fugitive properties of self-will (where the soul departed out of the balanced state <note>original: "temperature"; referring to the harmonious "temperamentum" of the soul's original nature</note> into the fugitive life of the divided properties of the body) back into her, so that *Virgin Venus* might be again animated <gloss>or soul-ized</gloss> and regain the Fire’s *Tincture* <note>original: "Tincture"; in Boehme’s thought, the spiritual essence or life-force that transforms matter</note>, in which its joy and its life consist.

<margin>☞</margin>
11. For *Virgin Venus* is the splendor of the white in the *Sun*, understood in this place; but the stability of the shining is not its own. The spiritual water is its own property, which water arises out of the fire where the separation begins in the *saltpeter* <note>original: "Salniter"</note> in *Mars’s* Sun. Then *Virgin Venus* separates within itself and covers herself with a copper vesture; for *Mars* would have her for his own property. But he defiles her exceedingly in his malignity and spatters her with earth and rust; for he cannot have her as his spouse unless he gives her his own fire-will as a property—and that he will not do. Therefore, they strive for a long time; they are married, but they are unfaithful to each other.

12. And even then comes the *Sun* and opens the *Sun* of *Mercury*, which is the fourth Key, where you shall see great wonders: how God has created the Heaven and the Earth, and moreover the foundation of the four elements. And if you then observe rightly, you shall see your own proper *genius* <note>the guiding spirit or inner nature</note> unfolded before you and see how the Word has become man—namely, the expressed Word in its re-expressing <gloss>or speaking itself forth</gloss> into the separation <note>original: "severation"</note> of the powers. You will see how *Virgin Venus* is severed and how the forms of nature take her into them and treat her in a pitiful state. They take her into their own domination and change themselves within her into a purple color. They would murder, but she is <margin>☞</margin> their baptism to the new life in this place.

<margin>☞</margin>
13. The fifth Key is *Virgin Venus* herself, with which she opens her <margin>* Otherwise, God.</margin> gold (namely, the *Sun*), so that she gives her will and fair garland to the murderers. Thus, she stands as one pregnant <gloss>or humbled</gloss>. Then the artist supposes that he has the new child, but he is far enough from it until its *birth*.

14. The sixth Key is *Luna* <note>the Moon</note>. When the Sun opens this, then *Mars, Jupiter*, and *Saturn* must all forsake their own will and let their fugitive, aspiring *pomp* fall; for the Sun in *Luna* takes them into the incarnation. Then the artist begins to be sad and thinks...

<summary>This page outlines the alchemical and spiritual progression through several "keys," using the planets as symbols for the soul's journey from the conflict of Mars toward the transformative light of the Sun and Venus.</summary>

he has lost, but his hope shall not be ashamed; for the *Moon* in its opened *Sun* is so hungry after the true Sun, that she attracts it with force and effect into them; whereupon *Mars* quails in his wrath and dies away in his own right, and then Virgin *Venus* receives him and insinuates with her love into him. Whereby *Mars* in *Jupiter* and *Saturn* is quick <note>original: "quick", meaning alive or active</note> in this love of a joyful life, and all the six properties give their will into *Venus*; and she gives her will to the *Sun*, and then the life is born, that stands in the *Temperature* <note>original: "Temperature", referring to the alchemical state of perfect balance or harmony</note>.

<margin>☞</margin> 15. Dear Mr. *Doctor*, the pen is not to be trusted <note>a common phrase meaning the matter is too secret or complex to put fully in writing</note>, yet have a care regarding the work; it will be so, and no otherwise; do not move it <note>disturb the process</note>, lest *Mercury* be enraged before his opening; for outwardly he is evil, but inwardly he is good and the true life, yet *Mars* is the cause of life. Also, they do not proceed so plainly and punctually in order with their opening, although the opening is done in *Order*; but the sensual <note>original: "sensall", likely referring to the wheel of the senses or the essential nature</note> wheel turns itself about and winds inward, until *Saturn* comes with his will into the internal ground, and then he stands in the Temperature and produces no longer inclinations <note>toward conflict</note>. But all that you now see are the revolting, fugitive spirits; and they vaunt with Virgin *Venus*, but they all live in whoredom; and they must be converted and turn into the inner ground, so that they may be fixed. This is done until Virgin *Venus* loses her material, gross <gloss>impure</gloss> water, in which the adulterers wanton with her in false-will, so that she may become holy and spiritual; and then the Sun shines in her, which changes nature into love.

<margin>☞</margin> 16. Loving Mr. *Doctor*, the philosophical body is the spiritual water from the fire and light; *namely*, the power of the fire and of the light. <margin>☞</margin> When it is severed from its grossness through the opening of all the properties of nature, then it is truly spiritual. Then the *solar* spirit receives no other property at all into itself, except only that which is able to reach its sensual Sun in the opened state; for the Sun takes nothing into itself but its own likeness. It takes its heaven out of the earth (if you will understand me aright), for it is its food, from which it generates a young Sun in itself, which is also called *Sol* <note>Latin: "Sun"</note>; but it is a body. Therefore I say to you: keep yourself diligently and precisely to it; you shall truly rejoice if God lets you live so long, if only you have the right *Father* <note>referring to the primary material or guiding principle in the alchemical work</note>, which I have found for you and am greatly in love with.

<margin>☞</margin> 17. This is well known to me, for I have lately seen it, at which I do not only wonder but rejoice; therein much is revealed to me, and although I might write somewhat more extensively, yet it is not necessary in this process; also, the *pen* is not to be trusted; it may be done another time. And I pray you to keep this letter secret and in faithfulness; if I come to you, I may entrust you with something which

...which I have lately seen and received; yet I shall go as far as I dare if opportunity allows and the nearby troubles do not hinder me. Mr. *Doctor*, having gained insight <note>original: "become seeing"</note>, read the treatise on *Election* <note>Refers to the doctrine of Divine Election or Predestination</note> with inward deliberation <gloss>or ponderings</gloss>. It has more within its internal depth <note>original: "ground"</note> than can be expressed outwardly in reference to the sayings of the Scripture. <margin>* Text: In the secrets <note>original: "In Arcanis"</note>.</margin>
19. Be faithful in the *Mysteries* and consider the wicked world, in its greed, unworthy of them. Regarding what you cannot understand parabolically <note>through parables or symbolic language</note>, questions are necessary. Somewhat more shall be revealed to you; yet I am prohibited by the *Prince of the Heavens* from doing *that* <insert>plainly</insert>; however, I am well permitted to do so in the manner of the blossoming earth <note>using natural metaphors or the "Signature of All Things"</note>.

20. Therefore, imitate the *bees* that gather honey from many flowers. Frequent writing might be of service to you, yet do as you please. God receives God, and need receives need <gloss>each thing receives its like</gloss>.

---

<header>The Twenty-Third Epistle.</header>

# THE TWENTY-THIRD EPISTLE.
## TO CHRISTIANUS STEENBERGER
### DOCTOR OF MEDICINE. <note>original: "Physick"</note>

---

> *Our salvation is in the life of Jesus Christ in us.*

---

<margin>1.</margin>
Worthy, learned, dear Christian friend; I offer all hearty wishes of divine love and grace: desiring that the fountain of divine love might be opened unto you through the sun of life, from which the *divine water springs*; as I do not doubt that the Bridegroom has called his Bride—*namely, your soul* <note>original: "Viz. Your soule"</note>—to this well-spring, seeing that I understand God has placed you under the cross and tribulation.

<margin>2.</margin>
This is the first mark and sign of the noble *Sophia* <note>In the author's theology, Sophia represents Divine Wisdom, often depicted as the spiritual bride of the soul.</note>, with which she marks her children; for she is accustomed to manifesting herself through the thorns of God’s anger, like a fair rose on a thorny bush, so long as the soul keeps its vow and fidelity. For there must be a faithful and firm bond and covenant between the soul and this burning fire of God’s love.

<margin>☞</margin>
3. A person must set themselves to such a purpose that they will enter into Christ’s bitter passion and death, and die therein daily to their sins and evil vanities, and pray earnestly to God for the *renewing of their mind* and understanding. He must be anointed and illuminated <meta>The text continues on the next page with the suffix "-nated".</meta>

<summary>In this opening to an epistle, the author comforts Dr. Christianus Steenberger by explaining that his current tribulations are a sign of divine favor and the mark of "Sophia" (Divine Wisdom). He encourages the reader to enter into the death of Christ daily to renew the soul.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Sophia, Divine Wisdom, tribulation, suffering, Christianus Steenberger, spiritual rebirth, Christ's passion, covenant</keywords>

--- Page 87 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "new birth" and the spiritual union between the believer and Christ, following the previous explanation of how a soul is transformed by divine grace.</meta>

<page-num>369</page-num>

->**The Threefold Life**<-

...born of the Holy Spirit and put on Christ, along with his suffering, death, and resurrection, so that he may be a true branch on the vine of Christ, in whom Christ himself works and rules, according to the internal foundation <note>original: "ground"</note> of his Spirit.

4. This *Mystery* is understood <note>original: "comprehended"</note> through faith, where the deity and humanity are then joined together according to that same internal foundation. This happens in the same way that fire thoroughly heats <margin>\* Or, makes the iron red hot.</margin> iron; the iron keeps its own substance, but as long as the fire burns within it, it is changed into pure fire.

5. It is not that the creature grasps it by its own power, but rather that the creature is grasped by it when the will completely resigns itself to God. Then the Spirit of God rules in this resigned <note>In Boehme’s theology, "resignation" (Gelassenheit) refers to the total surrender of the self-will to God's will.</note> will. This will is the true *Temple of the Holy Spirit* in which Christ dwells essentially. This does not happen in an imaginary, thought-like, or creaturely way, but like fire in iron, or like the sun in a plant, where the sun’s influential power forms and makes itself *substantial* <note>original: "essentiall"</note> within the *tincture* <term>In alchemy and Boehme's thought, a "tincture" is the spiritual essence or life-force that gives a thing its specific qualities.</term> of the plant.

6. The same is true regarding the spirit of man: when the holy power of God forms and images itself in man’s spirit and faith, it becomes a spiritual being or essence. Only the soul's mouth of faith can grasp this; the earthly man of flesh and blood, who is mortal, cannot. It is an *immortal being* in which *Christ* dwells within man. It is the Heaven of God stamped upon the "little world" <note>The "little world" refers to the microcosm, or the individual human being as a reflection of the entire universe.</note>, and it is a revelation of the throne or seat of God, where Paradise springs forth again and bears fruit.

7. Therefore, the *Dragon* <note>The "Dragon" often symbolizes the selfish, fallen nature or the devil's influence within the soul.</note> must first be slain. Even though it still clings to the earthly flesh like the rind or bark on a tree, the spirit lives in God, as Saint Paul says: "*our citizenship <note>original: "conversation," meaning one's way of life or place of belonging</note> is in Heaven*." As *Christ* also said: "*He who eats my flesh and drinks my blood abides in me, and I in him*"; and also, "*without me, you can do nothing*."

8. <margin>☞</margin> Therefore I say, if anyone is a true Christian, he is so in *Christ*. He is born and innate <note>original: "innate"; meaning born into or naturally part of.</note> in the life and spirit of Christ and puts on the resurrection of Christ. In this way, the satisfaction of Christ is shared with him, and Christ overcomes sin, death, the Devil, and Hell within him. Thus, he is reconciled and united with God in Christ.

9. For the *new birth* is not a grace assigned <note>original: "imputed"</note> from the outside, such that we only need to comfort ourselves with Christ’s merits while continuing in the hypocrisy of sin. No, it is a childlike, innate grace. God has placed Christ, along with justification, upon the convert so that Christ also redeems him within himself by the power of his resurrection from God’s anger. Otherwise, he is no Christian, regardless of how much he may flatter himself or make devout, hypocritical displays.

Z
<meta>catchword: 10. Concerning</meta>

<summary>Boehme explains that true Christianity is an internal, spiritual transformation where Christ dwells within the believer like fire in iron, rather than a mere external or intellectual belief. He emphasizes that this "new birth" requires the total surrender of the human will to God's power.</summary>

### The Twenty-Third Epistle

10. Concerning the interpretation of certain words (and also what you requested of me) which are specified in my book called *Aurora* (which contain very hidden meanings, the knowledge of which was given to me by the Most High); I want you to understand that at present it is not advisable to write at length and *expressly* about them in *letters*, seeing that the times are dangerous and the enemy of Christ <note>original: "Enemy of Christ"; likely referring to the religious authorities or spiritual forces opposing Boehme's mystical teachings</note> is horribly raging and raving. Until a little more time has passed, I will give you only a brief hint to consider further.

11. First, there is a twofold meaning of the <term>Northern Crown</term> <note>original: "Northern Crowne"; the constellation Corona Borealis, which Boehme uses to symbolize a spiritual awakening or a "reformation" beginning in the North</note>. The first points to the *Crown of Life*, *namely*, the Spirit of Christ, which shall be manifested in the midst of the great darkness—that is, in the remorse and distress of the conscious nature of the conscience. Where a specific movement of the soul is present, there the Bridegroom comes—that is, the power of Christ in the midst of such a movement.

<margin>☞</margin>
12. The other significance is a *symbol of the outward kingdom*, where great confusions, entanglements, and contentions shall occur when the nations stand in controversy. There also the symbol of *victory* is suggested, as it exists in the spiritual figure: how things shall go, which people shall at last *conquer*, and how, during such a lamentable time of tribulation, Christ shall be made manifest and known. After and during that miserable time, the great mysteries shall be revealed, so that men shall be able to know—even within *Nature*—the hidden God in Trinity. Through this knowledge, the foreign nations shall be converted and become Christians. This also signifies how sectarian contentions in religion shall be destroyed in such a manifestation, for all gates will be set open. Then all unprofitable <term>praters</term> <note>original: "Praters"; those who talk idly or deceptively about divine things without true spiritual understanding</note>, who currently lie like bars before the *Truth*, will be done away with. All shall acknowledge and know Christ, and this manifestation shall be the last. Then the Sun of Life shall shine upon all nations; and even then the Beast of Iniquity with the *Whore* <note>Symbols from the Book of Revelation representing corrupt political and religious systems</note> will end their days, which is symbolized by the characters *Ra. Ra. Ra. P. in R.P.* <note>Mystical symbolic characters used by Boehme to represent spiritual and historical cycles</note> as is seen in the *Revelation*.

13. We do not dare at present to make this extensive interpretation any clearer; everything will reveal itself. Then men shall see what it was, for there is yet *a completely different time* to come.

14. Concerning the <term>language of nature</term> <note>the idea that the essence of things is expressed in their names or sounds, a key concept in Boehme's mysticism</note>, I assure you that it is a reality; but what I understand of it, I cannot teach or give to another. Indeed, I can provide a sense of how it is to be understood, but it requires much space, and there must be a personal conference and conversation about it. It is *not* to be set down in *writing*.

15. Also, concerning the <term>Philosophical work of the Tincture</term> <note>the "Tincture" refers to the alchemical or spiritual power that transforms base nature into divine light</note>, its progress is not to be described so crudely and plainly. Although I do not possess it in <term>praxis</term> <note>original: "Praxis"; practical application or physical experimentation</note>, the seal of God lies before it to conceal...

<summary>Boehme discusses the symbolic meanings of the "Northern Crown" and the "Language of Nature," predicting a future time of spiritual revelation and the end of religious conflict. He cautiously avoids being too explicit due to the dangerous political and religious climate of his time.</summary>
<keywords>Northern Crown, Aurora, Bridegroom, Trinity in Nature, sectarianism, Beast and Whore, Language of Nature, Tincture, Jakob Boehme, prophecy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion from the previous page regarding the spiritual requirements for alchemical and mystical understanding, emphasizing that the "new birth" is a prerequisite for achieving the "Tincture."</meta>

## The Twenty-Third Epistle.

<margin>* Enter into the new birth</margin>
the true ground of it, upon pain of eternal punishment, unless a man knew for certain that it might not be misused; there is also no power to attain it, unless a man first becomes *that* himself which he seeks in it; no skill or art is of use, unless one gives the <term>Tincture</term><note>In alchemy, the Tincture is a substance capable of transforming or purifying other substances; in Boehme's theology, it represents the divine power that transforms the soul.</note> into the hands of another. He cannot prepare it unless he is certainly in the new birth.

16. Two <term>*central fires*</term><note>original: "Centrall fires"; likely referring to the internal spiritual forces or the alchemical principles of "sulfur" and "mercury" within the soul.</note> belong to it, in which the power of all things consists; these may easily be attained if a person is rightly prepared. Therefore, Sir, do not trouble and toil yourself in the manner and way you mentioned, with any gold or minerals; it is all false. The best in heaven and in the world, from above and below, must be an ingredient to it, which is far off and near at hand. The place is *everywhere* where it may be had; but everyone is not fit and prepared for it, nor does it cost any money, except what is spent on time and bodily maintenance; otherwise, it might be prepared with two <margin>† Four shillings.</margin> florins or less.

17. The world must be made heaven, and heaven the world; it is not of earth, stones, or metals, and yet it is of the *ground* of all metals; but a *spiritual being*, which is surrounded by the four elements, which also changes the four elements into one; a <term>*double mercury*</term><note>original: "doubled Mercury"; an alchemical term for a purified or "philosophical" mercury, representing the union of opposites.</note>, yet not quicksilver or any other mineral or metal.

<margin>* A book entitled, *Wasserstein der Weisen*.</margin>
18. Read the <term>*Water-Stone of the Wise Men*</term><note>original: "Water-Stone of the Wise men"; a reference to the Philosopher's Stone (Lapis Philosophorum) and specifically to the 1619 book *Wasserstein der Weisen*.</note>, which is in print; there is much truth in it. Moreover, it is clear: the work is easy, and the art is simple; a boy of ten years might make it; but the wisdom in it is great, and the greatest *mystery*. Everyone must *seek it himself*; it is not for us to break the *seal of God*, for a fiery *mountain* lies before it, at which I myself am amazed and must wait to see whether it is God’s will. How should I teach others expressly about it? I cannot yet make it myself, although I know something of it; and let no man seek more from me than I have already clearly enough indicated. I commend you, together with all the children of God, into the love of *Jesus Christ*.

---

->**JACOB BOEHME.**<-

<page-num>173</page-num>
<header>The twenty-fourth Epistle.</header>

# THE TWENTY-FOURTH EPISTLE.
## In Answer to a QUESTION,
### WHY,
### The Statue of a Deceased Person Wept, or Shed Tears?

---

1. Regarding the question, it is difficult to understand, and there is a need for a Joseph <note>A reference to the biblical Joseph, who was famous for interpreting mysterious dreams and omens.</note> to explain <margin>* Or, resolve it.</margin> it. For it is a magical thing and very wonderful <note>In this context, "wonderful" means full of wonder or supernatural.</note>; therefore, it is hard to answer, for it proceeds from the <term>Magia</term> <note>original: *Magia*; in the author's philosophy, this refers to the spiritual power or "will" that acts upon matter.</note>.

2. Yet I will briefly explain and show you what my opinion is on the matter; not that I wish to conclude or pass an absolute, final judgment concerning it, but rather leave you and other enlightened men of God to your own opinions and understanding of it. Whether God has enabled me to prove <note>original: "prove"; here meaning to test, verify, or demonstrate the truth of the matter.</note> it, I leave to your judgment, as you know the condition of the mentioned person better than I; for all things proceed according to the time, measure, and limit of that specific thing.

3. A hard, rough stone wall <margin>† Or, a statue of stone: fashioned by a stone-cutter, or an image set up as a monument.</margin> has no life that is mobile or active, for the elemental, growing <note>original: "vegetable"; referring to the basic life force or "soul" shared by plants and minerals.</note> life stands mute and still within it. It is enclosed within the first "Impression" <note>A term in Behmenist philosophy referring to the process where spiritual energy "compresses" or "hardens" into physical matter.</note>; yet, it is not as if it were nothing. There is nothing in this world where the elemental as well as the sidereal <note>original: "Sydereall"; relating to the stars or celestial influences.</note> dominion does not reside. However, it is more mobile, active, and working in one thing than in another; and we cannot say that the four elements, together with the stars, do not have their daily influence on all things.

<summary>The author begins to address a report of a weeping statue, framing it as a "magical" event that requires spiritual insight to interpret. He explains that even inanimate stone contains elemental and celestial influences, though they are usually dormant.</summary>
<keywords>Magia, weeping statue, omen, elemental life, sidereal dominion, Joseph, stone monument, spiritual philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 91 ---
<meta>This page continues the twenty-fourth epistle, specifically discussing the spiritual composition of matter and the state of the soul after death.</meta>

### The Twenty-Fourth Epistle.

4. But since this is a *hard stone*, the *miracle* is above the usual and ordinary course of *Nature*. Therefore, we can in no way say that it has a natural cause in the *stone*, as if the operation of the *stone* should move and put this forth; rather, it is a *magical* motion from the *spirit*, whose *image* is hewn out and portrayed in the *stone*.

5. For a *stone* consists of three things, and so all *beings* consist of these three things, but enclosed in a twofold property: namely, in a spiritual and in a corporal property. And those three things, in which everything in this *world* consists, are *Sulphur*, *Mercury*, and *Salt* <note>Boehme uses these Paracelsian terms as spiritual principles representing structure (Salt), motion/life (Mercury), and substance/fire (Sulphur).</note> in two properties: the one *heavenly*, the other *earthly*. Just as *God* dwells in *time*, and *time* in *God*, yet *time* is not *God* but is from *God*, like a portrayed *image* of *eternity*.

6. So *man* likewise is from *time*, and also from *eternity*, and consists of three things: namely, of *Sulphur*, *Mercury*, and *Salt*, in two parts. The one is from time—namely, the outward body—and the other stands in *eternity*—namely, the *soul*. Seeing then that *man*, *time*, and *eternity* stand in one *dominion* within *man*, we are to consider the *question* from that perspective.

<margin>* The Microcosm from the Macrocosm <note>original: "Microcosmus ex Macrocosmo"</note></margin>
7. For man is a *little world* from the *great world*, and has the property of the whole *great world* within him. For *God* said to him after the fall: *you are earth, and to earth you shall return*. That is, *Sulphur*, *Mercury*, and *Salt*; in these three stand all things in this *world*, whether *spiritual* or *corporal*, except for the *soul*. The soul stands in such a property according to the rights of the *eternal nature*, as I have sufficiently demonstrated in my *writings*.

<margin>☞</margin>
8. Now when man dies, the outward light in the outward *Sulphur* is extinguished and goes out with its outward fire in which the life has burned. Then the body falls to dust and enters again into that from which it came. But the *soul*, which is brought forth out of the *eternal nature* and infused into *Adam* by the spirit of *God*, cannot die; for it is not from time, but from the *Eternal Generation*.

<margin>† Or, set its mind upon.</margin>
9. Now if the *soul* has † put its desire into any temporal thing and has imprinted itself with it <insert>[ or strongly set its *imagination* upon it ]</insert>, then it has impressed the property of that thing into its desire and holds it magically, as if it held it bodily. Indeed, it cannot hold the body—understand this as the elemental body—but it holds the *sidereal body* <note>sidereal body: the "starry" or astral body, the vehicle of the soul between the physical and the purely spiritual.</note> until the *stars* also consume it. It often happens that *people* appear after their death in *houses* in their own bodies, but the body is cold, dead, and numb; the spirit of the *soul* only puts it on by using the *astral spirit* for as long as it takes for the physical body to putrefy.

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that all matter and humanity are composed of the triad of Sulphur, Mercury, and Salt, representing both earthly and heavenly properties. He further describes how a soul obsessed with temporal things can "magically" inhabit its sidereal or physical body even after death until the body decays.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Sulphur, Mercury, Salt, Microcosm, Macrocosm, sidereal body, astral spirit, eternity, afterlife, alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>This page continues the Twenty-fourth Epistle, likely addressed to a nobleman or intellectual peer, discussing the spiritual and "astral" causes of supernatural phenomena involving the deceased.</meta>

## The twenty-fourth Epistle.

10. Also, many a body is so strongly possessed by the astral (or starry) spirit through the *desire* of the soul that it takes a long time to decay. For the soul’s desire brings the <term>sidereal spirit</term><note>original: "sydereall spirit." This refers to the "starry" or astral influence that Böhme believed governed the physical body and its connection to the material world.</note> into the body, so that the elements are, as it were, impressed with an astral life. This is especially true if the soul has not yet attained rest, and if during the life of the body it had focused its imagination strongly on something (and taken it to heart) and the body died before it had released that desire and removed it from that thing. Therefore, the will still continually runs in that same impression; it would gladly resolve its cause but cannot. Thereupon, it seeks the cause or reason for its detention and would gladly rest in *eternity*, but the impressed thing continues its active work and drive until the stars consume it. Formerly, in the *Catholic religion* <note>original: "Popish Religion"</note> there was some treatment of this matter, but without *sufficient* understanding.

11. <margin>*Or, Shed tears.*</margin> Now you may easily consider how it happened that the *engraved gravestone shed water, or wept*. It did not happen from the power or virtue of the stone itself, but from the strength and might of the *spirit* to whom the stone belongs and whose *image* it bears. Also, it is not done from the soul’s own essence, but *magically* through the astral *spirit*. The constellations in the *spirit* of the soul have impressed themselves into the sidereal spirit in the stone, all according to the soul’s desire. It has thereby signified that there was something weighing heavily on its mind when it lived; and this sadness (or anxiety of spirit) was still in the sidereal spirit. For *Christ* said, "*Where your heart is, there is also your treasure*"; also, in the *Revelation of Jesus Christ*, it is written, "*Our works shall follow us*."

12. Dear Sir, it is not appropriate for me to judge further in this; consider whether the person mentioned did not have something weighing heavily upon her before her end—whether anyone had done her wrong, or she had done wrong to anyone—or whether the care for her husband and children (insofar as she was a holy person) troubled her. Seeing any of them going on in an evil course, she might, through the power of the sidereal spirit acting through the stone, give such an admonition for improvement. Noble Sir, consider this yourself; I may likely hit upon the truth among all these mentioned things. But since I never knew the person and know nothing of her, I leave the judgment to your *favor*. You know better than I what her condition was in this matter. I write only of the *possibility* of how it may be and pass no further judgment or determination.

13. But as for the fact that this might be mocked and accounted ridiculous, I do not care about that. I am not deceived; I understand (I bless God) this foundation <note>original: "ground"</note> very well; for I have not learned such knowledge...

<summary>Böhme explains the phenomenon of a "weeping gravestone" as a magical interaction between a soul's lingering earthly desires and the "sidereal spirit" imprinted on the stone. He suggests the phenomenon serves as a spiritual warning or a sign of an unresolved burden the deceased carried in life.</summary>
<keywords>astral spirit, sidereal spirit, weeping gravestone, afterlife, Jacob Böhme, soul's desire, spiritual impressions, Catholic Church, morality</keywords>

<page-num>175</page-num>

# The twenty-fourth Epistle.

...learned from, or by, man, but it has been given to me; and I would sufficiently support <note>original: "ground"</note> it with further and more extensive explanation if I were to write about the *Human Property* <note>original: "Humane Property"; referring to the essential nature or characteristics of humanity</note>—how man exists in *Life*, and how in *Death*.

<margin>* On the Signature of Things <note>Latin: *De Signatura Rerum*</note></margin>

14. I am sending you the book *Forty Questions* <note>Referring to Boehme’s *Forty Questions on the Soul* (1620), which addresses the nature of the soul and the afterlife</note>. There you may see a deeper *foundation*, which is nevertheless even more firmly established in the center of all beings within the book *The Threefold Life* <note>Referring to *The Threefold Life of Man* (1620)</note>, and yet much more so in the book *The Signature of All Things*. Furthermore, I urge you not to *mention* my judgment and explanation of this question *very much among frivolous people* <note>original: "light people"</note>. For fodder belongs to a cow, and understanding belongs to the intelligent. The wicked man judges wickedly; the man of understanding tests <note>original: "proveth"</note> all things. I speak from good intentions and affection.

->January 1, 1622.<-

->J. B. <note>Jacob Boehme</note><-

<summary>Jacob Boehme concludes his letter by directing the recipient to his other major works for deeper theological grounding and cautions against sharing these profound truths with frivolous people who lack discernment.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Forty Questions, Threefold Life, Signature of All Things, De Signatura Rerum, spiritual discernment, mysticism, human nature</keywords>

<page-num>176</page-num>
# THE 
# TWENTY-FIFTH 
# EPISTLE.
---
-> TO <-

<margin>* Carel von Endern.</margin>
# * CHARLES of ENDERN.
---

1. **N**oble, Right Honorable Sir, I wish and desire for you (from the most holy, omnipresent God, who is the fullness of all things and the power of all beings) a happy, joyful New Year and all prosperous *welfare*.

2. Although I am a simple man, I never intended in all my life to have communication and conversation with such *high-ranking persons* by means of the gift which God has bestowed upon me out of His mercy and love, or thereby to become known and acquainted with them. But when the *high light* was kindled in me and the *fiery impulse* <note>original: "instigation"</note> fell upon me, then my will was only to write what I saw in an effective, unique manner and knew in the Spirit; and I intended to keep my writings to myself.

3. I saw well enough what would come to pass, but it never yet entered my mind that I should esteem myself so much as to have my writings known; for I considered myself too simple. I intended to write down the *pearl-like garland* <note>original: "Pearle-like Garland"; likely a reference to his spiritual insights or a specific manuscript like "The Aurora"</note> only for myself and to imprint it on my heart.

<margin>☞</margin>
4. But seeing that I (as a very simple man) did not understand or take notice of this, and yet I now see plainly that God’s intention was far otherwise than what ever entered my mind, I have learned first this consideration: *that there is no respect of persons with God* <note>i.e., God does not show favoritism based on social rank or status</note>; *but he who depends on Him is accepted and beloved of Him, and He carries out His work in him; for He alone is high, and is pleased*—

<summary>In this opening of his 25th epistle, Jacob Boehme humbly addresses the nobleman Charles of Endern, explaining that his spiritual writings were originally intended for personal reflection rather than public or noble attention.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Charles of Endern, divine inspiration, humility, spiritual light, Pearle-like Garland, seventeenth-century mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 95 ---
<meta>This page continues a discussion on how God reveals Himself through the humble and the structure of human nature in relation to the Three Principles of divine existence.</meta>

<lang>English</lang>
<page-num>177</page-num>
<header>The twenty-fifth Epistle.</header>

...pleased <note>OCR reads "fed," likely a truncation of "pleased" or "satisfied" from the previous page.</note> to manifest himself in the weak, so that it may be known *that the kingdom and the power are his alone*.

5. And also that it does not lie in human searching and reason, or depend on the heavens and their powers; for they do not comprehend him. Instead, he is well pleased to reveal himself in the low and humble so that he may be known in all things. For the powers of the heavens also work themselves forth continually in shapes, forms, plants, and colors to manifest and reveal the holy God, so that he may be known in all things.

6. The manifestation of God can be made much more highly and clearly in man, since he is not only an essence <gloss>[ or being ]</gloss> out of the created world, but his power, matter, and peculiar being—which he himself is—stand and harmonize <note>original: "assimilateth"</note> with all three principles of the <term>Divine Being</term> <note>In the author's theology, the Three Principles represent the dark fire-world (the Father), the light world of love (the Son), and the outer produced world (the Holy Spirit/Nature).</note>.

7. And nothing has been diminished from the being of the divine creature in its fall except the divine light, in which it should live, walk, and be in God in perfect love, humility, meekness, and holiness. Thus, it should eat the <gloss>[ *Manna* ]</gloss>—or heavenly bread of the Word and divine power—and live in perfection like the angels.

<margin>☞</margin>
8. This light, which in the second principle shines eternally in God (which is the only cause of joy, love, humility, meekness, and mercy), is withdrawn and hidden from man in his fall. This happened because the first man (when he was formed in his "Mother of the great world") <note>The "Mother of the great world" refers to the external, physical universe or macrocosm.</note> set his *imagination*, *lust*, and *longing* upon the "Mother of nature" and desired the food of the first principle. In this first principle consist the origin and birth of nature, the source of anger, and the most *anxious* <note>original: "Anxious birth"; in this context, "anxious" refers to the harsh, conflicting qualities of the first stages of nature's development.</note> birth, from which all the tangible things of this world are made. Therefore, he became capable of that same nature, since he stood upon the *same root*.

9. Thus, according to the body and also according to the spirit, he has become a child of this created world, which rules, acts, and leads him. It also gives him his food <note>original: "meat"</note> and drink, and has conceived in him corruptibility and pain. He has acquired a *beastly* <note>original: "Beastiall"</note> body, which must again *decay* <note>original: "Corrupt"</note> into its mother.

10. For he should not have had this monstrous form; the constellation <note>The "constellation" refers to the astral or planetary influences that govern the physical and emotional life of fallen man.</note> of the great world should not dominate him. Instead, he had his own constellation within himself, which harmonized with the holy heaven of the *second principle* of the divine being; that is, with the *rising* and birth of the <term>Divine Nature</term>.

11. Now man is not so far broken and decayed as if he were no longer the first man whom God created; only he has acquired the monstrous form, which is corruptible and has its beginning only and merely from the most outward and third principle,

<sig>A a</sig>
<meta>catchword: and</meta>

<summary>The author explains that man was originally created to live in the divine light of the second principle but fell by desiring the lower, "anxious" nature of the physical world. This fall resulted in a "beastly" and corruptible body governed by the stars rather than by God's internal light.</summary>

<meta>Continuing from the previous page, the author describes how the soul interacts with the different "principles" or spiritual dimensions of reality, specifically the transition from a state of inner conflict to one of divine light.</meta>

<page-num>178</page-num>

### The Twenty-Fifth Epistle

...and has awakened and opened in him the gate of the first principle (which is the severe, earnest source <note>original: "Source"; here referring to the raw, foundational energy of existence</note>). This source burns in the great created world and is *wholly* ignited in the *damned*.

12. But the true man whom God created—who alone is the truly righteous man—is still hidden within this *corrupt* man. If he *denies himself in his animal <note>original: "beastiall"</note> form and does not live according to its actions, drives, and will, but instead surrenders himself to God with his whole mind, thoughts, and senses, then this man lives in God. God then works within him both the will and the deed, for everything exists in God*.

<margin>☞</margin>
13. The truly holy and heavenly man, who is hidden within the monstrous <note>In this context, "monstrous" refers to the distorted, fallen nature of humanity after the loss of Paradise.</note> body, is in heaven just as God is. Heaven is in him, and the *heart* or light of God is begotten and born in him. That is, God is in him and he is in God. God is *nearer* to him than his own *animal* body.

14. The animal body is not his own native country where he is at home; rather, with that body, he is *outside* of Paradise. But the true man, regenerated and born anew in Christ, is *not* in this world but in the *Paradise of God*. Although he remains in the body, he is nevertheless in God.

15. And though the animal body dies, nothing happens to the new man. Instead, he then comes forth directly out of the house of torment and conflicting wills into his native country. There is no need for traveling to a far distance where he might imagine he needs to go for things to be better; rather, God is manifested within him. <margin>☞</margin>

16. The soul of man originates from the first principle of God, but in that state, it is *not a holy being*. However, in the second principle, it is manifested in God and is a *divine creature*, for it is there that the divine light is born. Therefore, if the divine light is not born <gloss>begotten or brought forth</gloss> within it <gloss>namely, the soul</gloss>, then God is not in it. Instead, it lives in the most primal, earnest source <note>original: "Source"</note>, where there is an *eternal conflict* <gloss>enmity or contrary will</gloss> within itself.

17. But if the light is born, then there is joy, love, and pleasant delight in the creature, and the new man—which is the soul—is in God. How could there not be *knowledge* where God is present in the creature?

<margin>☞</margin>
18. Now, it does not depend on the willing, running, or toiling of the creature to know the depths of the Deity. For the soul does not know the *divine center* or how the *divine essence* is generated; rather, it depends on God’s will as to how He chooses to manifest it.

19. Now then, if God manifests Himself in the soul, what

<summary>This page explores the duality of the human condition, contrasting the "animal" body with the "heavenly man" who is reborn in Christ and exists in Paradise even while on Earth. It argues that spiritual peace and divine knowledge are gifts of God's grace manifested within the soul rather than the result of human effort.</summary>

<keywords>regeneration, divine light, first principle, second principle, animal body, heavenly man, soul, divine essence, Paradise, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

--- Page 97 ---
<meta>This page continues the twenty-fifth epistle, focusing on the soul's labor toward divine light and the internal manifestation of God. It follows a discussion of the soul's relationship with the divine presence.</meta>

<page-num>179</page-num>

### The Twenty-Fifth Epistle

19. What has the soul done to deserve this? Nothing; it has only the *longing* or laboring <note>original: "travelling," used here in the sense of "travailing," or the pains of childbirth</note> toward birth. It looks to God, in whom it lives, from whom the divine light becomes apparent and shines within it. The first fierce [earnest] Principle, from which movement originally arises, is changed into triumphant joy.

20. Therefore, it is very unjust that the world rages and raves so much—tyrannizing, reproaching, condemning, and reviling—when the gifts of God show themselves differently in each person, and not everyone has the same knowledge.

21. What can a man claim for himself if it is not born within him? This does not depend on a man’s choice, as if he could simply desire it or like it; rather, as his internal "Heaven" <note>Boehme often uses "Heaven" to describe a person's inner spiritual state or orientation toward the divine</note> is within him, so God is manifested in him.

<margin>[Reader's mark]</margin>
22. For God is not a God of destruction in the birth <gloss>or universal generation</gloss> but an illuminator, enkindler, and nourisher. Each creature has its own center within itself, whether it lives in the holiness of God or in the anger of God; *God will, however, be manifested in all creatures.*

23. If the world were not so blind, it might recognize the wonderful Being of God in all creatures. But now that it rages and raves, it does so entirely against itself and against the Holy Spirit of God. They shall one day be *astonished* by His light. They shall not hinder the Son whom the Laboring Mother <note>Likely a reference to the soul or the Church in the process of spiritual rebirth, often associated with the "Woman Clothed with the Sun" from Revelation</note> brings forth in her old age, for the heavens declare this.

24. God shall enlighten him against all the raging and raving of the Devil, and his light [splendor or glory] shall reach from the East to the West. I do not write of myself, but I only foresee that this is at hand and shall come to pass.

<margin>* Doctor Balthasar Walter, Forty Questions.</margin>
25. I would have gladly pleased you at this time with what I promised, wherein everything touched upon and hinted at here might be clearly explained. This includes what that good and well-known *Doctor <note>Balthasar Walter (1558–1631) was a physician and student of Boehme who famously posed forty questions about the nature of the soul</note> has desired regarding the origin, being, life, and purpose of man and his soul, and also his final end; however, it is not yet finished.

26. For the gifts that were once given to me by God are not completely dead and gone. Although they were hidden by the Devil and the world, they now often appear and show themselves more deeply and more wonderfully.

27. And very shortly (God willing) you shall receive something of this, for a higher beginning has been made toward it—especially regarding the ***THREE PRINCIPLES*** <term>The Three Principles: Boehme’s theological system describing the Dark World (divine wrath/fire), the Light World (divine love), and the Outward World (the visible universe)</term> of the...

<summary>In this letter, the author argues that spiritual knowledge is a gift from God rather than a human choice and rebukes the world for persecuting those with different spiritual insights. He also mentions his ongoing work answering Balthasar Walter's questions regarding the soul and his "Three Principles."</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Balthasar Walter, soul, spiritual rebirth, Three Principles, Holy Spirit, divine light, religious tolerance</keywords>

...the Divine Being, and so forth regarding *all the things* which are promised in my * book.

However, I will be † diligent through divine and earnest effort <note>original: "Exercise"; in this context, it refers to a disciplined spiritual or intellectual practice.</note>; whatever God wills shall be done. And with this, I commend you to the protection of the Almighty.
