<header>The twenty-sixth Epistle.</header>

# THE TWENTY-SIXTH EPISTLE.
### TO
<margin>* Charles von Endern.</margin>
## *CHARLES OF ENDERN.

---

<margin>1.</margin>
The love in the heart of God the Father, and the light of his power in the life of Jesus Christ, be our refreshment and help us toward the new birth, so that the true image may appear to God's honor and deeds of wonder, and cause the fair sprout of his lily <note>The "lily" is a recurring symbol in Böhme's work, representing the blossoming of the divine life or the "age of the lily" marked by spiritual purity.</note> to grow in us in the paradisiacal garden of Jesus Christ.

2. Noble and right honorable sir, I offer my humble and most willing service, along with my hearty wishes for all your temporal and eternal prosperity. I have received from the bearer, your servant, the measure of grain you sent to me, and I thank you most kindly. I pray that Almighty God blesses you in abundance for it.

<margin>☛</margin>
3. Noble sir, because you are so *humble* and do not look upon or care for what the world does—and because you do not value the subtlety <term>intellectual cleverness or cunning</term> [or pert <note>original: "pert prudency," meaning overconfident or saucy wisdom</note> prudence] of the high-ranking—but instead seek and labor for that which the Lord of Heaven builds, even though it appears as foolishness to this world. But it seems good in his sight to carry out his work through mean <term>lowly or common</term>, low, plain, and *childlike* people, so that he alone might be exalted and no man might have cause to boast. You likewise may surely hope for the same knowledge, which is more beautiful and excellent than all the pomp and riches of the world. For all that is temporal leaves and forsakes a person, but the honorable garland [or trophy] of Christ does not forsake a person in death; instead, it brings him to the heavenly, triumphant *host of angels* and into his true, eternal, native country [or home].

4. Now, seeing that we know and understand exceedingly well that we are only pilgrims in this world, staying in a strange lodging and in very great

<meta>The text ends mid-sentence with the catchword "danger," continuing onto the next page.</meta>

<summary>In this opening to a letter, Jacob Böhme thanks Charles of Endern for a gift of grain and commends his humility. Böhme emphasizes that God works through the lowly and childlike rather than the intellectually proud, promising an eternal reward that far outshines worldly wealth.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Charles von Endern, humility, new birth, lily, spiritual knowledge, pilgrims, divine grace</keywords>

--- Page 100 ---
<meta>This page continues the twenty-sixth epistle, where the author discusses the dangers of worldly life and the pursuit of spiritual wisdom over material wealth.</meta>

# The Twenty-Sixth Epistle

...danger, lying captive in a painful and harsh prison, and must continually fear and expect death. Therefore, Noble *Sir*, you do very well and act more wisely than the prudent of this world, in that you look around you and strive for your *Eternal native Country*, and not for the pomp, power, and riches of this world, as the high and great usually do.

5. I have no doubt that you shall obtain a very beautiful garland from the *Virgin* of the eternal wisdom of God. If this comes to pass, it will be of more value and acceptance to you than all temporary riches and this whole world with all its existence and splendor. If I had no effective knowledge of this <gloss>Crown or Trophy of Sophia <note>original: "Sophia" is the Greek word for Wisdom, often personified in mystical literature as a divine female figure.</note></gloss>, I would not write. For from the "wise" of this world (who only out of self-pride and without God's spirit claim and attribute all knowledge and understanding to themselves), I have received not only poor thanks, but also nothing else but derision and scorn. At this I rejoice, the more because I bear reproach for the sake of the *Name* and knowledge of *God*.

<margin>☛</margin>
6. For if my knowledge had been obtained in *their school*, then they would embrace and love their own; but because it is from another school, they do not know it. Moreover, they despise it, as they have done to all the prophets, *Christ*, and his *apostles*. This shall not trouble me or turn me aside; but as I have begun, I will depend on my God and Creator with greater earnestness and fixed resolution. I will commit myself to him; let him do with me what he pleases.

7. I attribute no wisdom to myself; moreover, I do not rely upon any purpose or intention of reason. For I see most plainly, and find it as clear as the sun, that God works in an entirely different way.

8. Therefore, if we act in a childlike manner and not through our own reason (but only depend on him with true desire and right earnestness, and put all our confidence in him), then we obtain the noble <term>Virgin of his wisdom</term> <note>This refers to the mystical concept of Divine Wisdom (Sophia), viewed as a spiritual guide or partner for the soul.</note> sooner than through our own clever inventions and sharp reasonings. For when she comes, she brings true wisdom and heavenly understanding with her, and without these, I know nothing.

9. But seeing that you have a great delight in reading such writings concerning the highest being (and as I hope it is God's providence), I will not withhold from you what the chief good has entrusted to me. Soon I will send you something more complete; for a very wonderful and excellent book has been started concerning *the life of man*. If the Lord is pleased to continue his hand over me, it shall clearly reveal what man is and what he must do to obtain the chief good.

<meta>10. For</meta>

<summary>In this letter, the author encourages his correspondent to seek eternal spiritual wisdom rather than worldly power, asserting that true divine knowledge comes through childlike faith rather than academic or intellectual pride.</summary>
<keywords>Sophia, Virgin of Wisdom, spiritual vs. worldly knowledge, divine providence, Jacob Boehme, mystical theology, The Life of Man</keywords>

# THE PREFACE
## TO
# THE READER.

Just as there is no book or treatise which this author has written that does not bear the imprints and signs of *Divine Light* and *knowledge*—qualities that can be clearly seen and are of special use and improvement to the impartial Christian reader—so also these letters of his <note>original: "Epistles"</note>, written at various times and on different occasions to several friends, and now gathered and bound together, may rightly be considered as a single book. This collection offers no small benefit and guidance to the reader, lover, and practitioner of that Divine Light and knowledge which his writings contain and present. 

<margin>☛</margin> Indeed, the mere *letter* <note>The "letter" refers to the literal, written words as opposed to the living spirit behind them.</note> of any man’s writings cannot *breathe* the *spirit* of *wisdom* into us, even if those works were written from a divine gift and a true manifestation of light within the authors themselves. This is because the sound of understanding cannot be captured or "painted" on paper, any more than the notes and symbols of music can provide the actual sound and melody to the ear. Instead, they serve as a direction for how the skillful *musician* shall play on the instru-

<summary>The preface introduces a collection of the author's letters, arguing that while written words cannot transmit spiritual wisdom directly, they serve as essential guides for the "practitioner" of divine knowledge, much like musical notation guides a musician.</summary>
<keywords>Divine Light, Epistles, spiritual wisdom, musical metaphor, religious practice, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

--- Page 106 ---
<meta>The top of the page is faded and partially illegible; there is significant darkening and possible water damage on the left side of the folio.</meta>
<meta>catchword: if</meta>

# The Preface to the Reader.

...ment; <note>The word fragment "-ment" likely completes a word like "judgment" or "instrument" from the preceding page.</note> so also our mind is like an organ or instrument. However, it sounds only according to the tune and note of that spirit which possesses and moves it. We convert and assimilate <note>original: "assimulate"</note> all things according to the spirit and will that is ruling and predominant in us. In that spirit, our mind, thoughts, and senses are ignited and inflamed. 

For if we have a mere astral <term>astral: in this context, referring to worldly or "star-governed" impulses rather than divine ones</term>, worldly, carnal, self-conceited, scorning, cavilling <note>original: "cavilling" – making petty or unnecessary objections</note>, Pharisaical <term>Pharisaical: hypocritically self-righteous, emphasizing the letter of the law over its spirit</term>, and hypocritical spirit, we will form and frame our notions, judgments, and criticisms accordingly. We will pervert all things to a wrong sense and *use*. 

But if the Divine Spirit of love, light, meekness, humility, self-denial, sincerity, and holy desires dwells, rules, and acts within us, then our understanding is accordingly holy, *divine, and real*. For *to the pure, all things are pure; but to the impure, all things are impure, for their very minds and consciences are defiled*. <note>A reference to the New Testament verse Titus 1:15.</note>

<margin>☞</margin> Just as this author did not receive his learning, gifts, and high endowments from the schools of this world, but from the school of divine wisdom and the illumination of the Holy Spirit, so too will the meaning and intent of his writings remain misunderstood by those who are merely trained and skilled in the literal and historical schools of this world. These people operate according to the canons, rules, and axioms of their own "wise" Pharisaical reason; they condemn, judge, and rashly criticize everyone who does not speak their particular phrase, style, and dialect. For

<margin>☞</margin> Even if we had everything that the prophets, Christ, and his apostles ever spoke and taught, and possessed the knowledge of all divine, enlightened men in their various gifts recorded in writings, and were to read them for many years; yet if we did not desire to become one with that same spirit of divine knowledge, and strive to have it innate and essential *within us* as a living expression of divine love and righteousness, those writings would be nothing but dark and merely historical to us. # The Preface to the Reader.

In us, time and eternity and all mysteries are couched and contained; they may truly be read in our own souls by the illumination of the Divine Spirit. For our mind is a true mystical *mirror* and looking-glass of divine and natural mysteries. 
<margin>3</margin>
<margin>☞</margin> We shall receive more real knowledge from one effective, innate, and essential glimpse, beam, or ray of light arising from the new birth *within us* than from reading many hundreds of authors. Through such reading, we scrape together an abundance of artificial works and concepts and build for ourselves a *Babel* <note>Babel: A reference to the Tower of Babel, symbolizing a confused or prideful accumulation of human knowledge that leads to misunderstanding.</note> of knowledge in our ideas and imagination. For in the true light, we receive the pledge and guarantee of *that Spirit* which *searches all things, even the depths of God* <note>original: "Searcheth all things, yea the depths of God"; a reference to 1 Corinthians 2:10.</note>. Let no man think that it is impossible; for in a Christian newly born in Christ, Christ—the light of life and the essence of Love—dwells. And in *Him are all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge*; and He expresses these in the humble, resigned soul <term>resigned soul: a soul that has surrendered its self-will to the will of God</term> in the manner and measure that He pleases, and as is best suited for the time and age.

These *epistles* <term>epistles: formal or didactic letters</term> are not filled with fine, complimentary styles and pleasing ideas of human art; nor with the learned quotations of ancient authors, or the witty insights of sharp reason, dressed up in *scholastic pomp* <note>original: "Scholastique pompe"; refers to the overly formal, academic, and rigid style used by university-trained theologians of the era.</note> and a pride of words to tickle and delight the imagination of the reader. Neither do they favor a sectarian spirit of hypocrisy...

<summary>The author explains that genuine spiritual understanding arises from an internal "new birth" and the Divine Spirit within the soul, rather than through the prideful accumulation of academic or "scholastic" knowledge. He frames his letters not as works of human art or intellectual wit, but as products of this internal divine light.</summary>

By this, we may come to find out from where the inward, radical <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for the essential being or "thing-ness" of an object</note>, working essence, true subsistence, and full existence of everything proceeds.

# A
## <margin>* Or Letter of Divine Wisdom.</margin> * Theosophical LETTER,
### WHEREIN
->The life of a true CHRISTIAN is described.<-

---

->Showing what a Christian is, and how he comes to be a Christian; and likewise what a titular, nominal, or historical Christian is, and how the faith and life of each differ.<-

---

> *The fountain of Jesus Christ’s heart be our quickening consolation, renewal <note>original: "renovation"</note>, and eternal life.*

---

1. **B**ELOVED, and much respected friend in Christ. Out of a sense of duty as a fellow member of the body of Christ <note>original: "member-like obligation"; referring to the "body of Christ" where all believers are parts of one whole</note> (just as one branch on a tree is bound to another) and a shared desire for our mutual spiritual work, I wish for you the open wellspring of grace which God has revealed in our human nature through Christ Jesus. I pray that it may spring up richly within you, and that the Divine Sun may thereby cast the influence of its beams of love into your soul. May it also stir up and open the soul's great magnetic hunger <note>Boehme uses "magnetic hunger" to describe the soul's powerful, innate attraction or longing for God</note> (which is the true divine mouth) for the flesh and blood of Christ, along with your physical well-being.

2. Since I have often heard from your dear friend, **D. K.**, and have also observed myself while in your presence, that you—through the Father's drawing <note>referring to the biblical concept in John 6:44 of God "drawing" souls to Christ</note>—possess a unique thirst and an earnest, sincere longing for His life, which He, out of His supreme love, has revealed...

<summary>This page introduces a letter describing the difference between a true, living Christian and one who is merely "nominal" or "historical." The author opens with a spiritual blessing, emphasizing the soul's "magnetic hunger" for Christ's presence.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, true Christianity, nominal Christianity, divine wisdom, magnetic hunger, grace, spiritual renewal, D. K.</keywords>

<page-num>2</page-num>
<header>The First Letter <note>original: "Epistle"</note></header>

in <term>Christ Jesus</term>; therefore I would not (at the request of Mr. D. and also of yourself) fail—out of a member-like and mutual obligation—to visit and salute you with a short <term>letter</term> <note>original: "Epistle"</note>, and so to restore, enliven, and refresh myself somewhat with you in the same fountain of the life of <term>Jesus Christ</term>. For it is pure joy to me to perceive that our <term>Paradisical Coral</term> <note>A mystical term, likely referring to the growth of the spiritual "pearl" or the new life of the soul in its divine state.</note> flourishes and brings forth fruit in my fellow members, to our eternal restoration and delight.

<margin>☛</margin>3. And hereupon, Sir, I will declare to you, out of my small gifts and knowledge: *What a Christian is, and why he is called a Christian*; namely, that only he is a Christian who has become capable of this high title within himself. He has resigned himself, with his inward depth <note>original: "ground"</note>, mind, and will, to the free grace in <term>Christ Jesus</term>, and has become in the will of his soul like a young child who only longs for the mother's breasts—who sincerely pants for the mother and sucks the breasts of the mother from which it lives.

4. In the same way, that person alone is a Christian whose soul and mind enter again into the first Mother, from whom the life of humanity originates; *namely*, into the <term>Eternal Word</term>. This Word has manifested itself with the true milk of salvation in our humanity (which is blind regarding God) and sucks this milk of the Mother into his hungry soul, from which the new spiritual man arises. By this, the fiery [dark and dry] soul, which proceeds from the Father's property <note>In the author's theosophy, this refers to the foundational, stern power of God that must be transformed by love.</note>, reaches and obtains the place of <term>God's love</term>. In that place, the <term>Father begets</term> his beloved <term>Son</term>, and there alone the <term>Temple of the Holy Spirit</term>, which dwells in us, is found. There also the spiritual mouth [or believing desire] of the soul, which eats <term>Christ's flesh</term> and drinks his <term>blood</term>, is understood—or rather, experienced and essentially enjoyed.

<margin>☛</margin>5. For only he is a Christian in whom <term>Christ</term> dwells, lives, and has his being; in whom <term>Christ</term>, regarding the internal depth of his soul, has arisen and been made alive in the heavenly <term>essence</term> which disappeared and departed in <term>Adam</term>. Indeed, I say, he alone is a Christian who has put on <term>Christ's Victory</term> (that is to say, <term>Christ's Incarnation</term>, humanity, sufferings, death, and resurrection against the anger of <term>God</term>, as well as <term>Hell</term>, the <term>Devil</term>, <term>Death</term>, and <term>Sin</term>) in his eternal depth. There, the <term>Seed of the Woman</term>—namely, <term>Christ</term> in his conquest—also conquers in him, daily trampling upon the serpent's head in the wicked, carnal will, and killing the sinful lusts of the flesh.

6. For in <term>Christ</term> alone are we received to be children of <term>God</term> and heirs with <term>Christ</term>; not by an outward, accidental <note>original: "adventitious"</note>, or foreign show of some separate, selected appropriation of grace, I say, nor through a

<meta>catchword: foreign <note>original: "strange"</note></meta>

<summary>The author defines a true Christian not by outward labels, but as one who has undergone an internal spiritual rebirth, returning to the "Eternal Word" like a child to a mother. This process involves the indwelling of Christ and the daily conquest of sinful desires through Christ's victory over death and sin.</summary>

-> ### The First Epistle. <-
<sig>B 2</sig>

...[st]range merit of grace [or the application of promises] assigned from the outside [or received through a <term>historical understanding</term><note>original: "Historicall apprehension"; this refers to an intellectual belief in biblical facts without personal spiritual transformation</note> of being justified and acquitted by another, as if we were criminals]. Instead, it comes through a childlike, regenerating, innate, and indwelling grace that belongs to us like a limb of a body. In this state, Christ—the conqueror of death—arises in us with his life-essence and power from our own death. He has a mutual ruling influence and operation within us, just as a branch lives on its vine, as the writings of the Apostles witness throughout.

<margin>☞</margin>
7. He is far from being a Christian who only comforts himself with the suffering, death, and atonement of Christ, applying and assigning it to himself as a pardon or gift of favor, yet still remains an unregenerated, wild, [worldly, and sensual] beast. Every ungodly man is such a "Christian." For everyone would gladly be saved through a gift of favor; even the Devil would very willingly become an angel again by grace received and applied from the outside.

8. But to turn and become like a child, to be born anew of God’s grace-water of love and the Holy Spirit—that does not please him. In the same way, it does not please the <term>titular Christian</term><note>A Christian in name only, rather than in practice or spirit</note>, who wants to wrap himself in the mantle of Christ’s grace [and claim his merits for himself by an intellectual laying of claim to a promise] and yet will not enter into spiritual <term>adoption</term> and the New Birth. This is despite Christ’s saying that he cannot otherwise see the Kingdom of God.

9. For what is born of the flesh is flesh and cannot inherit the Kingdom of God (*John 3*). To be fleshly-minded is enmity against God, but to be spiritually-minded is life and peace. He alone hears God’s Word who is born of God, for only the spirit of grace in Christ hears God’s Word.

10. For no man has ever seen God; the Son alone, who is in the immeasurable bosom of the Father, declares the Word and Will of God to us within ourselves. Thus, we hear and understand his will and good pleasure within ourselves and are willing to follow it. Yet we are often kept back by the outward, sinful flesh. Therefore, the operation or effect of that same divine power [purpose and godly resolution in our mind] does not always manifest in our outward form. However, it does enter the inward form in the inner spiritual world; concerning which St. Paul says, *Our <term>conversation</term> is in Heaven.*<note>original: "conversation"; in this context, it means "citizenship" or "way of life"</note> <margin>☞</margin>

11. All the saints of God, and especially St. Paul, have complained of this: that they had an earnest, sincere will and served God with the mind of the <term>internal ground</term><note>The deepest, most fundamental part of the soul or being</note>, but with the flesh they served the law of sin. Thus, the flesh lusts against the spirit. This lust and evil <term>concupiscence</term><note>Strong, often sinful, desire or appetite</note> is daily drowned and put to death in the death of Christ by the inward ground [or the center of light rege-

He explores the conflict between the spiritual mind and the sinful flesh, citing St. Paul’s struggle with the "law of sin."</summary>

<page-num>4</page-num>

# The First Epistle.

...generated in the dark abyss <term>abyss: in this context, the "unground" or bottomless depth of the soul, a key concept in Boehme's mysticism</term> of the soul; but this is only in those in whom Christ has arisen from death. Thus, there is no condemnation for those who are in Christ Jesus, who walk not according to the flesh, but according to the spirit, etc. For the bestial body belongs to the earth, but the spiritual body belongs to God. But whoever does not have it is dead while he lives, and neither hears nor perceives anything of the spirit of God; it is foolishness to him, as the Scripture says.

<margin>☛</margin>
12. Therefore, this point is not sufficiently understood or clarified by only teaching and maintaining an outward receiving and application of grace and the forgiveness of sins. The pardon of sin and our reception into filial adoption <note>original: "filiall adoption"; being accepted as children of God</note> and grace consists in the justifying blood and death of Christ. This occurred when Christ’s heavenly blood tinctured <note>original: "tinctured"; a term from alchemy meaning to infuse or transform the inner quality of a substance</note> us, and with the highest love of the Deity in the name *Jesus*, overcame the anger of God in our soul and inward divine ground—proceeding from the Essence of Eternity—and turned it again into divine humility, meekness, and obedience. By this, the rent, torn, and divided balance <note>original: "temperature"; here meaning the harmonious mixture of spiritual qualities</note> of our human nature <note>original: "property"</note> and spirit of obedience and goodwill entered again into the harmony and unity of the properties. <gloss>Namely, into Paradisiacal light, love, and life—that *Real Temperature* where variety concurs and accords in unity.</gloss>

<margin>☛</margin>
13. And even then, the wrath of the Father (which was awakened in the qualities of our life and had set itself as Lord and Master in soul and body, making us dead to the Kingdom of Heaven and children of wrath) was turned again into the harmonious love, likeness, and unity of God. Our own human will died in the death of Christ, turning away from its self-centeredness <note>original: "selfnesse"</note> and selfish willing. Then, the original human will (which God breathed from His spirit into *Adam*) sprang forth anew through the victory of God’s sweetness and love in Christ’s heavenly blood. Thus the Devil and Hell, which held men captive, were confounded and made a scorn. This was symbolized by the dry Rod of *Aaron*, which budded in one night and bore sweet almonds.

<margin>☛</margin>
14. Now, just as sin came from one man and passed from one upon all, so also the sweet grace and victory in Christ passed, pressed, and pierced from one upon all. Now, this death and the anger of God were broken in pieces in that one *Adamic* soul in Christ, and a possibility for grace was opened through the unlocking <note>original: "disclosing"</note> of death. Through this opened gate, the will of the soul may re-enter into its first Mother (from whom it came in the beginning)—that is, into the adoption or regeneration of a new life and will. There it may attain the sweet blood of Jesus Christ, which in Christ did unlock or break open the gate of death and changed the anger...

<summary>This page argues that true salvation is an internal transformation ("tincturing") of the soul's qualities rather than a mere outward application of grace. It describes how Christ’s sacrifice restores the human soul to its original, harmonious "Adamic" state, overcoming the "wrath" that previously dominated it.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the transformation of human nature through Christ, specifically how the soul's "fire-breath" is sweetened by divine love.</meta>

<page-num>6</page-num>

# The First Epistle.

...of God in our humanity into love within himself, wherein the poor captive soul drinks from God's fountain and allays and sweetens its fire-breath. From this, the new Paradisiacal <term>Paradisiacal: relating to Paradise or the original state of perfection</term> bud springs forth, and there the hunger and desire of the soul is made substantial and essential in the blood of Christ, in a heavenly manner.

15. Now, just as the undoing <note>original: "disclosing"</note> of death must be done in the person of Christ himself within our soul and humanity—so that the eternity in Christ (with which he came from heaven, and was also in heaven, *John 3*) overcame time (that is, the life and will of time) and changed time and its will into the eternal will of the Deity—and all this must be accomplished in our received human nature. Likewise, the desire of our soul must receive into itself that same eternal will in Christ (in which time and eternity stand in equal agreement and harmony). Through the power of that will, the soul must immerse or cast itself into the adoption of free grace in Christ, so that the same inward Paradisiacal ground which died in *Adam* might again spring forth in the will of obedience to Christ, through his heavenly blood and the human blood he assumed from us.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. The atonement and expiation <term>expiation: the act of making amends or reparation for guilt</term> must be made manifest and experienced within ourselves through that atonement which Christ once made. Indeed, the atonement and reconciliation were once fully finished and brought to pass in Christ’s blood and death; but that which was once wrought in Christ must also work in me. It must even **now** also be brought to pass in me through Christ’s shedding of his blood. Christ also pours forth his heavenly blood into the desire of faith in my poor soul and tinctures <term>tinctures: permeates or transforms with a spiritual quality; a term from alchemy used here to describe spiritual transformation</term> the anger of God which is within it, so that the first Adamic <note>original: "Adamicall"</note> image of God may again appear and become seeing, hearing, feeling, tasting, and smelling.

<margin>☞</margin>
17. For that same image which died in *Adam*—descending from the heavenly world's essence and being the true Paradisiacal image—does not dwell in the four elements. Its essence and life do not exist in this world, but in heaven (which is manifest in Christ within us); that is, in that one pure, holy Element from which the four elements <note>The four elements are earth, air, fire, and water, representing the physical world.</note> sprang forth at the beginning of time. This same inward, new spiritual man eats Christ’s flesh and blood; for he is, and lives in Christ. Christ is his trunk <note>original: "stock"</note>, and he is a branch on that trunk.

18. For every spirit eats of that from which it takes its origin. The animal (sensual), mortal soul eats of the spirit of this world—of the stars and elements, and of the kingdom of the world—but the true eternal soul (which was inspired out of the Eternal Word into man as divine life) also eats of its mother: namely, the holy, essential Word of God.

<meta>catchword: 19. But</meta>

<summary>This page explains that Christ's historical atonement must become a personal, internal experience where the soul is "tinctured" by divine grace. It describes the "new spiritual man" as a being that lives in the heavenly realm and is sustained by the Word of God rather than the physical elements.</summary>

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of the soul's redemption, moving from the necessity of Christ’s incarnation to the internal process of spiritual transformation and the rejection of merely external "imputed" righteousness.</meta>

# The First Epistle

19. Since it was impossible for the soul—after its own departure and separation from God in its excluded condition, quality, <margin>☛</margin> and spirit—to eat of the **Divine Word** and live in the holy element of love and humility, this same Word of life (being the soul's true Mother) came out to the abandoned soul in the valley of misery and the prison of Hell. He infused his heavenly essence into our human essence to serve as a body for the soul. With this, he embraced and clothed our poor, captive soul and reopened its heavenly mouth—which was previously dead in the anger of God—with the tincture <note>original: "tincture"; here, it refers to a spiritual essence or medicinal quality that transforms or heals the soul.</note> of love. Now, the poor soul can once again eat heavenly *manna* <term>Manna: Traditionally the food provided by God to the Israelites in the desert; here, it refers to spiritual nourishment from God.</term>. This eating was tested in Christ's person within the humanity he received from us during the temptation of Christ in the wilderness, where Adam—acting through Christ—again ate *manna* from Paradise for forty days.

20. Therefore, I say that no one becomes a true Christian through a mere show or specific form of grace that is claimed or attributed to oneself from the outside. If a person is a true Christian, his sins are not forgiven simply by speaking a word once, or by adopting a form or promise of absolution from the outside, as a lord or prince in this world might freely grant a criminal his life through an external favor. No, this has no value before God.

21. There is no grace or favor by which we reach adoption <margin>☞</margin> <note>Adoption: In a theological sense, being received as a child of God.</note> except only through the blood and death of Christ. God has foreordained and appointed him alone to be a throne of grace or propitiation <term>Propitiation: An action that regains favor or makes atonement; a sacrifice that turns away divine anger.</term> in His own love, which He has infused into him (in the *sweet name Jesus* out of *Jehovah* <note>Böhme often explored the mystical relationship between the Hebrew name for God, Jehovah, and the name of Jesus.</note>). He is the only sacrifice that God accepts to reconcile His anger.

22. Now, if this sacrifice or free offering of grace is to do me <margin>☞</margin> any good, then it must be worked and brought to pass within me. The Father must give birth to or imprint His Son in the desire of my faith, so that the hunger of my faith may take hold of and understand him. If the hunger of my soul’s faith does comprehend him in his promised Word, then I "put him on" throughout the entire process of justification in my innermost being. Immediately, the mortification <term>Mortification: The subduing or "killing" of the selfish or sinful nature.</term> and killing of anger, the Devil, death, and Hell begins and moves forward through the power of Christ’s death within me.

23. For I can do nothing; I am dead to myself, but Christ <margin>☚</margin> does it in me. When he arises in me, then I am dead to myself in terms of the true man, and he is my life. In that I live, I live for him and not for my own selfhood. For grace mortifies my own will and sets itself up as Lord in place of my selfishness, so that I may be an instrument of God, who does with me whatever He pleases.

24. And then I live in two kingdoms, namely with my newborn spirit of life (or spiritual man) in Paradise, in the Kingdom of Heaven...

<summary>Jacob Böhme argues that true Christianity is an internal transformation where Christ is reborn within the soul, rather than a mere external legal declaration of forgiveness. He describes the soul's "eating" of spiritual manna and the necessity of surrendering one's "selfhood" to become an instrument of God.</summary>
<keywords>justification, mortification, Divine Word, manna, selfhood, propitiation, internal transformation, Jesus, Jehovah, spiritual rebirth</keywords>

<page-num>7</page-num>

# The First Epistle

...in the inward spiritual world, and with the outward mortal man, in the vanity of time, in the kingdom of this world, in the dominion of the stars and elements, in the contrariety and malignant discord of the properties—where the yoke of sin still lives. This Christ takes upon himself in the inward kingdom of the divine world and helps my soul to bear it.

<margin>☛</margin>
25. For the yoke of this world is Christ’s burden, which he must bear until he shall again deliver up the kingdom to his Father, which he has given him. For he said, *“All power in heaven and earth is given to me by my Father.”* So also, this burden or charge is laid upon him, that he should bear God’s anger, hell, death, and all evil in us, as *Isaiah* says: *“He took on him our diseases and carried our sorrows”*; but we esteemed him stricken, smitten of God, and afflicted. <note>Isaiah 53:4</note>

<margin>☛</margin>
26. And from this, it follows that a Christian must be a bearer of the cross. For as soon as Christ is born in him, the assault of hell and the anger of God in the eternal nature begins. Then the hell in man is destroyed, and the "serpent" <note>Referring to the biblical serpent in Eden, symbolizing the devil or the root of sin.</note> is trodden underfoot. 

From this, great unrest, persecution, and reproach from the Devil and the corrupt world arise against the outward sinful man. Even then, the outward sinful man must allow himself to be condemned and judged to damnation by the "children of anger" <note>Those who live according to their worldly, sinful impulses rather than the spirit.</note> and by God’s severe justice in his anger. This occurs because another "man" <note>The new, spiritual self.</note> lives in him who is not like or conformable to the outward mortal man. Thus, God’s justice executes its judgment in anger upon the "house of sin," and all the ministers of God’s wrath help in its execution.

27. And even then, Christ helps to bear the yoke, and man is sacrificed in Christ’s process—through contempt and scorn, and through his suffering and death—to the justice of God in his anger, and thus becomes conformable to Christ’s image.

28. The Holy Scripture testifies in all places that we are justified from sins by faith in Christ, and not by the works of our own merit, but by the blood and death of Christ. This is indeed taught by many, but it is rightly understood by few who teach it to us.

29. We are indeed taught about <term>imputed grace</term> <note>original: "imputed grace" — The theological doctrine that a believer's righteousness is not their own, but is "credited" to them by God through Christ's merit.</note>, but as for what faith is, how it is begotten, what it is in its essence, real being, and substance, and how it lays hold on the merit of Christ along with grace—regarding this, the greatest part of teachers are dumb and blind. They depend on a <term>historical faith</term> <note>original: "Historicall Faith" (James 2) — A mere intellectual belief in the facts of the Bible without an inward, transforming spiritual experience.</note> which is only a bare knowledge or literal conjecture. With this, the "man of sin" <note>The unregenerate human nature.</note> tickles and comforts himself; and through imagination and blind persuasion, he flatters and soothes himself, calling himself a Christian, though he has not yet become either capable or worthy of...

<summary>This page explains that a true Christian must endure a spiritual struggle where Christ helps bear the soul's burdens while the "outward" sinful self is judged and disciplined. It critiques those who rely on a superficial "historical faith" rather than a deep, essential transformation through grace.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the theological discussion of the nature of a true Christian, contrasting external religious identity with the internal transformation of the soul through Christ's indwelling spirit.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>
<header>The First Epistle.</header>

[...holding] this so high a title, but is only a Christian in name and title, externally clothed with Christ’s purple mantle; of whom the Prophet speaks, saying: *They draw near to me with their lips, but their heart is far from me*; and Christ said: *Not all that say "Lord, Lord" shall enter into the Kingdom of Heaven, but they that do the will of my Father in Heaven.*

30. Now Christ alone is the will of the Father, in whom acceptance, grace, and adoption consist. No one can do the loving will of the Father except for that only throne of grace, Christ himself; as the Holy Scripture declares, no man can call God "Lord" without the Holy Spirit in him.

31. For we do not know what and how to pray before God as we ought, but he—even the Holy Spirit in Christ—makes intercession for us with unutterable sighs <note>original: "unutterable sighes," a reference to Romans 8:26</note> before God within ourselves, as it pleases God. We cannot attain anything by our own willing and knowing; He is too deeply hidden from us, for it does not lie in any man’s knowing, willing, running, and searching, but in God’s mercy.

<margin>☛</margin>
32. Now there is no mercy except in Christ; and if I am to reach that mercy, then I must reach Christ in me. Are my sins to be destroyed in me? Then Christ must do it in me with his blood and death, with his victory over Hell. *Am I to believe? Then the spirit, desire, and will of Christ must believe in my desire and will, for I cannot believe [on my own].*

33. *But he receives my will once it is resigned to him, and he encompasses it in his own will and brings it through his victory into God. There, he intercedes for the will of my soul within his own will before God; and so I am received as a child of grace in his will of love.*

34. For the Father has manifested his love in Christ, and Christ manifests that same love in my will when it is resigned to him. Christ draws my will into himself, and clothes it with his blood and death, and <term>tinctures</term><note>The author uses "tincture" in an alchemical or theosophical sense, meaning to spiritually infuse, transform, or imbue a substance with a higher quality.</note> it with the highest <term>tincture</term> of Divine power; and so it is changed into an angelic image and gains a Divine life.

35. And immediately that same life begins to hunger after its body—which body is the degenerated fiery soul into which the will in Christ has entered—so that the new life in Christ now also <term>tinctures</term> the soul. By this, the soul (in the spirit and nature of this <gloss>new</gloss> will) obtains a true Divine hunger and is made to long after and desire Divine grace. She <note>In the theology of this period, the soul (Latin: *anima*) was often referred to using feminine pronouns.</note> begins to behold and consider, in the spirit of this Divine will in Christ, what she is, and how she—in her qualities, inclinations, and dispositions—has departed from God and lies captive in the wrath of God. Then she acknowledges her abominations and also her deformity in the presence of God’s angels, and finds that she has nothing with which...

<sig>C</sig>

she is able to protect herself, for she sees that she stands in the jaws of death and hell, surrounded by evil spirits who continually shoot the strong influence of their evil desires into her to destroy and corrupt her.

36. And then she dives into that same newborn spirit of the will and wholly immerses herself into very humility itself. Thus, the spirit of Christ takes hold of her and brings her into the spirit of this new will, so that the soul essentially and experimentally feels and finds Him. At this, a divine glimpse and beam of joy arises in the soul, acting as a new eye in which the dark, fiery soul conceives the <term>Ens</term><note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term meaning the fundamental essence or "being" of a thing.</note> and essence of the divine light within herself. Thereupon, she hungers and thirsts after the grace of God, enters into an effectual repentance and sorrow, and bewails the evil which she has committed.

37. And in this hunger and thirst, she receives Christ’s flesh and blood; for the spirit of the new will (which in the beginning entered into the grace of Christ, and which Christ received into Himself) becomes now substantial and essential through the magnetic pressure, hunger, and desire of the soul.

38. And this essentiality is called ***Sophia***, being the essential wisdom or the body of Christ; and in this, faith in the Holy Spirit consists. *Here, Christ and the soul believe in one shared ground.*

<margin>☞</margin>
39. For true faith does not consist in mere thoughts or in simply assenting to history: *namely*, that a person imagines <insert>or forms an image in their mind</insert> that Christ died for their sins. <insert>Nor does it consist of a historical understanding or a blind persuasion of a particular election, where one clings to His merits and righteousness without the inborn righteousness of Christ and the life of God essentially working in them and birthing them into a new creature.</insert> For faith is a receiving of the promised grace in Christ; it receives Christ into itself. It presses Him into its hunger—along with His heavenly flesh and blood and the grace which God offers in Christ.

40. Christ feeds the soul with the essence of ***Sophia***, *namely*, with His own flesh and blood, according to His saying: "Whoever does not eat of the flesh of the Son of Man has no life in him; but whoever eats the same abides in Him." <note>A reference to John 6:53–56.</note>

41. And herein the <term>Testaments of Christ</term><note>Referring to the Sacraments, specifically the Lord's Supper.</note> and also the right Christian faith consist. For an insubstantial <gloss>unessential, conjectural, or merely verbal</gloss> faith is like a glimmering fire smothered in smoke or moisture; it would willingly burn but has no proper <term>Ens</term> to fuel it. But when a true <term>Ens</term> is given to it, then 그 little spark of fire increases, and a fair, shining light arises. Then it is manifest that such a fire and beautiful light lay hidden in the wood, which before was not known.

# The First Epistle.

42. And this is also to be understood in a child of God: while the poor soul is wrapped up in the anger of God, it is like a glimmering * <term>wick</term> <note>original: "Wiek"; here referring to a candle wick that is barely lit or smoldering</note> that would willingly burn but cannot because of the vanity of sins and the anger of God. But when the soul—being a little spark of Divine fire—receives into itself God’s Love-<term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical and theological term used by Boehme to describe a fundamental "essence" or "spiritual substance"</note> (that is, Christ’s flesh and blood), then that spark grows into a great fire and light. This light shines and sends forth its glow through beautiful virtues and good works. It lives with great patience under the vanity of this world, yet grows forth like a beautiful flower out of the wild earth.

43. We see a comparison <note>original: "similitude"</note> of this in the Sun and the Earth. Namely, if the Sun did not shine upon the Earth, no fruit could grow. But when the Sun shines upon the Earth and penetrates into the <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Ens"</note> of the Earth, the Earth receives the virtue or powerful influence of the Sun into itself. Consequently, a great hunger arises in the essence of the Earth for the power of the Sun. This hunger draws in and feeds upon the virtue and influence of the Sun. Through this hunger of the Earth's essence, which eagerly reaches for the Sun's essence, a plant <note>original: "Hearb"</note> is drawn out of the Earth with a stalk. In this plant, the essence and virtue of the Sun also ascend during its growth and flourishing. The Sun, with its beams of light, becomes substantial within the Earth's essence in the stalk and root. We see how, through the power of the Sun and stars in the spirit of the world, another body arises from the stalk that is different from the root in the earth. We also see how the stalk puts forth a bud into a beautiful flower, and afterward into fruit; and we see how the Sun, from time to time, ripens and sweetens that fruit.

44. It is the same with man. The ground of the soul is the Divine field; when it receives the Divine sunshine into itself, a Divine plant springs forth. This is the "new birth" of which Christ speaks. Now, this plant must be nourished from above by heavenly <note>original: "celestiall"</note> influence. It is drawn up in growth by the Divine Sun and the Divine Water, and preserved by the Divine constellation—that is, the Divine virtue or power of God—until it becomes a Divine body with a Divine and angelic form <note>original: "figure"</note>, just like the fruit on the stalk.

45. And just as the plant on the stalk must stand in rain, wind, and storms—in heat and cold—and allow the Sun to ripen it, so too must a Christian stand in this thorny world, within the awakened anger of God, in the Kingdom of the Devil, and among many wicked people. He must allow himself to be beaten with scorn and contempt, yet he must turn his hope and confidence away from all created things...

only and alone upon the Divine Sun, and allow it to ripen him and beget in him a heavenly fruit.

46. Neither temples of stone houses, nor human ordinances and forms of *word worship* <note>original: "Word worship"; here meaning formal religious services that consist only of spoken words without inner spiritual power</note> beget [or regenerate] a person to a newness of life. Instead, the Divine Sun—acting within the Divine Constellation of the powers of the Word of God in the temple of Jesus Christ within oneself—begets him from the fountain of life, Jesus Christ. Thus, he is a branch on the vine of Christ and produces good grapes which the Divine Sun ripens, so that God’s children (his loving fellow-members) may eat them. Through this, they also grow and flourish in and with him; these grapes are good doctrine, life, and works.

47. A person must reach the practice, effective performance, and fruitfulness of Christianity; otherwise, the new birth is not yet manifest in him, nor is the noble branch yet born. No amount of tickling the ears, soothing, comforting with promises or scriptural evidence, or boasting of a faith avails anyone at all if that faith does not make them a child conformed to God in essence and will—a faith that brings forth divine fruit.

48. Everything that men currently strive, dispute, contend, and fight about—thereby destroying and laying waste to countries and people—is only a mere husk without fruit [a form without power, a *Mammon* <note>original: "Mammon"; wealth or material greed personified as a false god</note> without Christian mercy]. It belongs to the fiery world for separation <note>refers to the spiritual process of "winnowing" or judgment where the worthless is burned away</note> [and shall be decided in the judgment of the Lord]. There is no true understanding in any faction; they all contend only over the name and the will of God, yet no faction will actually do His will. They care for nothing but their own glory, advancement, and the pleasures of the flesh. If they were true Christians, they would have no strife or contention.

49. A good tree bears good fruit for everyone. Although it must sometimes suffer the wind to break its branches and fruit, or the sun to wither and dry them—and even when the fruits are ripe, to be devoured by swine or trodden underfoot—despite all this, it still endeavors to bring forth more good fruit.

<margin>☞</margin>
50. And thus, a true Christian in Christ can desire nothing else but what Christ desires within him. Though he must allow his good fruit (which springs and grows from the internal man) to be often trampled upon and spoiled by the evil flesh and blood, as well as by the devil’s wind [the wicked censure and false interpretation of the hypocritical *Luciferian Serpent* <note>a reference to prideful deception and spiritual malice, associated with the fall of Lucifer</note>] and the wickedness of the world; yet the tree of the new plant in the life of Christ still stands and springs forth through the outward mortal man, maugre <term>despite</term> all opposition, irresistibly. It is just as eternity springs through time and gives life and power to time; and as the day

## The First Epistle.

...arises and breaks forth through the night, and changes the night into day, and yet the night remains there still in itself, and yet it is not known or discerned in the day. So likewise the Divine day springs and buds forth in us through our eternal night, and changes the night—namely, God’s Anger, Hell, Death, Anguish, and eternal destruction—into the Divine Day of joy and consolation; even though the dark night, with the <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens"; a Latin term for being or essence, here referring to the fallen nature inherited from the fall of man</note> of the Serpent and the poison in flesh and blood, rages and strives against it.

<margin>☞</margin>
51. Therefore, beloved Sir and Christian Brother, we have more need to endeavor for the growth and increase of the precious <term>Coral</term> <note>original: "Corall"; likely a metaphorical reference to a spiritual treasure or the "pearl of great price"</note>, and how we may come to it, than to run after the unprofitable chatterings and speculative fictions of pretended holiness, where one brother despises, reproaches, rejects, and proclaims the other a heretic—nay, hands him over to the Devil for the sake of an <term>Opinion</term> <note>subjective religious dogma or personal interpretation</note> which he has made for himself.

52. I tell and declare to you, in the knowledge which God has given me, that it is a mere deceit of the Devil, who thus brings us poor men into <term>Opinions</term> to despise and reproach one another. He causes us to cry down and hold one another as <term>Heretics</term> and <term>Fantastics</term> <note>original: "Fantasticks"; those who follow their own imaginations or "fancies" rather than true revelation</note> if we do not bind our conscience to his mental <term>Idol</term> or <term>Opinion</term>. Thus we strive and wrangle about the husk, and in the meantime, we lose love and faith and do not attain the new birth.

<margin>☞</margin>
53. Our whole religion is but a childlike work; namely, that we wholly forsake and disclaim our own knowing, willing, running, disputing, [and the forged conclusions of blind, hypocritical Reason, which bewitches the natural mind into the form and frame of its own wisdom]. We must unfeignedly and fully resolve within ourselves to enter into and persevere in the way which brings us again to our own native country which we lost [in Adam, when he, with his mind and full will, went out of Paradise into the spirit of this transitory world]. Thus we return to our <term>Mother</term> <note>referring to Divine Wisdom or the spiritual origin of the soul</note>, who in the beginning begot us and brought us forth out of herself.

<margin>☞</margin>
54. Now, if we will do this, then we must not come to her in our own self-will and way—in pride and ostentation, in applauding ourselves, or in contempt of her children, our fellow Christians and fellow members. For we are the lost <term>Son</term> <note>the Prodigal Son</note> who has become a <term>Swineherd</term>, and we have shamefully misspent our <term>Patrimony</term> <note>inheritance</note> with the fatted <term>Hogs</term> of the Devil and the <term>World</term>. We must enter into ourselves and well consider our condition and our <term>Father’s house</term>. We <margin>* Which loves all our brethren, even through our enemies.</margin> must set before us the mirror, or looking-glass, of the <term>Law</term> and <term>Gospel</term>, and see how far we have departed from God's righteousness and also from [unfeigned, impartial] brotherly love, and well examine our heart to see where it is inclined.

<summary>The author warns against the divisiveness of religious dogma and "opinions," urging believers to seek a "new birth" through humility and love rather than sectarian strife. He uses the parable of the Prodigal Son to illustrate the need for humanity to return to its spiritual origin by forsaking self-will.</summary>

55. Now, when we do this, we shall find in ourselves many hundred evil beasts, which we have set up in God's stead and do honor and love more than God. Even then, we shall see what hideous, horrible beasts were revealed in *Adam* through false desire <note>original: "lust"</note> or imagination, and why God said to *Adam*, *The Seed of the Woman shall break the Serpent’s head*; that is to say, these monstrous beasts.

56. First, we shall see in our desire the proud *Lucifer*, who has turned away from Divine and brotherly humility and despises <note>original: "contemneth"</note> the members of his own body. He has set himself up to be a God and Lord over them, and in him there is no Divine love to love either God or his brother.

<margin>\* Or, everything.</margin>
57. Secondly, we shall find a beast in our properties resembling the covetous, greedy swine, which wants to take everything for itself and alone devour and possess all. It desires more than it needs so that the proud *Lucifer* might be able to boast, brag, and vaunt himself—claiming that he is a God over \* *substance* and that he can domineer, having power and might over his fellow branches. We shall see how this proud *Lucifer* has broken himself off from the Tree of Life and the mutual growth and increase of love; he wishes to be a tree unto himself, and therefore he is a withered, dry branch in the eyes of God.

58. Thirdly, we shall find the poisonous, envious serpent in our properties, which tears and rages like a poison. I mean Envy, which wishes no man as much good as it wishes itself; it stings, rages, and rides in other men's hearts and slanders them with words. It only applauds its own haughty *Lucifer* and terms its falsehood <insert>and cunning hypocrisy</insert> an angel of God.

59. Fourthly, we shall find in our properties the fiery dragon sitting in hellish fire. I mean *Anger*, which—if greed <note>original: "covetousness"</note> and envy cannot obtain what they want—will lay violent hands upon it and take it by force. It is so raging mad that it bursts its own life out of mere malice and wickedness <note>original: "iniquity"</note>; it breaks in pieces in fiery malice and is a very dry branch on the tree, fit only for the fire.

60. Fifthly, we shall find many hundred evil beasts in our desire, which *Pride* loves and honors more than God. Greed <note>original: "covetousness"</note> draws them to itself as a treasure, with which *Pride* boasts and vaunts as if they were gods. By doing this, it withdraws sustenance and comfort from its brother's life, so that he is forced to spend his time in misery, trouble, and perplexity because of such violence and extortion.

61. Now, when a man beholds himself in this mirror of falsehood and perceives these evil beasts, then let him rightly...

The author encourages deep introspection to recognize these internal monsters as the "Serpent's head" that must be broken.</summary>

...imagine to himself that he is deeply guilty of them, and thereby consider the grievous fall of *Adam*. Let him think that all these evil desires and vain imaginations do wholly take their rise and origin in him from the "Monster of the Serpent," <note>The "Monster of the Serpent" refers to the distorted, ego-driven nature that the author believes replaced the divine image in humanity after the Fall.</note> through the influence and creeping in of the Devil into our first Parents.

<margin>☞</margin>
62. For all the properties of the desires lay in one harmony and steadfast unity in *Adam*; they loved one another and were in mutual agreement. But such false desires have risen in Man through the envy of the Devil, who stirred up the false lust and imagination in *Adam* and *Eve* to test the inequality [of the properties and spirit of this mixed World] and to taste what good and evil were. He tempted them to feel heat and cold, and to try the multitude and variety of the properties; so that now these desires attract, long after, and desire that which is like themselves. Every desire among these properties is a separate hunger of life in man, which has broken itself off from the harmonious unity. It opposes the love, likeness, and mutual society of its fellow branches, or brothers, and covetously desires to draw their life and maintenance to itself, and to make itself a Lord over others; it wishes to be a <term>Selfist</term>. <note>original: "Selfeist"; an early term for an egoist or someone driven by self-centeredness rather than divine love.</note>

63. All of this is contrary to the Divine will and foundation, and is a perjury against God; indeed, it opposes the course and order of nature. We see this by the trees and plants of the Earth: how all stand and grow lovingly one by another and rejoice in one Mother; and how one branch on the tree imparts its sap and power to the other, and they mutually serve each other.

64. For in such a lovely equality and harmony the life of Man (*John 1*) was inspired out of the eternal Word into the human Image, which was formed out of the <note>original: "Limus"; Latin for slime or mud, referencing the "dust of the ground" in Genesis 2:7.</note> *Limus* of the Earth. So it was that all the properties of life stood in an equal proportion in the <term>temperature</term>; <note>In this context, a "temperature" refers to a state of perfect balance and harmony among different qualities or "properties."</note> they existed in one Love and mutually loved each other.

65. But when the Devil mixed his poison and false desire into it, the properties of life were divided into many desires. From this, strife, discord, sickness, and infirmities—along with the cumbersome coarseness and mortal frailty of the body—have risen through the false desire and the creeping in of bestial properties. By reason of this, the Image of God (which was from the heavenly Essence) disappeared; concerning this, God said to them, *In the day that you eat of the Tree of the knowledge of good and evil, you shall die the death:* That is, you shall die to the Kingdom of God, just as it also came to pass.

<margin>* Or, Consider with ourselves.</margin>
66. And we must really imagine to ourselves <margin>Consider with ourselves.</margin> that this bestial, false desire in man is the Monster of the Serpent, and an enmity against God and the Kingdom of Heaven. We must realize that in that state, we are only the children of Hell and of the Anger of God, and cannot...

<summary>This page explains the spiritual consequences of the Fall, describing how Adam's original internal harmony was shattered by the Devil's temptation into a state of "Selfishness" and conflicting desires. The author uses the analogy of trees sharing sap to illustrate the lost divine order and explains that human mortality and "bestial properties" resulted from this original spiritual death.</summary>

# The First Epistle.

...inherit or possess the Kingdom of God. Neither is God manifest in any such desire, but only His anger and the property of the dark and earthly world. We live within it only for the vanity of this world and stand with it only upon the abyss of the dark world and the anger of God—that is, of Hell. Hell continually gapes and hungers for these properties and considers them its own fruits and children, which it shall reap and take into its barn. For they belong to it by the right of nature; these desires originate from it and have their root in the ground of Hell and destruction, and indeed nowhere else.

67. Therefore Christ said, *"Unless a man be born anew, he shall not see the Kingdom of God."* All these false wills and desires are predestined to damnation; if anyone wishes to see God, he must be converted, become like a child, and be born again in the Holy Spirit through the water of eternal life. Specifically, this occurs through the heavenly <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens" (Latin for "being" or "essence"); in Boehme’s theology, this refers to the spiritual substance or "spiritual seed" of Christ that must take root in the soul.</note> which God has revealed in Christ. This is so that the first true man—who died in *Adam* (proceeding from the essence of the heavenly world)—might spring forth again in Christ and become living.

68. All these evil beasts are condemned and must die within us. Though their desire clings and sticks to us in the flesh, they must all be mortified <note>original: "mortyfied"; meaning to put to death or subdue through self-discipline and spiritual exercise.</note> during this <gloss>[ life- ]</gloss> time in the soul. Specifically, this must happen in the inward ground, and the inward ground of the soul must be <term>tinctured</term> <note>original: "Tinctured"; in alchemy and Boehme's writing, a "tincture" is a transformative spiritual force or quality that "colors" or changes the nature of a thing.</note> by the true <term>tincture</term> in the blood of Christ. This allows the properties of the internal ground to live again in harmony and concord; otherwise, they cannot reach the Deity within themselves.

<margin>☞</margin>
69. Now when a person knows this, they can find no better way to be rid of their evil, beastly desires than to immediately, at that very instant, bring themselves—with all their strength—into such a strong will and earnest purpose that they will hate and abandon these evil beasts of the Devil for the time to come. They must see that these are only the Devil’s servants and resolve to return to their lost country, into adoption and atonement. They should view themselves as nothing other than the lost swineherd <gloss>[ or the prodigal son ]</gloss>; for they are indeed that lost swineherd and no better. They should immediately approach the Father with the conversion of their soul in deepest humility, acknowledging their unworthiness—admitting that they have shamefully and unworthily misspent and misused the free inheritance of Christ's merits—and so enter into repentance.

<margin>☞</margin>
70. Let them give up their earnest will to this with all their strength: that from this very time forward, they will repent and amend, and no longer love these evil beasts. But this must be in earnest, in real sincerity without delay, and not thinking of a day, a week...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how a person must renounce sinful desires and worldly attachments to turn toward God's grace.</meta>

<page-num>16</page-num>

# The First Epistle

...or year; but his mind must utterly and continually condemn them to the damnation of death, and no longer desire to love them; instead, he must count them as enemies and so resolve to turn himself to the mercy and grace of God.

71. When this is done (I speak as I have deeply known it in the precious light of grace), then he may turn himself to earnest praying in humility and beseech God for grace. This is necessary even though his heart says "no" completely and the **Devil** says, "Stay yet a while; it is not now good or convenient; you will have a better opportunity tomorrow." And when tomorrow comes, the Devil says again, "Tomorrow," and suggests to the flesh, "You must first do this or that; first gather a treasure for yourself so that you do not need the world, and then enter into such a religious life." Yet I say the mind must remain firm and steadfast in its purpose and think to itself: "These suggestions and thoughts are my evil, hungry beasts; I will kill these and drown them in the blood of **Christ's** love. None of them shall live any longer in me; I will have no more to do with them. I am now on the way to my ancient Father, who has sent his Son to me in my misery and distress, saying: *'Come to me, all you who are weary or heavy laden with sins, and I will refresh you.'* My Father will give the Holy Spirit to those who ask Him for it."

<margin>Imprint and settle this within his resolution.</margin>

72. Let him imagine and impress this into his very heart and come with the lost Son <note>A reference to the Parable of the Prodigal Son in Luke 15.</note> to the Father. When the Father sees that the mind of the soul is directed toward Him and would willingly and sincerely turn, yet cannot, then He will presently meet him. He will clasp the soul into the arms of His drawing power and bring her into the passion and death of **Christ**. There, through earnest repentance and sorrow, she will die to (or <term>mortify</term> <note>To "mortify" means to put to death or subdue the desires of the flesh.</note>) those abominable beasts and arise out of the death of **Christ** with a new will and a true divine desire. So, he will then begin to be another man indeed and will not at all value that which he previously loved and held as his treasure. It will be to him as if he had it and yet also had it not; and afterward, he will account himself—in all his power, ability, and possessions—as nothing but a servant and steward of God.

73. For as soon as he is able to master and overcome the proud **Lucifer** of pride, all the other evil beasts will grow weak and faint and lose their ruling power and dominion. Although they still live in this lifetime in the earthly flesh, they are only as a donkey which must carry the sack, or as a mad dog on a chain; their strength is broken.

74. For when **Christ** rises, **Lucifer** must lie captive; and if there is an earnest, full perseverance, such a precious jewel would follow...

<summary>This page outlines the psychological and spiritual struggle of repentance, comparing the rejection of sin to the return of the Prodigal Son and the subduing of "evil beasts" or vices. It explains that by overcoming pride, the believer transforms into a steward of God, breaking the power of sinful impulses.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of the mystical union between the soul and Divine Wisdom, concluding the first letter with an offer of further spiritual instruction.</meta>

<page-num>17</page-num>
-> # The First Epistle. <-

...as this pen cannot describe here; and only those know it who have been at the heavenly marriage, where the noble <term>Sophia</term> <note>original: "Sophia"; referring to Divine Wisdom, often personified in mystical tradition as the bride of the soul</note> is betrothed to the soul. Concerning this, Christ said that there is greater joy in heaven over one sinner who repents than over ninety-nine righteous people. This joy is also found in the "heaven" within a person during this betrothal or marriage; this is understood by our school-fellows <note>original: "Schoole-fellowes"; likely referring to fellow spiritual seekers or students of the same mystical path</note>.

75. Dear Sir and Christian brother, I thought it good (with sincere Christian intentions toward you) to remind you of this. I lay it out from my little treasury <note>original: "little Treasury"; a humble reference to the author's collection of spiritual insights or writings</note> with childlike simplicity, not intending to promote or show off myself. Rather, I do this out of a true and hearty desire, wishing that this might also be felt in your heart, and that I might find some recreation and refreshment with you as a fellow member of Christ's body. Though I am absent in person, I am present with you in spirit and cooperate with you in these divine gifts; I do this at your request, as previously mentioned.

76. And if my goodwill finds a place with you, and God should open the door of His mystery, then I might perhaps have other, even more precious jewels in my little treasury through which Time and Eternity may be understood. I am ready and willing to offer you my service in this regard. And so, I commend you and your family to the sweet and pleasant love, grace, and will of *Jesus Christ*.

# A Letter to
## CASPAR LINDERN,
### Customer <note>original: "Customer"; here referring to a customs official or tax collector</note> at *BEUTEN* <note>likely Bytom, a city in Upper Silesia</note>.

---
->WHEREIN<-
->Is described the plain and simple way<-
->which the Author took for the attainment<-
->of his high Knowledge:<-
---
->**A L S O,**<-
---
->His Censure <note>original: "Censure"; in this context, it means a formal judgment or critical evaluation rather than a condemnation</note>, Judgment, and answer,<-
->concerning various authors of different<-
->opinions, tending to lead Christians<-
->into the excellent and desired way<-
->of Love and Union.<-

<margin>1.</margin>
> **T**HE open fountain in the heart of Jesus Christ refresh us and lead us to himself, that we may live in his power and rejoice in him; so that we may love and understand one another and enter into one single **Will.**

2. Highly respected and discerning Sir, my most worthy friend in the love and humanity of *Jesus Christ*; my hearty desires from God in our *Immanuel* <note>Hebrew: "God with us"</note> for prosperity upon soul and body being first offered; I inform you, Sir, that I have received your letter. In it, I perceive that you are a seeker and great lover of the Mystery <term>The Mystery</term><note>Often referring to the *Mysterium Magnum*, the hidden spiritual knowledge of God’s nature and the origins of the universe</note>, or of the knowledge of God, and that you diligently take care to pick up some divine crumbs everywhere, having a great desire and hunger for them.

3. On my part, this makes me very joyful, that God does...

<meta>This page continues the author's reflection on the spiritual connection between believers, using the metaphor of branches on a tree (Christ) and the relationship between a mother and child. It follows a discussion of how God draws his children to Himself.</meta>

# The Second Epistle.

...thus draw and lead his children; as it is written: "Those who are driven by the spirit of God are the children of God." And just as one branch on a tree rejoices in the other and they mutually provide sap and assistance to one another, so also do the children of God in their Tree, *Jesus Christ*. At this, my humble person rejoices exceedingly: that God, from the fountain of his heart, draws us to himself as simple children of our Mother—even to the right breast and bosom of our Mother—so that we should long for him as children long for their mother.

4. And because (my beloved sir and brother in the love of Christ) I see and perceive that you thirst for the open wellspring of Christ, and likewise enjoy it according to the will of God, yet you still inquire after the joy of your brethren and desire (as a branch on the Tree) to mutually recreate, refresh, and satisfy yourself in them; it is also acceptable to me to impart my sap and my spirit—in the knowledge which <note>original: "God hath given me"</note> God has given me—unto my brethren and members, who are my fellow branches in the Tree, *Jesus Christ*. In this way, I rejoice in them, namely in their sap, power, and spirit; for it is the pleasant food of my soul to see that my fellow branches and members flourish in the Paradise of God.

5. But I will not conceal from you the simple, childlike way in which I walk in *Christ Jesus*; for I can write nothing of myself, except as a child who neither knows nor understands anything and has never been formally educated, but only that which the Lord deigns to know in me, according to the measure in which he manifests himself in me.

<margin>☞</margin>
6. For I never desired to know anything of the Divine Mystery, much less did I understand the way to seek or find it. I knew nothing of it, as is the condition of poor <term>Lay-men</term> <note>original: "Lay-men"; people without formal theological training or clerical status</note> in their simplicity. I sought only the heart of *Jesus Christ*, so that I might hide myself there from the wrathful anger of God and the violent assaults of the Devil. I earnestly besought the Lord for his Holy Spirit and his grace, that he would be pleased to bless and guide me in him, and take away from me whatever turned me away from him. I resigned myself wholly to him, so that I might not live according to my own will, but his, and so that he alone might lead and direct me, to the end that I might be his child in his Son, *Jesus Christ*.

7. In this, my earnest Christian seeking and desire—during which I suffered many a <note>original: "shrewd repulse"; a sharp, severe, or painful setback</note> harsh repulse, but finally resolved to put my life at the utmost risk rather than give up and leave off—the Gate was opened to me. In one quarter of an hour, I saw and knew more than if I had been for many years together at a University;

<summary>The author describes his spiritual journey, emphasizing that his deep theological insights came from a sudden divine revelation rather than formal education. He uses the metaphor of branches on a tree to describe the mutual spiritual nourishment shared between believers in Christ.</summary>

I knew and saw within myself all three worlds: namely, the divine, angelic, and paradisiacal <gloss>world</gloss>; then the dark world, being the origin of nature leading to the fire; and then thirdly, the eternal and visible world, being a procreation or external birth—or like a substance expressed or spoken forth from both the internal and spiritual worlds. And I saw and knew the whole being <gloss>or working essence</gloss> in both evil and good, and the mutual origin and existence of each of them; and likewise how the * pregnant mother brought them forth, so that I not only greatly wondered at it but also rejoiced exceedingly.

9. And immediately it came powerfully into my mind to set this down in writing as a memorial for myself; although I could hardly grasp it in my external self <note>original: external Man</note> and express it with the pen. Yet I had to begin working on these great mysteries like a child going to school. I saw it <gloss>as if in a great depth</gloss> internally, for I had a thorough view of the universe as if in a Chaos <note>here meaning a primordial state where all possibilities exist together</note>, wherein all things are contained and wrapped up, but it was impossible for me to explain and unfold it.

<margin>

☞
</margin>
10. Yet it opened itself within me over time, like a young plant; although this stayed with me for twelve years, and I was, as it were, † pregnant with it, and I felt a powerful drive and urge within me before I could bring it forth into an external form of writing. This later fell upon me like a sudden shower that hits whatever it lands on; that is exactly how it happened to me: whatever I could grasp and bring into the external <gloss>principle of my mind</gloss>, I wrote down.

11. However, afterward the sun shone on me for a good while, though not in a continuous or constant manner. For when it hid itself, I scarcely knew or understood my own work <gloss>or writings</gloss>. Thus, a person must acknowledge that his knowledge is not his own or from himself, but is God’s and from God; and that God knows <gloss>or manifests the ideas of His wisdom</gloss> in the soul of man in whatever manner and measure He pleases.

12. I intended to keep this writing with me all the days of my life and not deliver it into the hands of anyone; but it happened according
