<meta>The author continues describing the circumstances surrounding the publication of his first work, *Aurora*, and the spiritual struggle that followed its confiscation by church authorities.</meta>

<page-num>11</page-num>

# The Second Epistle.

According to the providence of the Most High, I entrusted a certain person with some of it; by which means it was published and made known without my knowledge or consent. As a result, the first book (called *Aurora* <note>Latin for "The Dawning of the Day," Boehme's first major work written in 1612.</note>) was <margin>* By Gregory Richter, Lord Primate of Görlitz.</margin> taken from me. Because many wonderful things were revealed within it (which the human mind was not immediately capable of comprehending), I was forced to suffer much from Reason <note>original: "Reason" here refers to worldly intellectualism or religious authorities who relied on logic rather than divine revelation.</note>.

13. I did not see this first book for another three years; I supposed that it was dead and gone, until certain learned men sent me some copies of it and exhorted me to proceed and demonstrate my talent. To this, my outward reason would by no means agree, because it had suffered so much already for it. Moreover, the spirit of reason was very weak and timorous, for my "High Light" <note>Boehme’s term for his moments of divine illumination.</note> was also withdrawn from me for a good while. It glowed within me like a hidden fire, so that I felt nothing but anguish and perplexity. Outwardly I found contempt, and inwardly a fiery instigation; yet I was not able to comprehend that light until the breath (or inspiration) of the Most High helped me to it again and awakened new life in me. Then I obtained a better style in writing, as well as a deeper and more grounded knowledge. I could express everything better in outward language, as the book treating of the <margin>† Or, According to.</margin> *Threefold Life* † by the "Three Principles" <note>Boehme's metaphysical system describing the dark world, the light world, and the external world.</note> demonstrates; and the godly reader, whose heart is opened, shall see that it is so.

14. Thus, I have written now, not from the instruction or knowledge received from men, nor from the learning or reading of books. I have written out of my own book which was opened within me—the noble likeness of God. The "Book of the Noble and Precious Image" (meaning God’s own similitude or likeness) was bestowed upon me to read. Therein I have studied, like a child in its mother's house who watches what the father does and, in his childlike play, imitates his father. I have no need of any other book.

<margin>Note what man can find in himself.</margin>

15. My book has only three leaves; these are the Three Principles of Eternity. Therein I can find all that Moses and the prophets, Christ and his apostles, have taught and spoken. I can find therein the foundation of the world and all mysteries; yet it is not I who does this, but the Spirit of God, according to the measure that pleases Him.

16. For I have besought and begged of Him many hundred times that if my knowledge did not serve His glory and lead to the <margin>* Bettering or benefit.</note> improvement and instruction of my brethren, He would be pleased to take it from me and preserve me only in His love. Yet I found that by my praying and earnest desiring, I only ignited...

<summary>Jacob Boehme recounts how his first book, *Aurora*, was confiscated by Gregory Richter but later returned to him by supporters, leading to a renewed and deeper spiritual inspiration. He emphasizes that his mystical knowledge comes not from human books, but from an internal "book" of divine likeness containing the three principles of eternity.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Aurora, Gregory Richter, Three Principles, Threefold Life, divine revelation, mysticism, Görlitz, spiritual illumination</keywords>

<page-num>22</page-num>
-> ### The Second Epistle. <-

the fire burned more strongly within me; and in such passion, knowledge, and revelation, I produced my writings.

17. Yet I did not intend to make myself known through them among the kinds of people I see reading them now; I still thought I was writing only for myself. Although the spirit of God, within the Mystery of God in my own spirit, showed me clearly enough what purpose it served, my outward human reason was always opposed to it—except sometimes when the "morning star" <note>A metaphor for spiritual enlightenment or the dawning of divine wisdom</note> arose. Even then, my reason was merely excited by it and danced along as if it had understood the "Pearl" <note>A reference to the "Pearl of Great Price" (Matthew 13:45-46), representing the kingdom of heaven or ultimate divine truth</note>, though it was actually far from understanding it.

<margin>☞</margin>
18. God dwells in the Noble Image <term>Noble Image: The divine spark or original spiritual form of humanity created in God's likeness</term>, but not in the spirit of the stars and elements; he possesses nothing but himself, in his own likeness. And although he possesses something (as indeed he possesses all things), nothing can grasp or understand him except that which originally arises and springs from him—namely, the soul in the likeness of God.

19. Furthermore, all my writings are like those of a young student who is just starting school. According to his will, God has brought my soul into a wonderful school; and in truth, I cannot claim or take credit for anything myself, as if my own "selfhood" <note>original: "selfehood"; the ego or independent human identity acting apart from God</note> were anything or understood anything.

<margin>☞</margin>
20. No one should think more highly of me than what he sees; for the work of my studying or writing is not my own. I have it only according to the measure that the Lord is pleased to give it to me. I am nothing but his instrument, through which he accomplishes what he wills. I tell you this, my beloved friends, for your instruction and information, so that no one should think of me as more than I am—namely, as if I were a man of high scholarship and deep intellectual understanding. For I live in weakness and infirmity, in the childhood and simplicity of Christ. My spiritual joy and recreation is in that childlike work which he has assigned to me. Indeed, I take delight in it, as if in a pleasure garden where many noble flowers grow; and in the meantime, I will enjoy and refresh myself with it until I once again obtain the Flower of Paradise in the "New Man" <term>New Man: The spiritually reborn person, as described in Pauline theology, who replaces the "Old Man" of fallen nature</term>.

21. But because, dear sir and beloved friend, I see and perceive that you are searching along this same path, I write to you diligently about my childlike journey. For I understand that you use various authors and writings, about which you desire my judgment. I shall share this with you as a fellow member of the body of Christ, as far as God has given me the knowledge, and only in a brief and short summary. In my book *The Threefold Life* <note>original: *The Threefold Life of Man*, one of Böhme's major works written around 1620</note>, you shall find it explained at length, according to all the details.

22. And this is the answer I give to you: namely, that "self-reason" <note>Human logic or intellect operating independently of divine inspiration</note> (which, being devoid of God’s spirit, is only taught and instructed

<meta>This page continues the author's critique of "Reason" (intellectualism without spiritual rebirth) and how it creates religious confusion and institutional dogmatism.</meta>

<page-num>23</page-num>
<header>The Second Epistle.</header>

...from the bare Letter) does cavil <note>original: "cavill" — to make petty or unnecessary objections</note>, taunt, deride, and despise whatever does not punctually agree and conform to the canons and institutions of the universities and high schools. I do not wonder at this, for such things are from without, while God’s spirit is from within. It is good and evil; it is like the wind, which is moved and driven to and fro. It <margin>* Highly prizes.</margin> *highly prizes* human judgment; and according to how the high and great ones—who have the respect and authority of the world—judge and censure, just so it gives its credit and verdict. It does not know the mind of the Lord, because the same is not in it; its understanding is from the stars, and it is nothing else but a counterfeit shadow of fancy in comparison to Divine wisdom.

<margin>☛</margin>
23. How can he judge Divine matters in whom the Spirit of the Lord is not? The Spirit of the Lord alone tries, proves, and judges all things, for to Him only all things are known and manifest. But *Reason* <note>Boehme often uses "Reason" to describe the fallen, human intellect that tries to understand God without spiritual enlightenment.</note> judges outwardly, and one *Reason* always squares its judgment and opinion according to another. The inferior judges and censures as his grand superior; the layman as the *Doctor* <note>original: "Doctor" — here meaning a Doctor of Divinity or a high-ranking academic theologian.</note>; and yet neither of them apprehend the sense, mind, and truth of the Lord without the Spirit of God, which judges in man and respects no man's person. The layman and the *Doctor* are both one to Him.

<margin>☛</margin>
24. Now, whereas the children of God have diverse and manifold gifts in writing, speaking, and judging, they do not all have the same manner of expression, phrase, and style. Whereupon self-*Reason* afterward uses artificial conclusions to draw out of them whatever serves its own purpose and frames a *Babel* <note>Babel: A reference to the biblical Tower of Babel, used here to represent religious confusion, sectarianism, and the "clutter" of human opinions.</note> for itself. From this, such a multitude and wearisome heap of opinions has risen, so that men have forged and invented diverse conjectures and ways unto God out of their writings. Men must then be forced to walk in those ways, which gives rise to such controversies and un-Christian contentions. For the present, men look only upon the strife of words and disputes about the Letter <note>The literal text of Scripture, as opposed to its spiritual meaning.</note>. Those who, according to their *Reason* and principles, overcome others by verbal jangling and exchanging one Scripture for another are applauded. But this is nothing but *Babel*, a mother of spiritual whoredom, where *Reason* does not enter in at the door of *Christ* through *Christ's* spirit, but presses in of itself and climbs up by its own might, strength, and pride. Being yet a stranger or unregenerated, it would always gladly be the fairest child in the house; men must honor and adore it.

<margin>☛</margin>
25. The children of God have a diversity of gifts, according to the rule of the Apostle; God gives an expression to every one as He pleases. The gifts and endowments of men fall out according to the...

<summary>Jacob Boehme argues that academic theology and human reason alone cannot judge divine matters, as they lead to sectarian confusion ("Babel") rather than spiritual truth. He emphasizes that the Spirit of God, rather than human authority or "verbal jangling," is the only true judge of Scripture.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's exploration of how the Holy Spirit interacts with the human soul, specifically focusing on the concept of spiritual "constellations" and the unique way God works through individuals. The previous page established that all things arise from a single divine source.</meta>

<page-num>24</page-num>

-># The Second Epistle<-

...the unsearchable will of God, and spring altogether out of one Root; which is the Mother of the Three Principles <note>original: "Three Principles," a central concept in Jakob Böhme’s theology referring to three "worlds" or realms: the dark fire-world (the Father), the light love-world (the Son), and the external created world (the Holy Spirit in nature)</note>. And just as the spirit of every soul is "constellated" <note>original: "Constellated," meaning the soul's internal spiritual arrangement or temperament is shaped by eternal forces, much like physical stars were thought to influence destiny</note> in the eternal Mother, even so is its revelation, understanding, and knowledge.

<margin>☞</margin>
26. For God does not bring a new or foreign spirit into us; rather, He opens our spirit with His Spirit—specifically, the Mystery of God's Wisdom, which lies within every person according to the measure, manner, and condition of their internal, hidden constellation. For Christ said, *My Father works, and I also work*. Now the Father works in the essence of the soul's character <note>original: "property"</note>, and the Son works in the essence of God’s own image—that is, in the Divine Likeness <note>original: "Similitude"</note> or Harmony.

27. The character of the soul belongs to the Father; for Christ said, *Father, the men were yours, and You have given them to Me, and I give to them eternal life*. Since, then, the character of the soul is from eternity—belonging to and coming from the Father—He has therefore worked in it from eternity and still works in that same image toward eternity: in light and darkness, to either of which the *will* of the soul's character inclines and surrenders itself.

28. Seeing then that the Father’s character or wisdom is immeasurable and infinite, and that He, being Wisdom itself, works—and yet through His wisdom all things arise—it follows that the souls of men are variously constellated. Indeed, they arise and originally proceed out of one single Essence, yet their operation is diverse and manifold, all according to God's wisdom. Now the Spirit of Christ opens the character of every soul, so that each speaks from its own nature about the wonders in the wisdom of God.

<margin>☞</margin>
29. For the Spirit of God makes no new thing in man, nor does He infuse a foreign spirit into him. Instead, He speaks of the wonders in the wisdom of God through man—and that not only from the eternal constellation, but likewise from the external constellation. That is, through the spirit of the external world, He opens in man the internal constellation of the soul, so that he must prophesy and foretell what the external heaven performs and produces. He is also driven to speak through the *Turba Magna* <note>original: "Turba Magna," Latin for "Great Turmoil," referring to the disruptive, chaotic force in nature and humanity triggered by sin and the wrath of God</note>, just as the Prophets many times spoke and denounced to the people the punishment which, by God's permission through the *Turba Magna*, should come upon them for their violence and sinfulness. [And their bitter curses, wicked arguments, and wrathful indignation in their envious will against one another awaken the Sword of Anger in the *Turba Magna*.]

<margin>☞</margin>
30. Now the Spirit of God speaks in His children in various ways; sometimes in one person it speaks by the internal and eternal—

<summary>This page discusses how the Holy Spirit works through the pre-existing "constellation" or spiritual makeup of the human soul to reveal divine wisdom. It explains that while all souls originate from one essence, they manifest God's wonders differently based on their unique character and their response to both eternal and external influences.</summary>

<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Three Principles, constellation, soul, Turba Magna, divine wisdom, prophecy, internal vs external world</keywords>

--- Page 143 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how spiritual knowledge is revealed through different "constellations" or spiritual alignments within the human soul.</meta>

<page-num>29</page-num>
# The Second Epistle.

<margin>☞</margin> the eternal constellation of the soul, concerning eternal punishment or reward, and God’s curse or blessing. In another sense, it speaks through the external constellation <note>original: "Constellation"; here referring to the spiritual and astrological alignment of the stars and planets as they influence human life and the material world</note> regarding the fortune or misfortune, the prosperity or adversity of this world; it also speaks of the rise and advancement of powers and authorities, and likewise of the ruin and destruction of countries and cities, as well as strange and wonderful alterations in the world.

31. And though it often happens that the spirit of the outward world plays with its own imaginative representations within man, and—by its own power and strong influence—insinuates itself into the human spirit to show various strange and marvelous figures. This only finds a place among those who rely solely on their own reason and proud self-will; it is from such people that <margin>☜</margin> false prophets arise. Yet I say that everyone speaks from their own constellation: one speaks truly and sincerely through the manifestation of God’s Spirit, while another speaks uncertainly through the manifestation of the external *astral* <term>astral: relating to the stars or the celestial spirit that influences the physical world</term> spirit, relying on conjecture and guesswork. Both speak from the same constellation, but he who speaks from the mouth of another and likewise judges the *Mystery* <note>original: "Mystery"; referring to the "Mysterium Magnum," the hidden spiritual truths of God and the cosmos</note> without personal knowledge is in Babel <note>original: "Babel"; a state of spiritual confusion, linguistic division, and false religious systems</note> and is entangled in opinion. He willfully occupies himself with things that his heart has not experienced as true; instead, [ he pins *his faith upon the sayings of other men.* ]

32. And I say further, that all those precious men who have been illuminated by God (some of whose writings you may have at hand) have spoken from their own manifestation and revelation. Each spoke according to his understanding or the measure of his capacity; yet the center is the soul, and the light is God. Revelation is achieved and brought to pass by the opening or manifestation of God’s Spirit through the constellation of the soul.

<margin>☞</margin>33. All the prophets since the beginning of the world have prophesied about Christ in different forms—one in this manner, and another in that. They have not all concurred in one style, phrase, and form; instead, each spoke as the Spirit of God revealed it to him in the eternal constellation of the soul. Yet they have all spoken from one center and ground. And it is even so nowadays: the children of God all speak from the revelation of Christ’s Spirit, which is God’s; and everyone speaks according to his capacity, or that idea of wisdom which is formed in his mind. Therefore, I remind you as a friend and urge you not to listen to the vain babbling and chatter of human reason. Do not be moved by the proud censure and judgment of that reason, so as to condemn or despise any man's gifts; for he who does so condemns the Spirit of God.

<sig>E</sig>
<meta>catchword: 34. These</meta>

<summary>The author explains that spiritual revelation varies based on an individual's "constellation" or internal spiritual alignment, distinguishing between divine truth and the confusion of "Babel." He argues that while prophets and believers may use different styles and capacities, they all speak from the same divine center if they are truly illuminated by Christ's Spirit.</summary>

<page-num>26</page-num>
<header>The Second Epistle.</header>
<meta>This page continues the author's response regarding his judgment of other writers and the nature of spiritual unity.</meta>

<margin>☛</margin>
34. Regarding these authors whom you mention, and others besides (about whom you desire my judgment, and whom I have not read entirely but only in part), I have no desire to judge or despise them—God forbid! Far be that from me, even though they have not all written in the same style and form of expression. For knowledge is diverse and manifold. Yet it behooves me to test (according to my gifts) their heart and will. But since I find that their heart and spirit flow and spring from one and the same Center—namely, from the spirit of Christ—I therefore rest content on that Center. I leave their expression to the *Highest Tongue*—that is, to the spirit of God’s wisdom—which opens and reveals to everyone through wisdom in the measure and manner that He pleases.

35. I judge no one, for to condemn anyone is a false and idle arrogance, a vain chattering. The spirit of God Himself judges all things. If that spirit is in us, why do we need to worry about such chatter? I much rather rejoice in the gifts of my brothers. If they have had a different manner of gifts to present than I, should I judge them for it?

36. Does any herb, flower, or tree say to another, "You are sour and dark; I will not stand by you"? Do they not all have one Mother <note>Referring to the Earth or Nature as the common source of life</note> from which they grow? In the same way, all souls proceed from One, and all men from one. Why then do we boast and glory in being the children of God, while we are more unwise than the flowers and herbs of the field? Is it not so with us? Does God not impart and reveal His wisdom to us in different ways? Just as He brings forth and manifests the *Tincture* <note>original: "Tincture"; in the author's mystical vocabulary, this refers to a spiritual essence or the "soul" of a substance that has transforming power</note> of the *Mystery* <note>original: "Mystery"; likely referring to the *Mysterium Magnum*, the hidden, chaotic source of all manifestation</note> in the earth through the soil with beautiful plants, so He does *in us humans*. We should rather congratulate and heartily love one another because God reveals His wisdom so variously *in us*. But he who judges, condemns, and despises in a wicked way only acts out of pride to show himself off and be seen; he is the *Oppressor in Babel* <note>A reference to the biblical Tower of Babel, symbolizing spiritual confusion, sectarian division, and the "city of pride"</note>, a perverse meddler <note>original: "stickler"; here meaning someone who stirs up unnecessary contention or insists on petty points</note> who incites conflict and strife.

<margin>☛</margin>
37. The true test <note>original: "Tryall"</note> of God’s children is this, which we may securely and safely follow: namely, an humble heart that neither seeks nor honors itself, but continually seeks the good of his brother in love; that does not seek its own profit, pleasure, and applause, but seeks after righteousness and the fear of God. The plain and simple way to come to God—so far as it has been made known to me—is this: that a person departs from their sinful ways and makes an earnest, constant resolution never to continue in those sins they have committed; and in forsaking and turning away from them, they must not despair or doubt God’s grace.

<summary>The author argues against judging other spiritual writers, emphasizing that diverse expressions of faith often spring from the same divine source. He defines the true mark of a believer as humility, love for others, and a sincere turning away from sin.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, spiritual unity, religious tolerance, humility, Tincture, Mystery, Babel, repentance, grace</keywords>

--- Page 145 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's spiritual advice, transitioning from a discussion of divine judgment to the necessity of child-like surrender to God's will.</meta>

<page-num>29</page-num>
<header>The Second Epistle.</header>

<margin>☛</margin>
38. **And although reason suggests doubts (by which a sinner is terrified, and stands amazed and astonished at the anger of God),** yet let the will only, in all simplicity and unfeigned sincerity, directly cast itself into the mercy of God. Let it wholly lie down and shelter itself in the suffering and death of Christ, and surrender itself to God through Christ; like a child that betakes itself to the lap of its mother, wishing to do only that which is the mother’s will. It only cries and calls to the mother; it always hopes to receive its refreshment from the mother, and it only longs for the mother’s breasts. Even so must our desire be wholly and only turned and directed to our first Mother, from whom we, in *Adam*, departed when we entered into *Self-Will*.

39. Therefore **Christ** says, *Unless you are converted and become as little children, you cannot see the Kingdom of God*. Also, you must be born again (that is, we must wholly reject and depart from our own reason, and return again into resignation <note>original: "resignation [and selfe-denyall]"; likely referring to the German concept of Gelassenheit, or yielding one's will to God</note> in the bosom of our Mother, and give up all disputing; and, as <margin>☛</margin> it were, dull or kill our reason) so that the spirit of the Mother [*that is, the Eternal Word of God*] may take shape in us. It must blow upon or ignite the divine life in us, so that we may find ourselves in the spirit of the Mother, in the cradle, if we desire to be taught and led by God.

40. And if we will be taught and led by God, then we must arise again from the cradle and wholly submit and give ourselves up to him, so that God’s spirit may be in us *entirely both the will and the deed*. We must acknowledge the knowledge to be his and not ours, so that he alone may be our *knowing*.

<margin>☛</margin>
41. **We must take no thought or anxious care about what we are to know and how we will know, but we must simply enter into the incarnation and birth of Jesus Christ, and into his suffering and death. We must continually, with all willingness, tread in his footsteps and follow him, thinking that we are here only upon our pilgrim's path, where we must walk through a dangerous way to enter again, through Christ, on the narrow path into our native country from which Adam has led us astray. In this way alone lies the *Pearl* of the *Great Mystery* <note>original: Mysterium magnum</note>. All studying, book-reading, seeking, searching, and grounding ourselves on accepted principles or conventional views besides and without this way are but dead means. They do not obtain the *Virgin's Crown* <note>original: "Virgins Crowne [or the Pearle of Sophia]"; Sophia represents Divine Wisdom</note>, but instead gather together heaps of thistles and thorns which sting and irritate the children of God.**

42. Therefore, dear sir, since you have desired my knowledge and judgment, I have no better counsel and advice to give to you than to show you the way in which I myself walk; and

<sig>E 2</sig> <meta>The text continues on the next page with the word "upon".</meta>

<summary>The author argues that true spiritual knowledge comes not from intellectual study or human reason, but through child-like surrender to God and following the path of Christ's suffering. ...upon which the gate was opened to me, so that I have become learned without prior study; for all arts and sciences come from God, who finds all things [in and for humanity].

<margin>* Note this carefully.</margin>
43. I have no controversy with the children of God because of the variety and diversity of their gifts; I can reconcile them all within myself [I can interpret and understand them clearly for myself]. I only bring them to the center, and there I have the proof and touchstone of all things. Now then, if you will imitate and follow me, you shall find it to be so by experience, and afterward perhaps better understand what I have written.

44. A real, true Christian has no controversy or contention with anyone, for through <term>resignation in Christ</term> <note>original: "resignation in Christ"; the practice of surrendering one's will entirely to God</note>, he dies to all controversy and strife. He no longer asks about the way to God but instead wholly surrenders himself to the mother—namely, the Spirit of Christ. Whatever that Spirit does with him is all the same to him; whether it is prosperity or adversity in this world, life or death, it is all the same to him. No adversity or calamity reaches the **new man**, but only the "old man" of this world. The world may do what it pleases with that old man, for it belongs to the world; but the **new man** belongs to God.

45. This is my way, my dear friend, in which I walk and in which I must know things without prior knowledge. I do not plan, premeditate, or muse beforehand on what I am to write or speak; instead, I submit and resign myself to the knowledge of God, so that He may know through me whatever He pleases. In this way, I have obtained a <term>pearl</term> <note>original: "Pearle"; likely a reference to the biblical "pearl of great price," representing spiritual truth or the kingdom of God</note> which I value more highly than the entire external world.

<margin>* Note.</margin>
46. And though it often happens that the children of God are at odds with one another [or clash together] in their knowledge, this proceeds only from the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: "Turba"; a term used by Boehme to describe the source of spiritual disturbance, strife, or the "awakened wrath" within nature and reason</note> of external reason, which is in all people. God permits this so that people might be tested and exercised, so that by praying and pressing toward God, they might more earnestly and fervently kindle their spirit. Then the Spirit of God arises in the mystery of <term>humanity</term> <note>original: "Humanity"; here referring to the inner spiritual nature of man in union with Christ</note> like a burning and shining fire; and all things must serve for the best for the children of God.

47. But concerning some people in your neighborhood whom you mentioned, who sell everything they have and run to a <term>supposed Zion</term> <note>original: "Supposed Zion"; referring to those seeking a physical holy community or utopian colony</note>, I would advise them that it is better to stay at home; for Zion must be conceived and born within us. When they arrive at that place, they will find themselves the same as they were before; they must still live under the yoke of Christ regardless of where they are.

48. God is in heaven, and heaven is in man; and if a person desires to be in heaven, then heaven must be manifested and revealed...

<summary>The author argues that true spiritual knowledge comes through total surrender to Christ rather than intellectual study or physical pilgrimage. He explains that religious conflicts arise from human reason (the Turba) and that "Zion" must be found internally rather than in a physical location.</summary>

That which they think to run from, they are likely to run into; it would be more acceptable to God to walk at home in a godly, divine way, so that others might take example by them.

49. There are among them arrogant, proud, scornful, and deriding people who do nothing but condemn and despise others; and in many of them, it is only a received form and custom, and a spiritual pride or selfish Pharisaical devotion, as I myself can speak by experience. For I, in a Christian, brotherly, and friendly manner, besought and admonished one of them because of a book which he put forth, wherein I found some points of great importance against God and the ground of truth. I hoped that he would become seeing, but he answered in a proud, contemptuous, and slanderous manner, and gave forth such an answer wherein there were no characters or prints of God's spirit to be seen. Their *confession* [*of faith*] is rather an *opinion* than a true and sincere earnestness, for not all of them are what they boast and glory to be. There may be many honest hearts among them, but many of them are only historical and *titular* <note>original: "Titular"; meaning they hold the title of a believer in name only, without the internal reality.</note>, desiring only to show themselves and to be applauded, as I myself had experience of one of the chiefest among them. They may learn at home to despise other men [*without running to an outward, supposed Zion* <note>Zion: Here referring to a specific religious community or "holy" place people would travel to in hopes of finding salvation.</note>].

<margin>\* To revoke, or recall themselves.</margin>
50. It is the way of the children into God's kingdom, and moreover their way is \* *Revoca* <term>Revoca: Latin for "recall" or "call back"; a command to turn back toward God through self-reflection.</term>; and this they themselves make a show of, but privately they are as they were before. I wish to God they were as earnest as they pretend and claim to be, and then I would commend it as well; but to slander, condemn, and despise others is nothing else but *Babel* <note>Babel: Symbolizing confusion, division, and the prideful systems of the world.</note>. The world is already full of such people; I do not follow after such as those.

<margin>\* Hans Weyrauck.</margin>
51. Concerning \* John Weyrack <note>Also known as Hans Weyrauck, a contemporary writer and acquaintance of the author.</note>, so far as I can see by these writings of his, he may be one who walks in the love of God, provided his way is held in the real sincerity of the heart. But the fact that he taunts and dispraises others because of their knowledge of the light of nature <term>Light of nature: A mystical concept referring to the innate understanding of the natural world and its divine signatures.</term> shows that he has no knowledge in that area, and his gifts do not reach that far. And because he has no such gift, we must overlook it; yet for all this, we should esteem him as a true and honest brother. For God produces his gifts not only in simplicity, but in many in a high strain [or in a deep-grounded understanding or magical meaning <note>Magical: In this context, it refers to the spiritual and transformative power of divine wisdom, not stage magic.</note>]; for He alone is high, and He orders and directs all His works as He pleases.

52. In like manner, I answer to the rest of the authors whom you mention, some of whom were endowed with high gifts, but they

<margin>☞</margin>...were not sufficiently capable to comprehend everything; yet for their time, they have done enough. But because this present time has need of another *medicine* <note>original: "Medicine." In this context, it refers to a spiritual remedy or a new way of explaining divine truth to fit the current era's needs.</note>; therefore at this time also there are found other skillful, understanding knowers, and people who show the disease. All of this is according to God’s loving providential care, who does not want anyone to *perish*, but that all people should be *helped and cured*.

53. If the same authors were alive today, it is possible they might have written more clearly on some points and in a different form; although for their time they have done enough, and they are in no way to be despised or rejected, even though some points might be amended. But their doctrine concerning the *union of the Deity and Humanity is very clear*; and we may see how God's spirit has been in them. However, Reason turns all things to the worst; and by its false expositions and logical explanations <note>original: "glosses"</note>, it twists them into a perverse sense.

<margin>* Schwenckfeld <note>Referring to Caspar Schwenckfeld (1489–1561), a German theologian who emphasized the "inner word" and spiritual experience over external sacraments.</note></margin>
<margin>☞</margin>54. Schwenckfeld stumbles at this point, in that he holds Christ to be no <term>creature</term> <note>original: "Creature." In the 17th century, this meant any "created thing," including humans and the physical world, as opposed to the uncreated God.</note>. He has not yet understood the principles, and therefore it is impossible for him to distinguish how and in what way He is no creature. For in respect to the *Deity* (His divine nature), He is no creature; but in respect to the *Heavenly Essence* (*concerning which He said that He had come from heaven and was also in heaven*), He is <term>creatural</term> <note>original: "creaturall"; meaning He took on a created, limited form</note> within His humanity, and <term>uncreatural</term> <note>meaning uncreated, infinite, or existing as pure divine essence</note> outside of His humanity.

55. Just as we humans live in the four elements, and we ourselves are of the nature <note>original: "property"</note> of the four elements, and they are creatural within us; so, outside of us, they are uncreatural. Yet the unformed, uncreated elements outside of us in which we live, and the formed, created elements within us are but one thing; and so it is in the person of Christ.

<margin>☞</margin>56. The whole Angelical World (which is the <term>second Principle</term>) <note>In Böhme’s philosophy, the "Second Principle" is the world of light, love, and the divine kingdom.</note> is His bodily being or personal essence. As to the heavenly essentiality in the person of His humanity, it is *creatural* (limited to a created form), and outside of that Person, it is uncreatural. For He is the Father’s Heart and Word, and the heart is everywhere in the *Father*; so that where His heart is, there also is Heaven, and the divine essence surrounded by the complete fullness of Wisdom.

57. Concerning His soul, which He commended into His Father’s hands, and of which He said upon the Mount of Olives *that it was afflicted and heavy, even unto death* (Matthew 26:38); this soul also has the nature of our soul. For it was for the soul’s sake that God became man—so that He might bring the soul back into Himself and draw our will back to Him out of its earthly state. This soul is a creature.

58. And the <term>third Principle</term> (which is the external kingdom <note>The "Third Principle" in Böhme’s system represents the physical, visible world.</note>...)

<meta>The text ends mid-sentence; the catchword is "of."</meta>

He specifically critiques the theological views of Caspar Schwenckfeld on this point.</summary>

...of this world, which God through his wisdom has brought forth out of eternity) is also creaturely <note>original: "creaturall"; meaning relating to a created being rather than the Creator</note> in him; for the whole Deity has manifested itself in the man Christ. **Namely** <note>original: "Viz."</note>: that as God is all in the spirit, so likewise he is all in this man. We men are likewise even so, if we are born again of God; and this point (which exercises and troubles almost all others) may be easily amended and rectified. If it were well considered, there would not be so much condemning and contending; the spirit of God does not care for any controversy; he judges all things in himself.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* *Weigel* <note>original: "Weigelius"; referring to Valentin Weigel (1533–1588), a German theologian whose mystical writings influenced later thinkers like Boehme.</note></margin>
59. Also, *Weigel* writes that Mary is not the daughter of *Joachim* and *Anna*, and that Christ assumed nothing from us, but that she is an eternal Virgin. This indeed is true in respect to the mark or sign of the Covenant, according to the Virgin of the divine wisdom. But what would this profit me? <margin>☞</margin> What would become of my soul and my heavenly essentiality—which disappeared <note>i.e., was lost or faded</note> in Adam [which is the Paradisiacal Image]—if Christ had not assumed upon himself the essence of our soul and brought the disappeared image back to life? This is set forth at large in my book *The Threefold Life*.

60. Except for this, *Weigel* also writes very well, as we do, concerning the new birth and the union of humanity in Christ. I omit speaking of that here because I have written clearly and precisely about it; I neither condemn nor despise his writings, nor those who read them.

<margin>{</margin>
61. Does not a bee gather honey out of diverse flowers? And though one flower is better than another, yet she does not stop at that but takes what serves her purpose. If the sap and virtue of the flower does not please her, should she therefore thrust her sting into it, as the despiser and mocker usually does? <margin>☞</margin> Men contend and argue much about the shell [or the outside of knowledge and religion] but disregard the precious sap [of love and faith] which serves and leads to life.

62. What good does knowledge do for me if I do not live in and according to it? The knowing, as well as the will and the actual performance of the same, must be in me. The mantle of Christ’s suffering and satisfaction, which people now usually wrap around themselves, shall become a snare and hellish fire to many; for they only want to tickle and flatter themselves with the merits and satisfaction of Christ while still keeping their cunning hypocrisy and wickedness.

63. It is said, "*You must be born again, otherwise you shall not see the kingdom of God. You must become like children if you wish to see the kingdom of God.*" It is not enough only to contend and dispute about knowledge [and opinions], but you must become a new man [a new...

creature] who lives in God in righteousness and holiness; the wicked one must be cast out, and Christ must be put on. Then we are buried in his death, in and with him, and arise again with him and live eternally in him. What need do I then have to contend and wrangle about that which I myself am (which I have essentially in me, and of which no man can deprive me)?

64. I am at odds with no one, except only against the wicked, and the spirit rebukes him to his face. I desire to let you know this, and my intent is sincere and upright toward you.

65. As for my books, you may easily get them (I suppose) if you have a mind for them. For *Christianus Bernard*, a customs official <note>original: "Customer"</note> at *Zagan*, informs <note>original: "doth certifie"</note> me that he has lent two of them (namely, the book of the *Threefold Life*, which is the most important for teaching, and the *Forty Questions Concerning the Soul*) to your butler’s brother. If you make him aware of this, he will not deny you; but if not, then I will help you get them in another way. You may also get them from Mr. *Christianus Bernard* if you request them from him and you cannot get them closer at hand; I will write to him so that he shall lend them to you, for I seldom have my own at home. Yet, in case you do not get them, I will lend them to you one after another as soon as I can get them home.

66. The several books and their titles are these: the first book, called *Aurora*, climbs up out of infancy and shows you the creation of all beings; yet it is very mysterious and not sufficiently explained. It is of much and deep magical [cabalistic] or parabolic understanding or meaning, for there are many mysteries within it that are yet to come to pass.

67. The second is a large book of a hundred sheets; it treats the *Three Principles of the Divine Essence* and the Being of all beings. The same is a key and an alphabet for all those who desire to understand my writings. It treats the creation, as well as the eternal birth or generation of the Deity, repentance, the justification of man, and his paradisiacal life. It also covers the Fall, then the new birth, the Testaments of Christ, and the total salvation of man. It is very profitable to read, for it is an eye through which to know the wonders in the mystery of God.

<margin>* Constellation, inclination, disposition, complexion, profession, and condition.</margin>

68. Thirdly, a book of the *Threefold Life*; this has sixty sheets. It is a key for things above and below to all mysteries—to whatever the mind is able to think upon, or wherever the heart is able to turn and move itself. It shows the whole foundation of the three principles and serves everyone according to his *property* <note>original: "property," here meaning one's specific spiritual nature or "signature"</note>. In it, he may plumb the depth and find the resolution of all questions that reason is able to devise and propose. It is the most necessary book to serve your needs; you would soon be

<page-num>33</page-num>

...weary of all contentious books, if you entertain and take that into your mind.

69. Fourthly, the *Forty Questions about the Soul*; it consists of twenty-eight sheets <note>A "sheet" refers to the printer's quires; this gives the reader a sense of the book's length.</note> and treats all things that are necessary for a person to know.

70. The fifth book has three parts. The first part concerns the *Incarnation of Christ*. The second part is very deep and profound, treating Christ’s Passion, suffering, and death—how we must enter into Christ’s death and both die and arise again in and with Him, and why Christ had to die. It is entirely brought forth, expanded, and confirmed out of the Center through the three Principles <term>Böhme’s "three Principles" are the Dark World (divine wrath/fire), the Light World (divine love/majesty), and the Outward World (the visible universe).</term> in a very deep way. The third part is the *Tree of Christian Faith*, also demonstrated through the three Principles, which is very profitable to read.

71. The sixth book, or part of these writings, contains the *Six Points*, treating the greatest depths and secrets. Namely: how the three Principles mutually beget, bring forth, and support each other so that in Eternity there is no strife or mutual enmity between them, yet each Principle exists within itself according to its own property as if it were alone. They show where strife and disunity arise, and where Good and Evil have their origin, derived entirely from the "ground"—that is, out of the Nothing into the Something—and all within the ground and center of Nature. This sixth book is such a mystery (even though it is brought to light with plainness and simplicity) that no human reason or natural, astral intellect <note>original: "naturall, Astrall head-peece." This refers to an intellect governed by the stars and the physical world rather than divine illumination.</note>, no matter how sharp or academically learned, can sound, fathom, or understand it without the light of God. It is the key to all.

72. Seventhly, a small book for the Melancholy <term>In the 17th century, "melancholy" described a state of spiritual despair or clinical depression, often thought to have both physical and supernatural causes.</term>, written for those tempted and afflicted in spirit, showing where sadness and dejection of soul come from, and how they may be resisted and remedied.

73. Eighthly, a very deep book, *De signatura rerum* <note>Latin: "The Signature of All Things"</note>, regarding the meaning of the various forms and shapes in Creation. It shows the beginning, ruin, and cure of every thing. This enters entirely into Eternal Nature and then into Temporal, beginning <note>original: "inchoative"</note>, and external Nature and its form.

74. These are my books, besides some small treatises I have given here and there, of which I have kept no copy; for I have no need of them for myself, as I have enough in my "three leaves." <note>Böhme frequently used the metaphor of "three leaves" to describe the sources of his divine insight: the depths of God, the kingdom of heaven/hell, and the physical world.</note>

75. If my circumstances permit (for I must often travel because of my business), then I will visit you myself as soon as I come that way. It was my full intent to have seen you at

<meta>catchword: Weyko</meta>

He emphasizes that his deep spiritual insights require divine light to be understood, rather than mere academic learning.</summary>

...weeks after Easter, but God disposed it otherwise. By His providence, I met another man who led me away from my intended path to someone who had need of me; so that afterward I understood that my journey was directed by the Lord.

76. Mr. Balthasar Walter stayed this past winter and spring with Prince Augustus of Anhalt <note>original: "Tanbalt"; specifically Prince Augustus of Anhalt-Plötzkau</note> at Plötzkau <note>original: "Peltska"</note>, and has written to me from there. Now he is with the Earl of Gleichen <note>original: "Gleyken"</note>, three miles from Erfurt <note>original: "Erford"</note>; he is the Earl’s physician and is to stay with him for a whole year.

<margin>* Ezekiel Meth.</margin>
77. * Ezekiel Meth is also at the same Court, yet they are not both of one mind, as Balthasar’s letter—which I received three weeks ago—shows. If you have a desire to write and no messenger is traveling this way, please send your letters to Christianus Bernard, a receiver <term>a financial officer or tax collector</term> at Żagań <note>original: "Zagan"</note>; I have the opportunity to send to him weekly. He is a pious Christian companion.

78. If you find anything in my writings that is too difficult or obscure to be understood, please write it down and let me know. I will make it clearer for you so that you may understand it. I have written nothing for the "wise" and "fully taught" who are proud and promote themselves in their own knowledge—those who think they can walk alone and are already "rich" in wisdom. I have written only for the "babes and sucklings" who nurse at their mother’s breasts and are eager to learn.

79. Let him who can understand it, understand it; but let him who cannot, not criticize or quibble over it. I have written nothing for such critics and mockers; I have written for myself.

<margin>80.</margin>
80. But if a brother thirsts and asks water of me, I give him to drink. He shall find and feel through experience what I have given him, if the Lord grants him the grace to drink. I commend myself to your favor, and all of us into the pleasant and gracious love of Jesus Christ.

<header>The Third Epistle</header>

# A Letter to
# ABRAHAM OF SOMMERFELD,
## Concerning the Book
# AURORA:

---

## ALSO,
### A Description of his process, and the excellence and surpassing Virtue of
### *Sophia's Pearl.* <note>original: "Sophia's Pearle." In Jacob Boehme’s mystical theology, Sophia represents Divine Wisdom, and the "pearl" refers to the precious inner spiritual enlightenment or the "Pearl of Great Price" mentioned in the Gospels.</note>

---

> ***Light, Salvation, and Eternal Power flowing from the wellspring of life, Jesus Christ, be our refreshment and comfort.***

<margin>1.</margin> **N**OBLE LORD, (first wishing to you the grace of God, and all health and happiness) being informed that you bear a great delight, love, and affection for my writings, which until now have been unknown to you; I must answer you, that the same is likewise a much greater delight and surpassing joy in my spirit. This is because I understand that God drives and carries on his work in such great and high-ranking persons; this is not a thing commonly to be found in the world, for the temporal honor and pleasure of this life is often a hindrance to it.

<margin>2.</margin> But I can very well perceive in what manner God’s spirit has touched and awakened your noble heart, in token of which you have bestowed so much effort and expense upon this work. This work was written by a very simple and plain hand, without any art or great understanding [or large capacity in literal <term>literal endowments: formal education, academic training, or scholarly learning</term> endowments] but only in the knowledge and manifestation of the gifts of God; and more—

<sig>F 2</sig>
<meta>The text ends mid-sentence with the catchword "over," indicating continuity with the following page.</meta>

<summary>In this introductory letter, the mystic Jacob Boehme addresses the nobleman Abraham of Sommerfeld, expressing joy that a person of high social standing has taken an interest in his spiritual writings. Boehme emphasizes that his work "Aurora" was produced through divine inspiration rather than formal academic learning.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Abraham of Sommerfeld, Aurora, Sophia's Pearl, Divine Wisdom, spiritual enlightenment, mysticism, 17th-century theology</keywords>

--- Page 154 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of his writing process and his humble origins, moving from his personal motivations to the spiritual origins of his first book, Aurora.</meta>

<page-num>36</page-num>

### The Third Epistle

...moreover, it was not the author’s intent that it should come into the hands of such high-ranking persons. He wrote it only as a memorial to himself, to stir and rouse himself up from the dark and drowsy sleep of flesh and blood; he did not intend to create such a formal work.

<margin>☞</margin>
3. Indeed, there was a fiery instigation <note>original: "instigation"; a powerful inner prompting or driving force.</note>, but without any foreknowledge of this work. It lay hidden in him as a **mystery** which the spirit of **God** stirred up and awakened. At that point, a great longing and desire to write arose. Yet, in terms of his outward, natural self, the author had no desire, capacity, fitness, or ability for it. He sought only after the heart of **God**, wishing to hide himself there from the **storm** and raging **tempest** of the **Devil**.

4. He considered the nature of evil and its influences, and often the deceit of the **Devil** and the anger of **God**, as well as the love and mercy of **God**. Indeed, many a storm and strong encounter was held against Reason, as well as against flesh, blood, and the **Devil**. All of this happened under a powerful driving and prompting of the spirit, until at last a most precious **garland** or **diadem** <note>Boehme uses these terms to describe a state of spiritual enlightenment or divine favor.</note> was set upon his head. This hand cannot describe that experience in writing; rather, I wish that the reader of this *Epistle* might find it through their own experience. Then they would understand what the sweetness of **God** is, and would not marvel so much why a layman <note>original: "Lay-man"; a person who is not a member of the clergy or a formally trained scholar.</note> should undertake to meddle with such things [or write of such *mysteries*].

5. Therefore, I say now that when the precious grain of **mustard seed** <note>A biblical reference to Matthew 13:31, where the kingdom of heaven is compared to a small seed that grows into a large tree.</note> was sown, this work was brought forth to be written. It was then beheld as a **mystery** couched very deep, without a full understanding, yet with exceeding joy. As this work (being my first book) shows, the great **mysteries** are set down very simply, without sufficient explanation or expression. It is full of abbreviations and defects, like a sudden flower that passes by; whatever the spirit of **wonders** lands upon, it strikes.

<margin>☞</margin>
6. For the author was an uneducated man of very small understanding and shallow capacity compared to the learned, skillful, and expert. Indeed, he was a mere **child** in these **mysteries**. He did not even understand the way he should walk or what might befall him, except for what the spirit indicated and declared to him. He even set down in writing the persecution, disgrace, and ignominy that would fall upon him before it actually happened or before **reason** felt or knew of it through experience. He saw it as clearly as if it were already present, as can be seen in the book *Aurora*, which is the first part of my writings, composed before my persecution. Now it is a comfort to me that...

<summary>In this passage, Jacob Boehme describes the spiritual compulsion behind his writing, characterizing himself as an uneducated layman guided by divine inspiration rather than scholarly ambition. He reflects on the "mysteries" revealed in his first book, Aurora, including the accurate prediction of his own future persecution.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Aurora, mysticism, spiritual enlightenment, layman, divine inspiration, persecution, mustard seed, mysteries</keywords>

--- Page 155 ---
<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s autobiographical account of his spiritual journey, specifically following the period of silence imposed upon him by local authorities after the circulation of his first book, the Aurora.</meta>

<page-num>27</page-num>

-> # The Third Epistle <-

...that the Spirit of God showed and foretold me so much beforehand, so that I know what His counsel is in His way. Therefore, I willingly and patiently yielded myself under the Cross <note>The "Cross" here refers to the trials and persecutions Boehme suffered for his beliefs.</note> and committed my cause to God. I often entreated Him that if it did not come wholly from His own counsel, He should take it from me and not let me know or understand anything of that kind or proceed in that way.

<margin>☞</margin>
7. I also intended (after the persecution) <note>Boehme refers to the years between 1612 and 1618 when he was ordered by the city council of Görlitz to stop writing after the controversy surrounding his first book.</note> not to write any more, but only to keep myself still in obedience to my God and to let the Devil roar over me with his scorn, revilement, and derision. In this, many a hard combat was fought against him, and I cannot well tell or declare what I endured. But it went with me like a grain that is sown into the earth, which, against all reason, springs up fresh in all storms and tempests; whereas in winter all seems dead and reason says, "now all is gone." Thus, the precious grain of mustard seed sprang up again under all dispraise, contempt, disdain, and derision, as a lily, and returned with a hundredfold increase, and also with deeper and more peculiar knowledge, and came forth again in a fiery instigation <note>original: "instigation"—here meaning a powerful internal divine impulse or driving force.</note> or forcible driving.

8. But my *external* man would write no more; it was somewhat discouraged and timorous until it reached the point where the *internal* man <note>In Boehme's theology, the "internal man" is the spiritual soul that is reborn in Christ, while the "external man" is the earthly, fallen nature.</note> captured and overpowered the external. Even then, the *Great Mystery* did *appear*; and then I understood God's counsel and cast myself upon His will. Also, I would not invent or feign anything out of reason, nor would I give way and place any more to reason. Instead, I resigned my will to God’s will, so that my reason might be as it were dead; so that He (the Spirit of God) might do and work what and how He pleased. I willed nothing in my own reason, so that it might be His will and deed alone.

<margin>☞</margin>
9. And when this was done, then the internal man was armed and received a very faithful guide, and to him I wholly yielded my reason. I did not study or invent anything, nor did I give reason leave to dictate what I should write—except only that which the Spirit showed me as in a great *depth* and a full *chaos* in the *mystery* <note>Boehme often uses "Chaos" and "Mystery" (Mysterium Magnum) to describe the divine source or the unformed potentiality from which God brings forth the world.</note>. Yet this was without my own sufficient comprehension, for the creature is not like God, who performs and comprehends all things at *once* in His wisdom.

10. And there was then a purpose in me again to write something, and in the space of nine months *three books* were made. One was concerning the three principles of the *Divine Essence*—that is, of the Being of all beings—wherein the *Great Mystery* has somewhat opened and revealed itself. In that work are many excellent things contained, much deeper than in this first work [namely, the *Aurora*].

<meta>catchword: which</meta>

<summary>Jacob Boehme describes how his spiritual "internal man" overcame his "external" fear of persecution, leading him to break his silence and write three new works through divine inspiration rather than human reason.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Aurora, The Three Principles of the Divine Essence, internal man, external man, divine inspiration, Mysterium Magnum, persecution, Görlitz, mustard seed</keywords>

<meta>The author continues describing his various writings and their origins to his correspondent, specifically mentioning a large work on the "threefold life" and a set of answers to forty theological questions.</meta>

<page-num>38</page-num>

# The Third Epistle.

(which your Honor has sent here for me to examine), and it contains about a hundred sheets of paper.

11. After this, another work was produced containing sixty sheets. It treats of the **threefold life of man** and also of the whole **Creation**—a great open gate to the Mystery <note>original: "Mystery"; likely referring to the *Mysterium Magnum*, the hidden or divine nature of the universe</note>. It is truly a wonder that surpasses and goes beyond the reach of all reason. Even I, in my own reason, wonder and marvel at what God intends to do, using such a humble <note>original: "meane"</note> instrument for such weighty matters. For in that work are revealed and explained the mysteries about which the world has contended and fought ever since the heavy fall of *Adam*. Yet such a foundation <note>original: "Ground"</note> has never been brought to light until now. Nevertheless, it shall not be understood by the worldly, but by the children of God, as is already manifest and known.

12. Thirdly, forty questions were sent to me by a learned and insightful man who is also a lover and great friend of the Mystery. He encouraged me to answer them according to these gifts and the spirit. These are indeed very profound questions, containing the great depths and secrets of the origin of the soul and all the secrets or mysteries of the Mystery. In response, an answer has been produced in which the world might well rejoice, if the anger, iniquity, and malice of the Devil did not hinder it. Yet the counsel of God must stand.

<margin>☞</margin>
13. Now, because I perceive that your noble mind and heart have a singular hunger and thirst for such mysteries, and that you do not care for the world which despises them, I recognize God's counsel in this. It is my bounden duty to share these things with you; for bread belongs to the children, and they are worthy of it, but pearls must not be cast before swine <note>A biblical reference to Matthew 7:6, advising not to share sacred things with those who will not appreciate them.</note>. My spirit and mind show me clearly that your Honor does not search for such things out of curiosity, but by the prompting and guidance of the Spirit. This is like the Spirit which often led *Peter* to *Cornelius* <note>A reference to Acts 10, where God divinely orchestrates a meeting between an apostle and a seeker.</note>, so that he might declare the words of eternal life to him.

14. Although I am a stranger and very simple, your desire and will embolden me to write to your Honor. I do so with a simple hand <insert>in a plain and coarse phrase and style</insert>—for God’s gifts are not bound to any human arts. I am more daring with you because I see that your noble heart is so low and humble that you would send for me, a person who is but humble <note>original: "meane"</note> and lowly. Since this is the case, I also firmly hope that the Spirit of God shall open the doors and gates of the mysteries for your soul and grant you a right understanding to apprehend and know His wonderful gifts, which I heartily wish for your Honor.

<page-num>39</page-num>
# The Third Epistle.

<margin>☞</margin> 15. My writings will seem somewhat strange to you, for in some places the zeal is vehement and earnest—especially against **Belial** <term>Belial</term> <note>original: "Belial"; a biblical name for personified wickedness, lawlessness, or the Devil.</note> and the **Antichrist**, who is known by God in His anger [or who has come up for judgment in His wrath]. Therefore, I say that I could not, nor dared not, write otherwise than as it was given and dictated to me. I have continually written as the Spirit dictated, and I did not give way to human Reason [or to the wisdom of the natural and astral spirit]. I also do not acknowledge this as a work of my own Reason, which was too weak; rather, it is the work of the Spirit, who has shown what He intends to do, what shall come to pass, and also what has already been done. 

For He proceeds out of the abyss into the "byss" <term>Abyss into the Byss</term> <note>original: "Abysse into the Bysse"; in Jakob Böhme’s mystical philosophy, the "Abyss" is the bottomless void, while the "Byss" (from "basis") is the ground or foundation of being.</note> and searches through all things. He tries the heart and reins <term>reins</term> <note>original: "reyns"; an archaic term for the kidneys, once believed to be the seat of a person's deepest emotions and secret thoughts.</note> and proves the thoughts of men. Moreover, He hereby intimates and declares the Last Judgment: that He will test and examine every being through fire. I could not, nor should I, write at all (even in this fiery inspiration) unless I set it down exactly as the Spirit represented it. Therefore, I have made it as a *memorial* <note>original: "Memoriall"; a record or testimony for future reflection.</note> for myself; I have no other intention for it.

<margin>☞</margin> 16. But because you desire to read it, I will send it to you. I pray that you return it to me afterward, for I wish to keep it as a *memorial*. I am assured that—so long as your noble mind gives praise to God, reads it diligently, and takes this path to heart with a desire for understanding—the Lord will open the door of His love to you in the Mystery. He will crown you with the diadem <note>A crown or headband worn as a symbol of sovereignty.</note> of His wisdom, which is more precious than the created heavens and this world. 

For the precious **Philosopher’s Stone** <term>Philosophers Stone</term> <note>original: "Philosophers Stone"; in alchemy, a legendary substance that could turn base metals into gold and grant eternal life. Böhme uses it as a metaphor for the ultimate spiritual truth or Christ.</note>, the ground of all mysteries and secrets, lies within it. This same diadem [or garland of wisdom] is set with this Stone. This crown of light in the Holy Spirit is what the soul puts on like a garment, becoming a new body in and for the Kingdom of God. In this state, the soul is a child of God and is able to stand in the fire of God’s anger without any hurt or grief. It can overcome the Devil, Death, and this world. 

Furthermore, the soul can rule over the stars, the poisonous influences of the constellations, and this outward life—which is otherwise impossible for human Reason. This wisdom gives a knowledge of things that no art [or literal acumen <term>acumen</term> <note>original: "acumen"; mental sharpness or academic skill derived from books.</note> from external Reason] is able to search out or dive into. It sees through Heaven and Earth, and it reaps where it has not sown. It does not ask the question, "Is it true or not?" It carries the sign of truth and righteousness within itself. It holds all virtues in hope; there is no fear of God’s anger in it. It provides a very joyful hope, and confirms and assures the soul that it is a child of God.

<meta>Catchword: 17. This</meta>

<summary>The author explains that his writings are dictated by the Holy Spirit rather than human reason, serving as a spiritual memorial. He promises the reader that diligent study will lead to divine wisdom and a "new body" capable of overcoming death and the influence of the stars.</summary>
<keywords>Belial, Antichrist, Astral Spirit, Abyss and Byss, Last Judgment, Philosophers Stone, Diadem of Wisdom, Reason vs. Spirit, Mystical Knowledge</keywords>

--- Page 158 ---
<meta>The previous page concludes with Jacob Boehme discussing the spiritual path and the nature of divine revelation. <margin>☛</margin>
17. This garland is a Virgin, a chaste purity, and divine Beauty. It is a joy of life; it comforts and rejoices the mind in affliction. It goes along with a person into death, yet it has no death or dying in it. It lives from Eternity and is a guide into Heaven and the joy of the Angels. Its taste is more precious and pleasant than all the joys of this world; and he who once obtains it esteems it higher than all the goods and riches of this world. It cannot be paralleled with anything except the <term>Deity</term><note>original: "Deity"; here referring to the Godhead or the essence of God</note>, but it lies hidden in a dark valley. The world does not know it; the Devil blows against it like a storm of wind and often so covers and disguises it that Reason does not recognize it. But it springs forth in its own time like a fair lily with manifold fruits; it is sown in tears and grows in tribulation and affliction, but it is reaped with great joy. It is contemned and despised by human reason, but he who obtains it holds it as his best treasure.

<margin>☛</margin>
18. Such a garland is placed upon him who seeks after it with earnestness and wholly resigns himself unto it. However, one's own "self-reason" in flesh and blood does not obtain it, as my writings fully testify; for what is written there, the author has known by experience. There is no strange hand or spirit foisted in. I do not write this for my own vainglory (my boasting is only in God) but as a rule and direction for the children of God, so that they may know what reward God gives to those who put their trust and confidence in Him and disregard the dispraise and contempt of the world.

19. I also wonder how you and many others in <term>Silesia</term><note>A historical region in Central Europe, mostly in modern-day Poland</note> have obtained my writings, for I have no acquaintance with any of them. I am so private regarding their publication that the citizens here around me know nothing of them, except for the first part. That part was taken from me <term>Per-force</term><note>By force</note> by a person in the <term>Mystery of Babel</term><note>Boehme's term for the confused and corrupt state of the institutional church</note> who, out of envy, persecuted the work and proclaimed it to be <term>Heresy</term>. Nevertheless, they never actually read it, nor was it ever properly examined as was fitting.

20. Indeed, I never asked any man's advice about it, nor have I ever committed it to the censure and judgment of man to this very hour; instead, I commended it to God. Yet by this, I know and acknowledge the way of God; I understand that it is known not only in <term>Silesia</term> but also in other countries without my prior knowledge. I must even say that he who so persecuted it has actually published it; for my intent was to keep it by me as long as I lived, and I wrote it for myself alone.

<margin>* The Three Principles, and the Threefold Life.</margin>
21. But what God purposed in His counsel is now manifest, and it shall appear even more clearly when the two last books—*<term>The Three Principles</term>* and *<term>The Threefold Life</term>*—shall be read. At these, I myself, in the external man, do exceed-

<summary>Jacob Boehme describes the spiritual "garland" of divine wisdom and explains how his private writings were spread throughout Silesia and beyond by the very persecution intended to suppress them.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Silesia, divine wisdom, Mystery of Babel, heresy, Three Principles, Threefold Life, spiritual resignation</keywords>

<sig>G</sig>

<margin>☛</margin>
...exceedingly wonder and marvel at what God intends and will do. For I acknowledge myself to be altogether unworthy and ignorant, and yet the greatest and deepest mysteries are revealed to the internal man <note>original: "internall man"; the spiritual or inner self as opposed to the physical person</note>, which I give to you and other lovers of God to consider in humility. For in truth, I cannot say at all that it is the work of my understanding or reason; rather, I acknowledge it to be a *wonder* wherein God will reveal great things, into which my reason speculates and continually marvels.

22. For I never in all my life studied these mysteries, and likewise knew nothing of them, for I am a layman <term>layman: a person without professional or specialized knowledge, specifically one not trained in the university "Schools" or the clergy</term>. And yet I must bring such things forth to light which all the high schools or universities have not been able to do—to whom, notwithstanding, I am but a child by comparison, having none of their arts or wisdom. I must write wholly from another school. And what is even greater than all this, *the language of nature is made known to me* <note>original: "Language of Nature"; a mystical concept suggesting an intuitive understanding of the essence of things and their names</note>, so that I can understand the greatest mysteries in my own *mother tongue*.

<margin>☛</margin>
23. Though I cannot say that I have learned or comprehended it, as long as the hand of God stays upon me, I understand it. But if it hides itself, then I do not know my own labor and am made a stranger to the work of my own hands. By this, I may see how altogether impossible a thing it is to search out and apprehend the mysteries of God without God's spirit. Therefore, I ascribe and attribute nothing to myself; it is not my work, and I do not desire any human applause or honor for it.

<margin>☛</margin>
24. I am only a simple, humble instrument; God works and makes what he pleases. What God wills, that I also will; and whatever he does not will, I also do not will. If it is his will for me to know anything, then I will know it; but if he does not will it, then neither do I. *I will be nothing, and dead, so that he may live and work in me as he pleases; I have cast myself wholly into him, so that I may be safe and secure from the Devil*.

25. And though I must leave my outward body and life to the disposal of the world and suffer the Devil to roar against me, yet I will trust neither the Devil nor the world with my internal man. Neither will I do (according to the inward man) what the world wants me to do. And although my outward man is bound and <margin>☛</margin> obliged to the world—and in its obligation and allegiance must obey the laws and ordinances of the world and do what the outward *obligation* requires of me—yet my internal man shall only be obedient to God and not to the world. For he is not in the world but has made himself dead to it, so that God might live in him and be both the will and the deed in him. And though I cannot say that it is possible to live so [<insert>in perfection</insert>], yet my will is...

<summary>The author describes himself as an uneducated layman who serves as a humble instrument for divine revelation, claiming an intuitive understanding of the "Language of Nature." He emphasizes a total submission to God's will and a distinction between the outward person, who obeys worldly laws, and the internal man, who is obedient only to God.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of his spiritual drive and the origins of his writing, following his description of the internal struggle between the spirit and the "external life."</meta>

<page-num>42</page-num>

# The Third Epistle.

...is so directed and focused. Neither the world nor the Devil shall break this, even if my outward life should fail and perish, and though Reason often flatly contradicts it. Even when temptation appears in heaps, causing terror and sadness to the external life (where the spirit also hides itself, as if all were dead and gone), it always brings forth new fruits in abundance.

26. I have related this to you at length so that you may know and acknowledge what kind of man I am, what the beginning and cause of my writing is, and from what Art and spirit it was produced or generated. Also, for what purpose: namely, to keep it as a memorial for myself. But because I perceive sincere, religious hearts thirsting for it, I will not conceal it from them. Instead, I share it <insert>but impart</insert> in a brotherly and Christian way, entrusting and committing it to God so that He may work and do what He pleases in them. We are bound and obliged to do this for one another.

27. Lastly, I ask you to keep my name secret among the learned, for I know that a humble layman <note>original: "meane Lay-man"</note> is considered ridiculous and contemptible by men *learned in <term>Scholastic Art</term>* <note>Scholastic Art refers to the formal, academic theology and philosophy taught in universities of the period, which Boehme, a shoemaker, had not studied.</note>. Although God has His children among them as well, I do not care to have my name attached to it or authorized as mine. 
<margin>☛</margin>
For the praise belongs to God, who is the giver. I do not seek to make a great and glorious name for myself; instead, Christ is my reward, my name, and my glory. I hope to have the glory of it in the life to come before angels and men, and to rejoice there with the saints in Christ, as my writings clearly testify.

28. Concerning the book ***Aurora***, which your Honor has sent for me to examine, I have read some of it and find that it is my work and well copied. Some syllables are left out for the sake of brevity, yet the sense and meaning are not diminished by this. I am content regardless, since (so far as I have examined in haste) I have found no additions. The great <term>Mysteries</term> <note>Deep spiritual truths or hidden divine knowledge.</note> are contained very deeply within it. They were understood and grasped by the author, but it was not very easy for Reason to comprehend them at first. Although they were well known in the depths, the author was not accustomed to expressing them. When heavenly joy met him, he only followed the spirit's guidance. However, the "wild nature" <note>The unrefined or unredeemed human self.</note> is not immediately <term>regenerated</term> <gloss>made into a new creature</gloss>. 
<margin>☛</margin>
Indeed, if a kernel is sown, a tree grows. But if the virtue <note>original: "vertue," meaning the inherent power or life-force.</note> is great—if the power of the resolution is strong and the practice is sincere and constant—the tree grows up sooner and is recognized more quickly.

<meta>catchword: 29. In</meta>

<summary>Boehme explains his motivations for sharing his writings despite his lack of formal education, requests anonymity to avoid academic ridicule, and reflects on the early drafts of his book "Aurora." He emphasizes that spiritual growth and the ability to express divine mysteries develop gradually, like a tree growing from a seed.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Aurora, Scholasticism, spiritual regeneration, humility, divine revelation, Layman, spiritual growth</keywords>
