<meta>image quality: high contrast, heavy ink; catchword: brought. This page continues Boehme's explanation of his writing process and the divine providence that led to the publication of his works despite persecution.</meta>

29. In the other three books, you shall find the mysteries <note>original: Mysteris</note> more clearly, and so throughout, the further you read, the deeper they go; each book from the first is grounded *ten times deeper*. So the fourth is a very clear mirror <note>original: mirrour</note> wherein the Great Mystery <note>the *Mysterium Magnum*, a central concept in Boehme's thought representing the source of all things</note> is sufficiently and visibly seen and understood—yet only by its "Children" <note>those who are spiritually awakened or have the "inner light"</note>. Reason shall remain blind, for the Spirit of God dwells not in the outward principle, but in the inward; it proceeds from the inward into the outward [the principle of this world], yet the outward world does not comprehend Him.

<margin>* The Book of the Three Principles.</margin>
30. But sir, I must tell you that the book *Aurora* was not finished, for the Devil intended to make a bonfire of it because he saw that the day would break forth in it. But for all that, the "day" has even overtaken the *Aurora* [or morning], so that it is already clear daylight. About thirty more sheets belong to it, but because the "storm" <note>this refers to the persecution and legal prohibitions Boehme faced in his hometown of Görlitz after the *Aurora* began to circulate</note> broke them off, it was not finished. In the meantime, it has become daylight, and the "morning" is extinguished; since that time, the work has been to bring forth the day. Yet it shall remain as it is for an eternal remembrance, because the deficiency is restored and supplied in the *Second* <margin>* The Book of the Three Principles</margin>; the fault and blame for the deficiency is to be attributed to the Enemy.

<margin>☞</margin>
31. But I will blame no one except the falsehood and iniquity of the Devil, who is an enemy to all good. He even confuses and entangles kings; how then should a poor, humble <note>original: meane</note> man, being employed in such a work, be so quickly recognized and known? If it is surely affirmed that he is a layman <note>term: Layman; someone who is not a member of the clergy or professionally trained in theology</note> and also unlearned, the very wise and those skilled in the arts will be offended at the plainness and simplicity of such a thing. When a scholar hears one speak of such wonders and deep mysteries in such a humble and simple way [without scholastic <note>term: Scholastic; relating to the formal, complex academic methods and jargon used in universities</note> pomp of words and artificial terms, and the phrases of logic and rhetoric], then he thinks it is a rhapsody <note>term: Rhapsody; here used to mean a disorganized or confused heap of ideas</note> [or some confused heap of notions packed together, an enthusiastic <note>term: Enthusiastic; in this era, "enthusiasm" often referred to someone claiming direct divine inspiration in a way that appeared fanatical or irrational</note>, fantastical hotchpotch of whimsies, or a bundle of nonsense]. For he does not understand the gifts of God and is also unable to see into the heart of another. Therefore, I will disturb no man and desire none to trouble himself about it; but I confess that it is God's providence—otherwise, this book would have still lain in a corner.

32. But thus it was published without my knowledge, consent, or will—and by the persecutors themselves! This I acknowledge to be from the providence and appointment of God, for I had no copy of it for myself. Neither did I ever know those who have it; I do not have it myself, and yet it has been copied out and

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that his writing deepens with each subsequent book and discusses how his first work, *Aurora*, was left unfinished due to persecution but was ultimately published through God's providence.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Aurora, spiritual mysteries, scholasticism vs. layman, religious persecution, divine providence, The Book of the Three Principles</keywords>

--- Page 162 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme's reflections on the dissemination of his work and the nature of his divinely inspired writing process.</meta>

<page-num>44</page-num>
<header>The Third Epistle.</header>

...brought to my sight and hands four times already; so I see that others have published it. I consider it a wonderful work, that the grain grows against the will of the enemies; for that which is sown by God, no one can stop or hinder [from growing].

<margin>☞</margin>
33. But as for what you and others also misunderstand in my book *Aurora* (which appears to be wrong in your understanding, and which also needs some clarification and explanation), you shall find it sufficiently cleared up at length in my third and fourth books. In those, there is an open gate to the mysteries of all beings; there is even nothing in Nature that cannot be fundamentally investigated and established through this method. For it shows and reveals the *Stone of the Wise* <note>original: "Stone of the wise men," the Philosopher's Stone, a legendary substance in alchemy said to be capable of transmuting metals and granting eternal life</note> to all the secrets and mysteries in both the Divine and earthly mystery. By this knowledge and understanding, all the metals of the Earth may be brought to the highest degree or perfection—yet only by the children of *Divine Magic* <note>original: "divine Magia," referring to the spiritual art of working with God's creative power</note>, who have the *Revelation* [or experimental science] of it.

34. I see it clearly enough, but I have no *manual operation* <note>original: "manuall operation," practical laboratory skills or craftsmanship</note>, urge, or technical skill for it. I only set forth an open mystery, for which God shall stir up his own laborers. Let no man seek the physical work from me, or think they can get the knowledge and practice of the Philosopher's Stone [or universal tincture] from me. Although it is known clearly and could be explained more clearly, I have surrendered my will and will write nothing except what is given to me; in this way, it may not be my own work, lest I should be *imprisoned in the Turba* <note>original: "Turba," a term used by Boehme to describe the spirit of confusion, noise, or divine wrath that disrupts the harmony of the soul</note>.

35. And if you want anything copied from these writings now sent to you, it is necessary that the transcriber be a learned and understanding man. Many syllables are not fully written, nor do they all follow grammatical spelling <note>original: "autography"</note>; in many words, some letters may be missing, and sometimes a capital letter stands for a whole word. This is because technical skill did not write this, nor was there any time to consider how to set it down precisely according to the correct usage of letters. Instead, all was ordered according to the direction of the Spirit, which often moved in haste. Consequently, the penman’s hand (being unaccustomed to it) often shook. Although I could have written in a more accurate, beautiful, and plain manner, the reason I did not was that the burning fire often forced its way forward with *speed*, and the hand and pen had to hasten directly after it. For it comes and goes like a sudden shower: whatever it lands upon, it hits. If it were possible to comprehend...

<summary>Boehme discusses the alchemical secrets contained in his later books and explains that his writing is a result of rapid, divine inspiration rather than calculated literary effort.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Aurora, Philosopher's Stone, Divine Magia, Turba, divine inspiration, alchemical symbolism, writing process</keywords>

--- Page 163 ---
<meta>The author continues discussing the difficulty of expressing spiritual insights in writing and mentions sending specific manuscripts to the recipient.</meta>

<page-num>45</page-num>

# The Third Epistle

...comprehend and write all [which my mind beholds in the Divine CHAOS <note>original: Divine CHAOS; here meaning the unmanifest source of all existence from which the light and dark worlds emerge</note>], it would then be three times more and more deeply grounded.

36. But it cannot be, and therefore more than one book has been made—more than one philosophical discourse, each progressively deeper. This was done so that what could not be contained in one might be found in the other. It would be well if, at last, only one could be made out of *all* of them and all the others removed [*or laid aside*], for the multiplicity and plurality cause strife, conflict, aversion, and wrong understandings. This happens because of the hasty conclusions and conjectures of the *Reader*, who does not know how to test and discern the spirit that uses such wonderful phrases; in these instances, Reason often supposes that the text contradicts itself, and yet in the depths, it is not contrary at all.

<margin>☛</margin>
37. Out of this misunderstanding [*or the false interpretations of Reason and literal, outward scholarship applied to the writings of holy men*], the *Great Babel* <note>In this context, Babel represents the confusion of religious sects and institutional churches that argue over literal words rather than spiritual truth</note> upon earth has been brought forth. In this state, men contend for nothing but words, while leaving the spirit of understanding in the Mystery alone. But its end and its number are found and committed to the *Turba* <note>original: Turba; a term used by the author to describe the spirit of turmoil or the divine wrath that dissolves and destroys false human structures</note>; for the beginning has found its limit, and there is no longer any withholding or staying [*of the wrath of God upon Babel*]. It cannot be quashed by any power or force of arms.

<margin>* *The Forty Questions*</margin>
38. I speak not of and from myself, but from that which the spirit shows, which no man can resist. For it stands in its *Omnipotence* and does not depend on our thinking and willing, as the *Fourth Book of these writings does exceedingly deeply declare; it is strongly grounded in the light of nature <note>The 'light of nature' refers to the innate human capacity, illuminated by God, to understand the workings of the universe</note> and can be demonstrated in all things.*

39. Further, I want you to understand that in these writings which are now sent to you, the author sometimes uses the word *We* and sometimes *I*. Understand that by the word *We*, he means the spirit speaking through two persons (plural); and by the word *I*, the author refers to himself. I give you this as direction and information to remove any wrong apprehension or suspicion.

40. And herewith I send you the *Fourth Part*, being the forty Questions, and therein you may behold yourself. At the next opportunity, I will send you the *Second* and *Third Part* if you desire to have them. I pray you to return this one to me at the next occasion, for I must send it to the man who composed and sent me the Questions. And so I commend and commit you to the love of God.

<summary>The author explains that his multiple writings are intended to cover the depths of divine mystery which a single book cannot hold, while warning against the sectarian confusion (Babel) that arises from literalist readings. He also clarifies his use of the pronouns 'We' and 'I' and arranges for the sharing of his manuscript, The Forty Questions.</summary>
<keywords>Divine Chaos, Great Babel, Turba, The Forty Questions, Light of Nature, Jacob Boehme, spiritual discernment, manuscripts</keywords>

# The Third Epistle

I heartily wish that God would illuminate your **NOBLE** heart and give you the ability to rightly understand the sense and meaning of the *Author* regarding the internal principle <note>original: "internall Principle"; in Boehme’s theosophy, this refers to the inner spiritual world or the divine essence within the soul</note>, wishing you all temporal and eternal welfare.

<page-num>47</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Epistle.</header>

## A Letter to
# PAUL KEYM:
### BEING
## An answer to him concerning
#### *Our Last Times.*
---
In which he treats of *the first resurrection of the dead* and of the *thousand-year Sabbath.* <note>The "thousand-year Sabbath" refers to the Millennium, a period of peace and holiness prophesied in the Book of Revelation.</note>
---
### ALSO,
Regarding the fall of BABEL <note>In this context, "Babel" symbolizes the corrupt state of organized religion or a confused society.</note> and the new building in ZION. <note>"Zion" represents the restored, spiritual community of true believers.</note>
---

->I.<-

> May light, salvation, and eternal power, flowing from the fountain of the heart of Jesus Christ, be our quickening <note>original: "quickning"; meaning life-giving or soul-reviving</note> consolation.

<margin>2.</margin>
Worthy and much esteemed sir, and good friend in the illumination of the Holy Spirit; and in the love of our Lord Jesus Christ, beloved brother: I received from Mr. C. E. the letter you sent me, dated about the 20th of *July*, along with two small treatises <note>original: "Treatises annexed"; short formal essays or written works attached to the letter</note> attached. Through these, I understand that you have received and read some of my short manuscripts concerning the wisdom of God; and as you affirm, they bring you joy. Moreover, you carry a great desire and longing for them, as you are engaged in a similar exercise in the wisdom of God.

<meta>catchword: 3. Which</meta>

<summary>In this opening of the Fourth Epistle, the author greets Paul Keym and acknowledges receiving his letter and essays, noting their shared interest in divine wisdom and the "Last Times."</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Paul Keym, Resurrection, Millennium, Babel, Zion, spiritual illumination, eschatology</keywords>

<lang>English</lang>
<page-num>48</page-num>
### The Fourth Epistle.

<meta>This page continues the author's discourse on spiritual awakening, contrasting the state of spiritual confusion (Babel) with the return to divine truth (Zion).</meta>

<margin>☞</margin>
3. On my part, this also rejoices me to see that even now the time is at hand when the right divine understanding and true knowledge of God is again springing forth in *Zion* <note>original: "Zyon"; used here to symbolize the true spiritual church or state of grace</note>. The ruined *Jerusalem* shall be built up again, and humanity’s true image—which disappeared and died out in *Adam*—is again manifesting itself in *Zion* with a true human voice. God is pouring out His spirit into us so that the precious pearl, through the power and light of the Holy Spirit, is once again known, sought, and found.

4. By this, we clearly see and understand the blindness we have lived in for a long time within *Babel* <note>original: "Babel"; used to symbolize the confusion and corruption of institutional or worldly religion</note>, going astray in carnal, evil ways. Because of this, we have forsaken the true *Jerusalem*, shamefully wasted our inheritance <note>original: "Patrimony"</note>, and lightly valued our angelic trophy or crown—that is, the beautiful image of God within us. Instead, we have wallowed in the filth of the Devil; under a pretense of divine obedience, we have played with the serpent and walked in purely erroneous ways. The divine light now sets this before our eyes and exhorts us to return like the Prodigal Son <note>original: "lost Sonne"</note> and enter into the true *Zion*.

5. This return is not made through historical assumptions <note>original: "Historicall supposals"; refers to believing in religious history intellectually without experiencing spiritual truth personally</note>, opinions, or blind convictions, as if we already understood these things very well. That is not *Zion*, but *Babel*, which confesses God with the mouth and makes devout speeches with the lips, but in the heart clings to the Great Whore of Babylon—to the dragon of self-love, pride, greed, and pleasure. Such a heart tries to present itself as if it were a virgin; no, this is not the Virgin in *Zion*. It must be done with total sincerity.

<margin>☞</margin>
6. We must be born of God in *Zion*, and both know and do His will. God’s spirit must testify to our spirit that we are God’s children—not only in our speech with knowledge and guesswork, but in the heart in actual deed. It cannot be merely a "holy-seeming" way without power [existing only in formal ways of word-worship and rounds of hollow prayers <note>original: "Lip-labour"</note>, in which the captive conscience mistakenly places the power of godliness]. The Devil mocks at this and does not care for it. Instead, we must put on the helmet of righteousness and of love, as well as of chastity and purity, if we intend to wage battle against the Prince of this World <note>the Devil</note>. He does not care at all for any outward show [or for the long and loud vocal cries of blind devotion]. It must be power that overcomes him, and that power must shine forth in goodness and the holy fruits of Christianity; in this way, we may fight for the noble prize, or crown of life.

<margin>☜</margin>
7. For we have a powerful warrior against us; he attacks us in body and soul and quickly casts us down. There is no other way to overcome him but with power found in humility, which alone is

<meta>The text breaks off as the author prepares to explain how humility is the key to spiritual victory; catchword: able</meta>

<summary>The author calls for a move away from the empty rituals and intellectual "Babel" of institutional religion toward a sincere, heart-centered "Zion" characterized by spiritual rebirth and humility.</summary>
<keywords>Zion, Babel, spiritual rebirth, Adam, inner light, humility, hypocrisy, Holy Spirit, Jerusalem, Christian warfare</keywords>

--- Page 167 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's exhortation to spiritual vigilance and the practical application of divine wisdom, following the discussion on quenching the "poisonous fires" of the enemy.</meta>

...able to quench his poisonous fires with which he fights against us—both from the outside and within us—against our noble image.

<margin>☞</margin>
8. Therefore, beloved sir and brother in Christ, since you apply yourself to Divine Wisdom and labor in it, it is right and necessary that we encourage one another to be vigilant in withstanding the Devil. We must continually keep before our eyes the path we ought to walk and actually travel along it; otherwise, we accomplish nothing. If we know that the world is blind in <term>Babel</term><note>original: "Babel"; in the author's work, this refers to the "City of Confusion," representing the corrupt, fragmented, and hypocritical state of institutional religion and sectarian conflict.</note> and goes astray, then we must be the first to effectively leave <term>Babel</term> so that the world may see that we are in earnest.

<margin>☞</margin>
9. It is not enough for us to expose and reveal <term>Babel</term> if we are still found acting as <term>Babel</term> does. If we do that, we testify that God reveals His light to us so that we can see, yet we choose to do nothing but works of darkness. That very light which enlightens our understanding shall witness against us—showing that the Lord has called us and shown us the way, but we refused to walk in it.

10. It is *well* that we expose <term>Babel</term>, but we must *take heed* of the spirit, mind, and type of knowledge with which we do so. It is good to be zealous, but the heart must be upright toward God; otherwise, we run without being sent, and God does not recognize or acknowledge us in our journey. By acting this way, the Devil mocks us and leads us into the side-paths of error.
<margin>☞</margin>

11. Besides, the Holy Scripture declares that our works and words shall follow us. Therefore, we must seriously consider with what spirit and knowledge we approach the high <term>Mysteries</term><note>Spiritual truths that are hidden from the natural mind and can only be understood through divine revelation or "illumination."</note>. He who seeks to tear down something evil must set up something better in its place; otherwise, he is not one of God's builders, nor does he labor in <term>Christ's Vineyard</term>. It is not good to tear a building down unless a man knows how to rebuild it in a better frame and form.

<margin>☞</margin>
12. For God alone is the <term>Master-builder</term> of the world; we are only servants. We must take great care how we labor if we wish to receive a reward, ensuring that we have learned His work in His school and do not run before we are sent or while we are still incapable of His service. Otherwise, we will be found to be <term>unprofitable servants</term>. I say this with great affection and in all faithfulness, so that we may instruct and direct one another in what we ought to do, ensuring our labor is accepted by God.

13. For the obscure <term>Mysteries</term> cannot be known in any other way except through the Holy Spirit. We cannot draw conclusions about hidden things unless we have actual knowledge of them and find, through the experimental illumination of God, that what we claim is the truth and the will of God, and that it also agrees with His Word; and
<margin>☞</margin>

<meta>This page continues the Fourth Epistle, building on the concept of spiritual illumination. The text ends on the catchword "in," which connects to the beginning of the next page.</meta>

...grounded in the light of Nature; for without the light of Nature, there is no understanding of Divine Mysteries.

14. The great Building of God is manifest in the light of Nature; therefore, he whom God’s light illuminates may search out and know all things, although knowledge is not given in one and the same way and measure to everyone. God’s wonders and works are boundless, infinite, and immense; they are revealed to everyone according to his gifts, and he to whom the light shines has pure <note>original: "meere," meaning unmixed or absolute</note> joy in God’s works.

15. Also, that which is old and passed more than a thousand years ago is as near and as easily known in the light as that which is done today. For a thousand years before God are scarcely as much as a minute, or the twinkling of an eye is to us; therefore, all things are near and manifest to His spirit—both that which is past and that which is to come.

16. And if we see in His light, then we must declare His wonders, and manifest and praise His glorious Name, and *not bury* our talent <note>A reference to the Parable of the Talents in Matthew 25:14–30, regarding the responsibility to use God-given gifts.</note> in the earth; for we must deliver it to our Master with *increase*. He will require an account from us of how we have traded with it. Without knowledge or certain illumination from God, no man should presume to judge, or be a doctor <note>In this context, a "doctor" refers to a learned teacher or theologian.</note> or master in the Great Mystery; for it is not committed or commanded to him. Instead, he must labor to attain the true light, and then he goes rightly to work in the School of God.

<margin>* Their livelihoods <note>original: "Livings," referring to the income or property attached to a church office.</note></margin>
17. For there are many masters to be found who presume to judge in the Mystery, and yet they are not known or sent by God. Therefore, their *school* is called *Babel* <note>Babel signifies confusion; here it refers to corrupt institutional religious systems.</note>, the Mother of Whoredom <note>A biblical metaphor for spiritual infidelity or corruption.</note> upon Earth. They flatter on both sides; they play the hypocrite with God and also serve the Devil. They call themselves the shepherds and pastors of *Jesus Christ*; they run, and yet are not sent, much less does God own them. What they do, they do for their own honor and for their *bellies'* sake; they would not run at all if they did not gain from it in their course of spiritual whoredom and hypocrisy.

18. They have turned the right and exceedingly precious Mystery of God into a mystery of their own whoredom and pleasure. Therefore, the Spirit calls it Babel—a confusion—where men practice a hypocritical service and worship of God. They acknowledge Him with their tongues but deny Him in their power; they dissemble and flatter God with their lips, but in their hearts, they embrace and love the Dragon (from the Book of *Revelation*).

19. We must not be like these people if we wish to obtain the Divine Mystery and be capable of receiving the light. Instead, we must wholly commit our way to God and resign ourselves to Him, so that God’s light may shine in...

<summary>The author argues that true spiritual knowledge comes from divine illumination rather than institutional authority, warning that those who teach without this "light" belong to the "Babel" of corrupt religion.</summary>

in us; so that he may be our intelligence, knowing, willing, and also doing. We must become his children if we want to speak of his Being and walk or labor within it; for he does not commit his work to a stranger who has not learned his work or the mystery of his wonders in nature and grace.

<margin>☞</margin>
20. I have read your books and have found in them your great diligence and much labor, in that you have gathered together texts of the Holy Scripture in great abundance. I also understand that you are in good earnest about it, and that you would gladly <note>original: "fain"</note> clearly prove and explain the obscure terms and passages of Scripture concerning the End Times, the first resurrection of the dead, and the thousand-year Sabbath. Likewise, you wish to reveal and describe the ruin of *Babel* <note>original: "Babel," a reference to the city of Babylon, often used symbolically for a state of spiritual confusion or a corrupt church</note> and the new building in *Zion* <note>original: "Zyon," representing the true church or the kingdom of God</note> of which the Scripture speaks in many places.

21. First, regarding *Babel*: how it has grown up and how it shall again be destroyed is sufficiently clear. The *Destroyer* is already active and is now about the work. He began long ago; however, the world will not see or take any notice of it.

<margin>☞</margin>
22. Men cry *Mordio* <note>original: "Mordio," an old cry of "murder" or "distress"</note> [murder, confusion, and destruction to their adversaries], and yet there is no outside enemy; rather, it is only the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba," a Latin term used by Boehme to describe a disruptive or chaotic spiritual force, often associated with divine wrath or the breaking of harmony</note> which has grown up in the midst of *Babel* in her wickedness and unrighteousness. It has reached its limit and destroys only that which has for a long time been worthless, useless, and selfish—things which should have been rejected all along. For where God should have been honored and loved, and our neighbor loved as one loves oneself, men have instead set up abominable and bestial greed, deceit, falsehood, and wicked craft under a hypocritical show and pretense of holiness. They have cared for and loved falsehood in the place of God and have thus made of the Mystery an abominable, vicious *Babel* full of reproaches, insults, and contentions. There, they have used sweet speeches and enticing words of human wisdom [with feigned interpretations and explanations of Scripture] to blindfold our eyes and bind our consciences. They have led us captive in a very deceitful way toward the glory and magnificence of the great Whore <note>A biblical reference from the Book of Revelation, usually interpreted by Boehme as a symbol of spiritual corruption or a false church system</note>, so that she has fattened her adulterous offspring and dominated our bodies, souls, goods, and estates.

23. This bastard is now at odds with himself about the great prey and spoil, and he himself reveals his own wickedness and great shame, so that we may see what "good" was ever in her. For the great wickedness she has committed plagues her—and no outside thing—so that it may be seen that her prostitution has been

He acknowledges the recipient's scholarly work on the End Times and the eventual restoration of "Zion."</summary>

-> # The Fourth Letter <-

<warning>The image is dark with heavy ink bleed-through from the reverse side and some staining on the left margin. The text at the very top is slightly obscured by the page edge.</warning>

...have been manifold; and that the Devil had beset and caught us in many different nets; and that one spiritual adultery <note>original: "whoredome," here referring to the "Mystery of hypocrisy and iniquity"</note> runs in opposition to another; and they are malicious, biting, devouring, destroying, and flaying each other in a hostile manner.

<margin>☛</margin>
24. For great pain has come upon her, and she shall now give birth to the great iniquity with which she has become fully pregnant. Therefore, she cries out because of her labor and woe which has fallen upon her; and she speaks of the child which she shall bring forth; namely, of murder, covetousness, and tyranny. She uncovers her beautiful features and shows what she is in her heart; now, for those who refuse to recognize her, there is no remedy.

25. The *Book of Revelation* says, *"Go out from her, my people, so that you may not be partakers of her plagues"* <note>Revelation 18:4</note>; for she has filled her cup full with the abominations of her spiritual adultery <note>original: "whoredome"</note> in the anger of God. She shall drink that same cup and be forced to burst herself by it.

26. And this is what I say of *Babel* <note>Babel: A term used by Boehme to symbolize the confusion, corruption, and division within established, worldly church institutions.</note>, that she is a whore and shall suddenly break into pieces and be destroyed, and no stranger shall do it; the spirit of her own mouth strangles her; her own *Turba* <term>Turba</term> <note>Turba: A Latin term used by Boehme to describe a spirit of disturbance, wrath, or the "awakened" anger of nature that leads to destruction.</note> destroys her. She cries for vengeance and murder upon "heresy," and yet she does it not for God’s sake, but for her own illegitimate offspring <note>original: "Adulterous Bratt"</note> and her belly-god <note>original: "Belley-God," referring to those who serve their own physical appetites and greed rather than God.</note>. For otherwise, if it were for God, she would enter into His command and His will of *Love*, where *Christ* says: *"Love one another, for by this men shall know that you are my disciples."*

<margin>☛</margin>
27. The Kingdom of God does not consist of war and insults, or in an external show of luxury <note>original: "delicious dayes"</note>. The children of God are not to be found in these things, but in love, in patience, in hope, and in faith under the cross of *Jesus Christ*. Through these, the Church of God grows toward the Sacred Ternary <term>Sacred Ternary</term> <note>Sacred Ternary: A reference to the Holy Trinity, which Boehme often views as a divine reality to be manifested within the soul of the believer.</note> [into a heavenly, paradise-like essence] and the new angelic man, hidden within the old, springs forth in God. This is my certain knowledge, briefly summarized, concerning this article; you may see more of it in my writings.

28. Secondly, concerning *Zion* <note>Zion: In Boehme's theology, Zion represents the true, internal, and spiritual church of reborn believers, contrasted with the external "Babel."</note>, I speak and declare according to my knowledge, even as the spirit shows it to me: that there shall surely come an ending and removal of the *Deceit* [or the mystery of iniquity by which men are blinded]. *Zion* shall be found only by the children of *faith*—not universally, as if there should no longer be any wicked men.

<margin>Note.</margin>
29. For the oppressor shall be the cause of *Zion* being born. When men shall see how *Babel* is a whore, then many children shall be found...

<meta>The text ends with the catchword "found," leading into the description of the rebirth of the faithful on the following page.</meta>

<summary>In this passage, Boehme prophesies the inevitable self-destruction of "Babel" (the corrupt church) through its own internal "Turba" or wrath, while announcing the birth of "Zion," the true spiritual community founded on love and the "new angelic man."</summary>

<margin>☞</margin>...found in Zion, and seek the Lord; but the Oppressor shall dog them, and denounce them as heretics; he shall also persecute and put them to death, and where one is killed, there shall ten—even a hundred—rise up in his place.

30. But the general **Zion** appears first in the greatest misery; when **Babel** <note>original: "Babel" — Here referring to the corrupt and divided state of the worldly church, which the author believes is destined for destruction.</note> comes to ruin, then it shall stand desolate and miserable. And the children of Zion shall then say, "How has the Lord forsaken us? Come, we beseech you, let us seek His face; let us cease from strife and war. Have we not, alas, made our country desolate? Is not all store and **provision** wasted and spent? Are we not brothers? Why do we fight? We will now enter into love and unity, and seek the Lord, and no more fight and destroy ourselves; we will be content. Are we not all pilgrims and strangers here, seeking our native country?"

<margin>☞</margin>31. In this time, a Zion shall truly be found, and the heavens shall drop down their dew, and the earth yield her richness; yet not so perfectly that wickedness should be wholly done away with, for it shall continue until the end, of which Christ says, *Do you think that when the Son of Man comes, there will be faith upon the earth?* <note>original: "Thinkest thou that when the Sonne of man shall come, that there will be Faith upon the Earth ?" (Luke 18:8)</note> And though the children of Zion shall have a fiery deliverance, so that they shall remain despite the will of the Devil—insomuch that God will work great things, as in the time of the Apostles—yet it will not endure until the end. For as it was in the days of **Noah**, when he entered into the ark, so shall the coming of the Son of Man be, as it is written.

32. But I acknowledge and I know that the Holy Ghost shall be in the hearts of the faithful in Zion; for Zion shall not be an external thing, but within the **new man** <term>new man</term> (the spiritually reborn person in Christ). It is already born; he who would seek it, let him only seek himself and depart from the **old Adam** <term>old Adam</term> (the unregenerate, sinful nature of humanity) into a new life; and he shall find whether Jesus is born in him.

<margin>☞</margin>33. If he does not find it, let him enter into himself and seriously consider himself; and so he shall find **Babel**, and her workings in him. These he must destroy and enter into God's Covenant; and then Zion will be revealed in him, and he shall be born with Christ in **Bethlehem of Judea** in the dark stable—not in **Jerusalem**, as reason would gladly have it—so that Christ should be born in the **old ass** <note>The "old ass" refers to the stubborn, animal nature or the unrefined human intellect that must be made a servant to the new spiritual life.</note>. The old ass must become a servant and serve the new man in Zion.

34. But as for the idea that in **four hundred years** there shall be a pure golden age, I know nothing of it; it is not revealed to me. Also, the **limit of the world's end is not revealed to me**. I cannot speak of any four hundred years, for the Lord has not commanded me to teach it. I commend it to God's power and leave it for those to whom God would vouchsafe the knowledge of it. Since, therefore, I have not as

<summary>In this passage, Boehme explains that the true "Zion" is an internal spiritual state of the "new man" rather than a physical or political kingdom, and he rejects specific chronologies for a future "golden age" or the end of the world.</summary>

<page-num>54</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Epistle</header>

...yet having understood it, I rest satisfied in His <unclear>grace</unclear>; yet I despise no man, if he has the knowledge and command to teach in such a way.

<margin>☛</margin>
35. For the fourth Book of *Esdras* <note>An apocryphal book of the Bible often cited in mystical or apocalyptic traditions; many theologians during this period debated its authority.</note> is not sufficient, as I understand it, to give a positive assurance of it. Yet I wait for my Savior Jesus Christ and rejoice that I may find my Lord. When I have Him, then I hope—after the death of my "old Adam" <note>A theological term for the sinful, fallen nature of humanity that must "die" to allow for spiritual rebirth.</note>—to fully recreate myself in the still rest of Zion, and to wait in my God, expecting what He will do with me in His and my Zion. For if I only have Him, then I am in and with Him in the Eternal Sabbath, where no strife or contention of the ungodly can reach me in my *New man* <note>The spiritual self that is reborn through Christ.</note>. In the meantime, I do rejoice in this miserable *Vale of Tabernacles* <note>original: "Vale of Tabernacles"; a poetic way of describing the temporary, fragile, and often sorrowful nature of earthly life.</note>.

36. The first resurrection of the dead to the thousand-year Sabbath (which is mentioned in the book of *Revelation*) has not been sufficiently made known to me—specifically, how it should be understood—since the Scripture does not mention it elsewhere. Christ and His apostles do not give a hint of it in other places, except only John in his *Revelation*. But whether they shall be a thousand *solar years*, or how it may be interpreted, I leave it to my God, as I do not have full assurance. I leave it to those to whom God shall grant a right understanding of it, until God is pleased to open my eyes concerning these mysteries. <margin>☜</margin>

37. For these are secrets, and it does not belong to man to make conclusions about them without the command and light of God. But if anyone had knowledge and illumination regarding them from God, I would be ready and willing to learn, if I could see the basis for it in the light of nature <note>The understanding of the world through reason and observable natural laws, as opposed to direct divine revelation.</note>.

38. But since it is not right for me to hide my knowledge of it, so far as I understand it in the light of nature, I will therefore set down some suppositions or noteworthy opinions that are in my mind. I do not state these as positive affirmations, but offer them for consideration; for good and wholesome instructions may be drawn from them, and it is also profitable for man *to search in this way*. I will do it in all sincere uprightness to see if we might come somewhat closer to the matter; perhaps there may be some to whom God shall bestow such a gift, who will be stirred up to write more clearly because of it.

<margin>☛</margin>
39. First, is it certain that the world must continue for *seven thousand years*, and that one thousand of them should be a pure Sabbath? Seeing that God created all things in six days and began the rest on the sixth day toward evening (which is why the Jews begin their Sabbath on Friday evening); and *Elias* <note>Referring to the "Prophecy of Elias (Elijah)," a traditional Jewish and early Christian idea that the world would exist for 6,000 years (2,000 of chaos, 2,000 of the Law, and 2,000 of the Messiah) before a final 1,000-year Sabbath.</note> also says that the world should stand for only *six thousand years*; and Christ likewise declares that the days of tribulation shall be shortened for the sake of the elect, otherwise no man would be saved—which you apply to the fall of *Babel* <note>Often used to symbolize a state of spiritual confusion or the corrupt institutional church.</note> and to the time of *Zion*—but it seems as if *Christ* spoke of the...

<summary>The author expresses his uncertainty regarding the "thousand-year Sabbath" mentioned in Revelation, emphasizing his reliance on divine revelation over human speculation. He proposes to explore several theories about the timing of the world's end, including the tradition that the world will last six or seven thousand years.</summary>

<page-num>35</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Epistle.</header>

the fall of the *Jews*, and the end of the World, and foreshadows an evil End.

40. Also, Christ says that it shall be at the time of his coming to judgment as it was in the days of *Noah*, when men married and were given in marriage. Now we know very well (as the Scripture testifies) what kind of wicked world existed in the days of *Noah*, such that the Deluge had to come and destroy them. (*This would suggest and denote a very mean* <note>original: "meane"; here meaning unremarkable, lowly, or poor in quality</note> *Sabbath.*)

41. And though a man should otherwise explain the words of Christ concerning his coming, yet that would not be sufficient to prove it; especially since the Disciples of Christ always represent the End as being near; and *Paul* says that the end should come after *Antichrist* is revealed.

42. But that the Resurrection of the dead and the Last Judgment should be understood of both (namely, that the righteous shall arise to the thousand-year Sabbath, and among them some ungodly; and that *Gog* and *Magog* at the end of the thousand years should fight against the Saints), it seems to run quite contrary to the light of Nature <note>The "light of Nature" refers to natural reason or the inherent understanding of the physical and spiritual laws of the universe.</note>.

<margin>☞</margin>
43. For first, I cannot comprehend how the first Resurrection must come to pass, seeing the Saints shall have their works follow them, according to the words of the Spirit. Besides, we know very well that all our works are sown into the Great Mystery <note>original: "great Myſtery"; likely referring to the *Mysterium Magnum*, a term in Behmenist theology for the divine "chaos" or potentiality from which all creation proceeds</note>; that they are first brought forth into the four Elements, and so pass into the Mystery, and are reserved for the judgment of God, where all things shall be tried by fire, and that which is false shall consume in the fire; and the figures <note>The outward forms or representations of things.</note> shall fall into the Center of Nature—*namely, the dark Eternity*.

44. But if men's works shall follow them in the first Resurrection as you affirm, then God must truly move the Mystery (that is, Himself), which denotes the *Last Judgment*.

<margin>☞</margin>
45. For God has moved Himself but twice only from Eternity: once in the Creation of the World, and secondly in the Incarnation of Christ according to His heart. The first motion belongs to the Father of all beings, and the second to the Son according to God’s heart. Now, the third motion of the Holy Ghost is yet to be accomplished both in love and anger, according to all the *Three Principles* <note>In the author's mystical system, these are the three realms: the dark world (divine wrath/fire), the light world (divine love), and the outward physical world.</note>; where all whatever has been corrupted shall again be restored in the motion of the Holy Ghost, and each given to its owner.

<margin>☞</margin>
46. How then can the dead arise in their works without the motion of the Holy Spirit both in love and anger? Since the restoration of life consists only in Him. Moreover, I do not know how the first resurrection should come to pass, whether it should—

<meta>catchword: come</meta>

<summary>The author argues against a literal thousand-year earthly Sabbath, suggesting that the Resurrection and the "moving" of the Great Mystery signify the final Last Judgment rather than a preliminary event. He outlines the three major "motions" of God—Creation, Incarnation, and the future restoration by the Holy Spirit.</summary>

<page-num>86</page-num>

# The Fourth Epistle

...come to pass in the **twofold man** <note>original: "twofold man"—referring to Böhme's concept of man having both a spiritual/divine nature and a corruptible/earthly nature</note> (which cannot otherwise be understood), that is, in good and evil. But what perfect **Sabbath** <note>A state of spiritual rest or divine perfection.</note> can we hold in that state? Was not **Adam** unable to stand in it?

47. Now, if only the **new man** should arise, then he would not be within the **four elements** <note>The physical components of the material world: earth, water, air, and fire.</note> of this world. Moreover, the new body in Christ needs no resurrection; it lives eternally without any want, necessity, or death in Christ. It only waits for the time when God shall move the **Mystery** <note>original: "Myſtery"—the *Mysterium Magnum*, the hidden source or divine chaos from which all reality is manifested.</note>, where he shall *then put on the crown* of his wonders and works.

<margin>☞</margin>
48. *The manner of the Resurrection is this*: the Mystery shall restore whatever it has swallowed up. Man's works shall be placed upon him, and with them he shall pass through the fire; it shall then be tested to see what will endure the fire and what will not.

49. Now, I cannot understand how this would agree with dwelling upon the Earth. For if it should be in a paradisiacal manner that man should arise with his "wonders" <note>The spiritual fruits or deeds of a person's life.</note>, then it could not be done without the movement of the Great Mystery. Your writings say that even some wicked men shall arise; this shows that the Mystery must be moved. At that movement, the inflammation [or the Last Judgment by fire] must necessarily occur. If the Mystery were to be moved, it would not only move [awaken and raise up] a few, and that in only one source, seeing as the ungodly shall also arise.

50. Besides, you say that they shall all die at the end of the six thousand years. If so, there must then be a dwelling upon or an inhabiting of the Earth where the ungodly who arise should again marry and build. Of these, there would not be only a few (according to your opinion), but according to the Scripture, they shall be as the sand upon the seashore. Where else would **Gog and Magog** come from? Or how should they fight against the children of **Paradise**? For among the paradisiacal children, there is no strife. <margin>☞</margin>

<margin>☞</margin>
51. Also, it would not be necessary for them to die at the *END* of the six thousand years if they were to arise in the twofold body as we are now. But if they should arise in the new body, then no ungodly man can either see or touch them, just as we now *do not see Paradise*. Such is the new body: no ungodly man can fight against it.

52. What would they fight for? Are the **Saints** in Paradise? Then they make no use of the external elements, but only of the internal element, in which all four elements are held in unity. Thus, they have nothing to strive for, but they are separate in the **Source** <note>original: "Source"—likely referring to the *Qual* or the specific spiritual quality/disposition of the soul.</note>.

53. But should the ungodly die and also arise again in the four elements, this seems much more strange; but if they should arise...

<summary>Böhme argues against a literal, physical reign on Earth after the resurrection, questioning how spiritual beings in a "new body" could interact or fight with the ungodly in a material world.</summary>

<header>OF THE RESURRECTION</header>
<sig>I</sig>

<margin>☞</margin> <unclear>... in the four elements could not contain it, but the Abyss said they would still be separate as light and darkness;</unclear> what pleasure or satisfaction would God have in bringing the Saints back into the combat and source of the four elements <note>The "four elements" (earth, air, fire, water) refer to the material, mortal world, which was seen as a place of constant conflict and decay compared to the spiritual realm.</note>, to which they have been dead for so long? And yet, should they then begin to fight with the wicked? It would be much more fitting and agreeable for those who have suffered nothing here for Christ's sake; that is, for those who have not lost their lives for Christ's sake while on the Earth.

**54.** And even if you were to say that they should not fight, but that the Lord should fight for them, what pleasure could God take in raising the Saints and setting them again in the presence of the ungodly? Or would not the joy in *Abraham’s bosom* <note>original: Abrahams bosome; a biblical term for the place of comfort where the righteous dead await the resurrection.</note> be much greater than this in the four elements, from which natural strife and contention arise? But if they should dwell in Paradise without the four elements, then no strife or ungodly person can reach them.

**55.** Besides, to what end should the ungodly be upon the Earth if there is to be such a *Sabbath* <note>Refers to the "Millennial Sabbath," a literal thousand-year reign of peace on Earth predicted by some interpretations of the Book of Revelation.</note>? Their source is not in the four elements but in the <term>Abyss</term> <note>original: Abysse; in this context, the primal spiritual void or the realm of darkness/hell.</note>, where their soul goes when the body dies.

**56.** Besides, should no one dwell in the Sabbath except those who have died for Christ's sake (of which, truly, there cannot be such a number as is set down in the <term>Book of Revelation</term> <note>original: Revelation</note>) so that they would be sufficient to occupy the Earth? And should the ungodly also dwell upon the Earth and hold their *hellish Sabbath* <note>original: Hellish Sabbath</note>? This runs directly against the light of Nature.

**57.** Moreover, Christ says, *“That they shall marry, and be given in marriage, as in the days of Noah.”* Also, "two shall be grinding at one mill, and two sleeping in one bed, and one shall be taken and the other left," *when the last day shall come.*

<margin>☞</margin> **58.** Besides, Christ also says that when he comes to judge the world, *“All generations and nations shall see him and tremble before him”*; and the wicked shall wail and lament and say to the wise virgins, “Give us some of your oil.” All of this denotes a *general expectation of the last judgment* <note>original: last Judgement</note>.

**59.** For if, at the *last trumpet* <note>original: last Trumpe; the signal for the end of the world and the resurrection of the dead.</note>, two shall be lying in one bed (namely, one holy and the other ungodly), this shows no difference between them; and if the Saints are mixed with the ungodly, then truly there must be a poor Sabbath indeed.

<margin>☞</margin> **60.** When we look at the words of Christ and his Apostles, they do not agree with that idea <note>the idea of a physical thousand-year reign on Earth</note> in the least. Although mention is made of a thousand years in the <term>Book of Revelation</term>, yet this remains hidden from us; and we do not know when they may begin, or whether they have already begun. If the *first resurrection* <note>original: first Resurrection</note> be Pa— <note>The text likely continues as "Paradise" or "Paradisical" on the following page.</note>

<summary>The author argues against a literal, physical "thousand-year Sabbath" on Earth, suggesting that bringing the Saints back into the conflict of the material elements would be a step backward from the peace of Paradise. <header>The Fourth Book.</header>
<meta>The text continues from the previous page, concluding a thought about the nature of radical change or spiritual transformation.</meta>

radically; then it may be done without our knowledge.

61. They shall not live among us, nor shall they marry; for we die once to our status as *male* and *female*, and we shall not rise again as *male* and *female*. Instead, we shall live in *Paradise* in the form of *angels*, as written in *Matthew* 22:30.

62. Furthermore, the wicked, at the appearance of *Christ’s* coming, will beg the wise for the *Oil of Faith* <note>A reference to the Parable of the Ten Virgins in Matthew 25, where the foolish virgins beg for oil for their lamps.</note>; and you write that the fire of God (which is His anger and hellish source) shall be within them, and that they shall be tormented here upon the *Earth* within the four elements by the anger of God. However, the anger of God is not fully manifest in the four elements <term>four elements: The traditional physical elements of earth, air, fire, and water, which the author argues currently contain a mixture of both good and evil.</term>; for within them, good and evil are currently mixed together.

<margin>❦</margin>
63. But how can someone who is once dead to all goodness, and cannot even have a single good thought, beg the saints for *faith* and comfort? It suggests much rather that when *Christ* shall come to judge the world, everyone shall still live together in the flesh, within the four elements, where one shall be received and the other rejected. The sins of the wicked shall then come before His sight at the appearance of the severe countenance of God in the fiery zeal of the first principle <term>first principle: In this mystical context, the "first principle" refers to God's wrath or the "fire-world" that exists behind the physical world.</term>, so that the wicked person shall be terrified and would then gladly begin to be honest.

64. And although you mention that they shall only awake and not rise, this refers to the uncorrupted. Now you say that they shall live upon the *Earth* within the four elements and the saints shall live in *Paradise*. When this comes to pass, there will no longer be any strife or controversy, for they are eternally separated.

65. But if the saints were to live upon the *Earth* in *Paradise*, just as *Adam* did before the fall, and the ungodly were there to oppose them, then the saints would be in danger just as *Adam* was—they might again eat of the forbidden fruit and have to die once more.

<margin>☛</margin>
66. But if they are to be hidden from the ungodly for a thousand years and also hidden from the four elements, why should they then be revealed in the four elements at the very end so that *Gog* and *Magog* <note>Biblical names used to represent the nations or forces of evil that gather for a final battle against God's people.</note> could enter into battle with the *children of Paradise*? This agrees with neither *Scripture* nor *reason*.

67. The first epistle of Saint *Paul* to the *Corinthians*, chapter 15, does indeed teach of *Christ’s* resurrection and also of our *resurrection*, yet it does not speak of a third. It speaks first of *Christ’s* and then of *ours*; for Paul says there *that Christ is the first fruits* <note>original: "Christ is the first fruits"</note>, and then those *who belong unto Christ* will follow. This is the general *resurrection*. Although he says, *“Then comes the end,”* he does not mean a different resurrection by "the end"; rather, our resurrection is the end. This interpretation is much more logical than assuming God meant another resurrection would occur at the "end."

<summary>The author argues against a literal thousand-year reign on Earth where the saved and the wicked coexist, claiming instead that the resurrection transforms humans into angelic beings and that "the end" refers to a single, general resurrection.</summary>

or time; for just after our Resurrection comes the End of the World.

68. The dead shall first appear before the judgment before the end of this world and the four elements <term>four elements: in Boehme's time, these were earth, air, fire, and water, the building blocks of the physical world</term> arrives; for the End is the igniting of the fire and the final conclusion.

69. Also, the Apostles of Christ and all teachers from God have always represented the End as being near at hand. For *John* himself says in his epistle, *that we are in the end* <note>1 John 2:18</note>; he speaks indeed of the "last hour." But if the wicked were certain that he still had four hundred years until the End, how much more would he obsessively seek after riches for his children?

70. Besides, we are to look toward the End, for this world is defined and limited by the beginning of Creation, and then by the End where the Creation ceased—all of which was finished in six days. In such a span of time, the Mystery of God's Kingdom shall be completed; for a thousand years are like a single day to Him <note>Psalm 90:4</note>.

<margin>☞</margin>
71. Concerning the Seventh Day of Rest—whether or not the world shall yet remain at rest for a thousand years—this is hidden from mankind. We cannot certainly determine it; we must leave it to His power. I have no personal knowledge of it, seeing as the Scripture does not give clear evidence as to when the thousand years begin, what kind of years they are, or what they relate to. Therefore, I leave the matter to its own merit and will not hinder anyone who has certain knowledge or a command to teach otherwise. I offer this for your consideration, intending it for your benefit.

72. What more I might answer regarding this matter can be found sufficiently in my writings. Although I could provide a lengthy answer about it, I did not think it appropriate since this specific knowledge has not been granted to me. Consequently, I leave it alone, for I know that I must give an account for my works. I am returning your two books via the person delivering this letter and I thank you for them.

73. Concerning the End, or the deadline for *Babel's* downfall <term>Babel: Boehme used this term to represent the "city of confusion"—the corrupt institutional churches and political systems of his day</term>—namely, that *Babel* should be completely destroyed around the year 1630, according to your calculations (and although many others are of the same mind)—this likewise is not sufficiently clear to me.

<margin>☞</margin>
74. To me, indeed, it is granted to know that the time is *near*, and even now at hand, but the year and day I do not know. Therefore, I leave it to God’s counsel and to those to whom God chooses to reveal it. I cannot reach a conclusion without certain knowledge; otherwise, I would be found a liar before God.

75. But I wait for my Savior Jesus Christ and will see what He will do. If He wills that I should know it, then I will know it; if not, then I will not know it. I have committed my will, knowledge, un-

<center># The Fourth Epistle</center>

understanding, and desire unto him; he shall be my knowing, willing, and also doing; for without him there is mere danger and uncertainty.

76. Humans can hardly reach that which is before their eyes, much less that which is hidden and mystical, unless God is their light. I give you this answer out of good affection for you to consider; although I am a simple, common man, and was not trained in any worldly art [or not educated in any scholastic learning].

<margin>☞</margin>
77. But what I possess is the gift of God. I did not gain it through any art or study, but from the light of grace, which was the only thing I sought. Although my beginning was simple because of my childish understanding, since that time, God has worked something within me through his light and has opened my childish eyes.

78. As for the book *Aurora* (which is my first), it needs to be better explained in many places. At that time, full understanding was not yet born within me; for like a sudden shower that hits whatever it lands upon, so it was with the fiery instigation <note>original: "instigation"; here meaning a sudden spiritual impulse or flash of divine inspiration</note>.

79. Although I had no intention at all for anyone to read it, I wrote only of the wonders of God that were shown to me as a personal memorial for myself. It was circulated without my consent; it was taken from me by force and published without my knowledge. I intended to keep it with me as long as I lived and had no desire to be known for it among such high-ranking people, as has now happened.

80. But the Most High (in whose hands and power all things reside) had another purpose for it, as is now clear. I am informed that it is known in many cities and countries, which I both wonder at and do not wonder at. For the Lord accomplishes his work marvelously, far beyond and above all human reason, even if he should employ a shepherd <note>original: "Shepheard"; likely a reference to the author's own humble background as a shoemaker or a biblical allusion to King David's origins</note> in the work. Even though formal art and outward reason will give him no room or place, his purpose must stand against all the rages of the devil.

<margin>☞</margin>
81. And though I have not gained many days of pleasure from this, I must not resist his will because of that. I have written only according to the form as it was given to me, not according to other masters or writings.

82. Furthermore, my intent was only for myself. Although the spirit showed me how things would turn out, my heart desired nothing for itself, but committed everything to him to do with as he pleased.

83. I have not run around with it (not being called to do so) or made myself known to anyone. I can also say with truth that my own acquaintances knew the least about it; but whatever I have shown to anyone, the same...

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that his spiritual insights come from divine grace rather than formal education and describes how his first book, Aurora, was published against his original private intentions.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Aurora, divine grace, scholastic learning, spiritual inspiration, mystical writing, providence</keywords>

<page-num>63</page-num>

-># The Third Epistle.<-

... done upon earnest and persistent desire.

<margin>☞</margin>
84. Furthermore, I want you to understand—since you are reading my writings—that you should not view them as coming from a great *Master* <note>original: "Master"; here meaning a highly educated scholar or academic authority.</note>. You will not find formal "Art" <note>original: "Art"; referring to academic training, rhetoric, or worldly scholarship.</note> in them, but rather the deep earnestness of a devoted mind that thirsts after God. In that thirst, it has received great things, as the enlightened mind will clearly see. Without such light, no one can rightly know or understand them, as the *Reader* will surely discover.

85. And yet, it could not have been written more clearly or ready for understanding, even though I believe it is already clear and plain enough for such a deep subject. However, if anything seems too obscure or difficult, I could explain it in a simpler and plainer manner if it were pointed out to me.

86. There are other books besides this one written concerning the wisdom of God with a very deep sense and understanding. They treat the great depths of the wonders of God, but I do not have them at hand right now.

87. I have not given you a long answer regarding my judgment of your book about the thousand-year Sabbath and the four hundred years in *Zion* (which you believe you can prove with many passages from Holy Scripture). The reason is that I do not fundamentally or certainly know whether those *texts* can truly be applied to that meaning.

88. For there are many sayings in Scripture that seem to suggest only one *General Resurrection* of the dead, and they are very clear—especially the words of Christ in the four Gospels, which I consider to be the most certain.

89. It is the same regarding *Zion*: wickedness shall continue to the end, and though a *Zion* <note>Zion here refers to a promised period of spiritual renewal or a purified church on earth.</note> shall exist, it will not be entirely *universal*. *Babel* <note>original: "Babel"; usually referring to Babylon, symbolizing the corrupt, confused, or divided state of the worldly church.</note> shall go to ruin and take another form, yet not everyone called a child of *Zion* will truly be a child of God.

<margin>☞</margin>
90. Also, I have no personal revelation concerning the thousand-year Sabbath; I do not know enough to support it using Scripture, for we find one passage seeming to contradict another. Men may interpret the Scripture however they are inclined, but since I have no command from God regarding it, I will leave it alone and let everyone answer for their own opinion. I tell you this sincerely out of good-will, and I am, regardless, your faithful friend in the love of Christ.

91. On your forty-second and forty-third pages, where you write of the Mystery of the Souls departed [or separated], you question and cast suspicion on the opinion of *Theophrastus* <note>Likely referring to Theophrastus Paracelsus (1493–1541), a famous physician and mystic whose ideas influenced the author.</note> and others, as if they had not written correctly about the Mystery. It would be better if

<page-num>82</page-num>
# The Fourth Epistle.

...had been left out, seeing you have not understood those opinions, you say; and it seems exactly so. You shall find in my *Book of the Forty Questions* <note>original: "Booke of the Forty Questions"; this refers to Boehme's 1620 work "Forty Questions on the Soul."</note> [at approximately the thirtieth question] concerning the last Judgment, and also in other questions, sufficient and extensive information, if the same be read and rightly understood.

<margin>☞</margin>
92. There is no need of any further searching, for it is clear enough there: what the *Mystery* <note>original: "Mystery"; Boehme often uses this term to refer to the *Mysterium Magnum*, the hidden spiritual foundation of the universe from which all things emerge.</note> is that comprises both body and soul; and also what condition the separated souls are in, both regarding their expectation of the last Judgment and also, in the meantime, in respect to their habitation, source <note>original: "source"; in Boehme's vocabulary, this often implies the "quality" or "inner state" of a thing.</note>, life, and distinctions. I had thought that it was so deeply and highly grounded that the mind of man should be satisfied enough with it. If you neither have nor cannot set forth anything more fundamental, then it remains rightfully in its own place. The thousand-year *Sabbath* <note>A reference to the Millennial reign mentioned in Revelation.</note> and the four hundred years in *Zyon* <note>Zion</note> will only find fault with everything and bring it into suspicion; and though many objections might be made, they would be of no service or esteem.

<margin>☞</margin>
93. Moreover, the manifestation of the thousand-year *Sabbath* is not of much importance or concern to the world, seeing we do not have sufficient ground for it; it should rightfully rest in the *Divine Omnipotence*. We have enough in the *Sabbath* of the new birth; for that soul that obtains this *Sabbath* [of *Regeneration*] will, after the death of the earthly body, have *Sabbath* enough in *Paradise*. We may very well leave and entrust the other to the *Divine Omnipotence*, and wait on God for what he will do with us when we shall be in him and he in us.

94. For I suppose there should be a better *Sabbath* in God than in this world; and if man should dwell upon the earth in *Paradise*, then God must restore that which entered into the *Mystery* because of his curse, as is to be seen in the *Forty Questions*.

95. But as for your supposition that the righteous shall not be brought with their works before the judgment, this is contrary to the words of *Christ*, who says that all things shall be proved through the *Fire*.

<margin>☞</margin>
96. I do not say that they shall come *into* the judgment, for the judgment is for the wicked. Understand this as the judgment of *Anger* <note>In Boehme’s theology, the "Anger" is the dark, foundational principle of fire and strict justice, distinct from the light-world of love.</note>, of which the *Scripture* says the righteous—or as *Christ* says, "He that believes on me does not come into judgment." But understand by this the source or pain of the judgment; his words do hold that they shall all come together before the judgment and every one hear his sentence: *The ungodly depart hence, and the righteous come hither, etc.*

97. Also, every one shall stand forth with his own works in the *Mystery*, and they themselves shall be judged according to their works;

<meta>now</meta>

<summary>Boehme argues that his previous writings on the soul and judgment are sufficient, emphasizing that spiritual regeneration is more important than speculating about a literal thousand-year earthly Sabbath. He clarifies that while the righteous do not suffer the "pain" of judgment, they must still stand before God to be judged according to their works.</summary>
<keywords>Forty Questions, Last Judgment, Mysterium Magnum, Millennialism, Regeneration, Paradise, Divine Omnipotence, Works, Fire of Judgment</keywords>

--- Page 181 ---
<meta>Continuing from the previous page's discussion on how human works are tested by the "fire of God" before the final Sabbath of the Resurrection.</meta>

...how then shall their works in this world, which have been wrought in good and evil, be tested and separated in the fire of God? How shall they then, being unseparated, follow the saints in the Resurrection to the Sabbath and keep the Sabbath there? But if they follow them, then they must be tested and separated in the fire; then they shall have no more need to come before the judgment. But if they should keep a Sabbath without their works, then they are not perfect.

98. If we would speak of *Paradise* and understand it, then we must have clear eyes to see into it; for the internal Paradisiacal world and the external world hang within one another. We have only turned ourselves out of the internal into the external, and so we work in two worlds.

<margin>☛</margin>
99. Death cannot separate our works; the fire of God must do it. For they remain in one Mystery <term>Mystery: In Jacob Boehme’s philosophy, a "Mystery" (Mysterium) is a spiritual container or the hidden essential state where the inner reality of a person's life is stored.</term> until the judgment of God. Everyone, at the hour of the Resurrection, shall come forth in his own Mystery, and he shall see his works before him and feel them in him.

100. It is not understood that they shall answer for themselves with words, for the Kingdom of God consists and proceeds in Power. And although the ungodly shall cry out "Woe!" upon his abominations and seducers, yet every man’s work shall be summoned in **POWER**, which shall either rejoice or torment him.

101. Now the old body of this world is the Mystery of this world, and the new body is the Mystery of the Divine light-world. The soul is the Mystery of God the Father, and the Earth with the elements also has both Mysteries, which shall be moved through the Principle of the Father.

<margin>☛</margin>
102. And then the doors of the Mysteries shall be set open, and each shall give and set forth the figures <note>original: "Figures"; likely meaning the spiritual forms, patterns, or records of past actions.</note> which it has swallowed up; for the Principle of the soul must stand before the judgment with both Mysteries.

<margin>☛</margin>
103. Happy are they who shall have the body of Christ in the Mystery of the wrath <note>original: "fire of Gods anger"</note>. They shall have the soul's fire (the Principle of the Father) surrounded with the light-world and illuminated with the Divine Majesty. They shall feel no pain or hurt and shall pass through the fire without sensing it. There, the outward or third principle <term>third principle: The physical, material world as perceived by the senses.</term> shall be tested; and all earthliness or falsehood shall remain in the fire. But the works shall be renovated <note>original: "renovated"; meaning renewed or restored to a pure, spiritual state.</note> in the fire and freed from their earthly source and soil. Then the earthly Mystery remains in the fire and is food for the fire, from which the light arises; and the righteous person loses nothing.

<summary>This page describes the spiritual process of the Last Judgment, where human works are tested by divine fire to separate earthly falsehood from spiritual truth. The author explains that the internal and external worlds coexist, and at the Resurrection, a person's true essence (their "Mystery") will be revealed through power rather than words.</summary>
<keywords>Resurrection, Last Judgment, Fire of God, Mystery, Jacob Boehme, Paradise, Third Principle, Divine Light, Salvation</keywords>

--- Page 182 ---
<meta>The previous page discussed the nature of the soul and the body after death, focusing on how different elements of the human being return to their respective sources or "principles." The catchword "less" connects to the preceding discussion of the soul's state.</meta>

<margin>☛</margin>
104. For the works of Love, which broke forth in the new body, pass through the power of the soul through the fire and remain in the divine Image in the source of the light. But those works of the third Principle <note>original: "third Principle"; in the author's cosmology, this refers to the visible, material world</note>, that is, of this world, remain in the source of the soul.

105. But whatever has been wrought and acted in a completely evil and malicious manner in the third Principle—and yet has not been renewed in this world by earnest repentance and reconciliation toward one's brother—that falls into the **center of Nature**, which is the root of the **dark world**.

106. But the works of the ungodly shall not be able to remain in the fire; for the fire swallows them down into itself to the dark center—namely, the origin of nature where the devils dwell. And there also goes their souls' fire, being the Father's principle <note>In Böhme's system, the "Father's principle" represents the raw, fiery power of the divine will, which can become wrath if not tempered by the "Son's principle" of light and love</note>. For that fire of the soul shall have no matter to make it burn properly, but it shall be like a quenched, dark, painful source-fire—only an anguish that would gladly produce fire. This is called **God's wrath** (and not a principle), a death or a dying source.

107. For the principle of the Father, in which the right and true soul consists, is a flaming fire that gives light; and in that light is the precious Image of God. For that light does temper <note>original: "qualify"; meaning to modify or give specific qualities to</note> and sweeten the burning light with the essence of **Love**, so that it is a pleasing delight and a cause of **nature** and of **life**.

<margin>☛</margin>
108. Therefore, I tell you that you should not think it strange or misunderstand it when I or any other (let it be <term>Theophrastus</term> <note>Referring to Theophrastus Paracelsus, the famous 16th-century physician and occultist</note> or whoever it may be) write that man shall stand before the Judgment **with the body which he had here**. I perceive very well that you have not yet understood my writings in the *Book of the Threefold Life* and also in the *Book of the Incarnation of Jesus Christ* (which treats of Christ's suffering, dying, and rising again, and how we must enter into his death and arise out of his death). You shall find it explained and enlarged clearly enough there. But since you do not have them at hand yet, please have patience; you may very likely get to read them, and then you will be freed from your confusion and deep searching in this manner.

109. For they lay their foundations much deeper than your current understanding is in this. Only read them correctly and you shall truly find what the Mystery is, what the <term>magical Bysse</term> <note>Bysse (from the Greek "byssos") refers to a foundation or ground of being</note> and <term>Abysse</term> <note>The "Abyss" or "Unground"—the bottomless void or nothingness out of which God and the world emerge</note> are, and also what the Being of all Beings is. There is no need to consult with one person or another; he who understands the Great Mystery <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"; the source of all manifestation</note> from which all beings have proceeded and still proceed does not burden himself with such large, roundabout complexities.

110. You have undertaken a very hard labor which does nothing but perplex, eat up, and consume your life; it is completely unnecessary.

<summary>Jacob Böhme explains how human actions and the soul's "fire" are categorized into different spiritual principles after death, while advising the reader to study his deeper works on the "Great Mystery" to avoid unnecessary intellectual struggle.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Threefold Life, Third Principle, God's Wrath, Theophrastus Paracelsus, Bysse, Abysse, Great Mystery, Body of the Resurrection</keywords>
