<meta>This page continues the author's mystical discourse on the nature of divine and earthly "Mysteries," focusing on the spiritual transformation of the individual and the hidden nature of sacred knowledge.</meta>

<page-num>65</page-num>

<margin>☛</margin>
... he who finds it, and knows the great Mystery, finds all things within it; these require no literal demonstration. God, Christ, and eternity with all its wonders lie within it; the *Holy Spirit* is the Key to it. Are you in the "new birth" as you claim? Then there is no need for such difficult seeking with such hard labor. Seek only Christ in the Manger, in the dark Stable; when you find him, then you shall indeed find where he sits at the right hand of God.

111. Searching alone does nothing; the *Philosopher's Stone* is a very dark, disrespected stone of a gray color, but within it lies the highest *Tincture* <note>original: "Tincture" — in alchemy and Böhme's philosophy, this refers to a spiritual essence or power that can transform and heal.</note>. If you would search out the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum" — the hidden, divine source of all things.</note>, then take before you only the Earth with its metals, and so you may well find what the magical or Cabalistic <note>original: "Cabalisticall" — relating to Kabbalah, a tradition of Jewish mysticism often adapted by Christian mystics.</note> ground is.

<margin>☛</margin>
112. The deep and mystical numbers, which otherwise no man is able to fathom or find out, all lie in the Mystery. But he who finds it does not search after the numbers; he treats gold as if it were earth. He acts as one who has a costly treasure lying in an obscure place. The manger and swaddling clothes of Christ are more acceptable to him than the whole world with its figures [or external pomp and glory]. He hides the *numbers themselves*, for the outward kingdom must accomplish its wonders.

<margin>* Magi.</margin>
113. Why should the earthly Mystery be unveiled before its time? Enquire of the *Magi* <note>original: "Magists" — practitioners of Magia, or spiritual wisdom.</note>, who have understood the heavenly and earthly *Magia*; why have they kept the *Tincture* secret and not revealed it? There is no other cause at all but that the world is not worthy of it; likewise, the world is not worthy of the *Numbers of the Mystery*.

<margin>Note.</margin>
114. Therefore God has hidden them from us, so that the earthly Mystery might accomplish and fulfill all its wonders in us, and that all the vials <note>original: "Violls" — referring to the "vials of wrath" in the Book of Revelation.</note> of God’s anger might be poured forth on us. How can a man undertake to reveal such secret things *without the consent of the Mystery*? Indeed, he tampers with the outside of the Mystery, but if he wishes to enter, he must have the will of the Mystery.

115. The outward instigation <note>original: "instigacion" — an impulse or urging.</note> to manifest and reveal the Mystery proceeds from the *stars*. For they would gladly be freed from vanity, and they drive mightily in the *Magical Children* to manifestation; therefore we must test and examine the impulse, whether it proceeds from God's light, from God's spirit, or from the dominion and government of the stars.

<margin>❦</margin>
116. For God’s spirit speaks plainly of his Mystery; he only declares the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba" — a term used by Jacob Böhme to describe a state of inward disturbance, wrath, or "turmoil" in nature that leads to destruction or transformation.</note>, and leaves the *numbers* alone. He has once signed and sealed the Mystery with the power of the first principle in the *Seven Forms of Nature* to the wonders of God; and again he has signed it in Love in the humanity of Christ, with the *Seven Golden Candlesticks and lights*; and with these he continues

<summary>This page argues that spiritual truth and the "Great Mystery" are found through humble faith in Christ rather than intellectual searching or alchemy. The author explains that God hides the deepest secrets (the "Numbers") from the world because it is unworthy and must first undergo its own internal transformation.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the conclusion of the Fourth Epistle, where the author discusses the timing of divine revelations and the use of symbolic language.</meta>

until the Judgment; each number manifests itself in its own Age. No creature has the power to manifest the same, for he who has it dares not; otherwise, he transgresses the magical order and becomes an abomination to the Mystery.

<margin>Note ☞</margin>
117. And therefore the Prophets, and also Christ himself, have spoken entirely in parables, in a magical <note>original: "Magicall"; here referring to the mystical or spiritual symbolic language used to convey divine truths</note> manner. Even to this day, no one who is capable of understanding the Mystery dares to speak otherwise, unless there is a specific purpose of God for the Number to be plainly revealed. For example, *Daniel* clearly indicated the time of Christ with its own Number, as he was commanded to do. I tell you this sincerely and in all faithfulness, and also in true Christian love toward you—not out of contempt, but from my knowledge and gifts, since you requested it of me. I have given you a short hint of what you are to do in this matter, and I entreat you to look upon it in a brotherly way.

118. However, in whatever way I am able to serve you with my modest gifts, if you desire further assistance, it shall be done with good will—provided I perceive you are in earnest and that it will serve the honor of God and the welfare of mankind. And so, I commend you into the love of ***Jesus Christ.***

<meta>This page begins "The Fifth Epistle" of the collection, addressed to Paul Keym. It follows the conclusion of a previous letter or treatise.</meta>

<page-num>67</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>
<sig>K 2</sig>

# ANOTHER
# LETTER
# TO
# PAUL KEYM:

---

## Concerning the way to true Knowledge, and the Regeneration in CHRIST:

---

### Likewise concerning the thousand-year <term>Sabbath</term> <note>Also known as the Millennium, a period of peace and spiritual reign mentioned in the Book of Revelation.</note>; and how the *Mysteries* in the *Revelation* are to be understood.

---

->***Our Salvation in Christ Jesus.***<-

---

<margin>1.</margin> WORTHY and much-respected Sir, and beloved Brother in Christ; I have received your last letter, and in it I have once more understood and well-observed your zealous, inflamed mind in your intended labor and hard study. Secondly, I have noted your anxious, earnest desire for the light of true knowledge; and thirdly, your great thirst for the fountain and well-spring of Christ, in which the mind is refreshed, quickened <note>original: "quickned"; meaning to be brought to life or energized.</note>, comforted, satisfied, and appeased. Considering that I am a servant to my brothers, and no less than a debtor to them in the love of Christ, I shall, in that same love, show and impart to you what I know and what has been given to me, since your desire also requires it.

<summary>In this opening of the fifth epistle, the author acknowledges Paul Keym's earnest spiritual seeking and expresses a duty to share insights on regeneration and biblical mysteries.</summary>
<keywords>Paul Keym, Jacob Boehme, spiritual knowledge, regeneration, Thousand-year Sabbath, Book of Revelation, Christ Jesus</keywords>

--- Page 186 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's discourse on the "Fifth Birth," focusing on the necessity of spiritual union with Christ to achieve true divine understanding, contrasting it with limited human reason.</meta>

# The Fifth Birth

<margin>☛</margin>
2. Christ said, *I am the vine, you are the branches; he that abides in me, and I in him, shall bring forth much fruit; for without me you can do nothing*; also, he that abides in me and has my words abiding in him, he brings forth much fruit. Herein lies the whole ground, and it is the only root or spring to the fountain from which the *Divine understanding* flows. There is no other ground for the true and real knowledge of the wisdom of God; no other seeking, studying, or searching is of any use.

3. For every spirit searches only its own depth—for it reaches no further than its own natural capacity or instinct—and apprehends that in which it kindles itself. Although it may search within its own kindling, yet it finds nothing more than a type or representation of things, like a shadow or a dream. It is not able to behold the *Being* itself; for if it would see the *Being*, then it must be in the *Being*, and the *Being* in it, so that it may be capable of the *Being* and see truly within the *Being* itself.

4. Now then, seeing that we are dead in *Adam* to the Divine essence and have become blind and estranged, we have no power in ourselves. We know nothing of God through our Reason, but only the history—that there is a God. We neither feel his power nor see his light unless we return and become like children, who know nothing but are guided and ruled. Just as a child looks upon its mother and longs for her, and she in turn cherishes and brings it up, so must the external reason be blinded, beaten down, and quite quashed <note>original: "quasht"; meaning suppressed or completely silenced</note>.

5. And the desire must resign and cast itself into the grace and love of God, and not regard the opposition and contradiction of the outward reason, which says: "It is not so; God is far off; you must search, meditate, and represent him to yourself only by your own mental grasp; you must seek after his will, and how he has revealed himself; *so he will be known, and no otherwise*." This is how the external, historical, <term>Astral reason</term> <note>In the author's mystical philosophy, "Astral reason" refers to the intellect governed by the stars or the natural, fallen world, rather than divine revelation.</note> judges; and it also rules the whole world, except for a very small number of God's children.

<margin>* How he has revealed himself in his Word.</margin>
6. Christ said, *you must abide in me; for without me you can do nothing; you can neither know nor search out anything truly and fundamentally of God.* For *he that comes to me, him I will in no wise cast out; in me you shall bring forth much fruit; now every branch grows on its own tree and has the sap, power, influence, virtue, and property of the tree,* and bears fruit according to the quality, kind, and property of the tree.

<margin>☛</margin>
7. Thus likewise, he who desires to be taught by God and to have Divine knowledge must stand in the tree into which God has grafted us through <term>Regeneration</term> <note>The process of spiritual rebirth.</note>; he must have the sap and virtue of the

<summary>This page argues that true divine knowledge cannot be achieved through human logic or "Astral reason," but only through a spiritual union with Christ, likened to a branch drawing sap from a vine.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Divine understanding, John 15, Vine and Branches, Astral reason, Regeneration, humility, mystical theology</keywords>

--- Page 187 ---
<meta>This page continues a discussion on the spiritual "New Birth," using the metaphor of a tree and its fruit to describe the relationship between the soul and its divine source.</meta>

<page-num>69</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>

...the tree; otherwise he brings forth strange, unsavory, wild fruit, which does not have the taste and relish of the good tree. We must become like a child who understands nothing, but only knows its mother and longs for her.

<margin>☛</margin>
8. We must drink of the new milk of *Christ’s Incarnation* <note>Incarnation: the doctrine of God becoming human in the form of Jesus Christ</note> so that we may be made partakers of his flesh and spirit. His virtue and sap <note>sap: the vital life-force of a plant; here used metaphorically for spiritual life</note> must be our virtue and sap; we must become *God’s Children* through *Divine Eating and Drinking*.

9. *Nicodemus* <note>A Pharisee in the Gospel of John who questioned Jesus about how one can be "born again"</note> said, "*How can it be that a man should be born again in his old age?*" Yes, good *Nicodemus*, and good external earthly Reason: how could it be that *Adam*, who was a perfect image of God, perished in his perfection and became earthly? Did it not come to pass through *Imagination* <note>original: "Imagination" — in this context, the power of the mind to focus desire and thereby shape one's internal reality</note>, because he directed his desire, longing, and lust into the outward astral, elemental, and earthly kingdom [and set his mind upon the dominion of this world]? Whereupon he, in his desire, lust, and imagination, was immediately impregnated and became earthly. Thereby he fell into the sleep of the external *Magia* <note>original: "Magia" — the hidden or "magical" power of the soul's will to create or transform</note>; and so it is also with the *New birth*.

<margin>☛</margin>
10. Through *Imagination* and an earnest, serious desire, we become again impregnated by the Deity and receive the new body within the old. The new does not mix itself with the old, just as gold in a coarse and rough stone is quite another thing and has a different *Tincture* <note>original: "Tincture" — an alchemical term referring to the essential spiritual quality or "dye" that gives a substance its character</note> and *spirit* than the rough matter of the stone. Thus also is the new man within the old: the rough stone knows nothing of the gold, and likewise the earthly *Adam* knows nothing of the *Divine*, heavenly *Adam*. Therefore, there is strife within man, and man is at odds with himself.

11. The earthly *Adam* wants to see, feel, and taste, but he receives only a ray, a symbol, and a twinkling reflection from the internal man. At some times, he does indeed taste something of the Divine man, but not in essence. It is like the light of the sun, which disperses or swallows up the gloomy darkness so that it appears as if there were no darkness left at all; and yet the darkness is really hidden within the light, and becomes manifest again when the sunlight withdraws.

<margin>☛</margin>
12. Thus, the new man often swallows up the old through Divine power, so that the old man supposes he has grasped the Deity, whereas he is not capable of that essence. Instead, the Spirit of God from the new man passes through the old. But when that Spirit enters again into its Mystery <note>original: "Mystery" — the hidden, spiritual source or inner reality</note>, the old man does not know what happened to him. He seeks ways to come to God and searches for the purpose and will of God, yet finds nothing but invention, fiction, and opinion. He is very zealous in his opinions and does not know what he is doing; he find-

The author argues that just as Adam became earthly through focusing his desire on the world, a person is reborn by focusing their "imagination" and desire on the Divine.</summary>

--- Page 188 ---
<meta>This page continues a discussion on the spiritual "root" of the human soul and the distinction between the temporal (external) and eternal (internal) aspects of being.</meta>

...does not reach the root; for it is not capable or worthy of it; and this shows that it must die and perish.

<margin>☞</margin>
13. But the New Man <note>original: "New man," referring to the spiritual self reborn in Christ</note>, who arises through an earnest, serious will and purpose by means of the imagination <gloss>referring here to the effective operation of true faith</gloss>, remains steadfast in the rest of Christ. This "New Man" exists even in the Tree—which God the Father, by His movement when He moved Himself a second time according to His heart, grafted into the human soul. It springs forth in the life of God and grows and flourishes in the power, virtue, and sap of the Divine Essentiality <term>God's spiritual substance or being</term> within God’s love. This New Man receives divine knowledge and skill, not according to the desires of the external will (what the external man wants to know and investigate), but according to the measure of the internal heaven. The internal heaven ignites and enlightens the external, so that the understanding or intellectual faculty of the soul comprehends and understands the external world.

14. For God, who is both a spirit and a Being, has manifested Himself through the external world as a likeness <note>original: "similitude"</note>, so that the spirit might see itself essentially within that Being. Not only this, but also so that the creature <note>humanity or any created being</note> might contemplate and behold the being of God in its outward form and come to know it.

15. For no creature is able to see the Being of God outside of itself. The spirit sees God in the essence and brilliance <note>original: "Lustre"</note> of His majesty, and likewise sees Him within itself and within fellow creatures like itself. For God is Himself the spirit of all beings (meaning heavenly beings); so, when we see a divine creature, we see an image or likeness that proceeded from God’s Being. When we see the will and work of that creature, we see the will and work of God.

16. In this way, the New Man is born of God. Whatever it wills and does is God’s will and work; its knowledge is God’s knowledge, for we can know nothing of God without God’s Spirit.

17. The external world cannot see the internal world. However, if the internal draws the external into itself by a glimpse <gloss>an influence of light in its own idea or contemplation</gloss>, then the external world perceives the mirror or resemblance of the internal for instruction and direction. This shows that the external world takes its rise and origin from the internal, and that *our works shall follow us into the Mystery* <note>A reference to Revelation 14:13; "the Mystery" refers to the eternal source or "Mysterium Magnum"</note>. Through the separation of God’s judgment and the "fire of the Principle" <term>the spiritual force that separates the eternal from the temporal</term>, these works shall be placed into the eternal world.

<margin>☞</margin>
18. To this end, God created angels and humans for the sake of His wondrous deeds—so that the wisdom of the *Divine Power* might appear, and so that God might behold Himself in the resemblances and ideas of His creatures, finding joy in Himself through the beings created out of His own wisdom.

<summary>This page explores the spiritual rebirth of the "New Man," arguing that true divine knowledge comes from the internal heaven within the soul rather than external study. It explains that the physical world is a reflection of the eternal, created so that God may behold His own wisdom and wonders through His creatures.</summary>
<keywords>New Man, Divine Essentiality, Internal Heaven, Jakob Böhme, Mysticism, Divine Wisdom, Creation, spiritual optics</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's response to a correspondent struggling to understand deep spiritual concepts. The previous page likely established the correspondent's frustrations with the difficulty of the author's writings.</meta>

<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>
<page-num>78</page-num>

# The Fifth Epistle.

19. Loving brother, do not take it poorly that I speak frankly to you. You complain that you are not always able to reach, comprehend, or retain the divine mysteries. Furthermore, you say that you often get only a glimpse of them, and that my writings are *hard* and difficult for you to understand. I will therefore show you—according to the power and ability I have received from God—the nature of your hidden mystery, which you are currently unable to understand.

<margin>☞</margin>
20. Your intent and will are to keep [the light of the mystery] in a constant, steady understanding. This is the will of the *external* world within you; it longs to be capable of the Deity and to be freed from vanity. But the spirit of the external world must remain in constant labor and earnest *seeking*. For through its seeking, it finds the wonders of its own *Magia* <term>Magia: In this context, it refers to the creative or attractive power of the will that brings spiritual ideas into a tangible form or "image."</term>—namely, the pattern and resemblance of the internal world.

21. For God does not always move Himself, but the longing and earnest labor of the creature moves the *mystery*, so that the image or idea of divine wisdom may be sought and found. Therefore, Christ commands us to *seek and knock*, and at the same time promises to give us the pearl <note>original: "Pearle," referring to the "Pearl of Great Price" from the Gospel of Matthew, symbolizing the supreme value of the Kingdom of Heaven.</note> or jewel in the seeking.

22. The external world is also of God and from God. Man was created into the external world for this purpose: that he might bring external figures into the internal world, and that he might bring the end back into the beginning.

<margin>☞</margin>
23. The more a person longs for God, and the more they *pant* and run after Him, the more they come out of the end and into the beginning. This is not only for God's wonder but for their own edification. For the twig of a tree continually thirsts after the sap and power <note>original: "vertue," meaning inherent life-force or efficacy.</note> of the tree; it labors in desire for the tree and draws its sap and influence into itself, thus growing into a great branch. In this way, the anxious hunger and earnest longing in the *human mystery* draw the Kingdom of God into themselves. Regarding this, Christ said: *The Kingdom of Heaven suffers violence, and the violent take it by force.* <note>A reference to Matthew 11:12, interpreted here as the spiritual intensity required to "grasp" divine reality.</note>

24. A being or essence that is not attractive—that cannot draw things to itself—cannot grow or gain a body; instead, it starves and pines away. We see how the flame of a candle draws the fat <note>original: "fat," referring to the tallow or wax of the candle.</note> into itself and consumes it, yet from that consumption, it produces a shining light. So it is with man: he is shut up and enclosed (with his original divine essence) in the darkness of death, but God has opened that essence again to the soul through Christ.

25. Now the poor, captive soul is this very hungry *magical* fire, which again attracts the revealed divine essence to itself out of the *incarnation* of Christ; and so it feeds on God’s being.

<meta>The catchword "ing" at the bottom of the page likely leads into the word "incorporating" or "ingrafting" on the next page, continuing the metaphor of spiritual nourishment.</meta>

<summary>The author explains that the difficulty of understanding divine mysteries is a natural result of the struggle between the external and internal worlds, and that spiritual growth requires an active, "violent" longing to draw God's essence into the soul.</summary>
<keywords>divine mysteries, Magia, spiritual seeking, internal vs external world, incarnation, Jacob Boehme, spiritual growth, soul as fire</keywords>

--- Page 190 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's metaphysical explanation of the soul's nature, comparing its "eating" of spiritual essence to a candle's fire, and discussing the consequences of the Fall of Adam.</meta>

...ing, and takes it into itself, and from this [spiritual and essential] eating, consuming, or digesting, it gives forth a body of light, which is both like the Deity and capable of receiving it. Thus the poor soul becomes clothed with a body of light, like the fire in a candle, and in this body of light it finds rest. But in the darkness of this world [in its earthly carcass and clothing of clay, where the curse of God and all evil inclinations and false desires stick], it has anguish and trouble.

26 But now, since it is so that the soul has, along with Adam, put upon itself the earthly image, it must therefore bear it. Just as the fire of a candle must take its burning light from the dark lump of fat; if it had remained with Adam in God’s being and had not put on the earthly image, it would not have needed to bear it, but now it is bound to do so.

27 For Saint Paul says, *To whom you give yourselves as servants in obedience, his servants you are; whether it be to sin, leading to death, or to the obedience of God, leading to righteousness.* <note>Romans 6:16</note> Now seeing the soul has put on the earthly image, which produces nothing but fruit for death, and has yielded and devoted itself as a servant to sin, it has therefore now become the sinful servant of death.

28 Why has it fallen in love with a strange master that domineers over it? Had it only remained a child and not lusted after the tree of knowledge of good and evil, it would not then have needed to be in subjection to both governments <note>Referring to the conflicting realms of light/love and darkness/anger</note>. But because it wanted to be like God in both love and anger, according to both the <term>Principles of Eternity</term> <note>original: "Principles of Eternity"; in Boehme’s philosophy, these are the dark/fire world and the light/love world</note>, it must now bear the image and undergo the force and sway of both. Thus it must endure the burning fire [the curse and anger of God ignited in the divided properties of nature] until the day of separation.

<margin>☞</margin>
29 Therefore it is called "bearing the cross," for when the <term>Magical fire</term> <note>The primal, spiritual will or desire that "ignites" existence</note> arises, it creates a <term>Cross-like birth</term> <note>original: "Crosse-like-birth"; Boehme's term for the intersection of spiritual forces where the "light" is born out of the "dark" fire</note>; and one form of nature presses and completely pierces through the other. That is, one is contrary to the other, such as sweet against sour, sharp against bitter, and the fire against them all.

<margin>* Oh, thing.</margin>
30 And if the soul had let the body of light be its only Lord and Master, and had not focused its imagination on the <term>external kingdom of this world</term> (that is, on the spirit of the great world in the stars and elements) nor lusted after earthly fruit, then the wrath [or the working power of darkness in the curse of God, which is the departure of His love from a *thing] would have been, as it were, swallowed up in it. [It would not have been manifest or apparent.] There would not have been any sense, perception, or feeling of it. But since the soul has departed from the gentleness <note>original: "meeknesse"</note> of the light and gone out of the love of God, it now feels the wrath, or burning anger, of the eternal nature.

<summary>Boehme explains how the soul's desire for the "earthly image" forced it into a state of conflict between God's love and His wrath, described as a "cross-like birth" of competing natural properties. He argues that by turning away from God's light toward the material world, the soul became subject to the suffering and "burning anger" of eternal nature.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, soul, Adam, Fall of Man, body of light, Principles of Eternity, Magical fire, Cross-like birth, wrath of God, dualism, mysticism</keywords>

31. And therefore it must work, labor, and endeavor to obtain the light again; which is why the life of man stands in such anguish, in painful seeking, in continual abstinence and repentance. It earnestly desires the Divine Rest, yet is held back by the wrath of Nature <note>original: "wrath [ or fierceness of Nature ]"</note>.

32. The more the life desires to fly from the wrath of nature, the more strong and vehement the strife grows within that life. This is in addition to what the Devil stirs up and brings into his *nest* <note>original: "being the center of the soul"</note> through his poisonous incantations, magical imaginations, representations, and insinuations. He continually represents the magical image of the poisonous serpent before the soul, so that the soul might still focus its imagination upon it and kindle or inflame itself in that same poison, as happens daily; thus the fire of the soul becomes an evil, poisonous, burning brimstone fire.

33. Yet if the soul departs from the serpent-like image of the Devil and rejects the evil earthly tree <insert>on which the serpent has cunningly twined himself</insert>—which *tree* is pride, covetousness, envy, anger, and falsehood—and does not long after it, but makes itself as it were dead in this figure, as if it knew nothing of it; and if it casts away the very <term>concupiscence</term> <note>original: "concupiscence"; here meaning strong, misplaced worldly desire</note> and imagination itself, and desires only the love of God, submitting itself wholly to God’s will and working, so that He may be its only willing, working, and doing; then the Divine light begins to shine in it. It obtains an eye of true seeing, so that it is able to behold its own natural form and features, by which it steps into plain, straightforward, and meek humility.

<margin>☛</margin>
34. It wills nothing, and it also desires nothing, but resigns and casts itself into the bosom of its mother; like a child that desires nothing but its mother, inclining itself to her and longing only after her. It does not highly value any art, subtle reason, or much knowledge; and though it knows much, yet it is not puffed up or elevated in its own conceit by its knowledge. Instead, it leaves and resigns the knowing, willing, and working wholly to its Mother’s spirit, so that it might be both the will and the work within it.

35. I speak according to my knowledge: the Devil, in the power of God’s anger, continually shoots against and opposes this precious sprout of the soul <note>original: "or noble twig of Divine light and love, springing forth from the Tree of Life, *Christ*, *within us*"</note>. He casts vain insinuations, false desires, and earthly imaginations toward the root of nature—that is, toward the forms of the fire-life in the <term>first principle</term> <note>The "first principle" in Boehme's thought refers to the foundational, energetic root of existence, characterized by a "fire-life" or "wrath" that must be tempered by light.</note>—to enkindle them in their own natural working properties, which are self-pride, covetousness, envy, anger, and falsehood...

<summary>This page describes the spiritual struggle of the soul as it seeks to escape the "wrath of nature" and the Devil's influence to find Divine Rest. Boehme emphasizes the need for total resignation to God's will, comparing the soul's ideal state to that of a humble child.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual warfare, resignation, Gelassenheit, pride, humility, fire-life, first principle, Jacob Boehme, Christ within</keywords>

hypocrisy, lust, etc.] and would continually by all means completely destroy the precious sprout [or noble twig of grace]. He continually shoots his evil, poisonous rays into the soul's *Magical* fire <note>In Böhme’s thought, "Magical fire" refers to the soul's essential desire and will, which acts as a fire that can either burn with divine light or dark lust.</note> with evil lusts, desires, and thoughts; and provides strange matter or fuel for the soul's fire to burn or feed upon, so that it might by no means attain a shining light; he quenches, suppresses, and hinders it, so that his Kingdom might not be known.

36. But on the other side, the noble twig defends itself and will have nothing to do with the fierce, dark, and wrathful source; it arises and springs forth like a plant out of the wild earth, yet the Devil strives continually against it.

<margin>☛</margin>
37. Therefore, my dearly beloved friend, there is such strife and contention in man; and hence he sees the *Divine* light as in a mirror, and sometimes he gets a perfect glimpse of it. For as long as the twig of the soul can defend itself against the poison of the Devil, so long it has the shining light.

38. For when the *Magical* fire of the soul receives the *Divine* essence (that is the divine body, *Christ’s* flesh), then the Holy Spirit clearly arises and shines forth in the soul as a triumph, as He goes forth from *God* the Father through the word or mouth of the Son (that is, from the Heart of the sacred *Ternary* <note>original: "Ternary," referring to the Holy Trinity</note>) out of the *Divine* essentiality. And thus He goes or proceeds forth out of the being or essence of the noble lily-twig, which springs forth and grows out of the fire of the soul; which (lily-twig) is the true *Image of God*, for it is the newborn or regenerated spirit of the soul, the spirit of God’s will—the triumphing chariot of the Holy Spirit, in which He rides into the sacred *Ternary* into the *Angelical World*.

39. And with this twig, or Image [of *God* renewed in us], as previously mentioned, we are in *Christ* outside of this world, in the *Angelical World*, of which the old *Adam* <note>The "old Adam" refers to the fallen, sinful human nature inherited from the first man.</note> has no understanding or perception; also, he does not know it, just as the rough stone does not know the gold that grows within it.

### *The Gate of the True Knowledge of the Threefold Life.*

<margin>☛</margin>
40. Man is the true similitude or image of *God*, as the precious man *Moses* testifies; not only an earthly image (for the sake of which *God* would not have become man and put forth, united, and espoused His heart and spirit [in deepest love] to it after the Fall), but he is originally out of the Being of all beings; out of all the three worlds: *namely* <note>original: "Viz." (videlicet)</note>, out of the innermost nature-world, which is also the outermost; and is called the *dark world*, from which the principle of the *fiery nature* takes its rise, as is declared at large in my *Book of the Threefold Life.*

<meta>This page continues the author's theological explanation of the soul's fall from its original divine state and its current struggle within the physical world. It emphasizes the necessity of spiritual rebirth and the soul's role as a "laborer" in the material realm to reveal divine wonders.</meta>

# The Fifth Epistle.

41. And secondly, he is out of the light, or *Angelic World*, out of the true *Being of God*; and then thirdly, he is out of this external world of the sun, stars, and elements. He is an entire image of God, yet outside the *Being of all Beings*.

42. His first image stood in *Paradise*, in the *Angelic World*; but he lusted after the *external world* (that is, after the *Astral* <note>The "Astral World" refers to the realm of the stars and celestial influences that Boehme believed governed the physical body and mind.</note> and *Elemental world*), which has swallowed up and covered the precious image of the internal Heaven, and now rules in that likeness as if it were its own property.

43. Therefore *Christ* said: *You must be born again, or else you cannot see the Kingdom of God*. And therefore it is that the Word or Heart of God entered into the *human essence*, so that we, with our souls, might be able—through the power of the Word or Heart of God—to beget and bring forth again out of our soul a new twig or image, like the first one.

44. Therefore the old *body* <note>original: "Carcasse"</note> must rot, putrefy, and perish; for it is unfit for the *Kingdom of God*. It carries nothing but its own "Mystery" into its first beginning—that is, its wonders and works—understood in the essence of the first Principle, which is immortal and incorruptible, being the *magical fire* <note>In Boehme's thought, "magical fire" refers to the spiritual hunger or desire that constitutes the soul's core energy.</note> of the soul.

45. And not this alone, but he must bring and unite the *End* with the *Beginning*; for the external world is generated out of the internal and created into a physical <note>original: "comprehensible"</note> being. The wonders of this world belong to the Beginning, and they were known from eternity in the wisdom of God—that is, in the *Divine Magia* <term>Magia</term> <note>Boehme uses "Magia" to describe the supernatural, creative power of the will that brings forth substance from spirit.</note>—not in the physical substance, but in the mirror of the virgin-like wisdom of God, from which the *eternal nature* always arises [or takes its origin] from eternity.

<margin>Or, bring forth.</margin>
46. And to this end, the poor soul stands in the prison of the Astral and Elemental kingdom so that it might be a *laborer* and reunite the wonders of external nature with the World of Light, and bring them back into the beginning. And though the soul must now be bruised, pressed, and endure much, yet it is the *servant* in God’s vineyard, which prepares the precious wine that is drunk in the Kingdom of God. It is the only cause of understanding; the desire works in the Mystery, and manifests and brings forth to light the hidden wonders of God, just as we see plainly how man searches out and reveals the wonders of nature.

47. Therefore, we must not be amazed or strangely perplexed when the noble image is often hidden so that we cannot receive any refreshment or comfort. We must know that the poor soul is then being put into the vineyard, so that it should work and bring the fruit to be set upon *God’s Table*.

48. It then has a twig or branch of the wild vine given to it,

<summary>Boehme explains that man's soul is currently "imprisoned" in the physical and astral realms, but through Christ's intervention and spiritual rebirth, it serves as a laborer tasked with manifesting God's hidden wonders.</summary>

...it; it must trim and dress it so that it may shine into the divine and heavenly mystery; it must unite it with the kingdom of God. This is to be understood thus:

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* Instructions or admonitions.</margin>
49. As a plant or graft <note>original: "grift"</note> that is set works so long until it puts forth its branches and then its fruit, so must the twig <gloss>[ graft, or scion ]</gloss> of the soul, which stands overshadowed in a dark valley, continually labor so that it might come to bring forth fruit. This fruit is the noble and precious knowledge of God; when the same has grown in it, so that the soul knows God, then it yields its fair fruits, which are good ***doctrines, works, and virtues***. It leads to the kingdom of God; it helps to plant and build the kingdom of God; and then it is a true laborer in the vineyard of Christ.

50. And thus, that which I teach, write, and speak is nothing else but the same which has been wrought in me; otherwise, I could know nothing of it. I have not scraped it together out of histories and so formed opinions, as the ***Babylonical*** <note>original: "Babilonicall"; likely referring to institutional churches or academic theologians that Boehme viewed as confusing and corrupt, like the Tower of Babel</note> school does, where men eagerly contend about words and opinions. I have, by God’s grace, obtained eyes of my own and am able in myself to work in Christ’s vineyard.

51. I speak plainly and freely: whatever is patched together from conjecture and opinion (in which man himself has no divine knowledge upon which he makes conclusions), that <gloss>[ may ]</gloss> is ***Babel***, a whoredom <note>In the biblical sense of spiritual unfaithfulness or idolatry</note>; for conceit or thinking must not do it—no, not any opinion or conjectural apprehension—but the knowledge of God in the Holy Spirit.

<margin>☞</margin>
52. The children of God have spoken as they were driven by the Holy Spirit; they have planted many and diverse trees, but they all stand upon one root, which is the internal heaven. No one can find this unless he likewise stands upon the same root; therefore, the external heaven <note>The physical world or the mundane human intellect</note> cannot find them out or explain them by art <note>Human skill, logic, or academic study</note>.

53. The words of the holy children of God remain as a hidden mystery to the earthly man, and though he thinks that he understands them, yet he has no more than a dark, <gloss>[ historical ]</gloss> glimpse of them. As we see nowadays, how men wrangle and contend about Christ’s doctrine and worship, and fight about God’s will—how he must be served—whereas he is not served or worshipped with any opinion, but ***in the spirit of Christ***; and in truth, men serve God.

54. It does not depend on what ceremonies and manners we use; everyone labors in his work and gifts from his own ***constellation*** <note>In Boehme's mystical philosophy, this refers to a person's inner nature, spiritual alignment, or "signature"</note> and property, but all are driven and led by one and the same Spirit; otherwise, God would be finite and measurable, if the gifts...

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that true spiritual knowledge must be experienced personally through the Holy Spirit rather than learned through academic "conjecture" or institutional opinions, which he labels as "Babel." He emphasizes that while the "children of God" may express their faith through different gifts and methods, they are all rooted in the same internal, heavenly source.</summary>

# The Fifth Epistle

...were only one; but he is a common <note>original: "meane"</note> wonder—whoever understands him walks within his wonders.

<margin>Or, Take</margin>
55. I share this with you in all sincerity, out of a true Christian zeal, from my own source, gifts, and knowledge. I urge you to understand it in the right sense, as it is intended. I do not praise or promote myself, but I speak as a brother to your mind, to encourage and comfort you, so that you should not think the yoke of Christ is heavy. Often, the external man <term>external man: the physical, earthly self driven by reason and desire</term> clouds the internal man <term>internal man: the spiritual self or soul reborn in Christ</term>, so that the poor soul mourns for its image. Yet, that image is purified, truly conceived, and brought forth under suffering <note>original: "Tribulation"</note> [and the cross of Christ]. It is the same for me and other Christians; do not think it *strange*.

<margin>☞</margin>
56. It is very good when the poor soul is in combat; it is much better than when it is imprisoned and yet plays the hypocrite, making devout shows. It is written, *That all things shall work together for the good of those who love God.*

57. Now, when the struggle of the soul arises and continues because it longs to see God but cannot always reach Him, know that it fights for the noble trophy of which the external man knows nothing. Indeed, the Spirit of God fights within the natural soul for that which is supernatural, so that He may lead the creature into God. He would always gladly crown the soul with the precious image, if blind reason would only give Him room and allow the understanding to cooperate.

<margin>☞</margin>
58. We must work and strive against external reason, as well as against flesh and blood. We must completely oppose the assaults and objections of the Devil, always breaking them and casting them away, and resisting evil thoughts, impulses, and influences. With our whole soul, we must effectively press into God's mercy through prayer, supplication, or internal resignation <term>internal resignation: the spiritual practice of surrendering one's own will to the will of God</term>.

59. In this way, the precious grain of mustard seed is sown. If it is well-tended, it grows as large as a tree, and the fruits of Paradise later grow upon it. The soul feeds upon these fruits when it *prophesies* and *speaks of the Kingdom of God*. When the soul perceives the *Divine Magia* <term>Divine Magia: the creative power or spiritual wisdom of God by which He manifests His wonders</term>, it then *speaks of the wonders of God*.

60. For the Being of God is undivided; it requires no room or place, but it arises [moves and flies] within the spirit of understanding, like the brightness of the sun in the air. It shoots or flashes into the Image like lightning, by which the whole body is often ignited and enlightened.

<margin>☞</margin>
61. Furthermore, know that in this life we are laborers and not idle people, for the birth of life is a continual struggle and labor. The more we labor in God's vineyard, the more fruit we shall obtain.

<summary>This page discusses the internal spiritual conflict between the "external" earthly reason and the "internal" soul, encouraging the reader to persist in prayer and resignation to God. It describes the growth of faith as a mustard seed that eventually allows the soul to experience the "Divine Magia" and God's inner light.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, internal man vs external man, spiritual combat, Divine Magia, Mustard seed, resignation, Christian mysticism, spiritual labor</keywords>

--- Page 196 ---
<meta>Continuing from the previous page where the author discusses the spiritual significance of one's labors and the "Mystery" of God's deeds.</meta>

<page-num>78</page-num>
# The Fifth Epistle.

...and eternally enjoy; and it serves for our own edification, for our labor remains in our own *Mystery* <note>original: "Mystery," a term Boehme uses for the hidden divine potential or spiritual "womb" within the soul</note> towards God’s deeds of wonder, and for our own eternal *crown and glory* before God, as is set forth at length in my other *writings*.

> 62. Concerning the *Sabbath* <note>The author is likely referring to "Sabbatarianism" or the "Millennial Sabbath"—the belief in a literal thousand-year reign of peace on Earth</note> in *this world*, which you have written about and in which you still hold the same opinion: the knowledge and understanding of this has not been given to me. I also do not know how there can be any perfect existence in the "torment-house" of the stars and elements <note>The physical universe, which Boehme viewed as a place of conflict between opposing cosmic forces</note>. I cannot find such an understanding within the Mystery, given that the first man [Adam] was not able to stand even when the heavenly Governor ruled within him, but was overcome by the kingdom of the stars and elements. Might it not, therefore, still be dangerous?

> 63. When we consider its possibility and impossibility within the Mystery, it then appears as if there will be no Sabbath in the sad and sorrowful mirror of the Divine Being. For the Devil is a prince of this world; and even if he were bound for a thousand years in the dark world, the fierce, wrathful stars—as well as heat and cold—still hold rule and dominance in this world. Indeed, this world is nothing else but a *valley of misery*.

> 64. Now, if the dominion of the stars did not reach us or have any influence upon us, we would not be in this world at all, but in Paradise, where no wicked person could oppose us or even see us. For in Paradise, we are swallowed up or enclosed within God.

> 65. Just as we cannot see the angels with our earthly eyes, a wicked person of this world cannot see a new <gloss>[regenerate]</gloss> person in Christ. When we attain the "new man" in Christ, we are—regarding that new self—already in the Sabbath, and we only wait for the *redemption* <note>The final liberation from physical, earthly suffering and the "wicked" nature of mortal life</note> from this wicked earthly life.

<margin>☞</margin>
> 66. For we are with Christ in God; we are planted together with Him into His death. We are buried in Him and arise as the "new man" out of the grave with Him, and we live eternally in our own being or essence—that is, in His bodily form. We are with and in Christ, in God, and God is in us. Where, then, should we keep the Sabbath? Not in this world, but in the angelic world, in the world of light.

> 67. And if the wicked were to be tormented for a thousand years in this world, then the location <gloss>[of this world]</gloss> would have to be transformed or withdrawn into the dark world. For in this solar world, there is not yet any hellish torment; however, if the sun were taken away, it would indeed be so. Then the wicked would be even further separated from the judgment, and there would be a gulf of a "principle" <note>original: "Principle," Boehme's term for a fundamental realm or state of existence, such as the Light, the Dark, or the Physical world</note> fixed between them.

<meta>Catchword: 68. Moreover</meta>

<summary>In this passage, Boehme expresses skepticism toward a literal millennial Sabbath on Earth, arguing that as long as the physical stars and elements govern the world, it remains a "valley of misery." He suggests that true spiritual rest (the Sabbath) is an internal state found through regeneration in Christ, leading the believer into the "Angelic World" of light.</summary>

<keywords>Sabbath, Millennium, Jacob Boehme, regeneration, New Man, stars and elements, Paradise, the Mystery, spiritual redemption, solar world</keywords>

--- Page 197 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s theological argument regarding the nature of God’s judgment, the state of the soul after death, and the origins of evil, emphasizing that torment is a self-inflicted state resulting from the soul's own internal nature rather than a punishment actively willed by God.</meta>

<page-num>79</page-num>

<center>### The Fifth Epistle</center>

<margin>☞</margin>
68. Moreover, God is not a God of evil who desires revenge or torment, such that he would, out of his own vengeance, torment and plague the wicked a thousand years before the Judgment. The wicked person torments himself within his own <term>life’s birth</term><note>original: "Lifes-birth"; in the author's theosophy, this refers to the internal process by which a soul's nature is generated and sustained</note>. One form of life is an enemy to the other, and that shall indeed be his hellish torment; God bears no blame for it.

69. He never desired the fall of man; instead, the wrathful nature gained the upper hand, and the spirit of man's will (which is as free as God himself) freely and willingly yielded itself up to the combat, thinking it would dominate and rule.

70. It was out of pride that the Devil fell, and man also. If they had remained in humility, God would have continued to dwell in them; but they both departed from God by their own choice. Yet God loved the image of man so exceedingly that he himself, out of love, re-entered into that image <note>This refers to the Incarnation, where God became human in the form of Jesus</note>. Why, then, would he desire man's torment?

<margin>☞</margin>
71. In God, there is no evil desire. However, *his wrath*—which is the <term>dark world</term><note>In this context, the "dark world" represents the foundational, hidden source of fire and strength that becomes "wrath" when separated from God's light</note>—is a desire for evil and destruction. This is what brought the Devil and also man to their fall. The dark world caused the Devil to fall, and the *external wrathful nature* caused man to fall; yet both of these are tied and bound to one another. We would clearly see and feel this if the sun were taken out of this world.

72. Therefore, I say again: the righteous keep their <term>Sabbath</term><note>Here used to mean a state of eternal spiritual rest and peace</note> in <term>Abraham’s bosom</term><note>A biblical phrase (Luke 16:22) referring to the place of comfort where the righteous dead await the resurrection</note>, in Christ’s rest. For Christ has destroyed the wrathful death that held us captive for our sake; he has opened the way to life, so that as "new men" we are able to spring forth, blossom, and rest in him.

73. But the "old man" of the stars and elements <note>The physical body and the temporal personality governed by the material world and astrological influences</note> must remain in his own region—in his house of torment and misery—until he is committed to the Earth. Then, everything passes back into its own <term>Mystery</term><note>original: "Mystery" or *Mysterium Magnum*; the primordial source from which all physical and spiritual properties emerge</note> again. The soul remains in its own principle until the Judgment of God, when God shall again move and ignite the Mystery. Then, everything will separate itself into its own property; each world shall take in its own harvest, whether it be good or bad. It shall divide itself as light and darkness.

74. And therefore, I entreat you as a brother and a Christian: please take care that you grasp the Sabbath in the *rest of Christ*. Do not be so easily moved by the "enkindling of the spirit" <insert>or the fervent instigation of your mind</insert>, but search to see if you are able to ground your understanding in the <term>light of Nature</term><note>Wisdom gained through the observation of the natural world and its divine laws</note>. If you are able to ground it and reach it in the light of the eternal Nature, then indeed you may proceed; but show it to us plainly.

<summary>The author argues that God is not the source of human torment, but rather that the soul's own inner wrath and pride lead to its suffering. He describes the state of the righteous resting in Christ while the physical "old man" returns to the elements, awaiting the final separation of light and darkness at the Judgment.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, free will, fall of man, dark world, Abraham's bosom, Sabbath, light of nature, afterlife, divine wrath, theosophy</keywords>

--- Page 198 ---
<meta>Continues from the previous page's discussion of spiritual understanding and the nature of divine revelation.</meta>

<page-num>86</page-num>

### The Fifth Epistle.

...plainly so that we may see it; otherwise, our mind will be *unsatisfied* unless it finds the basis <note>original: "ground"</note>.

75. It is not to be proved with *Scripture*, which might seemingly be cited <note>original: "alleadged"</note> for it. Scriptures can just as easily provide the contrary and may well be applied in other ways. If my mind had not turned itself into the LOVE and REST of Christ, I would show it to you in the fashion of this present *controversial world*.

76. The *Revelation* <note>Referring to the biblical Book of Revelation</note> is spiritual and lies or remains <note>original: "coucheth or sticketh"</note> deep within the Mystery. It requires a highly illuminated mind and understanding which has the *power* to enter into the Mystery of God. It speaks *magically*; likewise, a magical understanding is required for it. In this [other] manner, I do not find the magical apprehension, for that is merely a literal <note>original: "historical"</note> understanding.

<margin>☛</margin> 77. He who sets out to study the *Heavenly Magia* <term>the divine creative power or wisdom</term> must know the heavenly figures of the form of the internal heaven: namely <note>original: "Viz."</note>, the center or life-circle from which all beings arise and from which this world is produced. If he does not have the magical guide within him, then let him not meddle with the heavenly figures; otherwise, the *Turba Magna* <term>the "Great Turmoil" or spirit of wrath and destruction that resists divine order</term> has the power to *spew* him out of the *Divine Magia*.

78. *John* the Evangelist, or whoever wrote the *Revelation*, knew the figures of the Divine *Magia*. And though he says that he was caught up <note>original: "ravished"</note> in the spirit and it was shown to him, these are still figures which remain in the Divine *Magia*. Even though they are revealed, they require a *Magus* <note>original: "Magist"</note> who understands *Thesaurinella* <gloss>the little treasury of wisdom or cabinet of the Divine Magia</gloss>.

79. He must understand all three principles with their figures, and then he has the power to open and reveal them; otherwise, his labor <gloss>writings or expositions</gloss> falls into the *Turba*. I speak sincerely: if you please, read my book *The Threefold Life* correctly, and there you shall find the root of the *Magia*. Although there are other much deeper works available, I wish for you to at least understand that one, for it has a sufficient foundation <note>original: "ground"</note>; otherwise, you will not be able to comprehend the others. If it then pleases you to search further, you may very well obtain them, provided there is earnestness; otherwise, they will remain silent <gloss>or seem to be only a bare, obscure history</gloss>.

80. For the foundation of it is deeply magical, as the illuminated mind shall indeed find if it will only dive into it. In that way, the *Revelation* is very easily understood, and in no other manner.

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that understanding the Book of Revelation requires a spiritual, "magical" insight into divine principles rather than a literal or controversial approach. He points to his work *The Threefold Life* as an essential foundation for grasping these deep mysteries and avoiding the destructive influence of the "Turba Magna."</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Revelation, Heavenly Magia, Turba Magna, Threefold Life, mysticism, spiritual understanding, John the Evangelist</keywords>

<page-num>81</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>
<sig>M</sig>
<meta>catchword: other</meta>

It shall not be understood at all except through the Mystery of God; he who is able to dive or sink himself into that, *finds whatever he seeks*.

81. Accordingly, I would very much <note>original: "faine"</note> like you to test your spiritual passion <note>original: "inflammation"; here meaning the state of being spiritually "set on fire" or inspired</note>, so that you might know the *Guide* of the internal world, and then also the *Guide* of the external world. In this way, the Magical School of both worlds might be known to you, and then the noble mind would be freed from opinion and conjecture, *for in conjecture there is no perfection*.

<margin>☛</margin>
82. The spirit must be receptive to and fitting for the Mystery, so that God's Spirit may be the guide in its vision; otherwise, it only sees in the outward Mystery: *namely*, in the external heaven of the constellations <note>original: "Constellation"; referring to the astrological and macrocosmic influences of the physical stars</note>, which often intensely ignite and drive the mind of man. Yet such a person does not have the Divine Magical School, which consists purely in a plain, childlike mind.

83. The external guide labors and contemplates only in the mirror <note>original: "Glasse"; a common metaphor for the indirect reflection of truth in the physical world</note>, but the internal guide contemplates in the Essence <term>Essence: The true, spiritual substance or "being-ness" behind physical appearances</term>. Yet the spirit is not able to do this unless God’s Spirit guides it; therefore, God makes the choice.

84. Whomever the heavenly school takes, he is made a *wise-gift* <note>original: "Ma-gift"; likely a wordplay combining "Magus" (a wise man or master of divine secrets) and "gift," suggesting one whose wisdom is a divine endowment rather than earned</note> without his own frantic effort. Although he must still work hard, he is chosen by God and *driven by the Holy Spirit*.

<margin>☛</margin>
85. Therefore, a person must test *which guide* has taken hold of him. If he finds that he has the Divine light shining in his vision—that his guide brings him into the *heavenly school* on the path of truth, love, and righteousness—and that he is thereby assured and confirmed in his mind with divine certainty, then he may proceed in his work.

86. But if he is in conjecture and doubt, and yet feels a fiery compulsion, then the guide is from this world. This guide ought to be tested by its will and purpose: whether it seeks God’s honor or *its own honor and applause*; whether it willingly resigns itself to the *Cross* <note>The "Cross" refers to the path of self-sacrifice and suffering for one's faith</note> and only desires to labor in Christ's vineyard and to seek the *good* of its neighbor; and whether it seeks God or *bread* <note>i.e., spiritual truth versus material gain</note>. Accordingly, his understanding must judge and either give it permission or reject and tame it as necessity requires.

87. As a brother, I would not conceal this from you in a Christian exhortation, and I entreat you to accept it in no other way than as it was meant—well-intentioned toward you, as my duty requires. For by your request, I am your debtor in Christ, bound to your anxious, seeking mind as one *member* of a body is bound to assist another.

88. Concerning your very Christian offer, I acknowledge and accept it as an act of love, just as one member comes to help the...

<summary>The author explains how to distinguish between divine inspiration and worldly impulse, emphasizing that true spiritual understanding comes from being "driven by the Holy Spirit" rather than by personal ambition or astrological influences.</summary>

<page-num>183</page-num>

## The Twenty-Sixth Epistle

10. For it goes very clearly through the three <term>Principles</term> <note>Böhme’s metaphysical system describing the dark world of fire, the light world of spirit, and the external world of nature.</note> and shows the whole foundation, so that a person (unless they will willfully be blind) may know God, the Kingdom of Heaven, and also themselves. It also shows our lamentable fall and the restoration in the life of Jesus Christ; it shall especially treat the beautiful <term>lilies</term> <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "Time of the Lily" represents a future age of spiritual peace, purity, and direct knowledge of God.</note> which God will bestow upon the last world, which will be very pleasant to read.

11. And though I do not know the full and whole foundation of what it shall be, yet I see it in great depth. I hope, if God grants enough grace, not to give up until it is finished; and also in the future to finish the promised writings which are now kept back by the oppressor <note>original: "Oppreſſour"; likely referring to the religious or civil authorities who censured Böhme's work.</note>, as well as <insert>my exposition</insert> on Moses, where the great wonders of God shall be clearly revealed in the light of day—which God will freely bestow and afford to the last world.

12. However, at present, everything is in <note>original: "Babel"; Böhme's term for the confused, corrupt institutional churches and political systems of his time.</note> **Babel**, and a great split shall be made there. Yet, let no one despair; for just as God helped the children of Israel with consolation during the Babylonian captivity and sent them prophets, even so shall lilies now also grow up in the midst of thorns—and this is wonderful.

13. Also, no one needs to think that the total destruction of the city of **Babel** shall happen right now; there shall truly be an exceedingly great split—one that men today do not believe. For the **Antichrist** is not *wholly* revealed, though he is certainly revealed in part.

14. People shall suppose that they have completely rooted him out, and after some sorrow, great joy shall follow. They shall make laws and covenants, together with severe, sharp articles of religion; yet for the *most part*, these will be for the advancement of their own honor and power. People shall suppose that the Holy Spirit of Heaven is speaking and that there is now a *golden world*; yet it remains full of God’s anger and is still in **Babel**, and the true essence of the right life in Christ is not yet within it.

15. Also, the *Rider* on the *Pale Horse* <note>A reference to the Fourth Horseman of the Apocalypse (Death) in Revelation 6:8.</note> shall follow and cut down *many* with the sickle. Yet, in the meantime, the *lily* blossoms among the wonders, against which the last *Antichrist* shall raise *persecution*. Even then his end comes, for the appearance of the Lord strikes him with terror.

<margin>Authority or permission.</margin>
16. And then **Babel** burns up in the zeal and anger of God; this is a wonder, of which I have no power to write more plainly. Yet, at that time, my writings shall be very useful; for a time is coming from the LORD which does not come from the <term>Starry Heaven</term> <note>The realm of worldly astrology and physical influence, as opposed to direct divine revelation from the spiritual realm.</note>.

17. Blessed is he who seeks the Lord with full earnestness, for He shall not be found in mere history, but in true <note>original: "affiance"; trust, faith, or a solemn commitment.</note> **affiance** and in the right...

<summary>Böhme discusses his forthcoming spiritual works and prophesies a period of religious upheaval, warning that a coming "golden world" will be a false peace before the true spiritual "Time of the Lily" arrives.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Three Principles, Babel, Antichrist, Time of the Lily, Pale Horse, Starry Heaven, prophecy, spiritual restoration</keywords>

<page-num>184</page-num>

### The Twenty-Sixth Epistle

...true surrender <note>original: "resignation"; a key concept in Boehme's theology referring to the soul's submission to God's will</note> into the life and into the teachings of Christ. Within that surrender, the Holy Spirit shall appear with wonders and powers which **Babel** <note>Boehme uses "Babel" to symbolize the institutionalized, divided, and confused religious systems of his day.</note> at present—in its own inventions [manufactured hypocrisies]—does not believe. Nevertheless, it is certainly coming and has already begun, though it remains hidden from the world.

18. I have sent you the complete work of the *Second Book* <note>Likely referring to *The Three Principles of the Divine Essence*, completed in 1619.</note> through **Mr. Fabian**, but I do not know whether you have received it; since that time, because of my travels, I have not spoken with **Mr. Fabian**. If you have not received it, you may request it from him. And so, I entrust you to the gentle love in the life of *Jesus Christ*, the *Son of God*.

->*Written in haste: Görlitz, Friday before Advent, 1619.*<-

# THE TWENTY-SEVENTH EPISTLE.

->TO<-

<margin>Charles of Endern.</margin>
## **CHARLES of ENDERN.**

---

1. **N**oble, Right Honorable Sir, I offer my humble, ready, and willing service, with all cordial wishes of God's love and grace to your "new creature" <note>original: "new creature" — a biblical term referring to a person spiritually reborn in Christ.</note> in the new man, in the body of *Jesus Christ*; as well as all temporal prosperity and blessing for the earthly body.

2. I have considered your noble heart and mind, which is enkindled and inflamed not only toward God, but likewise toward His children in love. This has exceedingly rejoiced me in *Christ*, and I must in a special manner consider your true earnestness and zeal, which I know and acknowledge. For you have endeavored much regarding my few writings of the first part, and have not regarded the murderous [or confounding cries] of the oppressor <note>The "oppressor" likely refers to Gregory Richter, the primary clerical critic of Boehme’s work.</note>, but sought it out yourself *to read it* and *to copy it out with your own hand*.

3. This gives me the persuasion that God has opened a little gate [of His wisdom] unto you, which was firmly shut to the oppressor; because he sought for it in *art* <note>original: "Art" — here meaning academic learning or human intellectual skill as opposed to divine inspiration.</note> and *pride*. Therefore, it was concealed from him, for he was offended by the hand of the writer and did not consider what is written in the Holy Scripture: *My power is mighty in the weak*; and how *Christ* thanked His Father that He had hidden it from the prudent and wise and revealed it unto babes, and said further, *Yes, Father! For so it has pleased you*.

4. Also, I am truly persuaded and convinced in my spirit that you have not done it out of any curiosity, but as it becomes the children of God, who seek the precious pearl <note>original: "precious Pearle" — a reference to the "Pearl of Great Price" from Matthew 13:45–46, a common metaphor for spiritual truth or enlightenment.</note> and are desirous of...

<summary>In this introductory portion of his 27th letter, Jacob Boehme thanks the nobleman Charles of Endern for his dedication to copying and studying Boehme's early writings despite opposition from religious authorities.</summary>
<keywords>Charles of Endern, Jacob Boehme, spiritual rebirth, the Oppressor, divine wisdom vs. human art, Pearl of Great Price</keywords>

--- Page 204 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's description of the "precious jewel" (divine wisdom or the inner light) and transitions into his personal motivations for writing to his correspondent.</meta>

<lang>en</lang>
<page-num>180</page-num>
<header>The twenty-seventh Epistle.</header>

of it; which if it is found, is the most precious *jewel*, which man loves far beyond his earthly life, for it is greater than the world, and more fair, beautiful, and excellent than the **sun**; it rejoices man in tribulation, and brings him out of darkness into the light; it gives him a *certain* spirit of hope in **God**, and leads him on the *right* path, and goes along with him *into death*, and brings him forth to life out of death; it quells the anguish of **hell**, and it is everywhere *his light*; it is **God's** friend in his love; it affords him *reason* and *discretion* to govern his *earthly body*; it leads him from the false and evil way; and whoever does obtain and keep it, it crowns him with its garland.

<margin>☛</margin>
5. Therefore, noble sir, I have no other reason to write to you but from a desire of sincere <note>original: "unfeigned"</note> love toward the children of **God**, so that I might refresh and recreate myself with them. I do it not for worldly goods or gifts (even though you have offered your kind and loving favors to me, though I am a *stranger*, in a courteous and friendly manner), but for the hope of *Israel* <note>original: "Israel" - here used figuratively to mean the spiritual community of believers.</note>, so that I may greatly rejoice with the children of **God** in *the life to come*; and my labor which I undergo here in love toward them shall then be well rewarded <note>original: "re-compens'd"</note> when I shall rejoice with my brothers, and *every person's work shall follow them*.

<margin>☛</margin>
6. Therefore I am in right earnest, since a *sparkle* of the precious *pearl* <note>original: "Pearl" - a common mystical reference to the "Pearl of Great Price" or divine wisdom within the soul.</note> is given to me, and **Christ** faithfully warns us *not to put it under the table, or to bury it in the earth*; and therefore we ought not *to fear man so much, who can kill the body only and then is able to do no more, but we ought to fear him who can destroy body and soul, and cast them into* **hell.**

7. And though in my time I shall receive but *little thanks* from some, who love their belly more than the kingdom of **heaven**, yet my writings have their gifts and stand for their time; for they have a very precious, worthy, earnest birth and descent; and when I consider myself in my poor, low, unlearned, and *simple person*, I then *wonder more* than my very adversary.

8. But since I know **experimentally** <note>original: "Experimentally" - meaning through direct spiritual experience or practical realization rather than mere theory.</note> in *power* and *light* that it is a mere gift of **God**, who also gives me a driving will for it, so that I must write what I *know and see*; therefore I will obey **God** rather than man, lest my office and stewardship be taken *away* from me again and given to another, which would eternally grieve me.

9. But seeing, sir, that you have gained a longing and delight to read the same, and that (as I truly hope) from the providence and appointment of **God**, therefore I shall not conceal it from you, seeing that **God** the Most High has called you through his wonderful counsel <note>original: "Counseil"</note> to publish the first work; when I thought the
->Oppressor<-

<summary>The author describes the transformative power of divine wisdom and explains that his writing is a spiritual duty born of direct experience rather than a search for material gain. He acknowledges his correspondent's role in publishing his work as part of God's providence.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, divine wisdom, Pearl of Great Price, spiritual experience, stewardship, providence, Israel, inner light</keywords>

<page-num>187</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-Seventh Epistle.</header>
<sig>Bb 2</sig>
<meta>The author continues describing his spiritual inspiration and the external opposition he faces regarding his writings.</meta>

The Oppressor <note>original: "Oppressour"; Boehme often used this term to refer to religious or political authorities who persecuted him for his writings</note> had devoured it; but even then it sprouted forth as a green twig, completely unknown to me.

10. And although I know nothing of myself regarding what God is about to do—and his counsel and the way he will go are also hidden from me, and I can say nothing of myself—the Oppressor might attribute it to me as *pedantic pride* <note>original: "Pedentick Pride"</note>. He might say that I would only arrogantly boast of my small gifts (which were given to me by grace) and thereby set forth the thoughts and imaginations of my heart for my own vain glory and boasting.

<margin>p</margin>
11. Yet I declare in the presence of God and testify before his judgment (where all things shall appear, and everyone shall give an account of his actions) that I myself *know* not what has happened to me, or how it goes with me, except only that I have a driving will.

12. Also, I do not know what I shall write; for when I write, the *Spirit dictates it to me* in great, wonderful knowledge, so that I often cannot tell whether I (in my spirit) *am in this world or not*. At this, I do exceedingly rejoice, and in that state, sure and certain knowledge is imparted to me.

13. And the more I seek, the more I find—and always deeper—so that I many times consider my sinful person too weak and unworthy to address such high *mysteries* <note>original: "Mysteries"; referring to the deep spiritual truths of the divine</note>. Then the spirit sets up my banner and says, "*Arise! You shall live in them eternally and be crowned with them; why are you amazed?*"

14. Therefore, Noble Sir, I give you to understand in a few words the ground and cause of both the will and the research of my writings. If you have a mind to read anything in them, I leave it to your choice. I send you now what was written formerly when I was with you—namely, from the beginning of the twenty-second chapter to the end—where, in very truth, the noble coral <note>original: "noble Corall"; likely a metaphorical reference to a precious spiritual truth or a specific section of his work</note> is opened. My mind shows me that you shall find pleasure and delight in it; and in this, you shall also obtain *a little pearl* <note>original: "a little Pearle"; another metaphor for spiritual wisdom</note> if your mind is approved and directed toward God.

15. Our known and good friend has the rest (which are about thirty sheets <note>original: "Sheets"; refers to hand-written pages or gatherings of paper</note>); he shall give them to you. There are very high and deep things contained in them, and they are very worthy of *preservation*. You shall likewise have them if you please to read them.

16. And although I intended to keep these things somewhat quiet and secret, yet it is manifest <note>Meaning: it has become known to others</note>. For I hear that haughty people desire them, through whom the enemy (being a destroyer) might make them disappear. For I know well what kind of enemy the Devil is, who contradicts and opposes me; therefore, I entreat you to deal wisely and carefully. I shall quickly (if need be) call for them.

<summary>Jacob Boehme describes how his writings are dictated by the Spirit rather than his own intellect, expressing concern that his manuscripts must be protected from "haughty people" and the Devil.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, divine inspiration, spiritual mysteries, persecution, manuscripts, the Oppressor, Devil</keywords>

17. 18. For we find a very precious pearl still budding—not currently known to me in the body, but blooming in the mind; and so, I commend you to the pleasant love of God.

<header>The Twenty-Eighth Epistle.</header>

# THE
# TWENTY-EIGHTH EPISTLE.

## TO
<margin>* Carol von Endern.</margin>
## *CHARLES of ENDERN.

->*The light, salvation, and eternal power flowing from the heart of* **Jesus Christ** *be our refreshment.*<-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
Noble and right Honorable Sir, with all heartfelt wishes for divine salvation and all prosperity: I cannot fail to greet you with this short letter, seeing that your courteous and generous heart has bestowed a measure of grain <note>original: "corne"—in this period, "corn" referred to any cereal crop, such as wheat or rye, rather than specifically maize</note> upon me. I gladly received it and return many thanks for it; and I shall pray to God the Creator and Preserver of all things, in whose power all things reside, to bless you in abundance for it.

2. And although I have *not* deserved this from you and am only a stranger to you, I recognize your kind heart in this toward the children of God. Because you are so very humble for the sake of God and His kingdom, and out of your high status in this world <note>von Endern was a member of the nobility</note> you cast yourself with your favor and love into the simple humility of God's children, I recognize this as the *fear of God* <term>fear of God</term> <note>a phrase meaning reverent awe and devotion</note> and a desire for communion with the children of God. In this communion and fellowship, we are all one body in *Christ*, consisting of many members and creatures.

<summary>Jacob Boehme writes to the nobleman Charles von Endern to express his deep gratitude for a gift of grain, praising the nobleman's humility and his spiritual fellowship with other believers.</summary>
<keywords>Charles von Endern, Carol von Endern, gratitude, humility, children of God, communion, Christian fellowship, charity</keywords>
