## The Twenty-Eighth Epistle

3. At this we should not only rejoice greatly, but also resign ourselves to Him in a unified love, so that His power may be effective and abound within us, and His Kingdom may be conceived and brought forth in us, so that we might be made mutual partakers of His being in a shared knowledge.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* Or, Draw upon the kind heart.</margin>
4. It is clearly manifest to us, not only in Holy Scripture but also in the light of nature, that if one person does good for another—especially if it proceeds from a kind, charitable goodwill, heart, and inclination—then the heart, spirit, and mind of the person who received that good is in turn inclined in favor and love toward their generous friend, and wishes them all blessing and good. As that person presents their own needs and condition before God, they likewise bring the condition of their faithful friend before and into God through their will, mind, and love. This, through the power of God, provides the *kind heart* with rich and abundant blessings, not only for this earthly life; by this means, a path and way is also made into the Kingdom of God. Thus, if the benefactor turns toward God and desires His love and grace, then his *friend’s love* (which has already brought him before God) helps him to press and *force his way* to God. It enables him to wrestle with the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: "Turba"; Boehme's term for the source of unrest, disorder, or divine wrath that disrupts the soul</note>, which is the corrupt infection [or poisonous desire] in which the Devil holds us captive. It assists him in destroying the limit or vessel of the *Anger* <note>original: "Anger"; referring to the divine wrath or the harsh quality of nature in Boehme's cosmology</note>. For my part, I am not only obligated but also entirely eager and willing to do this.

5. Since God has given me, out of His kind grace, a deep and high knowledge of His will and being, I am ready and willing to *serve you*. I will do so both through my prayers *for you* to God and through what I shall set down in writing, provided you acknowledge that it is from God (as I am truly convinced) and have a sincere desire to read it. I shall not hide it from you and will also be eager to have mutual and personal conversation with you.

6. If you should find any misunderstanding within it, I would sufficiently clarify it and inform you further. Or, if anything should seem too difficult to be understood, I would willingly explain it more *simply*. If you wish to *question* anything higher or more profound, I hope in God (to whose will I commit all things) that I will be permitted to reveal it. I shall carefully endeavor in all ways, through prayers to God, to show myself once more in acceptable services of love toward you. I hope in God that He will not deny me this, as my heart is entirely dedicated to it and strives that it

# THE TWENTY-NINTH EPISTLE.

## TO 
<margin>* Carol van Endern.</margin>
## * CHARLES of ENDERN.

1. **R**IGHT Honorable and beloved in *Christ*; I wish you the rich and effective blessing of God, in his power, so that the pearls grounded <note>original: "ground"</note> in the life of *Jesus Christ* may be revealed in the Divine shining light, in your own light of life within you; and that it may bring forth an abundance of fruit for Divine Contemplation and eternal joy.

2. Just as I have always known you to be a supporter <note>original: "Wel-wisher"</note> of the study of wisdom, my whole desire at present is—as an expression of thankfulness for the many courtesies and favors I have received—to be able to share with you what the Most High has recently given me from his well-spring of Grace.

3. And although I do not have the ability to do it whenever I wish, yet my mind is so completely set on fire in its center that I would very heartily and readily share it with my brothers in *Christ*. I continually entreat the *Lord* that he would be pleased to open the hearts of people so they might understand it, and that it may become a truly vital [essential] and living [effect] and operation within them.

4. With sincere intention, I would not hide from you that (since the New Year), at the request of some learned men and people of high status, I have written a treatise on *Election* <note>The theological doctrine of predestination, or how God chooses those who will be saved.</note> or of God's will concerning humanity. I have explained and expanded upon it from such a foundation [or deep basis] that all *Mysteries*—both of the outward, visible, *elemental* world and also of the hidden spiritual world—may be understood therein. To that end, I have specifically

<header>The Thirtieth Epistle</header>

# THE
# THIRTIETH EPISTLE.
# IMMANUEL.
<note>original: "Immanuel" — Hebrew for "God is with us," a common greeting in Jacob Boehme’s correspondence.</note>

1. **M**y very Christian brother and friend: I offer you first all heartfelt wishes of *Divine Love* and further illumination, along with true perseverance, constancy, and the patience to endure the *Cross of Christ*.

2. From the very beginning of your enlightenment, *God* has *sealed* you with the mark of *Christ* for further confirmation; he has *crowned* you to be a *champion* <note>A "champion" in this context is a spiritual warrior or a defender of the faith.</note> so that you might work as a true owner in his *service*.

3. And I urge you as a *Christian* to commit your cause to *God* through *prayer* and supplication in patience, and to stand quietly under the *Cross of Christ*. Be diligent with the *talent* <note>Referring to the "Parable of the Talents" (Matthew 25), meaning the spiritual gifts or responsibilities God has entrusted to a person.</note> entrusted to you; you shall see great wonders, and your *talent* will become more and more pleasant and beloved. For *Christ* has also taught us that we should *forsake* everything for his name's sake and cling only to him, for he requires a pure, *resigned soul* <term>Resigned soul: A soul in a state of "Gelassenheit," or total surrender of the self-will to God's will.</term> in which he will dwell.

4. <margin>☛</margin> You have no reason to be amazed or terrified; *God* knows well for what purpose he employs you. Commit yourself only to him in *patience*, and strive against *reason* <note>Boehme often uses "reason" to describe limited, earthly logic that opposes spiritual truth.</note> which gainsays and contradicts. In this way, you will die to the *world* and live for *Christ*. Then you will carry out your warfare correctly for the sake of his love, and from that you will gain the noble *crown* of *eternal life*, where we shall rejoice and enjoy one another eternally.

5. *God* has planted a *rose garden* for himself in your *young heart*. Take care that the *devil* does not sow thorns and thistles there. Another time will soon come when your rosebud shall bring forth its fruits. Do not strive to hold an *office* <note>A formal position of authority or public status.</note>, but stand still before the Most High, accepting whatever he wants for you. Let the *smoke* of the *devil* pass away; rejoice instead at this *reproach* <note>The social shame or criticism one faces for their religious convictions.</note> found in the mark of *Christ*, etc.

<meta>catchword: THE</meta>

<summary>In this letter, Jacob Boehme encourages a friend to remain patient and steadfast in his faith despite social opposition or intellectual doubt. He uses mystical imagery, such as the "rose garden" of the heart, to describe the internal growth of the soul through total surrender to God.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Immanuel, Cross of Christ, spiritual talent, Resigned Soul, Gelassenheit, Rose Garden, spiritual warfare, reason vs. faith</keywords>

<header>The Thirty-First Epistle.</header>
<sig>Cc 2</sig>

# THE
# THIRTY-FIRST
# EPISTLE.

->**Our Salvation [is] in the life of Jesus**<-
->**Christ.**<-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
1. **D**ear Sir and Christian Brother; I send all faithful, heartfelt, and sincere desires of my spirit for true Divine light, power, and knowledge, with intimate joy in Divine contemplation and in our eternal fraternity in the life of Christ.

2. I have received the letter which you sent, and I rejoice in the Lord my God, who so richly and abundantly imparts his grace unto us and so opens our hearts, so that we, in the joining <note>original: "conjecture"; here meaning the union or meeting point</note> of our gifts, desire to search into his wisdom and wonders.

<margin>☞ * Or, Tree of Pearl.</margin>
3. And your offered friendship is very pleasing and acceptable to me. According to what this letter conveys, I recognize you as a growing and very eager branch on the Vine of Christ; and also as my fellow member and twig on this * <term>Coral</term> <note>In 17th-century mysticism, coral or pearl often represented the pure, crystalline spiritual substance of the "New Man."</note>. I wish, in the power of my knowledge, that it may be a true, constant, and immovable earnestness; for I have no doubt that the precious <term>Coralline</term> branch of the New Birth is begotten in you out of Christ’s spirit and wisdom.

<margin>☞</margin>
4. Therefore, I would also gladly, with all my heart, impart my little sap, strength, and influence from God’s gifts unto my fellow-branches and twigs. I desire to help enliven and refresh them within my limited strength, and also to enjoy their gifts, as we are obligated and bound to do for each other—both by the command of God and by the right of Nature. I am led toward this in an especial manner by my gifts, and for this sake I have spent much time and effort, yet with great desire, longing, and delight; and it has been continually my earnest hope, and the very...

<summary>In this opening of the 31st Epistle, the author greets a fellow believer, celebrating their shared spiritual connection and the "New Birth" in Christ. He expresses a deep desire to share his spiritual insights and "sap" with others, viewing this mutual exchange of divine gifts as both a Christian duty and a natural inclination.</summary>
<keywords>Jesus Christ, New Birth, spiritual gifts, Vine and Branches, Coral, Tree of Pearl, fraternity, divine wisdom</keywords>

--- Page 214 ---
<meta>The author continues explaining his motivations and spiritual journey, focusing on his desire to serve his fellow believers in Christ.</meta>

...aim and purpose of my desire to serve my brothers in the Lord, in the *Vineyard of Christ*.

<margin>☞ |</margin>
5. And though I am a simple man—inexperienced and unpracticed in *scholastic learning* and the arts; nor have I ever been trained to exercise myself in the works of high masters or to comprehend great *mysteries* through my *reason*—but in my outward occupation I have been a *tradesman* <note>original: "Tradesman"; Boehme was a master shoemaker by trade</note>, by which I have honestly maintained myself for a long time. Yet my inward occupation and exercise, with a very earnest and strong desire, has entered into the *mortification* <note>mortification: the spiritual practice of "killing off" the sinful nature or ego</note> *of my sinful man*, inherited from the corrupt *Adam*. My goal was to die to my selfhood and self-will through the death of *Christ*, and to arise in His will into a new spirit and a will of *divine* renovation of mind and understanding.

<margin>* Presumed.</margin>
6. Thereupon, I once so strongly and fixedly *resolved rather to forgo my earthly life than to desist from this purpose and these combats*. What I have suffered therein, and because of it, the Lord knows, who led me through His judgment of my sins; but afterward He crowned me with the most beautiful, triumphant vision of His divine kingdom of joy. To express this, I have no pen sufficient, but I willingly grant <note>original: "beteeme"; to permit or graciously give</note> and heartily wish it to the *reader of this epistle* and to all the children of God.

7. From this triumphant light—or joyful, convincing illumination from God—that has been given to me, I have written for many years. For I obtained enough grace within it to see and know my own book, which I myself am: *namely*, the image of God. Moreover, I was able to behold and truly contemplate the center of all beings and to understand the *Formed Word of God*. I also came to understand the origin and meaning of the compacted, framed, or formed *sensual tongue* <note>sensual tongue: the "language of nature," where the sounds of words correspond to the essence of the things they describe</note> of all properties, and likewise the mental, unformed, *holy tongue*. In this state, I have written many profound books, which will be partly incomprehensible to *reason* without God's light.

<margin>☞</margin>
8. Although I, as an earthly, weak instrument according to the external man, could only with great difficulty at first—being an unpracticed, unlearned man—comprehend and bring this high work to understanding (or express it fitly for the understanding). This is to be seen in the *Aurora* <note>Aurora: Boehme's first book, written in 1612, which circulated in manuscript before being suppressed by local clergy</note>, which is the first part of my writings. I also did not intend to make it known to any man, but I wrote it as a personal memory of that very wonderful knowledge, contemplation, and felt illumination. And though the Spirit indicated to what end it should be, yet reason (namely, the external man) could not comprehend it, but saw only its own unworthiness and lowliness. I kept these writings (the *Aurora*) by me until at last I spoke of them to someone, by whose means they came before the *learned*, who presently...

<summary>Jacob Boehme describes his transition from a simple shoemaker to a mystic, detailing how his intense spiritual struggle led to a divine illumination that allowed him to understand the nature of God and language. He reflects on the difficult process of writing his first work, Aurora, which he originally intended only for his own spiritual memory.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Aurora, mysticism, spiritual illumination, mortification of self, Image of God, language of nature, scholastic learning, shoemaker, 17th-century theology</keywords>

<page-num>147</page-num>
<header>The first and third Epistles</header>
<warning>The top portion of the page is heavily shadowed and dark, making the first few lines difficult to read.</warning>

...manifestly studied and endeavored that it might be taken from me. In that instance, Satan thought to make a bonfire of it, and thereby to scandalize me and cast an odium <note>original: "Odium" - hatred, disgrace, or ill will</note> upon my person. Therefore, I have suffered much for the sake of Christ my Lord, so that I might follow Him correctly in His process <note>The "process" refers to the path of spiritual suffering and transformation modeled after the life of Christ.</note>.

<margin>☞</margin>
9. But just as it happened to the Devil concerning Christ, so it also happened to him concerning my writing. For he who desired and intended to persecute my works actually published <note>original: "published" - meaning the persecution unintentionally made the works public or widely known</note> them, and brought me into a greater, stronger, and more serious exercise. Through this, I was more practiced in judgment and stood stronger in opposing the Devil and his violent assaults in the *serpentine <term>Ens</term> <note>Ens: a Latin term for "essence" or "being." In this context, it refers to the corrupted, snake-like nature of fallen humanity.</note> of the earthly Adam*. I have more and more disclosed and broken open the *gates of the deep* [or the hidden depths of eternity] and have come into the clear light. This has reached the point that my writings are read with delight both far and near, and copied out by many *very learned doctors* and other persons of both high noble and low descent—and all this wholly without my own prompting or effort, but through God's providence.

<margin>* For a better understanding.</margin>
10. I would have very willingly shared some of them with you now, but I do not have them at hand and cannot get them so quickly at present. It is true that some treatises are written, so I hope many a hungry soul shall be refreshed; for the later writings are much clearer and better *understood than the first ones you mentioned to me. But if you would take as much trouble as you mentioned and come to me yourself, and in the fear of God converse with me in divine wisdom, I shall be glad. You may take your opportunity to visit me as you please, for I am continually occupied in writing. Therefore, I have laid aside my trade <note>The author, Jacob Boehme, was a shoemaker by trade before devoting himself to his mystical writings.</note> to serve God and my brother in this *calling*, and to receive my reward in heaven, even though I shall incur displeasure and an ungrateful hatred from *Babel* <note>Babel: A term often used by radical reformers to describe the institutionalized, "confused" churches of their day.</note> and the *Antichrist*.

<margin>† In the imitation of Christ.</margin>
11. I return many thanks for Mr. *Nagel's* greetings, and those of other Christian fellow members who are in the † pilgrimage of Christ, as I am informed. When your circumstances allow, greet them for me most kindly. Mr. *Elias Teikman* has not come to me yet, nor do I know where he is. Mr. *Balthasar Walter* has often mentioned him with love, but I do not know him except in the spirit, for I have only heard of him through others.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* Note.</margin>
12. Concerning my condition, I assure you at your request that I am (blessed be God) very well at present. However, I see in the spirit a great persecution and transformation approaching the country and the people. This is very near at hand, as is mentioned and foretold in my writings. It is high time to go out and flee from *Babel*; therefore, I cannot speak of any rest or settlement. *Everything will simply be full of misery, robberies, murders, and unheard-of devilishness in Christen-

<meta>catchword: Christen-</meta>

# THE THIRTY-SECOND EPISTLE.

---
->Our salvation consists in the effective, working love of Jesus Christ within us.<-
---

<margin>1.</margin> **M**y very loving and Christian friend: I wish you the highest peace with the hearty love of a fellow-member of Christ, desiring that the true Sun of the effective love of Jesus Christ may continually arise and shine in your *spirit, soul, and body*.

<margin>2.</margin> I received your letter, dated January 24th, fourteen days after Easter. I rejoice to see in it that you are a thirsty, fervent, and eager seeker and lover of the true foundation [of the knowledge of divine mysteries], which I perceive you have sought and searched for with diligence.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* Or, living spark.</margin>
<margin>† Or, constellation.</margin>
<margin>3.</margin> That my writings have come into your hands and please you is certainly caused by the appointment of God, who brings lovers to [that which they love]. He often uses strange means to satisfy the desires of those who love a thing, feeding them with His gifts [and graces] and putting an <term>Ens</term><note>original: "Ens". A philosophical and theological term meaning "being" or "essence." In the author's work, it often refers to the spiritual substance or seed of a thing.</note> of the true fire into their love so that it may burn rightly. You may rest assured that if you remain constant in your love for the *truth*, it will open, reveal, and manifest itself to you in its flaming love and make itself *certainly known*. However, the search for it must begin correctly; for we do not attain the true foundation of divine knowledge through the sharp searching and speculation of our reason from the outside. Instead, the searching must begin *from within*, in the hunger of the soul. Reason penetrates no further than its own <term>Astrum</term><note>original: "Astrum". Latin for "star." In this context, it refers to the "constellation" or the set of outward, worldly influences and natural patterns that govern human logic.</note> of the outward world, from which reason has its origin.

<margin>4.</margin> But the soul searches its own *Astrum*—that is, in the in-
<meta>The text ends mid-sentence on the word "in-", continuing the thought from the previous page's catchword "ward".</meta>

<summary>In this introductory portion of his 32nd letter, the author encourages a dedicated seeker of spiritual truth, explaining that divine knowledge is granted by God to those who search with their souls rather than relying solely on external logic or reason.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, divine mysteries, spiritual hunger, Ens, Astrum, reason vs. revelation, spiritual growth</keywords>

<page-num>200</page-num>
<header>The Thirty-Second Epistle</header>

inward spiritual world, from which this visible world has its origin, outflow, and production, and where its ground [and foundation] stands.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* The Great Mystery <note>original: Mysterium Magnum</note></margin>
5. But if the soul wishes to search its own <term>Astrum</term> <note>original: spiritual constellation; referring to the internal spiritual blueprint or star of the soul</note>—that is, the *Great Mystery* [or the eternal divine nature]—it must first wholly yield up all its power and its will to divine love and grace. It must become like a child and turn itself to its Center through repentance and a desire to do nothing except that which the Spirit of God is pleased to search through it [or employ it for].

6. And when it has thus yielded and resigned itself, seeking nothing but God and its own salvation—and also how it may serve and love its neighbor—and then finds within itself a desire to have divine and also natural knowledge, it may assure itself that it is then *drawn* [and inclined] *to it by God*. Then it may rightly search and find the deep foundation that is mentioned in my writings.

7. For the Spirit of God searches through that soul and brings it at length into the depths of the deity, as Saint Paul says: *the Spirit searches all things, yes, the deep things of God.*

<margin>☞</margin>
8. Dear Sir, it is a simple, childlike way that leads to the highest wisdom; the world does not know it. You do not need to seek for wisdom in remote places or travel into strange countries for it; she stands at the door of your soul and knocks. If she finds an empty, resigned, and free place in the soul, she will reveal herself there indeed, and rejoice in it more than the sun does in the elements. If the soul yields itself up to wisdom for full possession, then she penetrates it with her flaming fire of love and unlocks all mysteries to the soul.

9. Sir, you may perhaps wonder how a plain layman could come to understand such high mysteries, having never read them nor heard them from any man. But, dear Sir, I tell you that what you have seen in my writings is *but a glimpse of the mysteries*, for a man cannot write them down. If God should consider you worthy to have the light kindled in your soul, you would see, taste, smell, feel, and hear unspeakable words of God concerning this knowledge; and there is the true <term>Theosophical School of Pentecost</term> <note>A reference to the biblical event where the Holy Spirit descended on the apostles, granting them immediate divine understanding; Boehme uses it to describe direct revelation.</note> where the soul is taught † by God.

<margin>† From or by.</margin>
10. After this, there is no more need for searching or painful toiling [over it], for all gates stand open. A very simple, common man may attain it if he does not hinder himself by his own willful [imaginations] and striving; for it lies within man *already* and only needs to be awakened [stirred up or quickened] by the Spirit of God.

11. In my talent [or writings] (as in my simplicity I was able
<meta>catchword: able</meta>

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that divine wisdom is attained through spiritual resignation and a childlike heart rather than through travel or academic study. He describes this direct revelation as the "School of Pentecost," where the soul is taught directly by God's Spirit.</summary>

## The thirty-second Epistle.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* That is, the book entitled *The Way to Christ*, containing a treatise on Repentance, Resignation (or Self-denial), and Regeneration.</margin>
...able to describe it) you shall easily find the way to it, especially in this annexed *book, which is also part of my talent <note>original: "Talent"; referring to his God-given spiritual gifts</note>; and was published in print only a few weeks ago. Sir, I present this to you in love as to my Christian fellow member, and I exhort you to read it over often. Its virtue is such that the more it is read, the better it is liked (or, the longer it is known, the better it is loved). In this book, you will see a true, brief foundation—and it is a sure foundation—for the author has found it so by experience in this practice.

<margin>☞</margin>
12. But regarding the foundation of the high natural mysteries, for which you and Mr. Walter and Mr. Leonhart Elvern desire a further and clearer explanation, please inquire with Mr. Walter. I have sent an explanation and other new writings to both you and him. If you like them, you may have them copied. You will find very great knowledge in them. I wish that all of you mi<unclear>ght</unclear> truly understand it. I would have gladly made it plainer, but because of the great depth of the subject—and also in consideration of those who are unworthy—it may not be done.

<margin>☞</margin>
Christ says in Matthew 7:7:
> Seek and you shall find; knock and it shall be opened unto you. None can give this to another. Everyone must obtain it for himself from God. One may certainly give a manuduction <note>original: "manuduction"; a leading by the hand or a guided introduction</note> or direction to another, but he cannot give him the understanding of it.

13. Yet know that *a lily blossoms for you in the Northern Countries.* If you do not destroy it with the sectarian contentions of the learned, then it will become a *great tree among you.* But if you choose to contend rather than to know the true God, then the ray (or beam of light) will pass you by and hit only a few. Afterward, you shall be forced to draw water for the thirst of your souls among foreign nations.

14. If you will take it rightly into consideration, then my writings shall give you great progress and direction. The Signate-star <note>original: "Signate-star"; likely referring to a prophetic or celestial sign marking a new era of spiritual understanding</note> above your pole shall help you, for its time has come (or is born).

15. I will freely give you what the Lord has given me; only take care to use it rightly. It will be a witness for you against the mocker. Let no man look upon my person; this is a pure gift of God, bestowed not only for my sake, but also for yours and for all those who shall come to read them.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. Let no man look any longer for the *Time* to come; it is already born (begun or arrived). Whoever it shines upon, it hits. Whoever is awake sees it, and he who sleeps does not see it. The time has appeared and will soon appear fully. He who watches sees it; many have already felt it. But first, a great tribulation must pass over before it is wholly manifest. The cause of this is the contention of the learned, who tread the cup of Christ underfoot and argue about a child who was never worse treated since men...

# The Thirty-Second Epistle

# THE
# THIRTY-THIRD
# EPISTLE.

## Concerning the Persecution against
## ***JACOB BOEHME*** <note>original: "Beem"</note>,
### **BY**
## ***GREGORY RICHTER*** <note>original: "Rickter"</note>,
### **Primate, or Superintendent of**
### **GÖRLITZ** <note>original: "Gerlitz"</note>.

---

->*Our Salvation and Peace in the life of*<-
->*Jesus Christ.*<-

---

**Mr. *Marcus Mansern*,**

1. Much respected and beloved Sir; I send all cordial wishes of the steadfast, active love of our Lord *Jesus Christ* in *soul*, *spirit*, and *body*. I have received both of your letters and have observed your *Christian* heart, which is sincerely inclined with the love of a fellow-member <note>i.e., a fellow member of the body of Christ</note> toward me and the pure truth. I desire that God would ratify, strengthen, and preserve you in such a purpose, so that you may grow in the *Tree of Life*, *Jesus Christ*, and bring forth an abundance of good fruit.

2. Your own reason will allow you to know that the hand of the Lord, according to His will, has guided me until now and brought me 

<sig>D d 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: to</meta>

<summary>This page introduces the thirty-third epistle of Jacob Boehme, addressed to Marcus Mansern, which discusses the opposition he faced from Gregory Richter, the Superintendent of Görlitz.</summary>

<page-num>204</page-num>

# The Thirty-Third Epistle.

<margin>* Or, Cut off.</margin>
2. [I have attained] to such knowledge; with which I have again willingly and readily served many, seeing that I have not learned my knowledge in the schools or from books, but *from the great Book of all Beings, which the hand of the Lord has opened within me.

3. Since there is in that Book *a Cross* of true understanding, God signs his children (to whom he gives this Book to read) with that same Cross; on which Cross *human death is slain, and eternal life restored.*

4. I want you to know (as a brother) that this same mark is stamped on my forehead as a *Triumphant Trophy*, on which the Resurrection of *Jesus Christ* is imprinted.

<margin>☞</margin>
5. This mark is dearer to me than all the honor and goods of the world—that God has so highly esteemed me, an unworthy man, as to mark me with the *Conquering Sign* of his beloved son *Jesus Christ.*

6. The Devil is terrified by this sign of conquest, so much so that he must nearly burst with anger. Consequently, he has raised up a great storm against me from his Sea of Death and has cast his horrible floods upon me, thinking he could overwhelm and drown me.

7. But his violent streams have so far been completely ineffective; for the conquering Trophy of *Jesus Christ* has defended me and struck his poisonous, darting rays down to the earth. Through this, Satan's pharisaical <note>original: "pharisaicall"; referring to self-righteous, legalistic religious opponents, likened to the Pharisees of the New Testament</note> murderous poison has been revealed in many hundreds of people who, since that time, have turned themselves toward the mark of *Christ*. Thus, I see with joy that all things must turn out for the best for those who love God.

8. The report or outcry that reached you was nothing more than pharisaical revilement and scorn, spread by means of a scandalous, reproachful, and lying pamphlet of a single sheet written in *Latin* <note>original: "Latin tongue"</note>. In it, Satan has plainly set forth and exposed the pharisaical heart. By God's permission, this has happened so that people might learn to recognize and flee from the poison of this pharisaical heart.

9. And I confidently believe that the crudest devil dictated that pamphlet, for his claws are clearly and plainly discovered within it. It can be clearly seen that he is a liar and a murderer. It shall serve as a *warning* to many to take better care of their souls, as nearly all the learned men here ascribe this pamphlet to Satan.

10. The cause of this rage and fury was the printed book concerning *Repentance and True Resignation* <note>Referring to Boehme's work *The Way to Christ*, specifically the treatises on repentance and the resignation of the self to God</note>, which has been very profitable to many.

11. This severely <note>original: "shrewdly"</note> vexed the pharisaical spirit, that such a...

<meta>catchword: ground</meta>

<summary>Boehme claims his spiritual insights come directly from God rather than academic study and describes the intense religious persecution and "pharisaical" slander he has faced following the publication of his writings on repentance.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, spiritual knowledge, Book of All Beings, persecution, Pharisaical spirit, The Way to Christ, spiritual warfare, Latin pamphlet</keywords>

--- Page 223 ---
<page-num>205</page-num>
<header>The Thirty-Third Epistle</header>

ground should be revealed <note>original: "manifested"</note>; thinking that such a doctrine and life should be expected and required of him as well. This is not well-received so long as men live in the lust of the flesh and serve the "belly-god" <note>This term refers to those who prioritize physical appetites or gluttony over spiritual matters.</note> in carnal pleasure and delight.

12. Know, however, for guidance, that his insults and lies only serve to publicize and make known my book; so much so that everyone—from the high-ranking and learned to the lowly—desires to read it and holds it in great demand.

<margin>† Or, spread <note>original: "Sounded"</note></margin>
13. This book has, in a short time, spread throughout Europe and is highly valued, even in the *Prince Elector's Court of Saxony*. I have been invited there for a conference with high-ranking people, which I have agreed to attend at the end of the *Leipzig Fair*. Who knows what may be achieved? Perhaps the mouth of the bold slanderer may be stopped and the truth made known and revealed.

<margin>☛</margin>
14. I exhort and entreat you, therefore, to wait with patience, in *Christian* love and joy, for the coming and revelation of *Jesus Christ* and his appearance, which shall soon break forth and destroy the works of the Devil.

15. I do not fear the Devil. If God wishes to use me any longer as his instrument, he will no doubt defend me well enough. For the truth needs no defense; its defense is this, as *Christ* says: *"When you are persecuted for my name’s sake, and when they speak all manner of evil against you falsely for my sake, rejoice; your reward is in Heaven."* Also, *"let him who is just remain just still, and he who is wicked remain wicked still; everyone shall reap what he has sown."*

16. Seeing then that my way of life <term>conversation</term> <note>original: "conversation"; in the 17th century, this meant one's conduct, social dealings, or citizenship, rather than just talking.</note> is in Heaven, and as for my body and soul, I run toward that place where my spirit already dwells in *Christ*, what benefit then is temporary dignity and honor to me? I only suffer persecution in the body, and not in the soul.

<margin>* That is, completely stripped of this corrupt flesh, which cannot inherit the Kingdom of God.</margin>
17. What need have I to fear the shell that covers the spirit? When the shell is gone, I will be entirely in Heaven with an open <note>original: "naked"</note> face. Who will deprive or rob me of this? No one. Why then should I fear the world in a heavenly cause?

18. If the cause and matter were evil, why would I suffer disgrace and reproach, and live in trouble, misery, and fear? Why wouldn't I get rid of it? But if it is good, why should I despair, seeing as I know whom I serve? Namely *Jesus Christ*, who makes me conform to his image.

<margin>*Note.* ☛</margin>
19. Since he died and rose again, why should I not also be willing to suffer, die, and rise with him? Indeed, his *Cross* is my daily dying, and his *Ascension* into heaven is performed in me every day.

20. Yet I wait for the crown of victory <note>original: "conquest"</note> which *Jesus Christ* has prepared, and I am still in combat and wrestling as a champion.

<summary>In this portion of his 33rd epistle, the author reflects on the persecution he faces, noting that the slanders against him have unexpectedly increased his book's popularity among all social classes. He expresses his spiritual resolve, viewing his physical suffering as secondary to his spiritual life in Christ.</summary>
<keywords>persecution, Prince Elector of Saxony, Leipzig Fair, spiritual warfare, martyrdom, imitation of Christ, Jakob Böhme</keywords>

# The Thirty-Third Epistle

20. ...and I exhort you as my fellow-wrestler <note>Boehme uses "fellow-wrestler" as a metaphor for a spiritual companion sharing in the struggle of faith.</note> to fight a good fight in faith, and wait in patience for the Revelation of *Jesus Christ*; and continue to stand fast.

21. For this smoking fire-brand <note>original: "fire-brand"; likely a reference to a specific critic or the general heat of the persecution Boehme was facing at the time.</note> which now smokes shall shortly be consumed in the fire; and then the remnant shall rejoice. Then it will be manifest what I could have written to you—which for the present is slandered and reviled, yet only by the *Ignorant*.

22. But the wise will observe, mark, and take notice of it; for they observe the signs of the times and see the darkness and also the dawning of the day.

23. Beloved Mr. *Marcus*; it rejoices and gladdens me that you and your father are in good health. I have received the box of comfits <note>original: "comfits"; sugar-coated seeds, nuts, or fruit used as sweets or for medicinal purposes.</note> and return my thanks to you for them.

24. I would gladly write again to the *Apothecary* <note>original: "Apothecary"; a historical term for a pharmacist or medical professional.</note> at *Wildan* in *Livonia*, if I only had an opportunity to send mail that way; I pray, let me know when you have one.

25. That which my native country casts away, other nations shall take up.

I send to you and your father each a copy of my books; some are for good friends, for I heard that you had previously obtained some of them for all of you. <note>Paragraph 26 is unnumbered in the source text.</note>

27. I have none of my other writings at hand; I would have gladly sent some of them to you, but I hope to come to those parts shortly. Then, God willing, I will bring some along with me. I commend you to the kind and gracious love of *Jesus Christ*.

---

->Dated, March 5, 1624.<-

->J. B.<-

# THE THIRTY-FOURTH EPISTLE.
## TO FREDERICK CRAUSSEN.

---

->Our salvation is in the life of *Jesus Christ* in *us*.<-

---

1. **DEARLY** respected Sir; I send all hearty wishes for Divine Love, and all promised bodily health and prosperity. May it please God, according to His will, to ease and turn away your fragile <note>original: "crasie"; in this period, the word meant physically frail or sickly rather than mentally ill.</note> distemper <note>meaning: illness or ailment.</note>

2. The state of my body is still tolerable, for which I thank God; but it is completely smeared with the Pharisaical mud of slander <note>original: "dirt of Aspersion"</note>, so that the common people can scarcely discern or acknowledge me to be a man.

3. Satan is so entirely and grievously enraged against me and my printed book; he rages and raves so excessively in the chief Pharisee <note>This likely refers to Gregory Richter, the pastor of Görlitz, who was the author's primary religious opponent.</note> as if he would devour me. But for these past three weeks, the great fire has begun to be somewhat calmed, because he perceives that many hundreds of people are speaking out against him—those whom he has cursed, reviled, and condemned just as he has me. He has so horribly spattered me with lies that I can scarcely be recognized.

4. Thus *Christ* covers His bride and the precious pearl because of the unworthiness of men; for anger is kindled in them, and vengeance is at hand.

<margin>* Trial, or proof.</margin>
5. I pray for them and they curse me; I bless, and they revile me; and I stand in the * trial <note>original: "Proba"; a Latin term for a test or proof of character.</note> and bear the mark of *Christ* on my forehead.

<summary>The author describes the intense personal and religious persecution he is enduring from church authorities, comparing his suffering to a trial of faith while praying for his detractors.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Frederick Craussen, persecution, Pharisee, Gregory Richter, slander, trial of faith, religious conflict, 17th-century medicine</keywords>

--- Page 226 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's reflections on the spiritual necessity of suffering and the "process of Christ," likely following a discussion on personal trials or ecclesiastical opposition.</meta>

<page-num>208</page-num>
# The thirty-fourth Epistle.

6. Yet my soul is not dismayed by it; instead, it considers it a victorious sign of Christ. For a person must be properly placed into the process of Christ <note>the mystical journey of following Christ’s life, suffering, and resurrection</note> so that they may be made like his image.

7. For Christ must be continually persecuted, reviled, and slain; he is a sign that is spoken against <note>original: "a signe which is spoken against," a reference to Luke 2:34</note>. But upon whomever he falls, he bruises and destroys the inborn malice and iniquity of the Serpent <note>the Devil</note>.

8. Since I have taught others this way (as I must teach it), why should it be grievous and irksome to me to walk in it myself? Nothing is happening to me other than what I have told others would happen; it is going exactly as it should, for this is the pilgrim's path of Christ.

9. For true Christians are only guests and strangers here; they must travel through the Devil’s Kingdom to reach their own home, through the thistles and thorns of God's curse. Here we must wrestle steadfastly and overcome until we get through; *and then we shall be well rewarded.*

10. For those who now judge us shall be set before our eyes on the day of God's judgment, where we, along with Christ, shall pass sentence upon them. What will they say to us then? Will they not be silent <note>original: "dumb"</note> and judge themselves, just as they judge us now? Oh, if they could only consider it here, and now stop and turn back from it.

<margin>☞</margin>
11. I wish that God would have mercy upon them, for they know not what they do. They are in greater misery than those they persecute; they are poor imprisoned captives and slaves of the Devil, who so infects, torments, and poisons them that poison is their very life.

12. And therefore they rejoice in bringing forth the thorns and thistles of the Devil—namely, fruits produced in God's anger. The children of Christ (whom they persecute and drive toward their native country) should consider this well: they should pray to God for redemption, not only for themselves but also for these, their miserable, poor, captive, and imprisoned fellow members, asking that God would also enlighten <note>original: "illuminate"</note> them and bring them home.

13. Dear brother, it is now a time of great seriousness; let us not sleep. For the Bridegroom <note>Christ</note> passes by and invites his wedding guests; he who hears it goes along with him to the wedding.

14. But he who refuses, and wishes only to sleep in the lust of the flesh, shall be deeply sorry and grieved that he has slept away such a time of grace.

15. It seems strange to the world that those who are rejected by the Scribes—the learned priests and Pharise— <note>text cuts off; likely "Pharisees"</note> should be acceptable to God.

<meta>catchword: se]</meta>

<summary>The author describes the "pilgrim's path" of the Christian, arguing that persecution is a necessary part of being conformed to the image of Christ. He expresses pity for his persecutors, viewing them as spiritual captives, and urges his reader to remain spiritually awake during this "time of grace."</summary>
<keywords>persecution, process of Christ, pilgrim's path, spiritual warfare, judgment day, mercy, Bridegroom, time of grace</keywords>

--- Page 227 ---
<meta>This page continues the thirty-fourth epistle, maintaining the author's critique of the institutional Church and its focus on intellectual "opinions" over spiritual transformation.</meta>

<page-num>209</page-num>

## The thirty-fourth Epistle.

[ ...those whom they ] do contemn and persecute; and it does not look back to consider what the learned did to the Prophets, Christ, His Apostles, and their successors.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. But present-day Christendom is merely nominal <note>original: "Titular"</note> and verbal—formal and historical only, sticking to lip-service <note>original: "lip-labour"</note> and the devout hypocrisy of the mouth. *The heart is worse than when they were heathens.* Let us seriously consider this and not occupy ourselves with them, so that a seed may remain upon the earth and that Christ may not always be used only as a covering [for the cunning hypocrisy of iniquity].

17. Let us exhort and comfort one another, so that we may hold out in patience (for there is yet a great storm of affliction and tribulation coming), that we may persevere and stand steadfast.

18. For Christianity consists not only in words and knowledge, but in *power*. What are the present contentions about, if not merely conjectural knowledge <note>knowledge based on guesswork or logic rather than spiritual experience</note>, [mental idols, fantastical images, and opinions of men]? They wrangle and jangle in notions and images [which they have blindly persuaded their minds and consciences to adore], but *they deny the power* [of Christianity].

<margin>Text, images [or mental idols].</margin>
19. But there comes a time of trial, where it will be seen what their opinions have been [and what their formal church-ways of worship and blind Cain-like <note>A reference to the biblical Cain, whose superficial offering was rejected by God</note> hypocrisy have been] and how they have stuck to them; when they shall fall from one opinion to another and yet have no rest or certain stability.

20. Ah; their opinions are nothing but *heathenish idols*, just as they were before they had the name of Christ.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>Note</margin>
<margin>† Curiously contrived opinions,</margin>
21. The learned and the rulers seek nothing but their own profit, ambition, and honor therein; and they have set these things up in Christ’s place. But they are only opinion-peddlers [sellers of images and mental idols, the internal superstitious relics of their own reason, Babylonish <note>In this context, 'Babylonish' refers to the confusion of tongues and the spiritual corruption of the institutional church</note> imaginations] which they sell [or offer] for money. He who gives them much shall have devout commendations and praise; to him they will sell plausible † images <note>In this sense, "images" refers to intellectual doctrines or theological constructs</note>, and they do not care for their souls as long as they may gain and enjoy their temporal goods.

<margin>* Or, Perfidious Whore.</margin>
22. O, dark night! Where is Christianity? Has she not turned into a *faith-breaking adulteress* <note>original: "perfidious whore"</note>? Where is her love? Is it not wholly turned to copper, steel, and iron? By what may the present Christendom be known? What difference is there between her and *the Turks <note>Historically used to refer to Muslims or the Ottoman Empire</note> and other heathens*? Are they not just as good?

<margin>☞</margin>
23. Where is her Christian life? Where is the communion of saints, where we are but one in *Christ*—where *Christ* is only one in us all? There is not yet one branch on the tree of Christian life.

<summary>The author delivers a scathing critique of institutional Christianity, arguing that it has become a "nominal" religion of intellectual opinions and hypocrisy rather than spiritual power. He warns of coming tribulations and laments the loss of true Christian love and unity.</summary>
<keywords>hypocrisy, mental idols, Babylonish imaginations, opinion-peddlers, nominal Christianity, spiritual trial, communion of saints</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the thirty-fourth epistle, continuing the author's reflection on religious division and personal persecution.</meta>

<page-num>210</page-num>
### The Thirty-fourth Epistle

...like the other, and there have grown mere adverse twigs, in opposition to one another.

<margin>The confused entanglements of sects and opinions.</margin>
24. O brother, we who stand, let us be wary and watchful, and get out of Babel <note>original: "Babel"; in this context, the author uses the biblical city of confusion to represent the fractured and conflicting religious institutions of his day</note>; it is high time. Although they scorn and flay us, we will not worship the Dragon nor his image; for those who do so shall be eternally punished.

<margin>The New Man.</margin>
25. Do not be offended by my persecution; and even if the same happens to you, remember that there is another life, and that they only persecute our own enemy <note>referring to the "Old Man" or the sinful nature</note> whom we ourselves hate. They can take nothing more from us than the shell [or bark] in which the tree has grown.

26. But the tree stands in Heaven, and in Paradise, in the ground of eternity; no devil can root it out. Let the devil’s stormy gusts pass over it; their oppression and torment is our growth.

27. I am again moved by my adversaries, through the motion of God's anger, so that I may grow and wax great; for now my talent <note>original: "Talent"; referring to the biblical parable of spiritual gifts or "talents" that must be used rather than hidden</note> is published to my native country.

28. The enemy's intention is evil; yet by it, he publishes and spreads my talent abroad. It is greatly desired here, and many a hungry soul is refreshed by it; although the ignorant sort cast out their reproach and scorn against it, you shall yet hear wonderful things.

<margin>The time is born.</margin>
29. For the time has come—of which I was told three years ago by a vision—namely **OF REFORMATION**. I commit the end [the event or the time when it shall come to pass] to God; I do not yet know it perfectly.

30. And herewith I commend you to the love of Jesus Christ.

->Dated the 8th of May, 1624.<-

<summary>In the conclusion of this epistle, the author encourages his reader to remain steadfast against religious persecution, viewing his own trials as a means of spreading his spiritual message and signaling a coming "Reformation."</summary>
<keywords>Babel, persecution, spiritual growth, Reformation, vision, talent, religious conflict, 1624</keywords>

# THE THIRTY-FIFTH EPISTLE.
---
## A LETTER
---
->From<-
---
## *JACOB BOEHME.* <note>original: "JACOB BEHMEN"</note>
---
->Written from **DRESDEN** to **GÖRLITZ**, during the time of his persecution; dated June 13, 1624.<-
---
->TO<-
---
### *Doctor KOBERN.*
---
### *Immanuel.* <note>Hebrew: "God with us"</note>
---

<margin>1.</margin> **M**y very dear Sir and Christian brother; I heartily desire the continual working of the love of Jesus Christ in you, so that your <term>Tree of Pearl</term> <note>Boehme uses this term to describe the inner spiritual growth and enlightenment of the soul</note> may grow great under the <term>Cross</term> <note>A reference to the trials and spiritual sufferings of a believer</note> of our Lord Jesus Christ through such agitation and exercise. May we all, in that power, withstand in faith and in the meekness of Christ the wrathful enemy of Christ, who at present roars 

<summary>This page serves as the formal opening of Jacob Boehme's 35th epistle, addressed to Doctor Kobern from Dresden. Boehme offers spiritual encouragement to his friend, urging him to grow in faith despite the "Cross" of persecution they were facing.</summary>

--- Page 230 ---
<meta>Continuity note: This page continues a discussion on spiritual warfare and the opposition faced by those following the path of Christ, specifically mentioning the devil's rage against the author's teachings.</meta>

<page-num>111</page-num>

## The Thirty-fifth Epistle

<margin>☞</margin>
...like a raging lion; and he sets himself mightily against Jesus and his children, so that we may, along with *Paul*, fight a good fight and at length obtain the prize—*our salvation*—thereby. We must not, for the sake of temporary honor and pleasure, deny Christ in the Truth once acknowledged; for if we did, it would be very hard to recover it again.

2. For my part, I thank God in *Christ Jesus* that he has signed me with his mark and daily makes me like his image. I pray to him that I may continue steadfast; and also that he would deliver my enemies from the abominable death of eternal horror in which they currently lie captive without knowing it, and bring them also into the light so that they may know this way and enter into our eternal fraternity <note>original: "Fraternity"; referring to a spiritual brotherhood or community of believers</note>.

<margin>☞</margin>
3. Yet, to speak judiciously of these things, we certainly see before our eyes that this way is wholly opposite to the *Devil*, because he rages so much against it without cause. It is indeed a very great wonder that he should make such a great stir against so small a piece of devotion <note>The author likely refers to his book *The Way to Christ*, which faced intense clerical opposition</note>; surely it must not "relish," but smell foully to him. Meanwhile, there are many larger books to be found that are full of foolishness and folly—and partly of idolatry—which he does not oppose; but his spite is directed only against the Way to Christ, so that no one might walk in that path.

<margin>
Note: This followed in the seventh year after the author's death.

* *Gregory Richter*, the Primate of *Görlitz*. ☞
</margin>
4. For at this very time especially, he <note>the Devil</note> thinks to set up his kingdom powerfully, and yet he sees currently that it has breaches in it everywhere. For the assailant breaks in from many directions, especially from the *North* <note>A common 17th-century reference to the "Lion of the North" or the Protestant forces in the Thirty Years' War</note>, and we can do no better right now than to ignore this enemy and overcome him with patience under the cross of Christ and with earnest repentance; so at length he will become feeble and faint, and this firebrand will come to an end.

5. For he is but a fiery flash of God's anger, which must be quenched with divine love and humility. We must in no way add fuel to it to make it burn, but rather kill it with Christ's victory. Just as Christ with his love overcame the wrath of God and hell, and crushed death and mightily ruled over all his enemies, then such enemies in the anger of God shall not long prevail. As Christ has taught us, we should feed our enemies with food and drink and rejoice when they afflict us for his sake; our reward is in heaven.

<margin>
The temporal sword, or to make war.

* Or, reproaches.
</margin>
6. It is not good, out of our own self-purpose, to use a <gloss>† worldly sword <note>The marginal note explains this as using physical weapons or going to war</note></gloss> or weapon against such an enemy of Christ; for that would only make him stronger. Instead, with patience and prayer, we shall surely overcome him. I do not care about his unjust <gloss>revilings <note>The marginal note identifies these as "reproaches" or verbal attacks</note></gloss>; I have a clear conscience regarding him. The time is coming quickly that

<summary>The author encourages believers to face persecution with patience and divine love rather than physical force, identifying the Devil’s specific hatred for "The Way to Christ." He mentions his primary antagonist in Görlitz, Gregory Richter, and predicts the eventual failure of such spiritual enemies.</summary>

<page-num>213</page-num>
<header>The thirty-fifth Epistle.</header>
<meta>Continuity: This page continues a letter discussing the spiritual and physical opposition the author (likely Jacob Böhme) faces from local religious authorities.</meta>

# The thirty-fifth Epistle.

...that such an enemy will be cut in pieces by the sword of God’s power.

7. It is fitting that we should consider the time; for *Babel* <note>original: "Babel"—Böhme uses this to represent the confused and corrupt state of the institutional church and the world.</note> burns throughout the whole world, and there is woe in every street. Only, people do not see it yet, but are blind to it.

<margin>☞</margin>
8. I give you very great thanks for your brotherly faithfulness and Christian care in taking some of my wife's things into your keeping. If the High Priest <note>Likely referring to Gregor Richter, the head pastor of Görlitz who persecuted Böhme.</note> insists on assaulting my house, let him do it, so that it may be known in all countries what a maker of uproars he is. It will lead much to the "honor" of him and his followers; he will gain a great deal of credit in the *Electoral Council* <note>The administrative council of the Elector of Saxony.</note> for having stormed my house with his "trusty" officers and broken the windows. If others should do or cause such a thing, the *Council* would not tolerate them in the city.

<margin>\* Or, an evil report concerning the city.</margin>
<margin>† Pentecost.</margin>
9. Therefore, I marvel greatly that they make such an uproar in *Görlitz* and, without cause, provoke such a \* clamor abroad against the city. If the reason for it were investigated, it would sound very strange. Nevertheless, it must be so; for the time is born <note>meaning: the prophesied moment has arrived</note>. Something else will suddenly come; this is only a sign, type, or figure. Since they have so solemnly celebrated the † Feast of the Holy Ghost, He must surely assist them powerfully! By this, it may be clearly discerned what spirit they are children of and which spirit they serve; this will give us even more reason to avoid them. I suppose it is loathsome enough; it reeks of Pharisaical pitch and hellish soot. Oh, that God would finally have pity on the simple people who are so very blind and who pull down the judgment of God upon themselves, which will shortly be poured forth.

10. Their stability is easily seen; what they approve of at one time, they disallow at another. Oh, if some *Jesuits* should come and demand the church back from *Luther* again, what good *Papists* <note>original: "Papists"—A derogatory term for Roman Catholics used during the Reformation.</note> they would make!

<margin>☜</margin>
11. But let it go as it does; silence is best. They hunt for a gnat and think they have caught venison. But within it lies couched a small *grain of mustard seed of the Cross*, which put Christ to death; that seed shall burst their guts <note>A visceral metaphor suggesting that the truth of the "mustard seed" will grow so powerfully it destroys the corruption containing it.</note> and grow to be a great *tree*, and no one can resist this.

12. My wife does not need to have any window shutters made; if they want to break the windows, they may, and then the "fruits" of the High Priest will be seen. Let her have a little patience. If she cannot find a place in *Görlitz*, I will find a place for her somewhere else where she shall have peace enough. But let her stay inside at home and not go out, except out of necessity; let the enemy rage—he will not eat her up.

<summary>In this letter, Böhme discusses the religious persecution he and his family face in Görlitz, framing the local clergy's hostility as a sign of the corrupt state of "Babel" and the approaching divine judgment.</summary>
<keywords>Görlitz, Babel, Gregor Richter, persecution, Electoral Council, Pentecost, Jesuits, Luther, spiritual warfare</keywords>

--- Page 232 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's account of his time in Dresden, where he is navigating the political and religious circles of the Elector's court.</meta>

## The Thirty-Fifth Epistle.

<margin>* The Prince Elector of Saxony.</margin>
13. I must wait yet a while here, and wait to see what God shall please to do; for I have but newly gained a little acquaintance among the "Great ones" here, which happens daily. I am well yet, thanks be to God; but I have not had any opportunity to request anything for my *protection* from the Lords, as the *Prince Elector* <note>John George I (1585–1656), the ruler of Saxony during the Thirty Years' War.</note> has gone on a journey, and some of the chief Lords are with him.

14. Though I do not rely upon any worldly protection, I will venture it in God's name and trust him only, from whom I have received my Talent <note>original: "Talent"; Böhme refers to his spiritual insights as a "talent" entrusted to him by God, referencing the biblical Parable of the Talents.</note>.

<margin>† Doctor Aegidius Strauch.</margin>
15. Next Sunday, there is a conference appointed here at my landlord's, which the † *Superintendent* <term>Superintendent: A high-ranking administrative and spiritual leader in the Lutheran Church, equivalent to a bishop.</term> himself desires to have with me. I am also invited to supper, where some of the Prince Elector’s Counselors of *State* will be present; whatever passes there, I shall let you know at the first opportunity.

16. For he loves my *Treatise of Repentance*; only he would gladly ask, and hear from me personally, concerning some points which are too high for him—specifically, from what *Ground* <note>original: "Ground" (German: *Grund*); in Böhme’s theology, this refers to the fundamental source or origin of all existence and spiritual truth.</note> they flow. I am well pleased with this and shall observe the same.

17. I also expect the resolution of *Privy Counselor Loss* <note>Christian von Loss, an influential official at the Saxon court.</note>, whom I shall visit as soon as possible; and whatever happens there, I will let you know at the first opportunity. I hope all will go well; as God wills, so I will. Who knows what God wants done, or what he will do with me? I very much marvel myself at how I am led so wonderfully *without any intent or purpose* of my own.

<margin>☞ * Belonging to a table or desk.</margin>
18. By this bearer, I send two rixdollars <note>original: "Rixdollers"; large silver coins used as standard currency in the Holy Roman Empire.</note> to my wife for her needs; if she wants anything, she knows well where she may find it. The key to the *drawer lies in the parlor by the warming-pan on the shelf. *Your treatise* lies in the drawer; you may ask for it. Your letter which you sent to *Zittau* has not yet come to my hands. If anything happens, let me know; and if there is no reliable messenger at hand, just send it to Mr. Melchior Berndt at *Zittau*—he has an opportunity to send here every week—and let him know that he may send it away as we have agreed.

<margin>❦</margin>
19. Greet my wife and two sons for me, and exhort them to Christian patience and prayer, and to intend *no self-revenge*, so that the Enemy may have no advantage.

20. The business with Mr. *Fürstenau's* family is indeed not good; nevertheless, it will prove to be no great matter, for it is the High Priest's <note>This likely refers to Gregor Richter, the head pastor (Primarius) of Görlitz and Böhme's chief antagonist, who frequently attacked Böhme from the pulpit.</note> own reproach. It would even turn to his great disgrace if it were properly replied to; I wish his Prince only rightly knew of it, for he would protect him with a witness—they are the "good fruits" of the High Priest.

<vocab>Prince Elector of Saxony, Doctor Aegidius Strauch, Treatise of Repentance, Privy Counselor Loss, Rixdollars, Zittau, Melchior Berndt, Mr. Fürstenau</vocab>

<summary>Jakob Böhme describes his growing influence among the high-ranking officials and clergy in Dresden, including a scheduled meeting with the Superintendent to discuss his writings. He remains committed to divine providence despite the absence of the Prince Elector and sends domestic instructions and money back to his family.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Dresden, Prince Elector of Saxony, Aegidius Strauch, religious conference, providence, Zittau, Gregor Richter, Lutheranism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the thirty-fifth epistle (letter) of Jacob Boehme, likely written during a period of professional or religious controversy.</meta>

<page-num>215</page-num>

# The Thirty-fifth Letter.

<warning>Dark stains at the top center obscure portions of the header and the first line of paragraph 21.</warning>

21. <margin>☛</margin><margin>* Or, peal <note>original: "Peale"; a loud ringing of bells.</note>.</margin> I hope to visit you very soon. Even if I should return here again, it will not cost me my life <note>original: "my Neck"; an idiom meaning he does not fear execution or fatal consequences.</note>. It is <unclear>only</unclear> a wild outcry, for there is nothing in it but the *priest's* bell-clapper sounding so "sweetly" <note>Boehme is likely being sarcastic about the harsh rhetoric of the orthodox clergy.</note>. It is easy to judge whether it is *Christ’s* voice or the *Devil's*. You do not need to be so deathly afraid of this *dispute* <note>original: "Quarrell"</note>; it is not a *matter* <note>original: "businesse"</note> you need to be ashamed of. It is nothing but the bell of *Babel* <note>Boehme uses "Babel" to symbolize the confusion and false doctrines he saw in the organized church of his time.</note> which is rung to give warning [or to sound the alarm <note>original: "Alarum"</note>].

22. Simply stand firm in the Spirit of *Christ*, and then *Christ's* bell will also be rung. May God give them and all of us a good mind.

23. <margin>|</margin> Please greet Mr. *Frederick Renischen*. I could not do anything for him here yet, for things here depend very much on *favor*, and there are many people waiting whenever there is anything to be gained. I would very gladly serve him in *love* if I were only able. I cannot even advise myself yet until God helps me. My [ *son* ] *Jacob* shall remain at *Görlitz* <note>original: "Gerlitz"; the town where Boehme lived and worked.</note> so that his *mother* may have some comfort there until I can arrange things otherwise. This would have happened already if I did not have to stay here; let her only have *patience*. And so, I commend you to the *love of Jesus Christ*.

---

->Translated into *Low German* <note>original: "Nether-Dutch"; at the time, this term was often used for Dutch or the dialects of Northern Germany.</note> from *Jacob*<-
->*Boehme's* <note>original: "Behmens"</note> own handwriting; and from<-
->*Low German* into English.<-
